## III “A Chiel’s Amang Ye Takin’ Notes” / “一个机灵人正在你们中间记笔记”

===Original===
The attitude of the suspicious man tends to generate in others the kind of conduct that seems to justify his suspicions. In most of us there lurks a certain strain of mischief which trustfulness disarms but distrust encourages. The inexperienced kitten which approaches us confidingly with arched back and upright tail, soliciting caresses, generally receives the gentle treatment that it expects; whereas the worldly-wise tomcat, who, in response to friendly advances, scampers away and grins at us suspiciously from the fancied security of an adjacent wall, impels us to accelerate his retreat with a well-directed clod.

===Chinese===
疑心人的态度，往往会在别人身上催生出那类似乎恰好坐实其疑心的举动。我们多数人心里都潜藏着一分促狭，信任能将它化解，猜忌却会助长它。那只毫无阅历的小猫，拱着背、竖着尾，坦然向我们走近，讨要抚爱，通常便得到它所期待的温存对待；而那只深谙世故的老公猫，面对我们的友好亲近却撒腿跑开，躲到邻近墙头那自以为安全的所在，满腹狐疑地冲我们龇牙，倒惹得我们捡起土块，好好瞄它一记，催它快些逃命。

===Original===
Now the proceedings of Mr. H. Weiss resembled those of the tomcat aforesaid and invited an analogous reply. To a responsible professional man his extraordinary precautions were at once an affront and a challenge. Apart from graver considerations, I found myself dwelling with unholy pleasure on the prospect of locating the secret hiding-place from which he seemed to grin at me with such complacent defiance; and I lost no time and spared no trouble in preparing myself for the adventure. The very hansom which bore me from the Temple to Kennington Lane was utilized for a preliminary test of Thorndyke’s little apparatus. During the whole of that brief journey I watched the compass closely, noted the feel and sound of the road-material and timed the trotting of the horse. And the result was quite encouraging. It is true that the compass-needle oscillated wildly to the vibration of the cab, but still its oscillations took place around a definite point which was the average direction, and it was evident to me that the data it furnished were very fairly reliable. I felt very little doubt, after the preliminary trial, as to my being able to produce a moderately intelligible track-chart if only I should get an opportunity to exercise my skill.

===Chinese===
而韦斯先生的种种做派，恰似前述那只老公猫，招来的也是同一类的回报。对一个负责任的专业人士来说，他那套异乎寻常的防范既是侮辱，也是挑衅。撇开更严重的考虑不谈，我发现自己正怀着一种不大光明正大的快意，盘算着有朝一日查明他那秘密巢穴的所在——他仿佛正是从那里，带着那副洋洋得意的挑衅神情朝我龇牙；于是我为这场冒险做起准备来，抓紧一切时机，不辞一切辛劳。载我从坦普尔回肯宁顿巷的那辆双轮马车，就被我用来对桑代克的小器具做了一次初步试验。在那段短短的旅程中，我始终紧盯着罗盘，留意路面材料的脚感与声响，并为小跑的马匹计步计时。结果相当令人鼓舞。诚然，马车的震动引得罗盘指针狂乱摆动，但它的摆动毕竟围绕着一个确定的中心点，即平均方位；在我看来，它所提供的数据已相当可靠。经此初步试验，我几乎不再怀疑：只要有机会施展这番身手，我就能绘出一张大体看得明白的轨迹图。

===Original===
But it looked as if I should not. Mr. Weiss’s promise to send for me again soon was not fulfilled. Three days passed and still he made no sign. I began to fear that I had been too outspoken; that the shuttered carriage had gone forth to seek some more confiding and easygoing practitioner, and that our elaborate preparations had been made in vain. When the fourth day drew towards a close and still no summons had come, I was disposed reluctantly to write the case off as a lost opportunity.

===Chinese===
可看样子我是等不到这个机会了。韦斯先生答应很快再请我出诊，这话并没有兑现。三天过去了，他那边毫无动静。我开始担心，是不是自己话说得太直；那辆封着窗板的马车是不是已转出去，另寻一位更肯轻信、更好说话的医生；我们那番煞费苦心的准备是不是全做了无用功。到了第四天日暮时分，召唤依然未至，我便老大不情愿地打算把这桩案子当作一桩坐失的机会勾销了事。

===Original===
And at that moment, in the midst of my regrets, the bottle-boy thrust an uncomely head in at the door. His voice was coarse, his accent was hideous, and his grammatical construction beneath contempt; but I forgave him all when I gathered the import of his message.

===Chinese===
而恰恰在那一刻，正当我懊恼之际，药童把他那颗不好看的脑袋从门口探了进来。他嗓音粗嘎，口音难听，语法更是不值一哂；可等我明白了他的口信，这一切我统统原谅了他。

===Original===
“Mr. Weiss’s carriage is waiting, and he says will you come as quickly as you can because he’s took very bad tonight.”

===Chinese===
“韦斯先生的马车候着呢，他说请您越快越好，因为今晚他病得怪重的。”

===Original===
I sprang from my chair and hastily collected the necessaries for the journey. The little board and the lamp I put in my overcoat pocket; I overhauled the emergency bag and added to its usual contents a bottle of permanganate of potassium which I thought I might require. Then I tucked the evening paper under my arm and went out.

===Chinese===
我从椅子里一跃而起，匆匆收拾起这趟出诊的必需物件。小木板和灯放进了大衣口袋；我把应急提包翻检了一遍，在惯常的药品之外又添了一瓶高锰酸钾，心想兴许用得着。随后我把晚报往腋下一夹，出了门。

===Original===
The coachman, who was standing at the horse’s head as I emerged, touched his hat and came forward to open the door.

===Chinese===
我出门时，马车夫正站在马头旁边；他碰了碰帽檐，走上前来开车门。

===Original===
“I have fortified myself for the long drive, you see,” I remarked, exhibiting the newspaper as I stepped into the carriage.

===Chinese===
“您瞧，我给自己备足了粮草，好熬这趟远路。”我一面跨进马车，一面亮出那份报纸说。

===Original===
“But you can’t read in the dark,” said he.

===Chinese===
“可黑灯瞎火的，您没法看哪。”他说。

===Original===
“No, but I have provided myself with a lamp,” I replied, producing it and striking a match.

===Chinese===
“是没有，可我自备了一盏灯。”我答道，一面掏出灯来，划了根火柴。

===Original===
He watched me as I lit the lamp and hooked it on the back cushion, and observed:

===Chinese===
我点亮灯，挂到靠背垫上；他瞧着我做完这些，说道：

===Original===
“I suppose you found it rather a dull ride last time. It’s a longish way. They might have fitted the carriage with an inside lamp. But we shall have to make it a quicker passage tonight. Governor says Mr. Graves is uncommon bad.”

===Chinese===
“我琢磨着，上回这一路您怕是闷得慌。路可不近。他们原该给车里装盏随车灯的。不过今晚咱们得把路赶得快些。东家说格雷夫斯先生病得凶。”

===Original===
With this he slammed the door and locked it. I drew the board from my pocket, laid it on my knee, glanced at my watch, and, as the coachman climbed to his seat, I made the first entry in the little book.

===Chinese===
说罢他砰地关上车门，上了锁。我从口袋里掏出木板，放在膝头，瞥了一眼怀表，趁马车夫爬上驭座的当口，在小本子里记下了第一笔。

===Original===
“8:58 W. by S. Start from home. Horse 13 hands.”

===Chinese===
“8:58 西偏南。自家中启程。马高13掌。”

===Original===
The first move of the carriage on starting was to turn round as if heading for Newington Butts, and the second entry accordingly read:

===Chinese===
马车启动后的第一个动作便是掉头，看方向像是朝纽因顿巴茨去，于是本子上的第二笔相应记的是：

===Original===
“8:58:30 E. by N.”

===Chinese===
“8:58:30 东偏北。”

===Original===
But this direction was not maintained long. Very soon we turned south and then west and then south again. I sat with my eyes riveted on the compass, following with some difficulty its rapid changes. The needle swung to and fro incessantly but always within a definite arc, the centre of which was the true direction. But this direction varied from minute to minute in the most astonishing manner. West, south, east, north, the carriage turned, “boxing” the compass until I lost all count of direction. It was an amazing performance. Considering that the man was driving against time on a mission of life and death urgency, his carelessness as to direction was astounding. The tortuousness of the route must have made the journey twice as long as it need have been with a little more careful selection. At least so it appeared to me, though, naturally, I was not in a position to offer an authoritative criticism.

===Chinese===
可这个方向没保持多久。很快我们便转向南，再转向西，又转回南。我坐着，眼睛死死盯住罗盘，勉强追赶它那些迅疾的变化。指针不停地来回摆动，却始终摆在一个确定的弧度之内，弧的中心便是真实方位。但这方位以最惊人的方式一分钟一分钟地变。西、南、东、北，马车一路转个不停，把罗盘上的方位“点了个遍”，转到我彻底数不清了方向。这真是一场令人叹为观止的表演。想想这人是在跟时间赛跑、办着一桩人命关天的急差，他对方向的不经意实在叫人骇然。这路线弯弯绕绕，只要选路时稍多用一分心，路程本不必拉长一倍。至少在我看是这样，不过话说回来，我自然也轮不上发表什么权威的批评。

===Original===
As far as I could judge, we followed the same route as before. Once I heard a tug’s whistle and knew that we were near the river, and we passed the railway station, apparently at the same time as on the previous occasion, for I heard a passenger train start and assumed that it was the same train. We crossed quite a number of thoroughfares with tramlines﻿—I had no idea there were so many﻿—and it was a revelation to me to find how numerous the railway arches were in this part of London and how continually the nature of the road-metal varied.

===Chinese===
据我判断，我们走的还是上回那条路线。有一回我听见拖船的汽笛，知道已行近河畔；我们还经过了那座火车站，看来与上回正是同一时刻，因为我听见一列客车启动，便认定还是同一班车。我们穿过了相当多条敷设电车道的大街——我原先全不知道竟有这么多——而这一带的铁路拱桥竟如此密集、路面铺料的性质又变得如此频繁，着实让我长了一番见识。

===Original===
It was by no means a dull journey this time. The incessant changes of direction and variations in the character of the road kept me most uncommonly busy; for I had hardly time to scribble down one entry before the compass-needle would swing round sharply, showing that we had once more turned a corner; and I was quite taken by surprise when the carriage slowed down and turned into the covered way. Very hastily I scribbled down the final entry (“9:24 S.E. In covered way”), and having closed the book and slipped it and the board into my pocket, had just opened out the newspaper when the carriage door was unlocked and opened, whereupon I unhooked and blew out the lamp and pocketed that too, reflecting that it might be useful later.

===Chinese===
这一回的旅程绝不沉闷。方向不停的变换、路面性质不断的变化，忙得我不亦乐乎；因为一笔记录还没草草记完，罗盘指针往往又猛地一转，说明我们再度拐过了一个街角；待到马车放慢、拐进那条带顶的廊道时，我竟全无防备。我慌忙草草记下最后一笔（“9:24 东南。入带顶廊道。”），合上本子，连同木板一并塞进口袋，刚把报纸抖开，车门便开了锁、被拉开；于是我摘下灯吹灭，也揣进口袋，心想它往后兴许还有用处。

===Original===
As on the last occasion, Mrs. Schallibaum stood in the open doorway with a lighted candle. But she was a good deal less self-possessed this time. In fact she looked rather wild and terrified. Even by the candlelight I could see that she was very pale and she seemed unable to keep still. As she gave me the few necessary words of explanation, she fidgeted incessantly and her hands and feet were in constant movement.

===Chinese===
与上回一样，沙利鲍姆太太端着一支点亮的蜡烛站在敞开的门口。只是这一回，她的自持差得多了。事实上，她看去颇有几分狂乱惊恐。即便借着烛光我也看得出她脸色惨白，而且似乎静不下来。她向我交代那几句必要的说明时，坐立不安，手脚一直在动个不停。

===Original===
“You had better come up with me at once,” she said. “Mr. Graves is much worse tonight. We will wait not for Mr. Weiss.”

===Chinese===
“您最好这就跟我上楼。”她说，“格雷夫斯先生今晚病重多了。我们将不等韦斯先生。”

===Original===
Without waiting for a reply she quickly ascended the stairs and I followed. The room was in much the same condition as before. But the patient was not. As soon as I entered the room, a soft, rhythmical gurgle from the bed gave me a very clear warning of danger. I stepped forward quickly and looked down at the prostrate figure, and the warning gathered emphasis. The sick man’s ghastly face was yet more ghastly; his eyes were more sunken, his skin more livid; “his nose was as sharp as a pen,” and if he did not “babble of green fields” it was because he seemed to be beyond even that. If it had been a case of disease, I should have said at once that he was dying. He had all the appearance of a man in articulo mortis. Even as it was, feeling convinced that the case was one of morphine poisoning, I was far from confident that I should be able to draw him back from the extreme edge of vitality on which he trembled so insecurely.

===Chinese===
她不等回答，便快步上楼，我跟在后面。屋里的情形与上回大致无异。病人却不然。我一进屋，床上传来的一阵轻而规律的咯咯声便给了我一个再明白不过的危险警讯。我抢步上前，低头看那僵卧的人影，警讯愈发确凿。病人那张死人似的脸愈发像死人了；眼窝陷得更深，皮肤青得更透；“鼻尖尖得像一支笔”，而他若还不曾“念叨绿色的田野”，那是因为他似乎连那个也谈不上了。这若是一例病症，我会立刻断言他垂危待死。他浑身上下，全是濒死之际（in articulo mortis）之人的模样。即便如此——我虽确信这是吗啡中毒——也远不敢自信能把他从生机的绝壁边缘拉回来；此刻他正颤颤巍巍、摇摇欲坠地悬在那道边缘上。

===Original===
“He is very ill? He is dying?”

===Chinese===
“他病得很重？他要死了？”

===Original===
It was Mrs. Schallibaum’s voice; very low, but eager and intense. I turned, with my finger on the patient’s wrist, and looked into the face of the most thoroughly scared woman I have ever seen. She made no attempt now to avoid the light, but looked me squarely in the face, and I noticed, half-unconsciously, that her eyes were brown and had a curious strained expression.

===Chinese===
是沙利鲍姆太太的声音；极低，却急切而紧张。我手指还搭在病人腕上，转过脸去，正对上我平生所见被吓得最彻底的一张女人的脸。此刻她再不设法躲避灯光，而是直直地望着我的脸；我还半是不自觉地注意到，她的眼睛是棕色的，带着一种古怪的、绷紧了的神情。

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered, “he is very ill. He is in great danger.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”我答道，“他病得很重。他处境极度危险。”

===Original===
She still stared at me fixedly for some seconds. And then a very odd thing occurred. Suddenly she squinted﻿—squinted horribly; not with the familiar convergent squint which burlesque artists imitate, but with external or divergent squint of extreme near sight or unequal vision. The effect was quite startling. One moment both her eyes were looking straight into mine; the next, one of them rolled round until it looked out of the uttermost corner, leaving the other gazing steadily forward.

===Chinese===
她仍旧目不转睛地瞪了我好几秒。接着便发生了一件非常古怪的事。她突然斜起眼来——斜得怕人；不是滑稽戏艺人爱模仿的那种常见的对眼，而是极深近视或两眼视力不齐者特有的外斜式、即分散性斜视。那效果着实吓人一跳。前一刻，她两眼还直直望进我眼里；后一刻，其中一只已骨碌一转，转到眼眶最外的角落里张望，剩下另一只兀自平视前方。

===Original===
She was evidently conscious of the change, for she turned her head away quickly and reddened somewhat. But it was no time for thoughts of personal appearance.

===Chinese===
她显然觉察到了这一变化，因为她赶忙把头扭开，脸也红了几分。可眼下哪是讲究仪容的时候。

===Original===
“You can save him, doctor! You will not let him die! He must not be allowed to die!”

===Chinese===
“您能救他的，医生！您不会让他死的！绝不能容他死！”

===Original===
She spoke with as much passion as if he had been the dearest friend that she had in the world, which I suspected was far from being the case. But her manifest terror had its uses.

===Chinese===
她说这话时的那股热切，倒仿佛他是她在世上最亲爱的朋友；而我疑心实情远非如此。不过她那溢于言表的恐惧自有其用处。

===Original===
“If anything is to be done to save him,” I said, “it must be done quickly. I will give him some medicine at once, and meanwhile you must make some strong coffee.”

===Chinese===
“若要救他，还有什么可做的，”我说，“也必须马上做。我这就给他用药，与此同时，你必须煮些浓咖啡。”

===Original===
“Coffee!” she exclaimed. “But we have none in the house. Will not tea do, if I make it very strong?”

===Chinese===
“咖啡！”她失声道，“可家里一点也没有。我把茶沏得浓浓的，不行吗？”

===Original===
“No, it will not. I must have coffee; and I must have it quickly.”

===Chinese===
“不行，茶代不得。我要的必须是咖啡，而且必须快。”

===Original===
“Then I suppose I must go and get some. But it is late. The shops will be shut. And I don’t like leaving Mr. Graves.”

===Chinese===
“那我只好自己跑一趟去买。可是天晚了。铺子都上门板了。我又不想把格雷夫斯先生撂下。”

===Original===
“Can’t you send the coachman?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“不能打发马车夫去吗？”我问。

===Original===
She shook her head impatiently. “No, that is no use. I must wait until Mr. Weiss comes.”

===Chinese===
她不耐烦地摇了摇头。“不，那没有用。我必须等到韦斯先生回来。”

===Original===
“That won’t do,” I said, sharply. “He will slip through our fingers while you are waiting. You must go and get that coffee at once and bring it to me as soon as it is ready. And I want a tumbler and some water.”

===Chinese===
“那可不行。”我厉声道，“等你等完这阵，他早从我们指缝里溜掉了。你必须立刻去买咖啡，一煮好就端来给我。我还要一只平底玻璃杯和一些清水。”

===Original===
She brought me a water-bottle and glass from the washstand and then, with a groan of despair, hurried from the room.

===Chinese===
她从脸盆架上给我取来一只水瓶和玻璃杯，然后一声绝望的呻吟，急匆匆出了屋。

===Original===
I lost no time in applying the remedies that I had to hand. Shaking out into the tumbler a few crystals of potassium permanganate, I filled it up with water and approached the patient. His stupor was profound. I shook him as roughly as was safe in his depressed condition, but elicited no resistance or responsive movement. As it seemed very doubtful whether he was capable of swallowing, I dared not take the risk of pouring the liquid into his mouth for fear of suffocating him. A stomach-tube would have solved the difficulty, but, of course, I had not one with me. I had, however, a mouth-speculum which also acted as a gag, and, having propped the patient’s mouth open with this, I hastily slipped off one of the rubber tubes from my stethoscope and inserted into one end of it a vulcanite ear-speculum to serve as a funnel. Then, introducing the other end of the tube into the gullet as far as its length would permit, I cautiously poured a small quantity of the permanganate solution into the extemporized funnel. To my great relief a movement of the throat showed that the swallowing reflex still existed, and, thus encouraged, I poured down the tube as much of the fluid as I thought it wise to administer at one time.

===Chinese===
我毫不耽搁，立即用起手边的救治手段。往玻璃杯里抖进几粒高锰酸钾晶体，兑满水，我便走近病人。他的昏沉已深。我以他衰弱状况下尚称安全的力度，尽量用力摇撼他，却引不起丝毫抗拒或回应的动作。看来他能否吞咽大可怀疑，我不敢冒险把药液直接倒进他嘴里，唯恐把他呛住窒息。若有根胃管，难题便迎刃而解，可我身边自然没有。不过我带着一件兼作开口塞的张口器（mouth-speculum），用它撑住病人的口，我又急忙从听诊器上捋下一截橡皮管，往管的一端插进一只硬橡胶的耳窥器，权作漏斗。然后，把管的另一端顺着长度所及尽量深地送入食管，我小心翼翼地往这只现凑的漏斗里倾入少量高锰酸钾溶液。喉头一动，表明吞咽反射尚存，令我大大松了口气；受了这番鼓励，我便顺着管子，把我认为一次给药尚属稳妥的分量尽量灌了下去。

===Original===
The dose of permanganate that I had given was enough to neutralize any reasonable quantity of the poison that might yet remain in the stomach. I had next to deal with that portion of the drug which had already been absorbed and was exercising its poisonous effects. Taking my hypodermic case from my bag, I prepared in the syringe a full dose of atropine sulphate, which I injected forthwith into the unconscious man’s arm. And that was all that I could do, so far as remedies were concerned, until the coffee arrived.

===Chinese===
我给的高锰酸钾这一剂，足以中和胃里可能残留的任何合理分量的毒物。接下来要对付的，是已然吸收、正在发挥毒效的那部分药物。我从提包里取出皮下注射盒，在针管里备好足量的硫酸阿托品，随即注射进这昏迷者的手臂。在咖啡送到之前，救治手段方面，我能做的就只有这些了。

===Original===
I cleaned and put away the syringe, washed the tube, and then, returning to the bedside, endeavoured to rouse the patient from his profound lethargy. But great care was necessary. A little injudicious roughness of handling, and that thready, flickering pulse might stop forever; and yet it was almost certain that if he were not speedily aroused, his stupor would gradually deepen until it shaded off imperceptibly into death. I went to work very cautiously, moving his limbs about, flicking his face and chest with the corner of a wet towel, tickling the soles of his feet, and otherwise applying stimuli that were strong without being violent.

===Chinese===
我洗净收好针管，洗了橡皮管，然后回到床边，设法把病人从深沉的昏睡中唤醒。但这须得万分小心。手上稍有一步失当的鲁莽，那丝线般忽明忽灭的脉搏就可能永远停住；然而几乎可以断定，若不赶快唤醒他，他的昏迷便会渐渐加深，直到不知不觉间暗渡成死亡。我着手施为，极为谨慎：摆动他的四肢，用湿毛巾角轻抽他的脸和胸口，搔他的脚心，再施以种种强烈而不粗暴的刺激。

===Original===
So occupied was I with my efforts to resuscitate my mysterious patient that I did not notice the opening of the door, and it was with something of a start that, happening to glance round, I perceived at the farther end of the room the shadowy figure of a man relieved by two spots of light reflected from his spectacles. How long he had been watching me I cannot say, but, when he saw that I had observed him, he came forward﻿—though not very far﻿—and I saw that he was Mr. Weiss.

===Chinese===
我一心扑在抢救这位神秘病人上，竟没有察觉门开过；待到偶然一回头，才吃了一惊，只见屋子那头立着一条男人的人影，黑黢黢的，唯有眼镜上反射的两点光把它衬托出来。他窥伺了我多久，我说不上；但待他见我已察觉他，便走上前来——不过没走多远——我这才看出，他是韦斯先生。

===Original===
“I am afraid,” he said, “that you do not find my friend so well tonight?”

===Chinese===
“恐怕，”他说，“您今晚见我这朋友，情形不大好吧？”

===Original===
“So well!” I exclaimed. “I don’t find him well at all. I am exceedingly anxious about him.”

===Chinese===
“不大好！”我嚷道，“我看他根本就不好。我为他忧心极了。”

===Original===
“You don’t﻿—er﻿—anticipate anything of a﻿—er﻿—anything serious, I hope?”

===Chinese===
“您不至——呃——于预料会有什么——呃——什么不测吧，但愿如此？”

===Original===
“There is no need to anticipate,” said I. “It is already about as serious as it can be. I think he might die at any moment.”

===Chinese===
“谈不上预料，”我说，“眼下的情形已经严重到头了。我看他随时可能死去。”

===Original===
“Good God!” he gasped. “You horrify me!”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”他倒抽一口气，“您把我吓坏了！”

===Original===
He was not exaggerating. In his agitation, he stepped forward into the lighter part of the room, and I could see that his face was pale to ghastliness﻿—except his nose and the adjacent red patches on his cheeks, which stood out in grotesquely hideous contrast. Presently, however, he recovered a little and said:

===Chinese===
他这话说得并非夸张。他在激动之下向前跨进了屋里较亮的地方，我看得出，他的脸白得怕人——唯独鼻子和颊上紧邻的红斑例外，二者相衬，怪诞而丑恶，格外扎眼。不过片刻之后，他定了一定神，说道：

===Original===
“I really think﻿—at least I hope﻿—that you take an unnecessarily serious view of his condition. He has been like this before, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我倒真觉得——至少我希望——您把他的病情看得过重了，重得不必要。您知道，他从前也有过这样的。”

===Original===
I felt pretty certain that he had not, but there was no use in discussing the question. I therefore replied, as I continued my efforts to rouse the patient:

===Chinese===
我敢说他从前绝没有过，可这个问题争下去毫无意义。于是我一边继续设法唤醒病人，一边答道：

===Original===
“That may or may not be. But in any case there comes a last time; and it may have come now.”

===Chinese===
“有没有过且不论。可凡事总有个最后一回；这最后一回，说不定眼下就到了。”

===Original===
“I hope not,” he said; “although I understand that these cases always end fatally sooner or later.”

===Chinese===
“但愿没有，”他说，“不过据我了解，这类病例迟早总归一死。”

===Original===
“What cases?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“哪类病例？”我问。

===Original===
“I was referring to sleeping sickness; but perhaps you have formed some other opinion as to the nature of this dreadful complaint.”

===Chinese===
“我说的是昏睡病；不过，对这可怕病症的性质，您或许已另有见解。”

===Original===
I hesitated for a moment, and he continued: “As to your suggestion that his symptoms might be due to drugs, I think we may consider that as disposed of. He has been watched, practically without cessation since you came last, and, moreover, I have myself turned out the room and examined the bed and have not found a trace of any drug. Have you gone into the question of sleeping sickness?”

===Chinese===
我迟疑了一瞬，他接着说：“至于您那番症状或系药物所致的暗示，我想我们可以认定它已被排除了。自您上次来后，他一直被人看守着，几乎不曾间断；再者，我亲自把这屋子翻了一遍，连床铺也查过，未见丝毫药物的痕迹。昏睡病的问题，您研究过吗？”

===Original===
I looked at the man narrowly before answering, and distrusted him more than ever. But this was no time for reticence. My concern was with the patient and his present needs. After all, I was, as Thorndyke had said, a doctor, not a detective, and the circumstances called for straightforward speech and action on my part.

===Chinese===
答话之前，我把这人细细打量了一番，对他的疑心更甚于往昔。但此刻不是含糊其辞的时候。我牵挂的是病人和他眼下的急需。说到底，正如桑代克所言，我是医生，不是侦探；眼下的情形，要求我直言直行。

===Original===
“I have considered that question,” I said, “and have come to a perfectly definite conclusion. His symptoms are not those of sleeping sickness. They are in my opinion undoubtedly due to morphine poisoning.”

===Chinese===
“这个问题我考虑过了，”我说，“并且得出了一个完全明确的结论。他的症状不是昏睡病的症状。依我看，它们无疑源自吗啡中毒。”

===Original===
“But my dear sir!” he exclaimed, “the thing is impossible! Haven’t I just told you that he has been watched continuously?”

===Chinese===
“可是我亲爱的先生！”他嚷道，“这不可能！我不是刚刚告诉过您，他一直有人看守吗？”

===Original===
“I can only judge by the appearances that I find,” I answered; and, seeing that he was about to offer fresh objections, I continued: “Don’t let us waste precious time in discussion, or Mr. Graves may be dead before we have reached a conclusion. If you will hurry them up about the coffee that I asked for some time ago, I will take the other necessary measures, and perhaps we may manage to pull him round.”

===Chinese===
“我只能就我查见的表象下判断。”我答道；见他还要提出新的异议，我便接着说：“别让我们把宝贵的时间耗在争论上，不然不等我们辩出个结论，格雷夫斯先生只怕已经死了。您若肯去催一催我早先就要下的咖啡，我就采取其余必要的措施，或许我们还能把他救回来。”

===Original===
The rather brutal decision of my manner evidently daunted him. It must have been plain to him that I was not prepared to accept any explanation of the unconscious man’s condition other than that of morphine poisoning; whence the inference was pretty plain that the alternatives were recovery or an inquest. Replying stiffly that I “must do as I thought best,” he hurried from the room, leaving me to continue my efforts without further interruption.

===Chinese===
我这副斩钉截铁、近乎无情的态度显然镇住了他。他一定看得分明：这昏迷者的病情，除了吗啡中毒，我不准备接受任何别的解释；由此顺理成章的推论也昭然若揭——结局不外康复，便是验尸。他生硬地回了一句，说我“必须按自己认为最好的办法去做”，便匆匆出了屋，撇下我继续施救，再无人来打搅。

===Original===
For some time these efforts seemed to make no impression. The man lay as still and impassive as a corpse excepting for the slow, shallow and rather irregular breathing with its ominous accompanying rattle. But presently, by imperceptible degrees, signs of returning life began to make their appearance. A sharp slap on the cheek with the wet towel produced a sensible flicker of the eyelids; a similar slap on the chest was followed by a slight gasp. A pencil, drawn over the sole of the foot, occasioned a visible shrinking movement, and, on looking once more at the eyes, I detected a slight change that told me that the atropine was beginning to take effect.

===Chinese===
有好一阵子，这些努力似乎毫无成效。这人一动不动地躺着，冷漠如尸体，唯有那缓慢、浅薄而不甚规则的呼吸，伴着不祥的痰鸣。但不多时，生机回转的迹象开始不易察觉地一点点显露。湿毛巾在脸上利落地一抽，眼皮便觉察得到地颤了一颤；在胸口同样一抽，随之便是一声轻微的抽气。一支铅笔划过脚底，引出一目了然的缩避动作；而再查看眼睛时，我察出一点细微的变化，告诉我阿托品开始见效了。

===Original===
This was very encouraging, and, so far, quite satisfactory, though it would have been premature to rejoice. I kept the patient carefully covered and maintained the process of gentle irritation, moving his limbs and shoulders, brushing his hair and generally bombarding his deadened senses with small but repeated stimuli. And under this treatment, the improvement continued so far that on my bawling a question into his ear he actually opened his eyes for an instant, though in another moment, the lids had sunk back into their former position.

===Chinese===
这非常令人鼓舞，到此为止也相当令人满意，尽管欢喜还嫌太早。我留意给病人盖好保暖，继续这种温和刺激的功课：摆动他的四肢和肩膀，抚刷他的头发，总之用细小而反复的刺激，对他麻木的感官轮番轰炸。在这套疗法之下，起色持续显现，及至我朝他耳朵里大声喝问一句话时，他竟当真睁了一下眼——虽然转瞬之间，眼皮又落回了原位。

===Original===
Soon after this, Mr. Weiss reentered the room, followed by Mrs. Schallibaum, who carried a small tray, on which were a jug of coffee, a jug of milk, a cup and saucer and a sugar basin.

===Chinese===
此后不久，韦斯先生回到屋里，身后跟着沙利鲍姆太太；她端着一个小托盘，上面有一壶咖啡、一壶牛奶、一副杯碟和一只糖缸。

===Original===
“How do you find him now?” Mr. Weiss asked anxiously.

===Chinese===
“您看他现在怎么样了？”韦斯先生焦急地问。

===Original===
“I am glad to say that there is a distinct improvement,” I replied. “But we must persevere. He is by no means out of the wood yet.”

===Chinese===
“我很高兴地说，他有了明显起色，”我答道，“但我们还须坚持。他眼下还远没有脱险。”

===Original===
I examined the coffee, which looked black and strong and had a very reassuring smell, and, pouring out half a cupful, approached the bed.

===Chinese===
我察看那咖啡，色黑而浓，气味闻着极使人安心，便斟出半杯，走向床边。

===Original===
“Now, Mr. Graves,” I shouted, “we want you to drink some of this.”

===Chinese===
“来，格雷夫斯先生，”我大声喊道，“我们要你喝几口这个。”

===Original===
The flaccid eyelids lifted for an instant but there was no other response. I gently opened the unresisting mouth and ladled in a couple of spoonfuls of coffee, which were immediately swallowed; whereupon I repeated the proceeding and continued at short intervals until the cup was empty. The effect of the new remedy soon became apparent. He began to mumble and mutter obscurely in response to the questions that I bellowed at him, and once or twice he opened his eyes and looked dreamily into my face. Then I sat him up and made him drink some coffee from the cup, and, all the time, kept up a running fire of questions, which made up in volume of sound for what they lacked of relevancy.

===Chinese===
松垂的眼皮抬了一瞬，除此再无别的反应。我轻轻掰开那毫无抵抗的嘴，舀进两匙咖啡，随即便咽了下去；于是我如法炮制，隔一小会儿喂一回，直到一杯见底。这味新药的效力很快显了出来。他开始含含糊糊地咕哝，回应我冲他吼出的问题，还睁开过一两回眼，恍恍惚惚地朝我脸上望。然后我扶他坐起，让他就着杯子喝了些咖啡，自始至终，我不停地连珠炮式发问——问题虽多文不对题，却以音量补其不足。

===Original===
Of these proceedings Mr. Weiss and his housekeeper were highly interested spectators, and the former, contrary to his usual practice, came quite close up to the bed, to get a better view.

===Chinese===
对这些处置，韦斯先生和他的管家妇是兴致盎然的看客；前者一反常态，竟走到紧挨床边的地方，好看得更真切些。

===Original===
“It is really a most remarkable thing,” he said, “but it almost looks as if you were right, after all. He is certainly much better. But tell me, would this treatment produce a similar improvement if the symptoms were due to disease?”

===Chinese===
“这真是件十分了不得的事，”他说，“可看样子，到底还是您说对了。他确实好多了。不过请告诉我，倘若这些症状是疾病所致，这套疗法也能带来同样的起色吗？”

===Original===
“No,” I answered, “it certainly would not.”

===Chinese===
“不能，”我答道，“肯定不能。”

===Original===
“Then that seems to settle it. But it is a most mysterious affair. Can you suggest any way in which he can have concealed a store of the drug?”

===Chinese===
“那么，这事似乎就算定了。可这桩事太蹊跷了。您能想到什么法子，让他能把一批药藏得这样严实吗？”

===Original===
I stood up and looked him straight in the face; it was the first chance I had had of inspecting him by any but the feeblest light, and I looked at him very attentively. Now, it is a curious fact﻿—though one that most persons must have observed﻿—that there sometimes occurs a considerable interval between the reception of a visual impression and its complete transfer to the consciousness. A thing may be seen, as it were, unconsciously, and the impression consigned, apparently, to instant oblivion; and yet the picture may be subsequently revived by memory with such completeness that its details can be studied as though the object were still actually visible.

===Chinese===
我站起身，直视他的脸；此前我只能在最微弱的光线下打量他，这是头一回有机会细看，于是我端详得非常仔细。说来这是件怪事——尽管多半人人都留意过——视觉印象的接收与它完全传入意识，两者之间有时会隔上相当一段距离。一件东西可以仿佛是无意识地看见了，那印象似乎随即便被交付遗忘；然而日后记忆却可能把那画面完完整整地复活过来，以至其细节竟能如对实物一般加以推究。

===Original===
Something of this kind must have happened to me now. Preoccupied as I was, by the condition of the patient, the professional habit of rapid and close observation caused me to direct a searching glance at the man before me. It was only a brief glance﻿—for Mr. Weiss, perhaps embarrassed by my keen regard of him, almost immediately withdrew into the shadow﻿—and my attention seemed principally to be occupied by the odd contrast between the pallor of his face and the redness of his nose and by the peculiar stiff, bristly character of his eyebrows. But there was another fact, and a very curious one, that was observed by me subconsciously and instantly forgotten, to be revived later when I reflected on the events of the night. It was this:

===Chinese===
此刻我身上发生的大约正是这么一回事。我虽一心系于病人的状况，职业习惯的快捷细察，仍使我对眼前这人投去了一道审视的目光。那不过匆匆一瞥——因为韦斯先生许是被我盯得窘迫，几乎立刻便退回暗处去了——我的注意似乎主要落在两件事上：他脸色之苍白与鼻子之通红那古怪的对照，以及他眉毛那副又硬又扎手的怪状。但还有另一个事实，而且非常蹊跷，被我的潜意识记录下来，随即便忘了，直到后来回想这一夜的种种事件，才重新浮起。事情是这样：

===Original===
As Mr. Weiss stood, with his head slightly turned, I was able to look through one glass of his spectacles at the wall beyond. On the wall was a framed print; and the edge of the frame, seen through the spectacle-glass, appeared quite unaltered and free from distortion, magnification or reduction, as if seen through plain window-glass; and yet the reflections of the candle-flame in the spectacles showed the flame upside down, proving conclusively that the glasses were concave on one surface at least. The strange phenomenon was visible only for a moment or two, and as it passed out of my sight it passed also out of my mind.

===Chinese===
韦斯先生侧着头站在那里，我得以透过他一侧镜片，望见后面的墙。墙上挂着一幅装框的版画；透过镜片看去，画框的边缘毫不变样，没有变形、放大或缩小，就像隔着一平片窗玻璃；然而镜片上映出的烛焰倒立着，确凿证明这镜片至少一面是凹的。这桩怪现象只显形了一两秒钟，它从我的视野里逝去，也就从我的心头逝去了。

===Original===
“No,” I said, replying to the last question; “I can think of no way in which he could have effectually hidden a store of morphine. Judging by the symptoms, he has taken a large dose, and, if he has been in the habit of consuming large quantities, his stock would be pretty bulky. I can offer no suggestion whatever.”

===Chinese===
“想不出，”我回答他最后那个问题道，“我想象不出他能有什么法子，把一批吗啡藏得这样滴水不漏。从症状判断，他服下的剂量不小；他若素有大量服用的习惯，那份存货该相当占地方了。我提不出任何看法。”

===Original===
“I suppose you consider him quite out of danger now?”

===Chinese===
“想必您认为他现在已经完全脱离危险了？”

===Original===
“Oh, not at all. I think we can pull him round if we persevere, but he must not be allowed to sink back into a state of coma. We must keep him on the move until the effects of the drug have really passed off. If you will put him into his dressing-gown we will walk him up and down the room for a while.”

===Chinese===
“哦，还远谈不上。我想只要坚持下去，我们能把他救转来，但绝不能容他再陷入昏迷。必须让他不停走动，直到药效真正散尽。请您给他穿上晨衣，我们在屋里搀他来回走上几圈。”

===Original===
“But is that safe?” Mr. Weiss asked anxiously.

===Chinese===
“可那样安全吗？”韦斯先生忧心地问。

===Original===
“Quite safe,” I answered. “I will watch his pulse carefully. The danger is in the possibility, or rather certainty, of a relapse if he is not kept moving.”

===Chinese===
“相当安全，”我答道，“我会仔细盯住他的脉搏。危险在于：若不让他保持走动，病情反复的可能——或者说必然——便摆在眼前。”

===Original===
With obvious unwillingness and disapproval, Mr. Weiss produced a dressing-gown and together we invested the patient in it. Then we dragged him, very limp, but not entirely unresisting, out of bed and stood him on his feet. He opened his eyes and blinked owlishly first at one and then at the other of us, and mumbled a few unintelligible words of protest; regardless of which, we thrust his feet into slippers and endeavoured to make him walk. At first he seemed unable to stand, and we had to support him by his arms as we urged him forward; but presently his trailing legs began to make definite walking movements, and, after one or two turns up and down the room, he was not only able partly to support his weight, but showed evidence of reviving consciousness in more energetic protests.

===Chinese===
韦斯先生带着明显的不情愿和不以为然，取来一件晨衣，我们俩一起给病人穿上。然后我们把他从床上拖起来——浑身软塌塌的，却也并非全无抵抗——扶他站直。他睁开眼，先朝这个、又朝那个，像猫头鹰似的眨巴了一阵，咕哝出几句不知所云的抗议；我们置之不理，把他的脚塞进拖鞋，努力让他迈步。起初他似乎站立不住，我们只得架住他的胳膊往前搀；但不多时，他那两条拖沓的腿便迈出了确凿的步伐，在屋里来回走了一两趟之后，他不但能自己支住一部分体重，还以愈发有力的抗议，显示出意识正在回转。

===Original===
At this point Mr. Weiss astonished me by transferring the arm that he held to the housekeeper.

===Chinese===
正在这当口，韦斯先生做出一件叫我吃惊的事：他把他搀着的那条胳膊，交给了管家妇。

===Original===
“If you will excuse me, doctor,” said he, “I will go now and attend to some rather important business that I have had to leave unfinished. Mrs. Schallibaum will be able to give you all the assistance that you require, and will order the carriage when you think it safe to leave the patient. In case I should not see you again I will say ‘good night.’ I hope you won’t think me very unceremonious.”

===Chinese===
“您多包涵，医生，”他说，“我现在要离开一下，去料理一桩相当要紧的事务，一直撂着没办完。沙利鲍姆太太能给您一切所需的协助，等您觉得离开病人已无妨碍，她会吩咐备车。倘若我不再见到您，就趁此道个晚安。望您不要怪我失礼。”

===Original===
He shook hands with me and went out of the room, leaving me, as I have said, profoundly astonished that he should consider any business of more moment than the condition of his friend, whose life, even now, was but hanging by a thread. However, it was really no concern of mine. I could do without him, and the resuscitation of this unfortunate half-dead man gave me occupation enough to engross my whole attention.

===Chinese===
他同我握了握手，出了屋，留下我——如前所说——惊愕不已：世上竟有任何事务，在他看来比他朋友的安危更要紧，而那人此刻的性命，还只悬在一根线上。不过，这实在与我无干。没有他我也办得成事，而抢救这不幸的半死之人，已够我全神贯注、无暇他顾了。

===Original===
The melancholy progress up and down the room recommenced, and with it the mumbled protests from the patient. As we walked, and especially as we turned, I caught frequent glimpses of the housekeeper’s face. But it was nearly always in profile. She appeared to avoid looking me in the face, though she did so once or twice; and on each of these occasions her eyes were directed at me in a normal manner without any sign of a squint. Nevertheless, I had the impression that when her face was turned away from me she squinted. The “swivel eye”﻿—the left﻿—was towards me as she held the patient’s right arm, and it was almost continuously turned in my direction, whereas I felt convinced that she was really looking straight before her, though, of course, her right eye was invisible to me. It struck me, even at the time, as an odd affair, but I was too much concerned about my charge to give it much consideration.

===Chinese===
愁惨的屋里踱步又开始了，病人的咕哝抗议也随之而来。走着的时候，尤其是转身的时候，我不时瞥见管家妇的脸。可那几乎总是一张侧脸。她似乎避不肯同我对视，虽则也有过一两回；而每一回，她那双眼都正正常常地朝着我，毫无斜视的迹象。然而我总有个印象：她脸一转开，便斜起眼来。她搀着病人右臂，那只“转轴眼”——左眼——正朝着我，几乎一径转向我这一边；可我又深信，她其实望着正前方——当然，她的右眼我是看不见的。即便在当时，我也觉得这是桩怪事，只是我牵挂病人要紧，无暇多想。

===Original===
Meanwhile the patient continued to revive apace. And the more he revived, the more energetically did he protest against this wearisome perambulation. But he was evidently a polite gentleman, for, muddled as his faculties were, he managed to clothe his objections in courteous and even gracious forms of speech singularly out of agreement with the character that Mr. Weiss had given him.

===Chinese===
与此同时，病人继续迅速回醒。而他回醒得越多，对这趟累人的踱行也就抗议得越起劲。但他分明是位有教养的绅士，因为尽管神智昏乱，他仍能把不满裹在客气乃至温文的话语里——这份做派，同韦斯先生给他下的断语，实在格格不入。

===Original===
“I thangyou,” he mumbled thickly. “Ver’ good take s’much trouble. Think I will lie down now.” He looked wistfully at the bed, but I wheeled him about and marched him once more down the room. He submitted unresistingly, but as we again approached the bed he reopened the matter.

===Chinese===
“我谢谢您呐，”他口齿含混地咕哝，“劳你们费这老大的事。我想这就要躺下了。”他眼巴巴望着床，可我把他拨了个转，又押着他朝屋子那头走去。他顺顺从从地由我摆布，但待我们再度走近床边，他把这桩公案又翻了出来。

===Original===
“S’quite s’fficient, thang you. Gebback to bed now. Much ’bliged frall your kindness”﻿—here I turned him round﻿—“no, really; m’feeling rather tired. Sh’like to lie down now, f’you’d be s’good.”

===Chinese===
“实不敢当了，谢谢您。这就回床上去吧。多谢二位费心好意”——我把他掉转过去——“不，当真的；我觉着怪乏的。这就想躺下，您行个好。”

===Original===
“You must walk about a little longer, Mr. Graves,” I said. “It would be very bad for you to go to sleep again.”

===Chinese===
“您还得再走一会儿，格雷夫斯先生，”我说，“再睡着可对您大大不妙。”

===Original===
He looked at me with a curious, dull surprise, and reflected awhile as if in some perplexity. Then he looked at me again and said:

===Chinese===
他带着一种古怪的、迟钝的惊异望着我，寻思了一阵，似乎有些困惑。然后他再次望着我，说道：

===Original===
“Thing, sir, you are mistake﻿—mistaken me﻿—mist﻿—”

===Chinese===
“先生，我看您是弄错——弄错了我——弄——”

===Original===
Here Mrs. Schallibaum interrupted sharply:

===Chinese===
这时沙利鲍姆太太厉声打断了：

===Original===
“The doctor thinks it’s good for you to walk about. You’ve been sleeping too much. He doesn’t want you to sleep any more just now.”

===Chinese===
“医生觉得你走动走动对你有好处。你睡得太多了。他眼下不想让你再睡。”

===Original===
“Don’t wanter sleep; wanter lie down,” said the patient.

===Chinese===
“不想睡；就想躺着。”病人说。

===Original===
“But you mustn’t lie down for a little while. You must walk about for a few minutes more. And you’d better not talk. Just walk up and down.”

===Chinese===
“可你暂时还不许躺。还得再走几分钟。你最好别说话。只管来回走。”

===Original===
“There’s no harm in his talking,” said I; “in fact it’s good for him. It will help to keep him awake.”

===Chinese===
“他说话不碍事，”我说，“其实对他有益。还能帮他不睡着。”

===Original===
“I should think it would tire him,” said Mrs. Schallibaum; “and it worries me to hear him asking to lie down when we can’t let him.”

===Chinese===
“我看倒要把他累坏了，”沙利鲍姆太太说，“再说，听他一个劲儿求着躺下，我们又不能答应，我这心里也烦。”

===Original===
She spoke sharply and in an unnecessarily high tone so that the patient could not fail to hear. Apparently he took in the very broad hint contained in the concluding sentence, for he trudged wearily and unsteadily up and down the room for some time without speaking, though he continued to look at me from time to time as if something in my appearance puzzled him exceedingly. At length his intolerable longing for repose overcame his politeness and he returned to the attack.

===Chinese===
她说得又急又响，响得大可不必，病人不可能听不见。看来他领会了末尾那句里再明白不过的暗示，因为此后好一阵子，他只是拖着疲乏不稳的步子在屋里来回走，一言不发，却仍不时看我一眼，仿佛我身上有什么东西让他大惑不解。到头来，那股对安歇的难以按捺的渴望终于压倒了他的礼貌，他重又发起了进攻。

===Original===
“Surely v’ walked enough now. Feeling very tired. Am really. Would you be s’kind ’s t’let me lie down few minutes?”

===Chinese===
“咱们想必是走得够了吧。真是乏得很。当真乏了。您行行好，容我躺上几分钟成不成？”

===Original===
“Don’t you think he might lie down for a little while?” Mrs. Schallibaum asked.

===Chinese===
“您不觉得可以让他躺一小会儿吗？”沙利鲍姆太太问。

===Original===
I felt his pulse, and decided that he was really becoming fatigued, and that it would be wiser not to overdo the exercise while he was so weak. Accordingly, I consented to his returning to bed, and turned him round in that direction; whereupon he tottered gleefully towards his resting-place like a tired horse heading for its stable.

===Chinese===
我搭了搭他的脉，断定他确已开始疲惫，以他这样的虚弱，运动不宜过度，见好就收才是上策。于是我准他回床，把他朝那个方向转过身去；他登时欢天喜地、一步三晃地朝他的安歇之所蹭去，活像一匹疲马奔向它的马厩。

===Original===
As soon as he was tucked in, I gave him a full cup of coffee, which he drank with some avidity as if thirsty. Then I sat down by the bedside, and, with a view to keeping him awake, began once more to ply him with questions.

===Chinese===
一等把他安顿进被窝，我便给了他满满一杯咖啡，他喝得颇为急切，像是口渴。然后我在床边坐下，为了不让他睡着，重新拿问题去轰炸他。

===Original===
“Does your head ache, Mr. Graves?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“您头疼吗，格雷夫斯先生？”我问。

===Original===
“The doctor says ‘does your head ache?’ ” Mrs. Schallibaum squalled, so loudly that the patient started perceptibly.

===Chinese===
“医生问：‘你头疼不疼？’”沙利鲍姆太太尖着嗓子喊道，声音大得病人明显一惊。

===Original===
“I heard him, m’dear girl,” he answered with a faint smile. “Not deaf you know. Yes. Head aches a good deal. But I thing this gennleman mistakes﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我听见了，亲爱的姑娘，”他带着一丝浅笑答道，“您知道，我不聋。是。头疼得凶。不过我看这位先生是弄错了——”

===Original===
“He says you are to keep awake. You mustn’t go to sleep again, and you are not to close your eyes.”

===Chinese===
“他嘱咐你要一直醒着。不许再睡着，也不许合眼。”

===Original===
“All ri’ Pol’n. Keep’m open,” and he proceeded forthwith to shut them with an air of infinite peacefulness. I grasped his hand and shook it gently, on which he opened his eyes and looked at me sleepily. The housekeeper stroked his head, keeping her face half-turned from me﻿—as she had done almost constantly, to conceal the squinting eye, as I assumed﻿—and said:

===Chinese===
“好嘞，波尔（Pol’n）。睁着就是。”话音未落，他便带着一脸无限的安详，当即把眼睛闭上了。我握住他的手，轻轻摇了摇，他于是睁开眼，睡意惺忪地望着我。管家妇抚着他的头，脸半背着我——她几乎一直如此，据我揣想，是为遮掩那只斜眼——说道：

===Original===
“Need we keep you any longer, doctor? It is getting very late and you have a long way to go.”

===Chinese===
“医生，我们还有必要留您吗？时候很晚了，您还要赶远路呢。”

===Original===
I looked doubtfully at the patient. I was loath to leave him, distrusting these people as I did. But I had my work to do on the morrow, with, perhaps, a night call or two in the interval, and the endurance even of a general practitioner has its limits.

===Chinese===
我迟疑地望了望病人。我对这伙人心存疑忌，实在不愿丢下他走。可明天我还有分内的活计，天亮之前说不定还要夜诊一两趟，何况就连开业医生的耐力，也是有限度的。

===Original===
“I think I heard the carriage some time ago,” Mrs. Schallibaum added.

===Chinese===
“我想，马车早些时候我就听到了。”沙利鲍姆太太又添了一句。

===Original===
I rose hesitatingly and looked at my watch. It had turned half-past eleven.

===Chinese===
我迟疑着站起身，看了看表。已经过十一点半了。

===Original===
“You understand,” I said in a low voice, “that the danger is not over? If he is left now he will fall asleep, and in all human probability will never wake. You clearly understand that?”

===Chinese===
“您可明白，”我压低声音说，“危险并没有过去？此刻若丢下他不管，他就会睡着，而十之八九将一睡不醒。您清楚这一点吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, quite clearly. I promise you he shall not be allowed to fall asleep again.”

===Chinese===
“明白，十分清楚。我向您保证，绝不会再容他睡着。”

===Original===
As she spoke, she looked me full in the face for a few moments, and I noted that her eyes had a perfectly normal appearance, without any trace whatever of a squint.

===Chinese===
说这话时，她正面直视了我好一会儿，我注意到，她那双眼看去全然正常，没有一丝一毫斜视的痕迹。

===Original===
“Very well,” I said. “On that understanding I will go now; and I shall hope to find our friend quite recovered at my next visit.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”我说，“既有这话，我就走了；我盼望下次来诊时，见咱们这位朋友已痊愈如初。”

===Original===
I turned to the patient, who was already dozing, and shook his hand heartily.

===Chinese===
我转向已经打起瞌睡的病人，热诚地摇了摇他的手。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Mr. Graves!” I said. “I am sorry to have to disturb your repose so much; but you must keep awake, you know. Won’t do to go to sleep.”

===Chinese===
“再会，格雷夫斯先生！”我说，“很抱歉，搅扰您安歇到这个地步；可您务必保持清醒，您是知道的。睡着可不行。”

===Original===
“Ver’ well,” he replied drowsily. “Sorry t’ give you all this trouble. L’ keep awake. But I think you’re mistak’n﻿—”

===Chinese===
“很好，”他睡意沉沉地答道，“抱歉给您添了这许多麻烦。会醒着的。可我想您是弄错了——”

===Original===
“He says it’s very important that you shouldn’t go to sleep, and that I am to see that you don’t. Do you understand?”

===Chinese===
“他嘱咐说，你千万不可睡着，还吩咐我盯着你，不让你睡。你明白吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, I un’stan’. But why does this gennlem’n﻿—?”

===Chinese===
“是，我明白。可这位先生干吗——？”

===Original===
“Now it’s of no use for you to ask a lot of questions,” Mrs. Schallibaum said playfully; “we’ll talk to you tomorrow. Good night, doctor. I’ll light you down the stairs, but I won’t come down with you, or the patient will be falling asleep again.”

===Chinese===
“好啦，你问一大堆问题也没有用，”沙利鲍姆太太打趣道，“咱们明天再跟你说话。晚安，医生。我掌烛送您下楼，就不陪您下去了，不然病人又要睡着了。”

===Original===
Taking this definite dismissal, I retired, followed by a dreamily surprised glance from the sick man. The housekeeper held the candle over the balusters until I reached the bottom of the stairs, when I perceived through the open door along the passage a glimmer of light from the carriage lamps. The coachman was standing just outside, faintly illuminated by the very dim lamplight, and as I stepped into the carriage he remarked in his Scotch dialect that I “seemed to have been makin’ a nicht of it.” He did not wait for any reply﻿—none being in fact needed﻿—but shut the door and locked it.

===Chinese===
既受了这道明白不过的逐客令，我便告退了，身后追来病人那恍恍惚惚的一瞥惊异。管家妇隔着栏杆举烛照我下楼；到了楼梯脚下，我透过沿过道敞开的门，望见车灯的一星微光。马车夫正立在门外，被那点极暗的灯光淡淡照着；我跨进马车时，他用他那苏格兰土话说，我“瞧着像是熬了个整宿”。他并不等什么回答——其实也无需回答——便关上车门，落了锁。

===Original===
I lit my little pocket-lamp and hung it on the back cushion. I even drew the board and notebook from my pocket. But it seemed rather unnecessary to take a fresh set of notes, and, to tell the truth, I rather shirked the labour, tired as I was after my late exertions; besides, I wanted to think over the events of the evening, while they were fresh in my memory. Accordingly I put away the notebook, filled and lighted my pipe, and settled myself to review the incidents attending my second visit to this rather uncanny house.

===Chinese===
我点亮随身的小灯，挂上靠背垫，甚至从口袋里掏出了木板和笔记本。可再记一套新记录似乎大可不必，而且说实话，方才那一番操劳之后我实在疲乏，很想偷这个懒；何况我还想趁着记忆新鲜，把今晚的种种事件想一遍。于是我收起笔记本，装上烟斗点着，安顿下来，回顾我二度造访这座颇不吉利的老宅所经历的种种情事。

===Original===
Considered in leisurely retrospect, that visit offered quite a number of problems that called for elucidation. There was the patient’s condition, for instance. Any doubt as to the cause of his symptoms was set at rest by the effect of the antidotes. Mr. Graves was certainly under the influence of morphine, and the only doubtful question was how he had become so. That he had taken the poison himself was incredible. No morphinomaniac would take such a knockdown dose. It was practically certain that the poison had been administered by someone else, and, on Mr. Weiss’s own showing, there was no one but himself and the housekeeper who could have administered it. And to this conclusion all the other very queer circumstances pointed.

===Chinese===
从容回味起来，这趟造访提出的问题着实不少，桩桩都亟待阐明。比如病人的病情。解毒剂的药效，已把对其症状成因的一切疑问打消了。格雷夫斯先生确凿正处于吗啡的作用之下，唯一成疑的只是：他是怎么落到这一步的。说毒药是他自己服下的，难以置信。任何吗啡瘾君子都不会服下这样一剂放倒人的分量。实际上可以断定，毒药是别人给他服下的；而照韦斯先生自己的说法，有可能下毒的，除他和管家妇之外再无旁人。其余种种十分蹊跷的情形，也无不指向这个结论。

===Original===
What were these circumstances? They were, as I have said, numerous, though many of them seemed trivial. To begin with, Mr. Weiss’s habit of appearing some time after my arrival and disappearing some time before my departure was decidedly odd. But still more odd was his sudden departure this evening on what looked like a mere pretext. That departure coincided in time with the sick man’s recovery of the power of speech. Could it be that Mr. Weiss was afraid that the half-conscious man might say something compromising to him in my presence? It looked rather like it. And yet he had gone away and left me with the patient and the housekeeper.

===Chinese===
这些情形都是些什么呢？如我所说，为数不少，尽管其中许多看着琐碎。头一件，韦斯先生那个习惯就着实古怪——他总是在我到达后过上一阵才露面，又在我离开前便早早遁去。更古怪的，还是他今晚借一个看着纯属借口的事由突然离去。那次离去，恰与病人恢复说话能力的时间相合。莫非韦斯先生是怕这半清醒的人当着我的面，说出什么于他不利的话来？看去颇像这么回事。可他偏偏走开了，把我一个人留给病人和管家妇。

===Original===
But when I came to think about it I remembered that Mrs. Schallibaum had shown some anxiety to prevent the patient from talking. She had interrupted him more than once, and had on two occasions broken in when he seemed to be about to ask me some question. I was “mistaken” about something. What was that something that he wanted to tell me?

===Chinese===
可待我细想，又记起沙利鲍姆太太曾露出几分焦虑，不让病人开口。她打断他不止一回，还有两次，正当他似乎要问我什么话时，被她截了过去。我在某件事上“弄错”了。他想告诉我的，究竟是件什么事呢？

===Original===
It had struck me as singular that there should be no coffee in the house, but a sufficiency of tea. Germans are not usually tea-drinkers and they do take coffee. But perhaps there was nothing in this. Rather more remarkable was the invisibility of the coachman. Why could he not be sent to fetch the coffee, and why did not he, rather than the housekeeper, come to take the place of Mr. Weiss when the latter had to go away.

===Chinese===
家中无咖啡，茶叶倒充足，这也曾让我觉得蹊跷。德国人通常不喝茶，咖啡倒是喝的。不过这或许没什么文章。更值得玩味的，是那位马车夫的全然隐身。为何不能打发他去取咖啡？韦斯先生走开后，为何又不是他、倒是管家妇来顶替主人的位置？

===Original===
There were other points, too. I recalled the word that sounded like “Pol’n,” which Mr. Graves had used in speaking to the housekeeper. Apparently it was a Christian name of some kind; but why did Mr. Graves call the woman by her Christian name when Mr. Weiss addressed her formally as Mrs. Schallibaum? And, as to the woman herself: what was the meaning of that curious disappearing squint? Physically it presented no mystery. The woman had an ordinary divergent squint, and, like many people, who suffer from this displacement, could, by a strong muscular effort, bring the eyes temporarily into their normal parallel position. I had detected the displacement when she had tried to maintain the effort too long, and the muscular control had given way. But why had she done it? Was it only feminine vanity﻿—mere sensitiveness respecting a slight personal disfigurement? It might be so; or there might be some further motive. It was impossible to say.

===Chinese===
还有别的疑点。我记起那个听着像“波尔”（Pol’n）的称呼，格雷夫斯先生用它称呼管家妇。那显然是某种教名；可韦斯先生称她，规规矩矩叫沙利鲍姆太太，格雷夫斯先生为何却用教名称呼这女人？再说这女人本人：那古怪的一闪即隐的斜视，又是怎么回事？就生理而言，并无神秘可言。这女人患有寻常的分散性斜视，而同许多患这种偏斜的人一样，只需狠狠用一用力，便能暂时把双眼摆回平常的平行位置。她试把这份力气撑得太久、肌肉的控制败下阵来时，便被我看出了破绽。可她为什么要这样做？难道只是女人的虚荣——纯粹出于对一点小小破相的敏感？也许是；也许另有更深的动机。那就无从说起了。

===Original===
Turning this question over, I suddenly remembered the peculiarity of Mr. Weiss’s spectacles. And here I met with a real poser. I had certainly seen through those spectacles as clearly as if they had been plain window-glass; and they had certainly given an inverted reflection of the candle-flame like that thrown from the surface of a concave lens. Now they obviously could not be both flat and concave; but yet they had the properties peculiar to both flatness and concavity. And there was a further difficulty. If I could see objects unaltered through them, so could Mr. Weiss. But the function of spectacles is to alter the appearances of objects, by magnification, reduction or compensating distortion. If they leave the appearances unchanged they are useless. I could make nothing of it. After puzzling over it for quite a long time, I had to give it up; which I did the less unwillingly inasmuch as the construction of Mr. Weiss’s spectacles had no apparent bearing on the case.

===Chinese===
正把这个问题翻来覆去，我猛然记起韦斯先生那副眼镜的蹊跷。这一回，我可碰上一道真正的难题。透过那副眼镜，我确实看得清清楚楚，仿佛隔着一平片窗玻璃；而它们映出的烛焰又确确实实倒立着，一如凹透镜面反射出来的光景。可它们显然不能既是平的又是凹的；然而它们偏偏兼有平与凹两者独有的性质。更难的还在后头。我既能透过它们看见不走样的物体，韦斯先生自然也能。可眼镜的功用，正在于改变物体的模样——放大、缩小，或作补偿性的变形。若是不改物体的模样，那便一无用处。我百思不得其解。苦苦琢磨了好半天，只好弃之不顾；好在韦斯先生这副眼镜的构造，同案子看不出有什么相干，我放弃得也不算太不甘愿。

===Original===
On arriving home, I looked anxiously at the message-book, and was relieved to find that there were no further visits to be made. Having made up a mixture for Mr. Graves and handed it to the coachman, I raked the ashes of the surgery fire together and sat down to smoke a final pipe while I reflected once more on the singular and suspicious case in which I had become involved. But fatigue soon put an end to my meditations; and having come to the conclusion that the circumstances demanded a further consultation with Thorndyke, I turned down the gas to a microscopic blue spark and betook myself to bed.

===Chinese===
到家后，我忐忑地翻看留言簿，见再无别的出诊等着，方才松了口气。我为格雷夫斯先生配好药水，交给马车夫，把诊室炉火的灰烬拢作一堆，坐下来抽当日最后一斗烟，一面再一次回味我卷入的这桩离奇而可疑的案子。但疲乏很快便终结了我的沉思；我断定，此案情势需要再同桑代克从长计议一番，于是把煤气灯拧到只剩一粒显微镜下才看得见的蓝色火星，上床去了。

## XVI An Exposition and a Tragedy / 一场解说与一幕惨剧

===Original===
“You may have wondered,” Thorndyke commenced, when he had poured out the coffee and handed round the cups, “what induced me to undertake the minute investigation of so apparently simple and straightforward a case. Perhaps I had better explain that first and let you see what was the real starting-point of the inquiry.

===Chinese===
“你们也许一直在纳闷，”桑代克斟好咖啡、把杯子一一递到各人手里之后，开了腔，“我为什么要把这样一桩表面上简单直截的案子，查究得如此细致入微。也许我还是先把这一点说明白的好，让你们看看这番查究真正的起点何在。

===Original===
“When you, Mr. Marchmont and Mr. Stephen, introduced the case to me, I made a very brief précis of the facts as you presented them, and of these there were one or two which immediately attracted my attention. In the first place, there was the will. It was a very strange will. It was perfectly unnecessary. It contained no new matter; it expressed no changed intentions; it met no new circumstances, as known to the testator. In short it was not really a new will at all, but merely a repetition of the first one, drafted in different and less suitable language. It differed only in introducing a certain ambiguity from which the original was free. It created the possibility that, in certain circumstances, not known to or anticipated by the testator, John Blackmore might become the principal beneficiary, contrary to the obvious wishes of the testator.

===Chinese===
“马奇蒙特先生、斯蒂芬先生，当初你们两位把这案子介绍给我的时候，我照你们陈述的事实做了一份十分简略的摘要，其中有一两点顿时引起了我的注意。头一点，就是那份遗嘱。那是一份非常古怪的遗嘱。它完全没有必要。它不含任何新的内容；不表达任何变更的意愿；就立遗嘱人所知，也应对不上任何新的情况。简而言之，它压根儿算不得一份新遗嘱，不过是前一份的重复，措辞换了一套不同而且更不合适的语言。它与原稿的差别只有一处：引入了原稿所没有的某种歧义。它造成了这样一种可能：在某些立遗嘱人不知道、也预料不到的情形之下，约翰·布莱克莫尔会违背立遗嘱人显而易见的意愿，成为主要的受益人。

===Original===
“The next point that impressed me was the manner of Mrs. Wilson’s death. She died of cancer. Now people do not die suddenly and unexpectedly of cancer. This terrible disease stands almost alone in that it marks out its victim months in advance. A person who has an incurable cancer is a person whose death may be predicted with certainty and its date fixed within comparatively narrow limits.

===Chinese===
“给我深刻印象的第二点，是威尔逊太太死亡的情形。她死于癌症。而人死于癌症，是不会骤然发作、出乎意料的。这种可怕的疾病几乎独一无二：它提前几个月就把它的牺牲者勾定了。一个身患不治之症癌症的人，他的死可以确凿预知，日期也能划定在一个相当窄的范围内。

===Original===
“And now observe the remarkable series of coincidences that are brought into light when we consider this peculiarity of the disease. Mrs. Wilson died on the twelfth of March of this present year. Mr. Jeffrey’s second will was signed on the twelfth of November of last year; at a time, that is to say, when the existence of cancer must have been known to Mrs. Wilson’s doctor, and might have been known to any of her relatives who chose to inquire after her.

===Chinese===
“现在请看，只要把病症的这一特性考虑进来，一系列惊人的巧合便显豁出来。威尔逊太太死于今年三月十二日。杰弗里先生的第二份遗嘱，签署于去年十一月十二日；也就是说，在那个时候，癌症的存在必定已为威尔逊太太的医生所知，而她的任何亲属，只要肯打听她的病情，也可能已经知道了。

===Original===
“Then you will observe that the remarkable change in Mr. Jeffrey’s habits coincides in the most singular way with the same events. The cancer must have been detectable as early as September of last year; about the time, in fact, at which Mrs. Wilson made her will. Mr. Jeffrey went to the inn at the beginning of October. From that time his habits were totally changed, and I can demonstrate to you that a change﻿—not a gradual, but an abrupt change﻿—took place in the character of his signature.

===Chinese===
“再者，你们会注意到，杰弗里先生习惯上那场引人注目的改变，恰恰以最奇特的方式与上述事件相合。这癌症至迟在去年九月间必定已经查验得出；事实上，大约正是在那个时候，威尔逊太太立下了她的遗嘱。杰弗里先生是十月初住进客栈的。从那时起，他的习惯彻底变了；而且我可以向你们证明，他的签名的笔性也起了一个变化——不是渐变，而是突变。

===Original===
“In short, the whole of this peculiar set of circumstances﻿—the change in Jeffrey’s habits, the change in his signature, and the execution of his strange will﻿—came into existence about the time when Mrs. Wilson was first known to be suffering from cancer.

===Chinese===
“简而言之，这一整组特殊的情事——杰弗里习惯的改变、他签名的改变、他那份古怪遗嘱的签立——都是在威尔逊太太初次确诊患癌的前后问世的。

===Original===
“This struck me as a very suggestive fact.

===Chinese===
“这一事实在我看来意味深长。

===Original===
“Then there is the extraordinarily opportune date of Mr. Jeffrey’s death. Mrs. Wilson died on the twelfth of March. Mr. Jeffrey was found dead on the fifteenth of March, having apparently died on the fourteenth, on which day he was seen alive. If he had died only three days sooner, he would have predeceased Mrs. Wilson, and her property would never have devolved on him at all; while, if he had lived only a day or two longer, he would have learned of her death and would certainly have made a new will or codicil in his nephew’s favour.

===Chinese===
“还有，杰弗里先生的死期巧得出奇。威尔逊太太死于三月十二日。杰弗里先生三月十五日被发现死去，看样子死于十四日，而十四日那天还有人见他活着。他若只早死三天，便死在了威尔逊太太前头，她的财产根本传不到他手里；而他若只多活一两天，就会得知她的死讯，几乎必定会另立一份新遗嘱或加一份遗嘱附录，把财产留给他的侄子。

===Original===
“Circumstances, therefore, conspired in the most singular manner in favour of John Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“可见种种情由，正在以最奇特的方式协力成全约翰·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“But there is yet another coincidence. Jeffrey’s body was found, by the merest chance, the day after his death. But it might have remained undiscovered for weeks, or even months; and if it had, it would have been impossible to fix the date of his death. Then Mrs. Wilson’s next of kin would certainly have contested John Blackmore’s claim﻿—and probably with success﻿—on the ground that Jeffrey died before Mrs. Wilson. But all this uncertainty is provided for by the circumstance that Mr. Jeffrey paid his rent personally﻿—and prematurely﻿—to the porter on the fourteenth of March, thus establishing beyond question the fact that he was alive on that date; and yet further, in case the porter’s memory should be untrustworthy or his statement doubted, Jeffrey furnished a signed and dated document﻿—the cheque﻿—which could be produced in a court to furnish incontestable proof of survival.

===Chinese===
“但还有一层巧合。杰弗里的尸体，是在他死后第二天，纯粹出于偶然才被发现的。它也可能几个星期、甚至几个月无人发现；倘若如此，他的死亡日期便无从确定。到那时，威尔逊太太的血亲必然要争议约翰·布莱克莫尔的权利——而且多半会胜诉——理由是杰弗里死在威尔逊太太之前。然而这一切不确定，都已被一桩情事预先堵死：杰弗里先生在三月十四日亲自把房租付给了门房——而且是提前付的——由此无可置疑地确定了他当日尚在人世；更有进者，为防门房记性靠不住、或其证词受人置疑，杰弗里还留下了一件签名注明日期的文书——那张支票——可以当庭出示，为他的存活提供无可辩驳的证明。

===Original===
“To sum up this part of the evidence. Here was a will which enabled John Blackmore to inherit the fortune of a man who, almost certainly, had no intention of bequeathing it to him. The wording of that will seemed to be adjusted to the peculiarities of Mrs. Wilson’s disease; and the death of the testator occurred under a peculiar set of circumstances which seemed to be exactly adjusted to the wording of the will. Or, to put it in another way: the wording of the will and the time, the manner and the circumstances of the testator’s death, all seemed to be precisely adjusted to the fact that the approximate date of Mrs. Wilson’s death was known some months before it occurred.

===Chinese===
“把这部分证据归结一下。这里有一份遗嘱，使约翰·布莱克莫尔得以继承一个人的财产，而这个人几乎可以断定无意把财产遗赠给他。这份遗嘱的措辞，似乎恰恰照着威尔逊太太病症的特点调校过；而立遗嘱人的死亡，又发生在一组特殊的情事之下，这组情事似乎又恰恰照着遗嘱的措辞调校过。或者换一个说法：遗嘱的措辞，连同立遗嘱人死亡的时间、方式与情形，全都像是精确地照着这样一个事实调校过的——威尔逊太太的大致死期，早在她去世之前几个月就已被知晓。

===Original===
“Now you must admit that this compound group of coincidences, all conspiring to a single end﻿—the enrichment of John Blackmore﻿—has a very singular appearance. Coincidences are common enough in real life; but we cannot accept too many at a time. My feeling was that there were too many in this case and that I could not accept them without searching inquiry.”

===Chinese===
“现在你们必须承认，这一大串巧合，全都朝着同一个目标协力合谋——叫约翰·布莱克莫尔发财——形迹实在十分蹊跷。现实生活中巧合本属司空见惯；但一次接受太多，却使不得。我当时的感想是：这案子里巧合太多，非经一番穷根究底的查究，我不能照单全收。”

===Original===
Thorndyke paused, and Mr. Marchmont, who had listened with close attention, nodded, as he glanced at his silent partner.

===Chinese===
桑代克停了下来。马奇蒙特先生方才一直凝神细听，这时朝他那沉默的合伙人瞟了一眼，点了点头。

===Original===
“You have stated the case with remarkable clearness,” he said; “and I am free to confess that some of the points that you have raised had escaped my notice.”

===Chinese===
“你把案情陈述得异常清晰，”他说；“我乐于承认，你提出的某几点，确实逃过了我的注意。”

===Original===
“My first idea,” Thorndyke resumed, “was that John Blackmore, taking advantage of the mental enfeeblement produced by the opium habit, had dictated this will to Jeffrey. It was then that I sought permission to inspect Jeffrey’s chambers; to learn what I could about him and to see for myself whether they presented the dirty and disorderly appearance characteristic of the regular opium-smoker’s den. But when, during a walk into the City, I thought over the case, it seemed to me that this explanation hardly met the facts. Then I endeavoured to think of some other explanation; and looking over my notes I observed two points that seemed worth considering. One was that neither of the witnesses to the will was really acquainted with Jeffrey Blackmore; both being strangers who had accepted his identity on his own statement. The other was that no one who had previously known him, with the single exception of his brother John, had ever seen Jeffrey at the inn.

===Chinese===
“我起初的念头，”桑代克接着说，“是以为约翰·布莱克莫尔趁鸦片癖把杰弗里的神志磨得衰弱，向他口授了这份遗嘱。正是在那时，我请求准许查看杰弗里的寓所；想尽量摸清他的情形，并亲眼看看那几间屋子是否带着常年吸鸦片者巢穴特有的那种污秽杂乱。可是，当我步行进城、一路把案情细想过后，便觉得这个解释很难贴合事实。于是我设法另想一个解释；翻检笔记的时候，我注意到两点似乎值得考虑。其一，遗嘱的两名见证人其实都不认识杰弗里·布莱克莫尔；两个都是生人，只凭他自报身份便认了账。其二，凡是原先认识他的人，除了他哥哥约翰这唯一的例外，谁也不曾在客栈见过杰弗里。

===Original===
“What was the import of these two facts? Probably they had none. But still they suggested the desirability of considering the question: Was the person who signed the will really Jeffrey Blackmore? The contrary supposition﻿—that someone had personated Jeffrey and forged his signature to a false will﻿—seemed wildly improbable, especially in view of the identification of the body; but it involved no actual impossibility; and it offered a complete explanation of the, otherwise inexplicable, coincidences that I have mentioned.

===Chinese===
“这两个事实有什么含义？多半没有。但它们仍然提示出考虑这个问题的必要：在遗嘱上签字的那个人，真是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔吗？相反的设想——有人顶了杰弗里的名，在一份假遗嘱上伪造了他的签名——看去荒唐得近乎不可能，尤其考虑到尸身已经验明；但这个设想并不包含真正办不到的事；而且，它能把方才那一连串否则无从解释的巧合，解释得干干净净。

===Original===
“I did not, however, for a moment, think that this was the true explanation, but I resolved to bear it in mind, to test it when the opportunity arose, and consider it by the light of any fresh facts that I might acquire.

===Chinese===
“不过，我片刻也不曾当真以为这就是实情；但我决意把它记在心里，一有时机便加检验，并拿日后可能获得的任何新事实来推敲它。

===Original===
“The new facts came sooner than I had expected. That same evening I went with Dr. Jervis to New Inn and found Mr. Stephen in the chambers. By him I was informed that Jeffrey was a learned Orientalist, with a quite expert knowledge of the cuneiform writing; and even as he was telling me this, I looked over his shoulder and saw a cuneiform inscription hanging on the wall upside down.

===Chinese===
“新事实来得比我预想的还快。当天晚上，我同杰维斯医生一起去新客栈，在寓所里遇见了斯蒂芬先生。从他口中我得知，杰弗里是一位博学的东方学家，对楔形文字颇具专家的造诣；而就在他对我讲这话的当儿，我越过他的肩头望去，看见墙上挂着一幅楔形文字铭文，却是头朝下倒挂着的。

===Original===
“Now, of this there could be only one reasonable explanation. Disregarding the fact that no one would screw the suspension plates on a frame without ascertaining which was the right way up, and assuming it to be hung up inverted, it was impossible that the misplacement could have been overlooked by Jeffrey. He was not blind, though his sight was defective. The frame was thirty inches long and the individual characters nearly an inch in length﻿—about the size of the D 18 letters of Snellen’s test-types, which can be read by a person of ordinary sight at a distance of fifty-five feet. There was, I repeat, only one reasonable explanation; which was that the person who had inhabited those chambers was not Jeffrey Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“这一点，只可能有一个合理的解释。姑且撇开那样的事实不谈——没有人会在不弄清哪一头朝上的情况下，就把挂片拧上画框——单说它是被挂倒了，这种颠倒也不可能逃过杰弗里的眼睛。他并不瞎，尽管视力不佳。画框长三十英寸，单个字符将近一英寸——约莫是斯内伦视力表 D 18 号字的大小，视力平常的人隔着五十五英尺也认得出。我再说一遍，只可能有一个合理的解释：住在那几间屋子里的，不是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“This conclusion received considerable support from a fact which I observed later, but mention in this place. On examining the soles of the shoes taken from the dead man’s feet, I found only the ordinary mud of the streets. There was no trace of the peculiar gravelly mud that adhered to my own boots and Jervis’s, and which came from the square of the inn. Yet the porter distinctly stated that the deceased, after paying the rent, walked back towards his chambers across the square; the mud of which should, therefore, have been conspicuous on his shoes.

===Chinese===
“这个结论后来又得到一个事实的相当佐证，那事实我如今一并提出。检查从死者脚上取下的鞋子底时，我发现上面只有街上寻常的泥。那种特殊的砂砾泥却不见踪影——那种泥沾在我和杰维斯的靴子上，来自客栈的院子。然而门房明明说过，死者付过房租之后，是穿过院子走回寓所去的；因此那种泥本该醒目地留在他的鞋上。

===Original===
“Thus, in a moment, a wildly speculative hypothesis had assumed a high degree of probability.

===Chinese===
“就这样，一个异想天开的假说，转眼间便有了很大的或然性。

===Original===
“When Mr. Stephen was gone, Jervis and I looked over the chambers thoroughly; and then another curious fact came to light. On the wall were a number of fine Japanese colour-prints, all of which showed recent damp-spots. Now, apart from the consideration that Jeffrey, who had been at the trouble and expense of collecting these valuable prints, would hardly have allowed them to rot on his walls, there arose the question: How came they to be damp? There was a gas stove in the room, and a gas stove has at least the virtue of preserving a dry atmosphere. It was winter weather, when the stove would naturally be pretty constantly alight. How came the walls to be so damp? The answer seemed to be that the stove had not been constantly alight, but had been lighted only occasionally. This suggestion was borne out by a further examination of the rooms. In the kitchen there were practically no stores and hardly any arrangements even for simple bachelor cooking; the bedroom offered the same suggestion; the soap in the washstand was shrivelled and cracked; there was no cast-off linen, and the shirts in the drawers, though clean, had the peculiar yellowish, faded appearance that linen acquires when long out of use. In short, the rooms had the appearance of not having been lived in at all, but only visited at intervals.

===Chinese===
“斯蒂芬先生走后，我和杰维斯把那几间屋子从头到尾查看了一遍；于是又有一桩古怪的事实显露出来。墙上挂着若干精美的日本彩色版画，幅幅都带着新近的水渍斑点。暂且不说杰弗里既曾花费心血金钱收藏这些珍贵的版画，断不至于任它们在墙上霉烂，单是这问题就来了：它们怎么会潮？屋里有一座煤气炉，而煤气炉至少有一桩好处，就是能保住干燥的空气。时值冬季，炉子照理会经常生着。墙壁怎么会这样潮？答案似乎是：炉子并不曾经常生着，只是偶尔点一点。这个推测又被对各房间的进一步查证坐实。厨房里几乎没有存粮，连做几样简单单身汉饭菜的家什也寥寥无几；卧室同样给人这种印象；洗脸架上的肥皂干缩龟裂；没有换下的旧衣；抽屉里的衬衫虽然干净，却带着亚麻布久置不用特有的那种泛黄的褪色。总之，这些屋子看去根本不像有人常住，只像不时有人来走一趟。

===Original===
“Against this view, however, was the statement of the night porter that he had often seen a light in Jeffrey’s sitting-room at one o’clock in the morning, with the apparent implication that it was then turned out. Now a light may be left in an empty room, but its extinction implies the presence of some person to extinguish it; unless some automatic device be adopted for putting it out at a given time. Such a device﻿—the alarm movement of a clock, for instance, with a suitable attachment﻿—is a simple enough matter, but my search of the rooms failed to discover anything of the kind. However, when looking over the drawers in the bedroom, I came upon a large box that had held a considerable quantity of hard stearine candles. There were only a few left, but a flat candlestick with numerous wick-ends in its socket accounted for the remainder.

===Chinese===
“可是，与这个见解相抵触的，是夜班门房的话：他常见杰弗里起居室里的灯凌晨一点钟还亮着，言下之意是那时才熄。灯可以留在空屋里亮着；但灯灭了，就意味着有人在场把它弄灭；除非安上什么自动的装置，叫它到一定时刻自行熄灭。这样的装置——譬如时钟的闹钟机芯，配上合适的附件——本是轻而易举的事；可我把各屋搜遍，也不曾发现这一类物件。不过，翻检卧室抽屉的时候，我碰到一只大盒子，原先装过相当数量的硬脂蜡烛。剩下的只有几支，但一只平底烛台的插口里塞满烛芯头，恰好交代了其余的下落。

===Original===
“These candles seemed to dispose of the difficulty. They were not necessary for ordinary lighting, since gas was laid on in all three rooms. For what purpose, then, were they used, and in such considerable quantities? I subsequently obtained some of the same brand﻿—Price’s stearine candles, six to the pound﻿—and experimented with them. Each candle was seven and a quarter inches in length, not counting the cone at the top, and I found that they burned in still air at the rate of a fraction over one inch in an hour. We may say that one of these candles would burn in still air a little over six hours. It would thus be possible for the person who inhabited these rooms to go away at seven o’clock in the evening and leave a light which would burn until past one in the morning and then extinguish itself. This, of course, was only surmise, but it destroyed the significance of the night porter’s statement.

===Chinese===
“这些蜡烛似乎把难题解开了。三间屋子都通了煤气，寻常照明原用不着它们。那么，它们是派什么用场的，还用掉这么多？后来我买到几支同一牌子的——普赖斯牌硬脂蜡烛，一磅六支装——拿来做实验。每支长七又四分之一英寸，不算顶上的锥尖；我发现它们在静止的空气中燃烧，速度是每小时一英寸多一点。可以说，这样一支蜡烛在静止空气里能烧六个钟头挂零。于是，住这些屋子的人便可以傍晚七点钟出门，留下一支点着的蜡烛，由它烧到凌晨一点以后自行熄灭。这当然只是揣测，但它把夜班门房那句话的意义给取消了。

===Original===
“But, if the person who inhabited these chambers was not Jeffrey, who was he?

===Chinese===
“但是，倘若住这几间屋子的不是杰弗里，那是谁呢？

===Original===
“The answer to that question seemed plain enough. There was only one person who had a strong motive for perpetrating a fraud of this kind, and there was only one person to whom it was possible. If this person was not Jeffrey, he must have been very like Jeffrey; sufficiently like for the body of the one to be mistaken for the body of the other. For the production of Jeffrey’s body was an essential part of the plan and must have been contemplated from the first. But the only person who fulfills the conditions is John Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“这个问题的答案似乎相当明白。只有一个人有强烈的动机来干这等骗局；也只有一个人干得成。此人若不是杰弗里，就必定生得非常像杰弗里；像到这种地步，一个人的尸身会被错认作另一个人的。因为拿出杰弗里的尸身，是整个计划必不可少的一环，必定从一开始就在盘算之中。而唯一符合这些条件的人，就是约翰·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“We have learned from Mr. Stephen that John and Jeffrey, though very different in appearance in later years, were much alike as young men. But when two brothers who are much alike as young men, become unlike in later life, we shall find that the unlikeness is produced by superficial differences and that the essential likeness remains. Thus, in the present case, Jeffrey was clean shaved, had bad eyesight, wore spectacles and stooped as he walked; John wore a beard and moustache, had good eyesight, did not wear spectacles and had a brisk gait and upright carriage. But supposing John to shave off his beard and moustache, to put on spectacles and to stoop in his walk, these conspicuous but superficial differences would vanish and the original likeness reappear.

===Chinese===
“我们从斯蒂芬先生那里得知，约翰和杰弗里虽说上了年纪以后相貌大不相同，年轻时却非常相像。但是，兄弟俩年轻时非常相像、到后来变得不像了的，我们会发现那不像出自表面的差异，本质的相像依然还在。就拿本案来说：杰弗里剃的是净脸，视力很差，戴眼镜，走路伛偻；约翰蓄着连鬓胡子和髭，视力很好，不戴眼镜，步履轻快，腰背挺直。然而，倘若约翰剃掉胡髭，戴上眼镜，走路再一佝偻，这些显眼却表面的差异便消失了，原本的相像便重新显了出来。

===Original===
“There is another consideration. John had been an actor and was an actor of some experience. Now, any person can, with some care and practice, make up a disguise; the great difficulty is to support that disguise by a suitable manner and voice. But to an experienced actor this difficulty does not exist. To him, personation is easy; and, moreover, an actor is precisely the person to whom the idea of disguise and impersonation would occur.

===Chinese===
“还有一层。约翰当过演员，而且是颇有阅历的演员。要知道，随便什么人，只要肯用心加练习，都化得出一个装束；大难处在于，要用相称的态度和嗓音把这个装束撑住。可对一位阅历丰富的演员来说，这个难处根本不存在。对他，冒名顶替易如反掌；况且，演员恰恰是最容易起化装和假扮这套念头的人。

===Original===
“There is a small item bearing on this point, so small as to be hardly worth calling evidence, but just worth noting. In the pocket of the waistcoat taken from the body of Jeffrey I found the stump of a ‘Contango’ pencil; a pencil that is sold for the use of stock dealers and brokers. Now John was an outside broker and might very probably have used such a pencil, whereas Jeffrey had no connection with the stock markets and there is no reason why he should have possessed a pencil of this kind. But the fact is merely suggestive; it has no evidential value.

===Chinese===
“有一件小事与这一点相干，小得几乎称不上证据，却值得一提。在从杰弗里尸身上取下的马甲口袋里，我找到一截‘康坦戈’铅笔头；那是卖给股票商和经纪人用的铅笔。约翰是个场外经纪人，用这种铅笔的可能性很大；杰弗里却同股票市场毫无干系，没有理由存这样一支铅笔。不过这个事实只是有所暗示，没有证据的价值。

===Original===
“A more important inference is to be drawn from the collected signatures. I have remarked that the change in the signature occurred abruptly, with one or two alterations of manner, last September, and that there are two distinct forms with no intermediate varieties. This is, in itself, remarkable and suspicious. But a remark made by Mr. Britton furnishes a really valuable piece of evidence on the point we are now considering. He admitted that the character of the signature had undergone a change, but observed that the change did not affect the individual or personal character of the writing. This is very important; for handwriting is, as it were, an extension of the personality of the writer. And just as a man to some extent snares his personality with his near blood-relations in the form of family resemblances, so his handwriting often shows a subtle likeness to that of his near relatives. You must have noticed, as I have, how commonly the handwriting of one brother resembles that of another, and in just this peculiar and subtle way. The inference, then, from Mr. Britton’s statement is, that if the signature of the will was forged, it was probably forged by a relative of the deceased. But the only relative in question is his brother John.

===Chinese===
“更要紧的推论，得从收集来的签名里去求。我说过，签名的变化起得很突然，去年九月间换过一两次手笔，此后便有两种截然分明的形态，中间没有任何过渡。这本身就很惹眼、很可疑。而布里顿先生的一句话，恰好为我们此刻考虑的这一点提供了一件真正有价值的证据。他承认签名的笔性经历了一个变化，但指出这变化并不触及笔迹的个人特性。这一点非常重要；因为笔迹，可以说，是执笔者人格的一种延伸。正如一个人的品格多少以家族相貌的形式同近亲分享，他的笔迹也往往同近亲的笔迹有一种微妙的相似。你们一定同我一样注意过，兄弟之间一个的笔迹长得像另一个的，何其常见，而且恰恰是以这种独特而微妙的方式。于是，从布里顿先生那句话得出的推论便是：倘若遗嘱上的签名是伪造的，伪造者多半是死者的一个亲属。而此处所说的亲属，只有他哥哥约翰。

===Original===
“All the facts, therefore, pointed to John Blackmore as the person who occupied these chambers, and I accordingly adopted that view as a working hypothesis.”

===Chinese===
“因此，种种事实都指认占据这几间屋子的人就是约翰·布莱克莫尔，我便把这个看法当作一个工作假说采纳了下来。”

===Original===
“But this was all pure speculation,” objected Mr. Winwood.

===Chinese===
“可这些全是凭空的臆测，”温伍德先生反驳道。

===Original===
“Not speculation,” said Thorndyke. “Hypothesis. It was ordinary inductive reasoning such as we employ in scientific research. I started with the purely tentative hypothesis that the person who signed the will was not Jeffrey Blackmore. I assumed this; and I may say that I did not believe it at the time, but merely adopted it as a proposition that was worth testing. I accordingly tested it, ‘Yes?’ or ‘No?’ with each new fact; but as each new fact said ‘Yes,’ and no fact said definitely ‘No,’ its probability increased rapidly by a sort of geometrical progression. The probabilities multiplied into one another. It is a perfectly sound method, for one knows that if a hypothesis be true, it will lead one, sooner or later, to a crucial fact by which its truth can be demonstrated.

===Chinese===
“不是臆测，”桑代克说，“是假说。这是寻常的归纳推理，同我们在科学研究里用的如出一辙。我起步时抱的是一个纯系试探的假说：在遗嘱上签字的，不是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔。我姑且这样假定；可以说，当时连我自己也不相信它，只把它当作一个值得检验的命题接受了下来。此后每得一个新事实，我便拿它问一句：‘是？’还是‘不是？’；但每个新事实都答‘是’，没有一个事实断然答‘不是’，于是它的或然性便以一种几何级数的势头迅速攀升。诸般概率彼此相乘。这是一个完全可靠的方法；因为你晓得，一个假说若是真的，迟早会把你引向一个决定性的事实，它的真实性便可借这个事实加以演示。

===Original===
“To resume our argument. We have now set up the proposition that John Blackmore was the tenant of New Inn and that he was personating Jeffrey. Let us reason from this and see what it leads to.

===Chinese===
“回到我们的论证上来。我们已经立起了这样一个命题：约翰·布莱克莫尔是新客栈的房客，而且一直在假扮杰弗里。让我们从这个命题推下去，看它导向何处。

===Original===
“If the tenant of New Inn was John, then Jeffrey must be elsewhere, since his concealment at the inn was clearly impossible. But he could not have been far away, for he had to be producible at short notice whenever the death of Mrs. Wilson should make the production of his body necessary. But if he was producible, his person must have been in the possession or control of John. He could not have been at large, for that would have involved the danger of his being seen and recognized. He could not have been in any institution or place where he would be in contact with strangers. Then he must be in some sort of confinement. But it is difficult to keep an adult in confinement in an ordinary house. Such a proceeding would involve great risk of discovery and the use of violence which would leave traces on the body, to be observed and commented on at the inquest. What alternative method could be suggested?

===Chinese===
“新客栈的房客若是约翰，杰弗里就必定在别处，因为要把他藏进客栈显然办不到。但他又不会在远处，因为一旦威尔逊太太死去、需要搬出他的尸身时，他必须一召即至、随取可得。而既要随取可得，他的人身就必须握在约翰手里，受他控制。他不能逍遥在外，因为那就有被人看见、认出的危险。他不能待在任何会使他接触生人的机构或场所。那么，他就必定处在某种禁闭之中。可是，要把一个成年人禁闭在寻常人家宅子里，谈何容易。这种做法被发觉的风险极大，还免不了动粗，会在尸身上留下痕迹，到验尸审讯时惹人注目、招人议论。还有什么别的办法可以想呢？

===Original===
“The most obvious method is that of keeping the prisoner in such a state of debility as would confine him to his bed. But such debility could be produced by only starvation, unsuitable food, or chronic poisoning. Of these alternatives, poisoning is much more exact, more calculable in its effect and more under control. The probabilities, then, were in favour of chronic poisoning.

===Chinese===
“最现成的办法，是把囚徒弄得虚弱到离不开床铺。可是这样的虚弱，只有靠饥饿、不合宜的饮食，或者慢性毒害才造得出来。在这几样当中，下毒远为精确，效力更可预计，也更易掌控。于是，可能性偏向了慢性毒害一边。

===Original===
“Having reached this stage, I recalled a singular case which Jervis had mentioned to me and which seemed to illustrate this method. On our return home I asked him for further particulars, and he then gave me a very detailed description of the patient and the circumstances. The upshot was rather startling. I had looked on his case as merely illustrative, and wished to study it for the sake of the suggestions that it might offer. But when I had heard his account, I began to suspect that there was something more than mere parallelism of method. It began to look as if his patient, Mr. Graves, might actually be Jeffrey Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“推到这一步，我想起杰维斯对我提过的一桩离奇病例，似乎正好给这种手法作个例证。回家路上，我向他细问缘由，他便把病人和种种情形讲得非常细致。结果相当惊人。我原只把他的病例当作一个例证看待，想研究一番，讨些启发。可是听完他的讲述，我开始疑心这里头不止是手法上的偶然相似。看起来，他的病人格雷夫斯先生，很可能就是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“The coincidences were remarkable. The general appearance of the patient tallied completely with Mr. Stephen’s description of his uncle Jeffrey. The patient had a tremulous iris in his right eye and had clearly suffered from dislocation of the crystalline lens. But from Mr. Stephen’s account of his uncle’s sudden loss of sight in the right eye after a fall, I judged that Jeffrey had also suffered from dislocation of the lens and therefore had a tremulous iris in the right eye. The patient, Graves, evidently had defective vision in his left eye, as proved by the marks made behind his ears by the hooked sidebars of his spectacles; for it is only on spectacles that are intended for constant use that we find hooked sidebars. But Jeffrey had defective vision in his left eye and wore spectacles constantly. Lastly, the patient Graves was suffering from chronic morphine poisoning, and morphine was found in the body of Jeffrey.

===Chinese===
“那些巧合非同小可。病人的相貌，同斯蒂芬先生对他叔父杰弗里的描述完全吻合。病人右眼虹膜震颤，显然害过晶状体脱位。而据斯蒂芬先生所讲，他叔父当年摔了一跤之后右眼骤然失明；我由此断定，杰弗里也害过晶状体脱位，因而右眼也有虹膜震颤。病人格雷夫斯左眼视力显然不济，证据是他耳后被眼镜弯腿的钩子压出的印子；因为只有打算常年佩戴的眼镜，我们才见配上弯腿。而杰弗里恰好左眼视力不济，常年戴着眼镜。最后，病人格雷夫斯害的正是慢性吗啡中毒，而杰弗里的尸身里也检出了吗啡。

===Original===
“Once more, it appeared to me that there were too many coincidences.

===Chinese===
“又一次，我觉得这巧合未免太多。

===Original===
“The question as to whether Graves and Jeffrey were identical admitted of fairly easy disproof; for if Graves was still alive, he could not be Jeffrey. It was an important question and I resolved to test it without delay. That night, Jervis and I plotted out the chart, and on the following morning we located the house. But it was empty and to let. The birds had flown, and we failed to discover whither they had gone.

===Chinese===
“格雷夫斯与杰弗里是否同一人，这个问题倒不难证其不然；因为格雷夫斯若还活着，就不可能是杰弗里。这是一个要紧的问题，我决意立刻检验。当天夜里，我和杰维斯把轨迹图绘了出来；第二天上午，我们找到了那所宅子。可是人去屋空，房子挂出了招租。鸟儿已经飞了，我们也没能查明它们飞向了何处。

===Original===
“However, we entered the house and explored. I have told you about the massive bolts and fastenings that we found on the bedroom doors and window, showing that the room had been used as a prison. I have told you of the objects that we picked out of the dust-heap under the grate. Of the obvious suggestion offered by the Japanese brush and the bottle of ‘spirit gum’ or cement, I need not speak now; but I must trouble you with some details concerning the broken spectacles. For here we had come upon the crucial fact to which, as I have said, all sound inductive reasoning brings one sooner or later.

===Chinese===
“不过，我们还是进了宅子，搜查了一番。卧室门上和窗户上那些粗大的门闩锁扣，说明那房间曾被当作囚室使用，这我已经对你们讲过。我们从炉栅下的灰堆里捡出的那些东西，也讲过了。日本毛刷和那瓶‘胶水’即粘合剂所给出的明白提示，眼下不必去说；但那副碎眼镜的几处细节，却须烦请诸位听我一叙。因为我们在这里碰上的，正是那个决定性的事实——如我所说，一切可靠的归纳推理，迟早会把人引到这样的事实跟前。

===Original===
“The spectacles were of a rather peculiar pattern. The frames were of the type invented by Mr. Stopford of Moorfields and known by his name. The right eyepiece was fitted with plain glass, as is usual in the case of a blind, or useless, eye. It was very much shattered, but its character was obvious. The glass of the left eye was much thicker and fortunately less damaged, so that I was able accurately to test its refraction.

===Chinese===
