“那副眼镜式样相当特别。镜框是莫尔菲尔兹（Moorfields）的斯托普福德（Stopford）先生发明的型式，即以他的名字行世。右眼镜片配的是平光玻璃，瞎眼或废眼所配的眼镜通常如此。那镜片碎得厉害，性质却一望即知。左眼镜片厚得多，幸而破损较轻，我得以精确测定它的屈光度。

===Original===
“When I reached home, I laid the pieces of the spectacles together, measured the frames very carefully, tested the left eyeglass, and wrote down a full description such as would have been given by the surgeon to the spectacle-maker. Here it is, and I will ask you to note it carefully.

===Chinese===
“到家以后，我把眼镜的碎片拼拢，仔仔细细量过镜框，检验了左眼镜片，写下了一份完整的描述，就像眼科医生开给眼镜匠的那样。描述在此，请诸位留意细看。

===Original===
> “ ‘Spectacles for constant use. Steel frame, Stopford’s pattern, curl sides, broad bridge with gold lining. Distance between centres, 6.2 centimetres; extreme length of sidebars, 13.3 centimetres.
> “ ‘Right eye
> plain glass
> “ ‘Left eye
> −5.75D. spherical
> −3.25D. cylindrical, axis 35°.’

===Chinese===
> “ ‘常戴之镜。钢框，斯托普福德式，卷边镜腿，宽鼻梁衬金片。两镜片中心间距 6.2 厘米；镜腿全长 13.3 厘米。
> “ ‘右眼
> 平光玻璃
> “ ‘左眼
> −5.75D. 球面
> −3.25D. 柱面，轴 35°。’

===Original===
“The spectacles, you see, were of a very distinctive character and seemed to offer a good chance of identification. Stopford’s frames are, I believe, made by only one firm of opticians in London, Parry & Cuxton of Regent Street. I therefore wrote to Mr. Cuxton, who knows me, asking him if he had supplied spectacles to the late Jeffrey Blackmore, Esq.﻿—here is a copy of my letter﻿—and if so, whether he would mind letting me have a full description of them, together with the name of the oculist who prescribed them.

===Chinese===
“你们看，这副眼镜特色十分鲜明，似乎大有机会借以查明身份。据我所知，斯托普福德式镜架，伦敦只有一家眼镜行承制——摄政街（Regent Street）的帕里-卡克斯顿（Parry & Cuxton）。于是我写信给卡克斯顿（Cuxton）先生——他认得我——问他可曾给已故的杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生配过眼镜——我这封信的副本在此——如若配过，可否劳驾给我一份详细的描述，连同开方的眼科医生的大名。

===Original===
“He replied in this letter, which is pinned to the copy of mine, that, about four years ago, he supplied a pair of glasses to Mr. Jeffrey Blackmore, and described them thus:

===Chinese===
“他回了这封信，别针别在我那封的副本上；信里说，大约四年前，他给杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生配过一副眼镜，描述如下：

===Original===
> “ ‘The spectacles were for constant use and had steel frames of Stopford’s pattern with curl sides, the length of the sidebars including the curled ends being 13.3 cm. The bridge was broad with a gold lining-plate, shaped as shown by the enclosed tracing from the diagram on the prescription. Distance between centres 6.2 cm.
> “ ‘Right eye
> plain glass
> “ ‘Left eye
> −5.75D. spherical
> −3.25D. cylindrical, axis 35°.’
> “ ‘The spectacles were prescribed by Mr. Hindley of Wimpole Street.’

===Chinese===
> “ ‘该镜系常戴之用，钢框，斯托普福德式，卷边镜腿，镜腿连卷端全长 13.3 厘米。鼻梁宽，衬金片，形状如随函附上的、自处方图样描下的样张所示。两镜片中心间距 6.2 厘米。
> “ ‘右眼
> 平光玻璃
> “ ‘左眼
> −5.75D. 球面
> −3.25D. 柱面，轴 35°。’
> “ ‘该镜由温波尔街（Wimpole Street）的欣德利（Hindley）先生开方。’

===Original===
“You see that Mr. Cuxton’s description is identical with mine. However, for further confirmation, I wrote to Mr. Hindley, asking certain questions, to which he replied thus:

===Chinese===
“你们看，卡克斯顿先生的描述与我所写的一字不差。不过，为进一步确证起见，我又写信给欣德利先生，提了几个问题，他答复如下：

===Original===
“ ‘You are quite right. Mr. Jeffrey Blackmore had a tremulous iris in his right eye (which was practically blind), due to dislocation of the lens. The pupils were rather large; certainly not contracted.’

===Chinese===
“ ‘您说得不错。杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生右眼（那只眼几乎全瞎）因晶状体脱位而虹膜震颤。瞳孔偏大；绝非收缩之状。’

===Original===
“Here, then, we have three important facts. One is that the spectacles found by us at Kennington Lane were undoubtedly Jeffrey’s; for it is as unlikely that there exists another pair of spectacles exactly identical with those as that there exists another face exactly like Jeffrey’s face. The second fact is that the description of Jeffrey tallies completely with that of the sick man, Graves, as given by Dr. Jervis; and the third is that when Jeffrey was seen by Mr. Hindley, there was no sign of his being addicted to the taking of morphine. The first and second facts, you will agree, constitute complete identification.”

===Chinese===
“这样，我们就有了三桩要紧的事实。其一：我们在肯宁顿巷发现的那副眼镜，毫无疑问是杰弗里的；因为世上若还存在另一副与它分毫不差的眼镜，就如同世上还存在另一张同杰弗里一模一样的脸，殊难置信。其二：对杰弗里的描述，与杰维斯医生所述病人格雷夫斯的模样完全吻合。其三：杰弗里就诊于欣德利先生的时候，毫无服吗啡成瘾的迹象。第一、第二两桩，诸位会同意，已构成完全的身份认定。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Marchmont; “I think we must admit the identification as being quite conclusive, though the evidence is of a kind that is more striking to the medical than to the legal mind.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”马奇蒙特说；“我想我们必须承认，这身份认定是完全确凿的；只是这类证据，打动医学的头脑，胜过打动法律的头脑。”

===Original===
“You will not have that complaint to make against the next item of evidence,” said Thorndyke. “It is after the lawyer’s own heart, as you shall hear. A few days ago I wrote to Mr. Stephen asking him if he possessed a recent photograph of his uncle Jeffrey. He had one, and he sent it to me by return. This portrait I showed to Dr. Jervis and asked him if he had ever seen the person it represented. After examining it attentively, without any hint whatever from me, he identified it as the portrait of the sick man, Graves.”

===Chinese===
“下一件证据，你们可就没有这种话好说了，”桑代克说。“它正投律师的心意，诸位听着。几天前我写信给斯蒂芬先生，问他手里有没有他叔父杰弗里近年的照片。他有一张，随即给我寄了来。我把这张肖像拿给杰维斯医生看，问他可曾见过画上的人。他对着照片细细端详——我没有给他丝毫提示——认出这正是那位病人格雷夫斯的肖像。

===Original===
“Indeed!” exclaimed Marchmont. “This is most important. Are you prepared to swear to the identity, Dr. Jervis?”

===Chinese===
“当真！”马奇蒙特失声道。“这至关重要。杰维斯医生，你可愿意宣誓确认这个身份？”

===Original===
“I have not the slightest doubt,” I replied, “that the portrait is that of Mr. Graves.”

===Chinese===
“我没有半点怀疑，”我答道，“这张肖像画的就是格雷夫斯先生。”

===Original===
“Excellent!” said Marchmont, rubbing his hands gleefully; “this will be much more convincing to a jury. Pray go on, Dr. Thorndyke.”

===Chinese===
“好极了！”马奇蒙特说着，喜滋滋地搓起手来。“这在陪审团跟前可就有说服力多了。桑代克博士，请讲下去。”

===Original===
“That,” said Thorndyke, “completes the first part of my investigation. We had now reached a definite, demonstrable fact; and that fact, as you see, disposed at once of the main question﻿—the genuineness of the will. For if the man at Kennington Lane was Jeffrey Blackmore, then the man at New Inn was not. But it was the latter who had signed the will. Therefore the will was not signed by Jeffrey Blackmore; that is to say, it was a forgery. The case was complete for the purposes of the civil proceedings; the rest of my investigations had reference to the criminal prosecution that was inevitable. Shall I proceed, or is your interest confined to the will?”

===Chinese===
“这一件，”桑代克说，“完成了我的调查的第一部分。至此我们得到了一个明确的、可以演示的事实；而这个事实，诸位可以看到，一下子了结了那个主要问题——遗嘱的真伪。因为，肯宁顿巷那人若是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔，新客栈那人便不是。而签署遗嘱的，却是后一位。所以，遗嘱并非杰弗里·布莱克莫尔所签；这就是说，那是一件伪造品。用于民事诉讼，案子至此已经完备；我其余的调查，都关系着那场势必到来的刑事追诉。我是接着讲呢，还是诸位的心思只在遗嘱上？

===Original===
“Hang the will!” exclaimed Stephen. “I want to hear how you propose to lay hands on the villain who murdered poor old uncle Jeffrey﻿—for I suppose he did murder him?”

===Chinese===
“让遗嘱见鬼去吧！”斯蒂芬嚷道。“我要听的是，你打算怎么把那个害死可怜的老叔父杰弗里的恶棍捉拿归案——我想，他总归是谋害了他吧？”

===Original===
“I think there is no doubt of it,” replied Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我想这是毫无疑义的。”桑代克答道。

===Original===
“Then,” said Marchmont, “we will hear the rest of the argument, if you please.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”马奇蒙特说，“就请你把论证的其余部分讲完吧。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Thorndyke. “As the evidence stands, we have proved that Jeffrey Blackmore was a prisoner in the house in Kennington Lane and that someone was personating him at New Inn. That someone, we have seen, was, in all probability, John Blackmore. We now have to consider the man Weiss. Who was he? and can we connect him in any way with New Inn?

===Chinese===
“很好，”桑代克说。“就现有的证据而言，我们已经证明：杰弗里·布莱克莫尔是被囚在肯宁顿巷那所宅子里的囚徒；另有某人在新客栈冒他的名。而那个人，如我们所见，十有八九是约翰·布莱克莫尔。现在我们得谈谈韦斯这个人了。他是谁？我们能不能把他同新客栈牵上什么关系？

===Original===
“We may note in passing that Weiss and the coachman were apparently one and the same person. They were never seen together. When Weiss was present, the coachman was not available even for so urgent a service as the obtaining of an antidote to the poison. Weiss always appeared some time after Jervis’s arrival and disappeared some time before his departure, in each case sufficiently long to allow of a change of disguise. But we need not labour the point, as it is not of primary importance.

===Chinese===
“顺便可以注意到：韦斯和那车夫显然是一而二、二而一的同一人。两人从未同时露过面。韦斯在场的时候，那车夫便连取解毒药这等火烧眉毛的差事也使唤不出来。韦斯总是在杰维斯到达之后过一阵子才现身，又在杰维斯离开之前一阵子便没了影，前后两段的空当，都尽够从容改一回装。不过这一点不必多费唇舌，它算不得首要的关键。

===Original===
“To return to Weiss. He was clearly heavily disguised, as we see by his unwillingness to show himself even by the light of a candle. But there is an item of positive evidence on this point which is important from having other bearings. It is furnished by the spectacles worn by Weiss, of which you have heard Jervis’s description. These spectacles had very peculiar optical properties. When you looked *through* them they had the properties of plain glass; when you looked *at* them they had the appearance of lenses. But only one kind of glass possesses these properties; namely, that which, like an ordinary watch-glass, has curved, parallel surfaces. But for what purpose could a person wear ‘watch-glass’ spectacles? Clearly, not to assist his vision. The only alternative is disguise.

===Chinese===
“回到韦斯身上。他显然做过重手笔的化装，这从他连烛光底下也不肯露面便可看出。但此事有一件正面的证据，其要紧另有所在。这证据，便是韦斯戴的那副眼镜；杰维斯对它的描述，诸位已经听过。这副眼镜的光学性质非常特别。你*透过*它看，它具有平光玻璃的性质；你*对着*它看，它却显出透镜的模样。可是，只有一种玻璃具有这样的性质；就是那种同寻常表玻璃一样、两面曲面彼此平行的玻璃。可是一个人戴上‘表玻璃’眼镜，图的是什么呢？显然不是为了帮视力。剩下的解释只有一样：化装。

===Original===
“The properties of these spectacles introduce a very curious and interesting feature into the case. To the majority of persons, the wearing of spectacles for the purpose of disguise or personation, seems a perfectly simple and easy proceeding. But, to a person of normal eyesight, it is nothing of the kind. For, if he wears spectacles suited for long sight he cannot see distinctly through them at all; while, if he wears concave, or near sight, glasses, the effort to see through them produces such strain and fatigue that his eyes become disabled altogether. On the stage the difficulty is met by using spectacles of plain window-glass, but in real life this would hardly do; the ‘property’ spectacles would be detected at once and give rise to suspicion.

===Chinese===
“这副眼镜的性质，给本案添了一个非常古怪而有趣的看点。在多数人看来，为化装或假扮而戴眼镜，似乎再简单容易不过。可是对一个视力正常的人，绝非如此。他若戴上适合远视的眼镜，透过镜片根本看不清东西；若戴上凹透镜、也就是近视镜，费力去看，那种紧张与疲乏会叫他的眼睛全然不管用。舞台上，这难题用普通窗玻璃的镜子来应付；现实生活中却行不通：那种‘道具’眼镜一眼就会被看破，惹人生疑。

===Original===
“The personator is therefore in this dilemma: if he wears actual spectacles, he cannot see through them; if he wears sham spectacles of plain glass, his disguise will probably be detected. There is only one way out of the difficulty, and that not a very satisfactory one; but Mr. Weiss seems to have adopted it in lieu of a better. It is that of using watch-glass spectacles such as I have described.

===Chinese===
“于是假扮者便落进了两难：戴真眼镜，他看不清东西；戴平光玻璃的假眼镜，他的化装多半要被识破。出路只有一条，而且不算怎样妥善；但韦斯先生似乎在没有更好办法的情形下选中了它。那就是用我方才描述的这种表玻璃眼镜。

===Original===
“Now, what do we learn from these very peculiar glasses? In the first place they confirm our opinion that Weiss was wearing a disguise. But, for use in a room so very dimly lighted, the ordinary stage spectacles would have answered quite well. The second inference is, then, that these spectacles were prepared to be worn under more trying conditions of light﻿—out of doors, for instance. The third inference is that Weiss was a man with normal eyesight; for otherwise he could have worn real spectacles suited to the state of his vision.

===Chinese===
“那么，从这副非常特别的眼镜里，我们得到些什么教益呢？首先，它印证了我们的见解：韦斯戴着化装。可是，要在那么一间光线极暗的屋子里使用，寻常的舞台眼镜本来也就尽够。因此，第二个推论是：这副眼镜是预备在更苛刻的光线下用的——比如在户外。第三个推论是：韦斯是个视力正常的人；否则，他本可以戴一副适合自己目力的真眼镜。

===Original===
“These are inferences by the way, to which we may return. But these glasses furnish a much more important suggestion. On the floor of the bedroom at New Inn I found some fragments of glass which had been trodden on. By joining one or two of them together, we have been able to make out the general character of the object of which they formed parts. My assistant﻿—who was formerly a watchmaker﻿—judged that object to be the thin crystal glass of a lady’s watch, and this, I think, was Jervis’s opinion. But the small part which remains of the original edge furnishes proof in two respects that this was not a watch-glass. In the first place, on taking a careful tracing of this piece of the edge, I found that its curve was part of an ellipse; but watch-glasses, nowadays, are invariably circular. In the second place, watch-glasses are ground on the edge to a single bevel to snap into the bezel or frame; but the edge of this object was ground to a double bevel, like the edge of a spectacle-glass, which fits into a groove in the frame and is held by the sidebar screw. The inevitable inference was that this was a spectacle-glass. But, if so, it was part of a pair of spectacles identical in properties with those worn by Mr. Weiss.

===Chinese===
“这些都是顺带的推论，我们回头还可以再谈。但这副眼镜还供给了一个要紧得多的提示。在新客栈卧室的地板上，我发现了一些被人踩碎的玻璃片。把其中一两片拼合起来，我们已能认出它们所属物件的大致性质。我的助手——他从前是个钟表匠——判断那物件是一块女表的水晶薄盖，我想杰维斯也是这个意见。可是，原件边缘残留的那一小弧，从两方面证明了它并不是表玻璃。第一，把这一小片边缘仔细描摹下来，我发现它的弧线是椭圆的一段；而如今的表玻璃，无一不是正圆。第二，表玻璃的边缘磨成单面的斜边，好卡进表圈或表框；而这物件的边缘磨成了双面的斜边，同眼镜片的边缘一样，嵌进镜框的凹槽，由镜腿螺丝扣住。不可避免的推论是：这是一块眼镜片。而倘若如此，它便是同韦斯先生所戴那副性质全同的一副眼镜上的一片。

===Original===
“The importance of this conclusion emerges when we consider the exceptional character of Mr. Weiss’s spectacles. They were not merely peculiar or remarkable; they were probably unique. It is exceedingly likely that there is not in the entire world another similar pair of spectacles. Whence the finding of these fragments of glass in the bedroom establishes a considerable probability that Mr. Weiss was, at some time, in the chambers at New Inn.

===Chinese===
“这个结论的要紧处，在我们想到韦斯先生那副眼镜的特异之处时，便显豁出来。它们不只是特别或罕见；它们多半是独一无二的。全世界未必再有第二副同样的眼镜。因此，在卧室里发现这些玻璃碎片，便确立起一个相当大的或然性：韦斯先生曾在某个时候到过新客栈的这套寓所。

===Original===
“And now let us gather up the threads of this part of the argument. We are inquiring into the identity of the man Weiss. Who was he?

===Chinese===
“现在让我们把这部分论证的头绪收拢来。我们查究的是韦斯这个人的身份。他是谁？

===Original===
“In the first place, we find him committing a secret crime from which John Blackmore alone will benefit. This suggests the prima facie probability that he was John Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“首先，我们发现他干的是一桩隐秘的罪行，而从中得利的，只有约翰·布莱克莫尔一人。这就提示出一个表面成立的或然性（prima facie probability）：他就是约翰·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“Then we find that he was a man of normal eyesight who was wearing spectacles for the purpose of disguise. But the tenant of New Inn, whom we have seen to be, almost certainly, John Blackmore﻿—and whom we will, for the present, assume to have been John Blackmore﻿—was a man with normal eyesight who wore spectacles for disguise.

===Chinese===
“其次，我们发现他是个视力正常的人，为化装而戴着眼镜。而新客栈那位房客——我们已经看到，他几乎可以断定是约翰·布莱克莫尔，此刻也不妨姑且假定就是约翰·布莱克莫尔——正是一个视力正常、为化装而戴眼镜的人。

===Original===
“John Blackmore did not reside at New Inn, but at some place within easy reach of it. But Weiss resided at a place within easy reach of New Inn.

===Chinese===
“约翰·布莱克莫尔不住在新客栈，而住在离客栈不远的地方。而韦斯，恰恰住在离新客栈不远的地方。

===Original===
“John Blackmore must have had possession and control of the person of Jeffrey. But Weiss had possession and control of the person of Jeffrey.

===Chinese===
“约翰·布莱克莫尔必定把杰弗里其人握在手里、置于控制之下。而韦斯，恰恰把杰弗里其人握在手里、置于控制之下。

===Original===
“Weiss wore spectacles of a certain peculiar and probably unique character. But portions of such spectacles were found in the chambers at New Inn.

===Chinese===
“韦斯戴的眼镜，性质特别，而且多半独一无二。而恰恰是这样的眼镜的碎片，在新客栈的寓所里被发现了。

===Original===
“The overwhelming probability, therefore, is that Weiss and the tenant of New Inn were one and the same person; and that that person was John Blackmore.”

===Chinese===
“所以，压倒一切的或然性是：韦斯与新客栈的房客本是同一个人；而那个人，就是约翰·布莱克莫尔。”

===Original===
“That,” said Mr. Winwood, “is a very plausible argument. But, you observe, sir, that it contains an undistributed middle term.”

===Chinese===
“这一番，”温伍德先生说，“论证得很动听。可是，先生，您当注意到，这论证里有一个不周延的中项。”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled genially. I think he forgave Winwood everything for that remark.

===Chinese===
桑代克和蔼地笑了。我想，单凭这一句话，他便把温伍德的一切都原谅了。

===Original===
“You are quite right, sir,” he said. “It does. And, for that reason, the demonstration is not absolute. But we must not forget, what logicians seem occasionally to overlook: that the ‘undistributed middle,’ while it interferes with absolute proof, may be quite consistent with a degree of probability that approaches very near to certainty. Both the Bertillon system and the English fingerprint system involve a process of reasoning in which the middle term is undistributed. But the great probabilities are accepted in practice as equivalent to certainties.”

===Chinese===
“您说得完全对，先生，”他说。“确实如此。也正因如此，这个论证不是绝对的。但我们不可忘记一件逻辑学家似乎偶尔也会忽略的事：‘不周延的中项’固然妨碍绝对的证明，却完全可以同一种近乎确凿的或然性并行不悖。贝蒂隆（Bertillon）系统和英国的指纹系统，推理过程中都含着一个不周延的中项。但巨大的或然性，在实践中已被当作确凿性来接受。”

===Original===
Mr. Winwood grunted a grudging assent, and Thorndyke resumed:

===Chinese===
温伍德先生勉勉强强咕哝了一声，表示同意；桑代克便接着说：

===Original===
“We have now furnished fairly conclusive evidence on three heads: we have proved that the sick man, Graves, was Jeffrey Blackmore; that the tenant of New Inn was John Blackmore; and that the man Weiss was also John Blackmore. We now have to prove that John and Jeffrey were together in the chambers at New Inn on the night of Jeffrey’s death.

===Chinese===
“至此，我们已经在三个方面提供了相当确凿的证据：我们证明了，病人格雷夫斯就是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔；新客栈的房客是约翰·布莱克莫尔；那个韦斯也是约翰·布莱克莫尔。现在还须证明：杰弗里死去的那个夜晚，约翰和杰弗里一同在新客栈的寓所里。

===Original===
“We know that two persons, and two persons only, came from Kennington Lane to New Inn. But one of those persons was the tenant of New Inn﻿—that is, John Blackmore. Who was the other? Jeffrey is known by us to have been at Kennington Lane. His body was found on the following morning in the room at New Inn. No third person is known to have come from Kennington Lane; no third person is known to have arrived at New Inn. The inference, by exclusion, is that the second person﻿—the woman﻿—was Jeffrey.

===Chinese===
“我们知道，从肯宁顿巷到新客栈去的，是两个人，而且只有两个人。其中一个，就是新客栈的房客——也就是约翰·布莱克莫尔。另一个是谁？杰弗里那时在肯宁顿巷，这一点我们已确知。他的尸身，第二天早晨却出现在新客栈的房间里。没有第三个人被查明从肯宁顿巷来过；也没有第三个人被查明到过新客栈。排他而论，结论便是：第二个人——那个女人——就是杰弗里。

===Original===
“Again; Jeffrey had to be brought from Kennington to the inn by John. But John was personating Jeffrey and was made up to resemble him very closely. If Jeffrey were undisguised the two men would be almost exactly alike; which would be very noticeable in any case and suspicious after the death of one of them. Therefore Jeffrey would have to be disguised in some way; and what disguise could be simpler and more effective than the one that I suggest was used?

===Chinese===
“再者；杰弗里必须由约翰从肯宁顿带到客栈。而约翰正在假扮杰弗里，化装得同他惟妙惟肖。倘若杰弗里不加化装，两人便几乎一个模样；这在哪种情形下都会惹眼，而在其中一人死去之后，就更可疑了。所以杰弗里必须设法化装；而还有什么化装，能比我所推测用了的那一种更简单、更见效呢？

===Original===
“Again; it was unavoidable that someone﻿—the cabman﻿—should know that Jeffrey was not alone when he came to the inn that night. If the fact had leaked out and it had become known that a man had accompanied him to his chambers, some suspicion might have arisen, and that suspicion would have pointed to John, who was directly interested in his brother’s death. But if it had transpired that Jeffrey was accompanied by a woman, there would have been less suspicion, and that suspicion would not have pointed to John Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“再者；那一晚杰弗里到客栈来时并非只身一人，这一点难免有人知道——那车夫。倘若这事实泄露出去，让人知道有一个男人陪他到了寓所，就可能生出疑心，而那疑心偏偏要指向与他兄弟之死有直接利害的约翰。可倘若传出去的是杰弗里由一个女人陪同，疑心便会小些，而且那疑心也就不会指向约翰·布莱克莫尔了。

===Original===
“Thus all the general probabilities are in favour of the hypothesis that this woman was Jeffrey Blackmore. There is, however, an item of positive evidence that strongly supports this view. When I examined the clothing of the deceased, I found on the trousers a horizontal crease on each leg as if the trousers had been turned up halfway to the knees. This appearance is quite understandable if we suppose that the trousers were worn under a skirt and were turned up so that they should not be accidentally seen. Otherwise it is quite incomprehensible.”

===Chinese===
“可见种种一般的或然性，都支持‘那女人就是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔’这个假说。此外，还有一件正面的证据，有力地支撑这个见解。我检查死者的衣物时，发现裤子的两条裤腿上各有一道横折痕，好像裤子曾在膝盖上方半途挽起过。倘若设想这裤子是穿在裙子里面、挽起裤脚以免不小心露出来，这外观便完全可以理解。否则，它便无从解释了。”

===Original===
“Is it not rather strange,” said Marchmont, “that Jeffrey should have allowed himself to be dressed up in this remarkable manner?”

===Chinese===
“杰弗里居然肯让人把自己装扮成这副模样，”马奇蒙特说，“岂不是相当蹊跷么？”

===Original===
“I think not,” replied Thorndyke. “There is no reason to suppose that he knew how he was dressed. You have heard Jervis’s description of his condition; that of a mere automaton. You know that without his spectacles he was practically blind, and that he could not have worn them since we found them at the house in Kennington Lane. Probably his head was wrapped up in the veil, and the skirt and mantle put on afterwards; but, in any case, his condition rendered him practically devoid of will power. That is all the evidence I have to prove that the unknown woman was Jeffrey. It is not conclusive but it is convincing enough for our purpose, seeing that the case against John Blackmore does not depend upon it.”

===Chinese===
“我看不然，”桑代克答道。“没有理由设想他知道自己是怎样一副穿着。杰维斯对他状态的描述，你们都听过了：不过是一具自动机器。你们知道，没了眼镜，他实际上等于瞎子；他又不可能戴眼镜，因为我们在肯宁顿巷的宅子里发现了它们。多半是先用面纱把他的头包了起来，然后才套上裙子和斗篷；但无论如何，他的状态已使他实际上全无意志可言。我要证明那无名女人就是杰弗里，证据就这么多。它算不上决定性的，但为我们的目的已足够服人；何况对约翰·布莱克莫尔的指控，并不系于这一点。”

===Original===
“Your case against him is on the charge of murder, I presume?” said Stephen.

===Chinese===
“你控他的罪名是谋杀，我料想？”斯蒂芬说。

===Original===
“Undoubtedly. And you will notice that the statements made by the supposed Jeffrey to the porter, hinting at suicide, are now important evidence. By the light of what we know, the announcement of intended suicide becomes the announcement of intended murder. It conclusively disproves what it was intended to prove; that Jeffrey died by his own hand.”

===Chinese===
“正是。而且你们会注意到，那个‘杰弗里’对门房所说、暗示自杀的那些话，如今成了重要的证据。以我们如今所知，那番预告自杀，便成了预告杀人。它恰恰彻底否证了它原本打算证明的东西——就是：杰弗里死于自己之手。”

===Original===
“Yes, I see that,” said Stephen, and then after a pause he asked: “Did you identify Mrs. Schallibaum? You have told us nothing about her.”

===Chinese===
“是，这个我明白。”斯蒂芬说。顿了一顿，他又问：“沙利鲍姆太太的身份，你查明了么？她的事，你一个字也没对我们讲过。”

===Original===
“I have considered her as being outside the case as far as I am concerned,” replied Thorndyke. “She was an accessory; my business was with the principal. But, of course, she will be swept up in the net. The evidence that convicts John Blackmore will convict her. I have not troubled about her identity. If John Blackmore is married, she is probably his wife. Do you happen to know if he is married?”

===Chinese===
“就我而论，我一直把她当作案外之人，”桑代克答道。“她是从犯；我办的是正犯。当然，她也会一并被收进网里。给约翰·布莱克莫尔定罪的证据，同样会给她定罪。她的身份，我没有费心去查。倘若约翰·布莱克莫尔结了婚，她多半是他的妻子。你们可知道他结过婚没有？”

===Original===
“Yes; but Mrs. John Blackmore is not much like Mrs. Schallibaum, excepting that she has a cast in the left eye. She is a dark woman with very heavy eyebrows.”

===Chinese===
“结过；不过约翰·布莱克莫尔太太同沙利鲍姆太太并不怎么相像，只有一处例外：她左眼斜视。她是个深色的妇人，眉毛极重。”

===Original===
“That is to say that she differs from Mrs. Schallibaum in those peculiarities that can be artificially changed and resembles her in the one feature that is unchangeable. Do you know if her Christian name happens to be Pauline?”

===Chinese===
“这就是说：在那些可以人为改变的特征上，她与沙利鲍姆太太不同；而在那唯一改不了的特征上，她与沙利鲍姆太太相像。你可知道，她的教名恰好叫波琳（Pauline）么？”

===Original===
“Yes, it is. She was a Miss Pauline Hagenbeck, a member of an American theatrical company. What made you ask?”

===Chinese===
“正是。她原是波琳·哈根贝克（Pauline Hagenbeck）小姐，美国一家巡回剧团的团员。你怎么想到问这个？”

===Original===
“The name which Jervis heard poor Jeffrey struggling to pronounce seemed to me to resemble Pauline more than any other name.”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯听见可怜的杰弗里挣扎着想喊出的那个名字，依我听来，同‘波琳’的相似，胜过同任何别的名字。”

===Original===
“There is one little point that strikes me,” said Marchmont. “Is it not rather remarkable that the porter should have noticed no difference between the body of Jeffrey and the living man whom he knew by sight, and who must, after all, have been distinctly different in appearance?”

===Chinese===
“有一处小地方令我纳闷，”马奇蒙特说。“门房认得那个活人，而尸身同他毕竟面目有别，门房竟没有觉察出一点差异来，这岂不是相当可怪么？”

===Original===
“I am glad you raised that question,” Thorndyke replied, “for that very difficulty presented itself to me at the beginning of the case. But on thinking it over, I decided that it was an imaginary difficulty, assuming, as we do, that there was a good deal of resemblance between the two men. Put yourself in the porter’s place and follow his mental processes. He is informed that a dead man is lying on the bed in Mr. Blackmore’s rooms. Naturally, he assumes that the dead man is Mr. Blackmore﻿—who, by the way, had hinted at suicide only the night before. With this idea he enters the chambers and sees a man a good deal like Mr. Blackmore and wearing Mr. Blackmore’s clothes, lying on Mr. Blackmore’s bed. The idea that the body could be that of some other person has never entered his mind. If he notes any difference of appearance he will put that down to the effects of death; for everyone knows that a man dead looks somewhat different from the same man alive. I take it as evidence of great acuteness on the part of John Blackmore that he should have calculated so cleverly, not only the mental process of the porter, but the erroneous reasoning which everyone would base on the porter’s conclusions. For, since the body was actually Jeffrey’s, and was identified by the porter as that of his tenant, it has been assumed by everyone that no question was possible as to the identity of Jeffrey Blackmore and the tenant of New Inn.”

===Chinese===
“你提出这个问题，我很高兴，”桑代克答道，“因为正是这个难题，在案子一开头也摆到了我的面前。但细细想过之后，我断定它只是一个想象出来的难题——前提是我们承认，两人生得颇有几分相像。不妨把自己放在门房的地位，循着他的心理走一遍。他听说布莱克莫尔先生房里的床上躺着一个死人。自然而然，他认定那死人就是布莱克莫尔先生——顺带说一句，此人前一夜还暗示过自杀来着。怀着这个念头，他走进寓所，看见一个男人躺在布莱克莫尔先生的床上，穿着布莱克莫尔先生的衣裳，同布莱克莫尔先生颇有几分相像。‘这具尸身可能是别的什么人’，这个念头从来不曾进过他的脑子。他若注意到什么相貌上的差异，也会把它归之于死亡的影响；因为谁都知道，一个人死了的样子，同他活着时总有些两样。约翰·布莱克莫尔不但把门房的心理过程算计得这样准，连人人都会根据门房的结论作出的那番错误推理，也一并算计到了；我以为，这正是他精明过人的明证。因为，尸身既然确实是杰弗里的，又被门房认作了他的房客，于是人人都以为，杰弗里·布莱克莫尔与新客栈房客是否同一人，已经没有半点疑问可言了。”

===Original===
There was a brief silence, and then Marchmont asked:

===Chinese===
短暂的沉默过后，马奇蒙特问道：

===Original===
“May we take it that we have now heard all the evidence?”

===Chinese===
“我们是不是可以认为，证据已经听全了？”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Thorndyke. “That is my case.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”桑代克答道。“我的案子，全在这里了。”

===Original===
“Have you given information to the police?” Stephen asked eagerly.

===Chinese===
“你向警方报信了么？”斯蒂芬急切地问。

===Original===
“Yes. As soon as I had obtained the statement of the cabman, Ridley, and felt that I had enough evidence to secure a conviction, I called at Scotland Yard and had an interview with the Assistant Commissioner. The case is in the hands of Superintendent Miller of the Criminal Investigation Department, a most acute and energetic officer. I have been expecting to hear that the warrant has been executed, for Mr. Miller is usually very punctilious in keeping me informed of the progress of the cases to which I introduce him. We shall hear tomorrow, no doubt.”

===Chinese===
“报了。我一拿到车夫里德利的证词，觉得证据已足以定罪，便去了苏格兰场，会见了助理警务总监。案子如今在刑事调查部的米勒警司手里——一位极其精明干练的警官。我一直在等着听拘票执行的消息，因为米勒先生对我引荐去的案子，向来不厌其详地随时见告。明天，我们想必就有音信了。”

===Original===
“And, for the present,” said Marchmont, “the case seems to have passed out of our hands.”

===Chinese===
“那么，就眼前而论，”马奇蒙特说，“这案子似乎已经出了我们的手了。”

===Original===
“I shall enter a caveat, all the same,” said Mr. Winwood.

===Chinese===
“尽管如此，我还是要去登记一份知会备忘。”温伍德先生说。

===Original===
“That doesn’t seem very necessary,” Marchmont objected. “The evidence that we have heard is amply sufficient to ensure a conviction and there will be plenty more when the police go into the case. And a conviction on the charges of forgery and murder would, of course, invalidate the second will.”

===Chinese===
“那似乎大可不必，”马奇蒙特不以为然。“我们方才听到的证据，定罪已绰绰有余；警方一接手，证据还会多得多。伪造和谋杀两罪一旦定谳，第二份遗嘱当然就失效了。”

===Original===
“I shall enter a caveat, all the same,” repeated Mr. Winwood.

===Chinese===
“尽管如此，我还是要去登记一份知会备忘。”温伍德先生重说了一遍。

===Original===
As the two partners showed a disposition to become heated over this question, Thorndyke suggested that they might discuss it at leisure by the light of subsequent events. Acting on this hint﻿—for it was now close upon midnight﻿—our visitors prepared to depart; and were, in fact, just making their way towards the door when the bell rang. Thorndyke flung open the door, and, as he recognized his visitor, greeted him with evident satisfaction.

===Chinese===
两位合伙人在这问题上，眼看就要起劲地争执起来；桑代克便提议，这事不妨等日后事态分明，再从容商议。众人依了这个话头——此时已近午夜——来客们便起身告辞；不想他们正朝门口走去，门铃响了。桑代克一把拉开门，认出来客，喜形于色地招呼道：

===Original===
“Ha! Mr. Miller; we were just speaking of you. These gentlemen are Mr. Stephen Blackmore and his solicitors, Mr. Marchmont and Mr. Winwood. You know Dr. Jervis, I think.”

===Chinese===
“哈！米勒先生；我们正说起您呢。这几位是斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔先生，和他的两位律师，马奇蒙特先生与温伍德先生。杰维斯医生，您大概是认得的。”

===Original===
The officer bowed to our friends and remarked:

===Chinese===
那位警官向我们的朋友们躬了躬身，说道：

===Original===
“I am just in time, it seems. A few minutes more and I should have missed these gentlemen. I don’t know what you’ll think of my news.”

===Chinese===
“看来我来得正巧。再晚几分钟，这几位先生就同我错过了。不知你们听了我的消息，会作何感想。”

===Original===
“You haven’t let that villain escape, I hope,” Stephen exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“你们总不至于让那恶棍跑了吧，我希望。”斯蒂芬失声道。

===Original===
“Well,” said the Superintendent, “he is out of my hands and yours too; and so is the woman. Perhaps I had better tell you what has happened.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”警司说，“他已经出了我的手心，也出了你们的手心；那女人也一样。我还是把经过对你们讲了吧。”

===Original===
“If you would be so kind,” said Thorndyke, motioning the officer to a chair.

===Chinese===
“有劳您了。”桑代克说着，请那位警官落座。

===Original===
The superintendent seated himself with the manner of a man who has had a long and strenuous day, and forthwith began his story.

===Chinese===
警司带着一个劳碌终日的人的神气坐了下来，随即讲起他的经过。

===Original===
“As soon as we had your information, we procured a warrant for the arrest of both parties, and then I went straight to their flat with Inspector Badger and a sergeant. There we learned from the attendant that they were away from home and were not expected back until today about noon. We kept a watch on the premises, and this morning, about the time appointed, a man and a woman, answering to the description, arrived at the flat. We followed them in and saw them enter the lift, and we were going to get into the lift too, when the man pulled the rope, and away they went. There was nothing for us to do but run up the stairs, which we did as fast as we could race; but they got to their landing first, and we were only just in time to see them nip in and shut the door. However, it seemed that we had them safe enough, for there was no dropping out of the windows at that height; so we sent the sergeant to get a locksmith to pick the lock or force the door, while we kept on ringing the bell.

===Chinese===
“一得到你们的消息，我们就领了两名案犯的逮捕令；我随即带着巴杰（Badger）巡官和一名警士，直奔他们的公寓。到了那里，管事人告诉我们，他们出门在外，预计今天中午前后回来。我们便在寓所左右守候；今晨约定的时刻前后，一男一女来到公寓，同描述正相符合。我们跟了进去，看他们进了电梯；我们也正要挤进电梯，那男的把缆绳一拉，人就上去了。我们毫无办法，只好撒腿奔楼梯，跑得简直像赛跑；但他们还是先到了自己那层，我们恰好赶上看见他们一闪身进了门，把门关上了。不过，看来他们已是瓮中之鳖；那么高的窗户，跳是跳不成的；于是我们派警士去找锁匠，开锁也罢撬门也罢，我们自己只管一个劲儿按铃。

===Original===
“About three minutes after the sergeant left, I happened to look out of the landing window and saw a hansom pull up opposite the flats. I put my head out of the window, and, hang me if I didn’t see our two friends getting into the cab. It seems that there was a small lift inside the flat communicating with the kitchen, and they had slipped down it one at a time.

===Chinese===
“警士走了约莫三分钟，我碰巧从楼梯口的窗口往外一望，看见一辆双轮马车在公寓对面停住了。我把头探出窗外，好家伙，我看见的不是别人，正是我们那两位朋友在上马车。原来那套公寓里还有一部小升降机，直通厨房，他们两人便一次一个，悄悄打那里面溜了下去。

===Original===
“Well, of course, we raced down the stairs like acrobats, but by the time we got to the bottom the cab was off with a fine start. We ran out into Victoria Street, and there we could see it halfway down the street and going like a chariot race. We managed to pick up another hansom and told the cabby to keep the other one in sight, and away we went like the very deuce; along Victoria Street and Broad Sanctuary, across Parliament Square, over Westminster Bridge and along York Road; we kept the other beggar in sight, but we couldn’t gain an inch on him. Then we turned into Waterloo Station, and, as we were driving up the slope we met another hansom coming down; and when the cabby kissed his hand and smiled at us, we guessed that he was the sportsman we had been following.

===Chinese===
“唉，我们自然像杂技演员似的冲下楼梯；可等跑到楼下，马车已经抢了个漂亮的先手，绝尘而去。我们奔到维多利亚街上，还望得见它在半条街开外，跑得像战车竞逐一般。我们赶紧又雇了一辆双轮马车，吩咐车夫盯住前一辆；于是我们也撒开腿，一路狂追——沿维多利亚街，过宽圣所（Broad Sanctuary），穿议会广场，跨威斯敏斯特桥，直下约克路；我们始终盯着前头那个家伙，却一寸也追不上去。后来我们拐进滑铁卢车站，正沿坡道往上赶，迎面下来另一辆双轮马车；车夫朝我们飞了个吻，粲然一笑，我们便猜到，他就是我们一路紧追的那位好汉。

===Original===
“But there was no time to ask questions. It is an awkward station with a lot of different exits, and it looked a good deal as if our quarry had got away. However, I took a chance. I remembered that the Southampton express was due to start about this time, and I took a shortcut across the lines and made for the platform that it starts from. Just as Badger and I got to the end, about thirty yards from the rear of the train, we saw a man and a woman running in front of us. Then the guard blew his whistle and the train began to move. The man and the woman managed to scramble into one of the rear compartments and Badger and I raced up the platform like mad. A porter tried to head us off, but Badger capsized him and we both sprinted harder than ever, and just hopped on the footboard of the guard’s van as the train began to get up speed. The guard couldn’t risk putting us off, so he had to let us into his van, which suited us exactly, as we could watch the train on both sides from the lookout. And we did watch, I can tell you; for our friend in front had seen us. His head was out of the window as we climbed on to the footboard.

===Chinese===
“可是，哪有工夫问话。那车站出入口杂沓繁多，眼看我们的猎物多半要溜掉了。不过，我碰了碰运气。我记得南安普敦快车大约在这个时候发车，便抄近道横过铁道，直奔它发车的月台。巴杰和我刚跑到月台尽头，离列车尾部约三十码，只见一男一女正在我们前头奔跑。这时车长吹响了哨子，列车开动起来。那一男一女连滚带爬，钻进了尾部的一节车厢；巴杰和我发了疯似的沿月台猛追。一个搬运工想拦我们，被巴杰掀了个跟头；我们俩跑得愈发拼命，趁列车刚刚加速，一跃跳上了车长守车的踏板。车长不敢冒险把我们撵下去，只得放我们进了他的车厢；这正中我们下怀，因为从瞭望口，列车两侧都看得住。我们也真看住了，我可以告诉你们；因为我们那位朋友，早瞧见我们了。我们跳上踏板的时候，他的头正探在窗外呢。

===Original===
“However, nothing happened until we stopped at Southampton West. There, I need not say, we lost no time in hopping out, for we naturally expected our friends to make a rush for the exit. But they didn’t. Badger watched the platform, and I kept a lookout to see that they didn’t slip away across the line from the offside. But still there was no sign of them. Then I walked up the train to the compartment which I had seen them enter. And there they were, apparently fast asleep in the corner by the offside window, the man leaning back with his mouth open and the woman resting against him with her head on his shoulder. She gave me quite a turn when I went in to look at them, for she had her eyes half-closed and seemed to be looking round at me with a most horrible expression; but I found afterwards that the peculiar appearance of looking round was due to the cast in her eye.”

===Chinese===
“不过，一直到车停南安普敦西站，什么事也没有。到了那里，不必说，我们一刻也不耽搁就跳下了车，因为我们满以为那两位朋友要夺路抢出出口。可是他们没有。巴杰守住月台，我在这一侧瞭望，提防他们从铁道那一面溜走。可还是不见他们的影子。于是我沿着列车，走向我看见他们钻进去的那节车厢。他们就在那里，靠外侧车窗的角落里，看样子睡得死沉；男人仰着头，张着嘴；女人偎着他，头枕在他的肩上。我走进去端详他们的时候，她着实吓了我一跳：她两眼半阖，似乎正回头瞪着我，那副神情可怕极了；不过我后来才明白，那副回头张望的模样，原是她眼睛斜视的缘故。

===Original===
“They were dead, I suppose?” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“他们死了，我料想？”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Yes, sir. Stone dead; and I found these on the floor of the carriage.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。死得透透的；这两样东西，是我在车厢地板上捡到的。”

===Original===
He held up two tiny yellow glass tubes, each labelled “Hypodermic tabloids. Aconitine Nitrate gr. ¹⁄₆₄₀.”

===Chinese===
他举起两只小小的黄色玻璃管，标签上都是：“皮下注射片。硝酸乌头碱 gr. ¹⁄₆₄₀。”

===Original===
“Ha!” exclaimed Thorndyke, “this fellow was well up in alkaloidal poisons, it seems; and they appear to have gone about prepared for emergencies. These tubes each contained twenty tabloids, a thirty-second of a grain altogether, so we may assume that about twelve times the medicinal dose was swallowed. Death must have occurred in a few minutes, and a merciful death too.”

===Chinese===
“哈！”桑代克失声道。“看来这家伙对生物碱毒物造诣不浅；而且他们出门还备着应急的章程呢。这两只管子，每只装二十片，合计三十二分之一格令；可见吞服下去的，约莫是药用量的十二倍。死亡想必几分钟内便发作了，还是一场没有苦楚的死。”

===Original===
“A more merciful death than they deserved,” exclaimed Stephen, “when one thinks of the misery and suffering that they inflicted on poor old uncle Jeffrey. I would sooner have had them hanged.”

===Chinese===
“这样的死，已经便宜得不像话了，”斯蒂芬失声道，“一想到他们加在可怜的老叔父杰弗里身上的苦难和折磨。我倒宁愿看着他们上了绞架。”

===Original===
“It’s better as it is, sir,” said Miller. “There is no need, now, to raise any questions in detail at the inquest. The publicity of a trial for murder would have been very unpleasant for you. I wish Dr. Jervis had given the tip to me instead of to that confounded, overcautious﻿—but there, I mustn’t run down my brother officers: and it’s easy to be wise after the event.

===Chinese===
“事情这样了结，更好，先生，”米勒说。“如今验尸审讯上，也用不着再逐条细问了。一场谋杀案的审判闹得满城风雨，对诸位可不是愉快的事。我倒情愿杰维斯医生当初把那条线索递到我手里，而不是交给那个该死的、缩手缩脚的——罢了罢了，不该说同僚的坏话：事后诸葛亮，人人会做。”

===Original===
“Good night, gentlemen. I suppose this accident disposes of your business as far as the will is concerned?”

===Chinese===
“晚安，诸位先生。我想，这桩意外把你们遗嘱上的那件事，也就了结了吧？”

===Original===
“I suppose it does,” agreed Mr. Winwood. “But I shall enter a caveat, all the same.”

===Chinese===
“我想是了结了，”温伍德先生表示同意。“不过尽管如此，我还是要去登记一份知会备忘。”

## Dedication / 献词

===Original===
To 

===Chinese===
献给

===Original===
My Friend 

===Chinese===
我的朋友

===Original===
Bernard E. Bishop

===Chinese===
伯纳德·E·毕晓普（Bernard E. Bishop）

## VI Jeffrey Blackmore, Deceased / 杰弗里·布莱克莫尔，已故

===Original===
Having made the above proposition, Thorndyke placed a fresh slip of paper on the blotting pad on his knee and looked inquiringly at Mr. Marchmont; who, in his turn, sighed and looked at the bundle of documents on the table.

===Chinese===
提出上述建议之后，桑代克又把一张新纸条放到膝上的吸墨纸垫上，用探询的目光望着马奇蒙特先生；后者则叹了口气，望着桌上那捆文件。

===Original===
“What do you want to know?” he asked a little wearily.

===Chinese===
“你想知道些什么？”他问道，语气里带着几分倦意。

===Original===
“Everything,” replied Thorndyke. “You have hinted at circumstances that would account for a change in Jeffrey’s habits and that would explain an alteration in the character of his signature. Let us have those circumstances. And, if I might venture on a suggestion, it would be that we take the events in the order in which they occurred or in which they became known.”

===Chinese===
“全部，”桑代克答道，“你已语及某些情形，既可解释杰弗里习性的变化，又可说明他签名笔性的改变。那就请把这些情形和盘托出吧。再者，恕我冒昧献上一议：不妨按各事件发生的先后、或为人所知的先后，依次叙来。”

===Original===
“That’s the worst of you, Thorndyke,” Marchmont grumbled. “When a case has been squeezed out to the last drop, in a legal sense, you want to begin all over again with the family history of everyone concerned and a list of his effects and household furniture. But I suppose you will have to be humoured; and I imagine that the best way in which to give you the information you want will be to recite the circumstances surrounding the death of Jeffrey Blackmore. Will that suit you?”

===Chinese===
“你这个人，难办就难办在这里，桑代克，”马奇蒙特抱怨道，“一桩案子在法律意义上已被榨取得一滴不剩，你却偏要从头再来一遍，把相关每个人的家史，连同他的一应器物、家具，都开列个明白。不过我想，也只好依着你的性子了；而依我看，要把你想知道的情况奉告于你，最好的法子莫过于叙说一番围绕杰弗里·布莱克莫尔之死的种种情形。这样可合你的意？”

===Original===
“Perfectly,” replied Thorndyke; and thereupon Marchmont began:

===Chinese===
“再合适不过。”桑代克答道。于是马奇蒙特开始讲道：

===Original===
“The death of Jeffrey Blackmore was discovered at about eleven o’clock in the morning of the fifteenth of March. It seems that a builder’s man was ascending a ladder to examine a gutter on number 31, New Inn, when, on passing a second-floor window that was open at the top, he looked in and perceived a gentleman lying on a bed. The gentleman was fully clothed and had apparently lain down on the bed to rest; at least so the builder thought at the time, for he was merely passing the window on his way up, and, very properly, did not make a minute examination. But when, some ten minutes later, he came down and saw that the gentleman was still in the same position, he looked at him more attentively; and this is what he noticed﻿—but perhaps we had better have it in his own words as he told the story at the inquest.

===Chinese===
三月十五日上午十一时左右，杰弗里·布莱克莫尔之死被人发现。据说当时有一名营造工匠正沿梯而上，去查验新客栈31号的檐沟，途经一扇上半敞开的二楼窗户时，朝屋里一望，看见一位绅士躺在床上。那位绅士衣装齐整，看样子是躺到床上歇息；至少那位工匠当时是这么想的，因为他不过是上楼途中路过那扇窗，而且很守本分，未作细察。可是约莫十分钟后他下了梯子，见那位绅士依旧保持着原先的姿势，便把他端详得更仔细了些；他所注意到的情形是——不过，这些情形，我们或许还是采用他本人在验尸审讯（inquest）上讲述这故事时的原话为好。

===Original===
“ ‘When I came to look at the gentleman a bit more closely, it struck me that he looked rather queer. His face looked very white, or rather pale yellow, like parchment, and his mouth was open. He did not seem to be breathing. On the bed by his side was a brass object of some kind﻿—I could not make out what it was﻿—and he seemed to be holding some small metal object in his hand. I thought it rather a queer affair, so, when I came down I went across to the lodge and told the porter about it. The porter came out across the square with me and I showed him the window. Then he told me to go up the stairs to Mr. Blackmore’s chambers on the second pair and knock and keep on knocking until I got an answer. I went up and knocked and kept on knocking as loud as I could, but, though I fetched everybody out of all the other chambers in the house, I couldn’t get any answer from Mr. Blackmore. So I went downstairs again and then Mr. Walker, the porter, sent me for a policeman.

===Chinese===
“‘我凑近了些细看那位绅士，便觉得他模样颇为不对劲。他的脸色白得很，或者不如说是蜡黄，像羊皮纸，嘴张着。他似乎没有呼吸了。他身边的床上放着一件什么东西，黄铜的——我认不出那是什么——他手里似乎还攥着一件小小的金属物件。我觉得这事颇为蹊跷，所以一下来，我就到门房去，把这事对门房说了。门房随我穿过广场出来，我指给他看了那扇窗。他便吩咐我上楼去敲二楼布莱克莫尔先生的寓所门，一直敲到有人应门为止。我上去敲了，而且放开嗓门一个劲儿地敲，可是尽管那一宅里其余寓所的人全被我敲了出来，布莱克莫尔先生那边却始终无人应声。于是我重新下楼，门房沃克先生（Mr. Walker）便打发我去叫警察。

===Original===
“ ‘I went out and met a policeman just by Dane’s Inn and told him about the affair, and he came back with me. He and the porter consulted together, and then they told me to go up the ladder and get in at the window and open the door of the chambers from the inside. So I went up; and as soon as I got in at the window I saw that the gentleman was dead. I went through the other room and opened the outer door and let in the porter and the policeman.’

===Chinese===
“‘我出了门，正在戴恩客栈（Dane’s Inn）旁边碰上一名警察，便把这事对他说了，他随我一同回来。他和门房商量了一阵，随后便叫我爬上梯子，从那扇窗钻进去，从里面把寓所的门打开。我便爬了上去；刚从窗口进屋，我就看出那位绅士已经死了。我穿过另一间屋子，打开临街的外门，放门房和警察进屋。’”

===Original===
“That,” said Mr. Marchmont, laying down the paper containing the depositions, “is the way in which poor Jeffrey Blackmore’s death came to be discovered.

===Chinese===
“这，”马奇蒙特先生放下那卷载有证词笔录的文件，说道，“便是可怜的杰弗里·布莱克莫尔之死被发现的原委。

===Original===
“The constable reported to his inspector and the inspector sent for the divisional surgeon, whom he accompanied to New Inn. I need not go into the evidence given by the police officers, as the surgeon saw all that they saw and his statement covers everything that is known about Jeffrey’s death. This is what he says, after describing how he was sent for and arrived at the Inn:

===Chinese===
“警员向督察作了报告，督察便遣人去请警区医官（divisional surgeon），并陪同他来到新客栈。警官们提供的证词，我无须细述，因为医官的所见与他们并无二致，他的陈述已把关于杰弗里之死的一切已知事实包罗净尽。以下便是他所说的话（在描述了自己如何被请去、如何抵达客栈之后）：

===Original===
“ ‘In the bedroom I found the body of a man between fifty and sixty years of age, which has since been identified in my presence as that of Mr. Jeffrey Blackmore. It was fully dressed and wore boots on which was a moderate amount of dry mud. It was lying on its back on the bed, which did not appear to have been slept in, and showed no sign of any struggle or disturbance. The right hand loosely grasped a hypodermic syringe containing a few drops of clear liquid which I have since analysed and found to be a concentrated solution of strophanthin.

===Chinese===
“‘卧室里，我见到一具五十至六十岁之间的男尸，其后经人当着我的面认明，正是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生。死者衣装齐整，脚上穿着靴子，靴上沾有几许干泥。尸体仰卧在床上，那床不像是有人睡过的样子，尸身亦无任何挣扎或骚动的迹象。死者右手松松握着一支皮下注射器，内盛几滴澄清的液体，此液我事后作了分析，查明是毒毛旋花子苷（strophanthin）的浓溶液。

===Original===
“ ‘On the bed, close to the left side of the body, was a brass opium-pipe of a pattern which I believe is made in China. The bowl of the pipe contained a small quantity of charcoal, and a fragment of opium together with some ash, and there was on the bed a little ash which appeared to have dropped from the bowl when the pipe fell or was laid down. On the mantelshelf in the bedroom I found a small glass-stoppered jar containing about an ounce of solid opium, and another, larger jar containing wood charcoal broken up into small fragments. Also a bowl containing a quantity of ash with fragments of half-burned charcoal and a few minute particles of charred opium. By the side of the bowl were a knife, a kind of awl or pricker and a very small pair of tongs, which I believe to have been used for carrying a piece of lighted charcoal to the pipe.

===Chinese===
“‘床上，紧贴尸体左侧，放着一支黄铜的鸦片烟枪，看那式样，我相信出自中国。烟斗里盛着少量木炭，还有一小块鸦片和一些灰烬；床上另有一点灰，像是烟枪跌落或搁下时从斗中洒出来的。卧室的壁炉台上，我找到一只小小的玻璃塞罐，内盛约一盎司固体鸦片；旁边另有一只较大的罐子，盛着敲成小块的木炭。还有一只碗，盛着数量可观的灰，混杂着半焦的炭屑和几粒烧焦的鸦片细末。碗旁放着一把小刀、一件形似锥子或尖钻的器物，还有一把极小的夹钳，我料想那是用来夹取燃着的炭火送到烟枪上去的。

===Original===
“ ‘On the dressing-table were two glass tubes labelled “Hypodermic Tabloids: Strophanthin ¹⁄₅₀₀ grain,” and a minute glass mortar and pestle, of which the former contained a few crystals which have since been analysed by me and found to be strophanthin.

===Chinese===
“‘梳妆台上放着两只玻璃管，标签上写着“Hypodermic Tabloids: Strophanthin ¹⁄₅₀₀ grain”（皮下注射药片：毒毛旋花子苷，每片五百分之一格令），另有一副小巧的玻璃研钵与研杵，钵中剩着几粒晶体，我事后加以分析，查明是毒毛旋花子苷。

===Original===
“ ‘On examining the body, I found that it had been dead about twelve hours. There were no marks of violence or any abnormal condition excepting a single puncture in the right thigh, apparently made by the needle of the hypodermic syringe. The puncture was deep and vertical in direction as if the needle had been driven in through the clothing.

===Chinese===
“‘经检验尸体，我判断死亡已约十二小时。除右腿股部一个针眼外，尸身别无暴力伤痕或任何异常状况；那针眼显系皮下注射器的针头所留。针眼深而垂直，仿佛针是隔着衣物扎进去的。

===Original===
“ ‘I made a postmortem examination of the body and found that death was due to poisoning by strophanthin, which appeared to have been injected into the thigh. The two tubes which I found on the dressing-table would each have contained, if full, twenty tabloids, each tabloid representing one five-hundredth of a grain of strophanthin. Assuming that the whole of this quantity was injected the amount taken would be forty five-hundredths, or about one twelfth of a grain. The ordinary medicinal dose of strophanthin is one five-hundredth of a grain.

===Chinese===
“‘我对尸体作了解剖检验，查明死因是毒毛旋花子苷中毒，毒物似是注入股部的。梳妆台上那两只玻璃管若装满，各可容二十片药片，每片合一格令之五百分之一的毒毛旋花子苷。假定这一数量全部注入体内，则摄入量为五百分之四十格令，约合十二分之一格令。而毒毛旋花子苷的寻常药用剂量，是五百分之一格令。

===Original===
“ ‘I also found in the body appreciable traces of morphine﻿—the principal alkaloid of opium﻿—from which I infer that the deceased was a confirmed opium-smoker. This inference was supported by the general condition of the body, which was ill-nourished and emaciated and presented all the appearances usually met with in the bodies of persons addicted to the habitual use of opium.’

===Chinese===
“‘我还在尸体内检出可观的吗啡（morphine）——鸦片的主要生物碱——由此我推断，死者是个烟瘾已成的鸦片烟客。尸体的总体状况也印证了这一推断：死者营养不良，形销骨立，呈现出惯常吸食鸦片之人尸体上习见的种种形相。’”

===Original===
“That is the evidence of the surgeon. He was recalled later, as we shall see, but, meanwhile, I think you will agree with me that the facts testified to by him fully account, not only for the change in Jeffrey’s habits﻿—his solitary and secretive mode of life﻿—but also for the alteration in his handwriting.”

===Chinese===
“这便是医官的证词。如我们后文所见，他后来又被再度传讯；不过此时，我想你们会同意我的看法：他所证明的种种事实，不但充分解释了杰弗里习性的改变——他那离群索居、行踪隐秘的生活方式——也充分说明了他的笔迹何以起了变化。”

===Original===
“Yes,” agreed Thorndyke, “that seems to be so. By the way, what did the change in the handwriting amount to?”

===Chinese===
“是的，”桑代克表示同意，“看来确实如此。顺便问一句，笔迹的变化究竟有多大？”

===Original===
“Very little,” replied Marchmont. “It was hardly perceptible. Just a slight loss of firmness and distinctness; such a trifling change as you would expect to find in the handwriting of a man who had taken to drink or drugs, or anything that might impair the steadiness of his hand. I should not have noticed it, myself, but, of course, the people at the bank are experts, constantly scrutinizing signatures and scrutinizing them with a very critical eye.”

===Chinese===
“微乎其微，”马奇蒙特答道，“几乎察觉不出来。只是稍许失去了几分稳健与清晰；就像一个人沾上了酒或毒品、或任何可能损害手上稳劲的东西之后，笔迹所起的那种细微变化。换了我，本是不会察觉的；不过，银行里的人自然是行家，日日审视签名，而且审视起来，那眼睛挑剔得很。”

===Original===
“Is there any other evidence that bears on the case?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“可还有别的证词与本案有关？”桑代克问。

===Original===
Marchmont turned over the bundle of papers and smiled grimly.

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特翻动那捆文件，冷冷一笑。

===Original===
“My dear Thorndyke,” he said, “none of this evidence has the slightest bearing on the case. It is all perfectly irrelevant as far as the will is concerned. But I know your little peculiarities and I am indulging you, as you see, to the top of your bent. The next evidence is that of the chief porter, a very worthy and intelligent man named Walker. This is what he says, after the usual preliminaries.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的桑代克，”他说，“这些证词与本案没有丝毫干系。就遗嘱而言，它们全然风马牛不相及。可是我知道你的那些小癖性，你也看得出，我正一味由着你，纵你所欲。下一份证词来自门房领班，一个名叫沃克的十分正派而聪敏的人。略过例行的开场套语，他陈述如下。

===Original===
“ ‘I have viewed the body which forms the subject of this inquiry. It is that of Mr. Jeffrey Blackmore, the tenant of a set of chambers on the second floor of number thirty-one, New Inn. I have known the deceased nearly six months, and during that time have seen and conversed with him frequently. He took the chambers on the second of last October and came into residence at once. Tenants at New Inn have to furnish two references. The references that the deceased gave were his bankers and his brother, Mr. John Blackmore. I may say that the deceased was very well known to me. He was a quiet, pleasant-mannered gentleman, and it was his habit to drop in occasionally at the lodge and have a chat with me. I went into his chambers with him once or twice on some small matters of business and I noticed that there were always a number of books and papers on the table. I understood from him that he spent most of his time indoors engaged in study and writing. I know very little about his way of living. He had no laundress to look after his rooms, so I suppose he did his own housework and cooking; but he told me that he took most of his meals outside, at restaurants or his club.

===Chinese===
“‘我已验看构成本次查讯对象的尸体。那是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生的遗体，新客栈三十一号二楼一套寓所的租客。我认识死者将近六个月，其间常见到他，也常同他交谈。他是去年十月二日租下那套寓所的，并随即迁入居住。在新客栈租房，须提交两名保证人。死者提交的保证人，一个是他的银行，一个是他的兄弟约翰·布莱克莫尔先生。我可以说，死者与我相当熟识。他是位娴静而有礼的绅士，惯于偶尔到门房来坐一坐，同我闲聊几句。我曾因几件小事，随他进过一两回他的寓所，注意到他桌上总摊着许多书籍文件。据他自述，他把大部分时间用在室内研读和写作上。至于他的起居饮食，我知道得很少。他没有雇洗衣妇照料房间，所以我料想家务和烹炊都是他亲手操持；不过他对我说过，他一日三餐多半在外头将就，下下餐馆，或是去他的俱乐部。

===Original===
“ ‘Deceased impressed me as a rather melancholy, low-spirited gentleman. He was very much troubled about his eyesight and mentioned the matter to me on several occasions. He told me that he was practically blind in one eye and that the sight of the other was failing rapidly. He said that this afflicted him greatly, because his only pleasure in life was in the reading of books, and that if he could not read he should not wish to live. On another occasion he said that “to a blind man life was not worth living.”

===Chinese===
“‘死者给我的印象，是位相当忧郁、意气消沉的绅士。他为自己的视力深感苦恼，曾好几次向我提起这件事。他对我说，他一只眼实际上已完全瞎了，另一只眼的视力也在迅速衰退。他说这件事令他痛苦不堪，因为他此生唯一的乐趣便是读书，倘若不能读书，他便不想活了。还有一回，他说“对一个瞎了眼的人，生命是不值得活的”。

===Original===
“ ‘On the twelfth of last November he came to the lodge with a paper in his hand which he said was his will’﻿—But I needn’t read that,” said Marchmont, turning over the leaf, “I’ve told you how the will was signed and witnessed. We will pass on to the day of poor Jeffrey’s death.

===Chinese===
“‘去年十一月十二日，他手里拿着一份文书来到门房，说那是他的遗嘱’——不过这一段无须念了，”马奇蒙特说着，翻过一页，“遗嘱是如何签署、如何见证的，我已经告诉过你们了。我们且跳到可怜的杰弗里去世的那一天。

===Original===
“ ‘On the fourteenth of March,’ the porter says, ‘at about half-past six in the evening, the deceased came to the Inn in a four-wheeled cab. That was the day of the great fog. I do not know if there was anyone in the cab with the deceased, but I think not, because he came to the lodge just before eight o’clock and had a little talk with me. He said that he had been overtaken by the fog and could not see at all. He was quite blind and had been obliged to ask a stranger to call a cab for him as he could not find his way through the streets. He then gave me a cheque for the rent. I reminded him that the rent was not due until the twenty-fifth, but he said he wished to pay it now. He also gave me some money to pay one or two small bills that were owing to some of the tradespeople﻿—a milkman, a baker and a stationer.

===Chinese===
“‘三月十四日，’门房的证词说，‘傍晚六点半左右，死者乘一辆四轮出租马车来到客栈。那正是大雾弥漫的一天。马车上除死者外还有没有旁人，我不得而知，但我想没有，因为将近八点时，他来到门房，同我略谈了一小会儿。他说他被大雾困住，什么也看不见了。他整个人成了睁眼瞎，只得央求一位陌生人替他叫车，因为他在街上寻不着路。随后他交给我一张支付房租的支票。我提醒他房租要到二十五号才到期，他却说他愿意现在就付。他还交给我一笔钱，托我代付欠着几家铺户的一两笔小账——欠送奶人的、欠面包坊的，还有欠文具店的。

===Original===
“ ‘This struck me as very strange, because he had always managed his business and paid the tradespeople himself. He told me that the fog had irritated his eye so that he could hardly read, and he was afraid he should soon be quite blind. He was very depressed; so much so that I felt quite uneasy about him. When he left the lodge, he went back across the square as if returning to his chambers. There was then no gate open excepting the main gate where the lodge is situated. That was the last time that I saw the deceased alive.’ ”

===Chinese===
“‘这事我觉得非常古怪，因为他向来亲自打理自己的事务，亲自偿付铺户的账目。他对我说，大雾刺激了他的眼睛，害他几乎不能视物，他恐怕自己不久就要全瞎了。他情绪极为低沉，低沉得让我心里着实为他不安。他离开门房后，便折身穿过广场往回走，像是回寓所去了。那时除了门房所在的正门，各处门户都已关闭。那是我最后一次见到活着的死者。’”

===Original===
Mr. Marchmont laid the paper on the table. “That is the porter’s evidence. The remaining depositions are those of Noble, the night porter, John Blackmore and our friend here, Mr. Stephen. The night porter had not much to tell. This is the substance of his evidence:

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特先生把文件放回桌上。“这便是门房的证词。其余几份证词笔录，来自夜班门房诺布尔（Noble）、约翰·布莱克莫尔，还有我们这位朋友斯蒂芬先生。夜班门房没有多少可讲的。他的证词大意如下：

===Original===
“ ‘I have viewed the body of the deceased and identify it as that of Mr. Jeffrey Blackmore. I knew the deceased well by sight and occasionally had a few words with him. I know nothing of his habits excepting that he used to sit up rather late. It is one of my duties to go round the Inn at night and call out the hours until one o’clock in the morning. When calling out “one o’clock” I often saw a light in the sitting-room of the deceased’s chambers. On the night of the fourteenth instant, the light was burning until past one o’clock, but it was in the bedroom. The light in the sitting-room was out by ten o’clock.’

===Chinese===
“‘我已验看死者的尸体，认明那是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔先生。我与死者素来面熟，偶尔也同他说上几句话。他的习性我一无所知，只知道他习惯熬得很晚。夜间巡行客栈、逐时报时直到凌晨一点，是我的职责之一。每逢喊“一点钟”的时候，我常看见死者寓所起居室里亮着灯。本月十四日夜间，那盏灯一直亮到一点过后，只是亮的是卧室。起居室的灯，十点钟以前就熄了。’”

===Original===
“We now come to John Blackmore’s evidence. He says:

===Chinese===
“接下来是约翰·布莱克莫尔的证词。他说：

===Original===
“ ‘I have viewed the body of the deceased and recognize it as that of my brother Jeffrey. I last saw him alive on the twenty-third of February, when I called at his chambers. He then seemed in a very despondent state of mind and told me that his eyesight was fast failing. I was aware that he occasionally smoked opium, but I did not know that it was a confirmed habit. I urged him, on several occasions, to abandon the practice. I have no reason to believe that his affairs were in any way embarrassed or that he had any reason for making away with himself other than his failing eyesight; but, having regard to his state of mind when I last saw him, I am not surprised at what has happened.’

===Chinese===
“‘我已验看死者的尸体，认出那是我兄弟杰弗里。我最后一次见他活着，是二月二十三日，那天我到他的寓所拜访过。他当时心绪显得极为消沉，对我说他的视力正在急速衰退。我知道他偶尔吸食鸦片，但并不知道那已积习难改。我曾几次三番劝他戒掉这一嗜好。我没有理由认为他的事务有任何拮据之处，也没有理由认为，除了视力衰退之外，他还有什么自寻短见的缘由；不过，念及我最后一次见他时他的精神状态，今日之事，我并不感到意外。’”

===Original===
“That is the substance of John Blackmore’s evidence, and, as to Mr. Stephen, his statement merely sets forth the fact that he had identified the body as that of his uncle Jeffrey. And now I think you have all the facts. Is there anything more that you want to ask me before I go, for I must really run away now?”

===Chinese===
“这便是约翰·布莱克莫尔证词的大意。至于斯蒂芬先生，他的陈述不过载明一项事实：他已认明那具尸体是他的叔父杰弗里。好了，我想，事实你们已经听全了。在我走之前——我实在得赶紧离开了——可还有什么要问我的？”

===Original===
“I should like,” said Thorndyke, “to know a little more about the parties concerned in this affair. But perhaps Mr. Stephen can give me the information.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”桑代克说，“再多少了解一下与本案有关各方的情况。不过，这些情况斯蒂芬先生或许能够见告。”

===Original===
“I expect he can,” said Marchmont; “at any rate, he knows more about them than I do; so I will be off. If you should happen to think of any way,” he continued, with a sly smile, “of upsetting that will, just let me know, and I will lose no time in entering a caveat. Goodbye! Don’t trouble to let me out.”

===Chinese===
“我想他能，”马奇蒙特说，“无论如何，他比我了解得多；那我就先失陪了。倘若你碰巧想出什么法子，”他面带狡黠的微笑接着说，“能推翻那份遗嘱，务必知会我一声，我必毫不耽搁，去递上一份知会备忘（caveat）。再会！不必费事送我出去了。”

===Original===
As soon as he was gone, Thorndyke turned to Stephen Blackmore.

===Chinese===
他一走，桑代克便转向斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“I am going,” he said, “to ask you a few questions which may appear rather trifling, but you must remember that my methods of inquiry concern themselves with persons and things rather than with documents. For instance, I have not gathered very completely what sort of person your uncle Jeffrey was. Could you tell me a little more about him?”

===Chinese===
“我打算，”他说，“向你提几个问题，这些问题或许显得相当琐碎；但你要记住，我的调查方法挂心的是人与物，而不是文书。比方说，你叔父杰弗里是个什么样的人，我了解得还很不够。你能多给我讲讲他么？”

===Original===
“What shall I tell you?” Stephen asked with a slightly embarrassed air.

===Chinese===
“要我讲些什么呢？”斯蒂芬问道，神色略带窘迫。

===Original===
“Well, begin with his personal appearance.”

===Chinese===
“唔，就先从他的外貌讲起吧。”

===Original===
“That is rather difficult to describe,” said Stephen. “He was a medium-sized man and about five feet seven﻿—fair, slightly grey, clean-shaved, rather spare and slight, had grey eyes, wore spectacles and stooped a little as he walked. He was quiet and gentle in manner, rather yielding and irresolute in character, and his health was not at all robust though he had no infirmity or disease excepting his bad eyesight. His age was about fifty-five.”

===Chinese===
“他的模样颇难描摹，”斯蒂芬说，“中等身材，约莫五英尺七英寸——头发浅色，微微花白，面庞剃得干干净净，身形清瘦单薄，生一双灰眼睛，戴一副眼镜，走路时微微伛着背。他举止娴静温和，性格却相当柔顺寡断；除了那双坏眼睛，他别无残疾病痛，只是身子骨谈不上强健。年纪么，约五十五岁。”

===Original===
“How came he to be a civil-service pensioner at fifty-five?” asked Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“他五十五岁上，怎么就领上了公务员的养老金？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Oh, that was through an accident. He had a nasty fall from a horse, and, being a rather nervous man, the shock was very severe. For some time after he was a complete wreck. But the failure of his eyesight was the actual cause of his retirement. It seems that the fall damaged his eyes in some way; in fact he practically lost the sight of one﻿—the right﻿—from that moment; and, as that had been his good eye, the accident left his vision very much impaired. So that he was at first given sick leave and then allowed to retire on a pension.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那是一场变故的缘故。他从马上重重摔了一跤，而他本是个神经相当脆弱的人，这一吓非同小可。此后有一段日子，他整个人垮得不成样子。不过，他退职的真正缘由，还是视力的衰退。看来那一摔多少伤到了他的眼睛；事实上，从那一刻起，他一只眼——右眼——就实际失明了；而那只眼原本是他的好眼，经此一变，他的视力便大受摧损。于是，他先获准休病假，随后获准退职领养老金。”

===Original===
Thorndyke noted these particulars and then said:

===Chinese===
桑代克记下这些细节，然后说道：

===Original===
“Your uncle has been more than once referred to as a man of studious habits. Does that mean that he pursued any particular branch of learning?”

===Chinese===
“方才不止一次有人提到，你叔父是个性好研读的人。这是不是意味着，他治过哪一门专门的学问？”

===Original===
“Yes. He was an enthusiastic Oriental scholar. His official duties had taken him at one time to Yokohama and Tokyo and at another to Baghdad, and while at those places he gave a good deal of attention to the languages, literature and arts of the countries. He was also greatly interested in Babylonian and Assyrian archaeology, and I believe he assisted for some time in the excavations at Birs Nimroud.”

===Chinese===
“是的。他是个热忱的东方学者。他的公职一度把他派到横滨（Yokohama）和东京（Tokyo），又一度派往巴格达（Baghdad）；客居那些地方期间，他对当地的语言、文学和艺术下了不少功夫。他还对巴比伦和亚述的考古学抱有极浓的兴趣，而且我相信，他曾在比尔斯尼姆鲁德（Birs Nimroud）的发掘工作中相助过一阵子。”

===Original===
“Indeed!” said Thorndyke. “This is very interesting. I had no idea that he was a man of such considerable attainments. The facts mentioned by Mr. Marchmont would hardly have led one to think of him as what he seems to have been: a scholar of some distinction.”

===Chinese===
“原来如此！”桑代克说，“这非常有趣。我全然想不到，他竟是位造诣如此可观的人。单凭马奇蒙特先生提到的那些事实，人们很难把他想成他实际上似乎已是的那种人：一位造诣不俗的学者。”

===Original===
“I don’t know that Mr. Marchmont realized the fact himself,” said Stephen; “or that he would have considered it of any moment if he had. Nor, as far as that goes, do I. But, of course, I have no experience of legal matters.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道马奇蒙特先生自己是否意识到了这一层，”斯蒂芬说，“或者说，即便意识到了，他是否会觉得这有什么要紧。其实说来，我也不觉得。不过话说回来，法律上的事，我当然毫无阅历。”

===Original===
“You can never tell beforehand,” said Thorndyke, “what facts may turn out to be of moment, so that it is best to collect all you can get. By the way, were you aware that your uncle was an opium-smoker?”

===Chinese===
“什么事实日后可能变得要紧，谁也无法预先断定，”桑代克说，“所以最好把能弄到手的统统收罗起来。顺便问一句，你原先可知道你叔父吸鸦片？”

===Original===
“No, I was not. I knew that he had an opium-pipe which he brought with him when he came home from Japan; but I thought it was only a curio. I remember him telling me that he once tried a few puffs at an opium-pipe and found it rather pleasant, though it gave him a headache. But I had no idea he had contracted the habit; in fact, I may say that I was utterly astonished when the fact came out at the inquest.”

===Chinese===
“不知道，我并不知情。我知道他从日本回国时带回过一支鸦片烟枪，但我一直以为那不过是件古玩。我记得他对我说过，他有一回试着吸了几口鸦片烟，觉得相当惬意，只是吸得头疼。可我万万没有想到，他竟已染上了这一嗜好；说实在的，验尸审讯上事实披露出来时，我完全惊呆了。”

===Original===
Thorndyke made a note of this answer, too, and said:

===Chinese===
桑代克把这个回答也记了下来，说道：

===Original===
“I think that is all I have to ask you about your uncle Jeffrey. And now as to Mr. John Blackmore. What sort of man is he?”

===Chinese===
“关于你叔父杰弗里，我想问的就这么多了。现在说说约翰·布莱克莫尔先生吧。他是个怎样的人？”

===Original===
“I am afraid I can’t tell you very much about him. Until I saw him at the inquest, I had not met him since I was a boy. But he is a very different kind of man from Uncle Jeffrey; different in appearance and different in character.”

===Chinese===
“只怕我谈不出多少来。若不是验尸审讯上见到他，打从我还是个孩子时起，就不曾再见过他。不过，他同杰弗里叔父是截然不同的两种人：外貌不同，性情也不同。”

===Original===
“You would say that the two brothers were physically quite unlike, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么照你说，这兄弟俩在体貌上是全然不相像的了？”

===Original===
“Well,” said Stephen, “I don’t know that I ought to say that. Perhaps I am exaggerating the difference. I am thinking of Uncle Jeffrey as he was when I saw him last and of uncle John as he appeared at the inquest. They were very different then. Jeffrey was thin, pale, clean shaven, wore spectacles and walked with a stoop. John is a shade taller, a shade greyer, has good eyesight, a healthy, florid complexion, a brisk, upright carriage, is distinctly stout and wears a beard and moustache which are black and only very slightly streaked with grey. To me they looked as unlike as two men could, though their features were really of the same type; indeed, I have heard it said that, as young men, they were rather alike, and they both resembled their mother. But there is no doubt as to their difference in character. Jeffrey was quiet, serious and studious, whereas John rather inclined to what is called a fast life; he used to frequent race meetings, and, I think, gambled a good deal at times.”

===Chinese===
“唔，”斯蒂芬说，“我不知道该不该这么说。或许是我把差别夸大了。我心里想到的，是我最后一次见到的杰弗里叔父的模样，和在验尸审讯上露面的约翰叔父的模样。那时节，他们确实大不相同。杰弗里瘦削、苍白、面庞剃净，戴眼镜，走路伛着背。约翰则略高一线，头发也略花白一线，双目视力良好，面色红润健康，举止轻快、腰背挺直，身子分明发福，蓄着一部胡须和八字胡，颜色乌黑，只极稀疏地夹着几缕灰白。在我看来，他们兄弟俩已不相像到了两个人所能达到的极限，虽说两人的五官其实属于同一个类型；不但如此，我还听人说过，他们年轻时彼此颇为相像，而且都生得像他们的母亲。不过两人性情之迥异，则是毫无疑义的。杰弗里娴静、严肃、好学；约翰则偏向于所谓放荡的生活：他惯于出入赛马会，而且我想，时不时还赌得相当凶。”

===Original===
“What is his profession?”

===Chinese===
“他操的是哪一行？”

===Original===
“That would be difficult to tell; he has so many; he is so very versatile. I believe he began life as an articled pupil in the laboratory of a large brewery, but he soon left that and went on the stage. He seems to have remained in ‘the profession’ for some years, touring about this country and making occasional visits to America. The life seemed to suit him and I believe he was decidedly successful as an actor. But suddenly he left the stage and blossomed out in connection with a bucket-shop in London.”

===Chinese===
“这就难说了；他的行当多得很；他是个再多面手不过的人。我相信他起先是订了师约，在一家大啤酒厂的实验室里当学徒，可不久便离开那里，登台演戏去了。他似乎在‘演艺一行’里待了几年，在英国各地巡回演出，偶尔也去一趟美国。那种生活似乎很合他的脾胃，而且我相信，他做演员是断然成功的。可是忽然间，他离开了舞台，摇身一变，在伦敦一家赌注经纪行（bucket-shop）里风光了起来。”

===Original===
“And what is he doing now?”

===Chinese===
“那么他如今在做什么？”

===Original===
“At the inquest he described himself as a stockbroker, so I presume he is still connected with the bucket-shop.”

===Chinese===
“验尸审讯上，他自称是证券经纪人，所以我料想，他仍同那家赌注经纪行沾着边。”

===Original===
Thorndyke rose, and taking down from the reference shelves a list of members of the Stock Exchange, turned over the leaves.

===Chinese===
桑代克站起身来，从参考书架上取下一册证券交易所（Stock Exchange）会员名录，翻检起来。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said, replacing the volume, “he must be an outside broker. His name is not in the list of members of ‘the House.’ From what you tell me, it is easy to understand that there should have been no great intimacy between the two brothers, without assuming any kind of ill-feeling. They simply had very little in common. Do you know of anything more?”

===Chinese===
“是的，”他把那册书放回原处，说道，“他必定是个场外经纪人。他的名字不在‘交易所’（the House）的会员名录里。照你告诉我的这些看来，不难明白这兄弟俩何以素来不甚亲密，而无须假定他们之间存有什么嫌隙。他们不过是没有多少共同之处罢了。此外你还知道些什么？”

===Original===
“No. I have never heard of any actual quarrel or disagreement. My impression that they did not get on very well may have been, I think, due to the terms of the will, especially the first will. And they certainly did not seek one another’s society.”

===Chinese===
“没有了。我从未听说他们真有过什么口角或龃龉。依我想，他们不甚相得这一印象，或许得归因于那份遗嘱的条款，尤其是第一份遗嘱。况且，他们也确实从不彼此过从。”

===Original===
“That is not very conclusive,” said Thorndyke. “As to the will, a thrifty man is not usually much inclined to bequeath his savings to a gentleman who may probably employ them in a merry little flutter on the turf or the Stock Exchange. And then there was yourself; clearly a more suitable subject for a legacy, as your life is all before you. But this is mere speculation and the matter is not of much importance, as far as we can see. And now, tell me what John Blackmore’s relations were with Mrs. Wilson. I gather that she left the bulk of her property to Jeffrey, her younger brother. Is that so?”

===Chinese===
“这算不上十分确凿，”桑代克说，“至于那份遗嘱：一个节俭的人，通常不大会情愿把积蓄遗赠给一位多半会拿它们到赛马场或证券交易所去痛痛快快小赌一场的先生。何况还有你自己呢；你的整个人生都还在前头，显然是更合适的遗产受主。不过，这只是凭空推想，就我们眼下的见识而言，此事无关宏旨。现在告诉我，约翰·布莱克莫尔同威尔逊太太的关系如何。据我领会，她把财产的大头留给了她的弟弟杰弗里。是这样么？”

===Original===
“Yes. She left nothing to John. The fact is that they were hardly on speaking terms. I believe John had treated her rather badly, or, at any rate, she thought he had. Mr. Wilson, her late husband, dropped some money over an investment in connection with the bucket-shop that I spoke of, and I think she suspected John of having let him in. She may have been mistaken, but you know what ladies are when they get an idea into their heads.”

===Chinese===
“是这样。她分文也没有留给约翰。事实是，他们两个几乎连话都不讲。我相信约翰待她相当刻薄，或者至少，她是这么认定的。她的亡夫威尔逊先生，曾在我方才说起的那家赌注经纪行的一桩投机上蚀掉一笔钱，我想她疑心是约翰把他引进的局。她或许看错了人，可是你知道，太太们一旦有个念头钻进了脑袋，会是什么样子。”

===Original===
“Did you know your aunt well?”

===Chinese===
“你同你姑母熟么？”

===Original===
“No; very slightly. She lived down in Devonshire and saw very little of any of us. She was a taciturn, strong-minded woman; quite unlike her brothers. She seems to have resembled her father’s family.”

===Chinese===
“不熟；只有极浅的一面之缘。她住在德文郡（Devonshire），同我们哪一家都很少见面。她是个沉默寡言、心志刚强的女人，全然不像她的兄弟们。她似乎生得像她父亲那一边的人。”

===Original===
“You might give me her full name.”

===Chinese===
“不妨把她的全名告诉我。”

===Original===
“Julia Elizabeth Wilson. Her husband’s name was Edmund Wilson.”

===Chinese===
“朱莉娅·伊丽莎白·威尔逊（Julia Elizabeth Wilson）。她丈夫的名字是埃德蒙·威尔逊（Edmund Wilson）。”

===Original===
“Thank you. There is just one more point. What has happened to your uncle’s chambers in New Inn since his death?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢。还有最后一点。你叔父死后，他在新客栈的那套寓所如今怎样了？”

===Original===
“They have remained shut up. As all his effects were left to me, I have taken over the tenancy for the present to avoid having them disturbed. I thought of keeping them for my own use, but I don’t think I could live in them after what I have seen.”

===Chinese===
“一直锁闭未动。既然他的全部遗物都留给了我，我便暂时把租约接了下来，免得那些东西被人扰动。我本想把那几间屋子留作自用，可是经了我所见的那番光景，我想我是住不进去的。”

===Original===
“You have inspected them, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么，你查看过那几间屋子了？”

===Original===
“Yes; I have just looked through them. I went there on the day of the inquest.”

===Chinese===
“看过；我只草草走了一遍。验尸审讯那天，我去了一趟。”

===Original===
“Now tell me: as you looked through those rooms, what kind of impression did they convey to you as to your uncle’s habits and mode of life?”

===Chinese===
“现在告诉我：你看过那几间屋子时，就你叔父的习性和生活方式而言，它们给了你一种怎样的印象？”

===Original===
Stephen smiled apologetically. “I am afraid,” said he, “that they did not convey any particular impression in that respect. I looked into the sitting-room and saw all his old familiar household goods, and then I went into the bedroom and saw the impression on the bed where his corpse had lain; and that gave me such a sensation of horror that I came away at once.”

===Chinese===
斯蒂芬歉然一笑。“恐怕，”他说，“就那方面而言，它们没有给我什么特别的印象。我看了看起居室，见到他那些旧日熟识的家常用具，随后走进卧室，看见他尸体躺过的床上留下的压痕；那给了我一种如此强烈的恐怖之感，我便立刻走开了。”

===Original===
“But the appearance of the rooms must have conveyed something to your mind,” Thorndyke urged.

===Chinese===
“可那几间屋子的景象，总该向你的心里传达了些什么吧。”桑代克敦促道。

===Original===
“I am afraid it did not. You see, I have not your analytical eye. But perhaps you would like to look through them yourself? If you would, pray do so. They are my chambers now.”

===Chinese===
“恐怕没有。您瞧，我没有您那双善于分析的眼睛。不过，或许您愿意亲自去看上一看？您若愿意，千万请去。那如今是我的寓所了。”

===Original===
“I think I should like to glance round them,” Thorndyke replied.

===Chinese===
“我想，我还是很愿意去环顾一遍的。”桑代克答道。

===Original===
“Very well,” said Stephen. “I will give you my card now, and I will look in at the lodge presently and tell the porter to hand you the key whenever you like to look over the rooms.”

===Chinese===
“那好，”斯蒂芬说，“我现在就给您一张我的名片，回头我到门房去交代一声，您几时想查看那几间屋子，门房便把钥匙交到您手上。”

===Original===
He took a card from his case, and, having written a few lines on it, handed it to Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
他从名片匣里取出一张名片，在上面写了几行字，递给桑代克。

===Original===
“It is very good of you,” he said, “to take so much trouble. Like Mr. Marchmont, I have no expectation of any result from your efforts, but I am very grateful to you, all the same, for going into the case so thoroughly. I suppose you don’t see any possibility of upsetting that will﻿—if I may ask the question?”

===Chinese===
“您肯这般劳神费心，真是太好了，”他说，“同马奇蒙特先生一样，您这番努力会得出什么结果，我毫无期待；不过，您把这案子查究得如此彻底，我仍然感激不尽。我是否可以问一句——您看不出有什么推翻那份遗嘱的可能么？”

===Original===
“At present,” replied Thorndyke, “I do not. But until I have carefully weighed every fact connected with the case﻿—whether it seems to have any bearing or not﻿—I shall refrain from expressing, or even entertaining, an opinion either way.”

===Chinese===
“眼下，”桑代克答道，“我看不出。但在仔细掂量过与本案相关的每一项事实——无论它看来有无干系——之前，我将克制自己不发表任何一方的意见，甚至不让自己心怀任何一方的成见。”

===Original===
Stephen Blackmore now took his leave; and Thorndyke, having collected the papers containing his notes, neatly punched a couple of holes in their margins and inserted them into a small file, which he slipped into his pocket.

===Chinese===
斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔随即告辞；桑代克把载有笔记的那几页纸收拢，在纸边上齐整地打出一对孔眼，把它们插进一个小文件夹，塞进了衣袋。

===Original===
“That,” said he, “is the nucleus of the body of data on which our investigations must be based; and I very much fear that it will not receive any great additions. What do you think, Jervis?”

===Chinese===
“这，”他说，“便是咱们这番调查所须依据的那套资料的内核；而且我深恐，它不会再有多大的增补了。你怎么看，杰维斯？”

===Original===
“The case looks about as hopeless as a case could look,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“这案子看上去，已是一桩案子所能有的最绝望的样子了。”我答道。

===Original===
“That is what I think,” said he; “and for that reason I am more than ordinarily keen on making something of it. I have not much more hope than Marchmont has; but I shall squeeze the case as dry as a bone before I let go. What are you going to do? I have to attend a meeting of the board of directors of the Griffin Life Office.”

===Chinese===
“我也正是这么想，”他说，“也正因为如此，我才格外起劲，定要从它身上做出点文章来。我的指望，不比马奇蒙特多多少；可在松手之前，我要把这案子挤榨得像骨头一样滴水不剩。你打算做什么？我还得去出席格里芬人寿保险公司（Griffin Life Office）的董事会。”

===Original===
“Shall I walk down with you?”

===Chinese===
“我陪你一道走过去？”

===Original===
“It is very good of you to offer, Jervis, but I think I will go alone. I want to run over these notes and get the facts of the case arranged in my mind. When I have done that, I shall be ready to pick up new matter. Knowledge is of no use unless it is actually in your mind, so that it can be produced at a moment’s notice. So you had better get a book and your pipe and spend a quiet hour by the fire while I assimilate the miscellaneous mental feast that we have just enjoyed. And you might do a little rumination yourself.”

===Chinese===
“你肯提出来陪我走，真是难为你了，杰维斯，不过我想还是独自去的好。我要把这些笔记在脑中过一遍，把本案的事实条理清楚。做到这一步之后，我才好接纳新的材料。知识若不切实装在你的脑中、能随叫随到地取用，便毫无用处。所以，你最好取一本书，带上烟斗，在炉火边安安静静地消磨一个钟头，容我消化消化咱们方才享用的这桌驳杂的精神盛筵。你自己也不妨做一点反刍。”

===Original===
With this, Thorndyke took his departure; and I, adopting his advice, drew my chair closer to the fire and filled my pipe. But I did not discover any inclination to read. The curious history that I had just heard, and Thorndyke’s evident determination to elucidate it further, disposed me to meditation. Moreover, as his subordinate, it was my business to occupy myself with his affairs. Wherefore, having stirred the fire and got my pipe well alight, I abandoned myself to the renewed consideration of the facts relating to Jeffrey Blackmore’s will.

===Chinese===
说完这话，桑代克便动身走了；我则依从他的劝告，把椅子挪近炉火，装上了烟斗。可我发觉，自己毫无读书的兴致。方才听到的这段离奇的历史，以及桑代克显然决意将它进一步查个水落石出的态度，都使我倾向于沉思。况且，身为他的下属，把心思用在他的事务上，本是我的本分。于是，拨旺了炉火，把烟斗点得透透之后，我便任由自己，重新细细思量起与杰弗里·布莱克莫尔遗嘱相关的种种事实来。

## V Jeffrey Blackmore’s Will / 杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的遗嘱

===Original===
My arrival at Thorndyke’s chambers was not unexpected, having been heralded by a premonitory postcard. The “oak” was open and an application of the little brass knocker of the inner door immediately produced my colleague himself and a very hearty welcome.

===Chinese===
我到达桑代克的寓所并不出于他的意料，因为事先已有一张预告行踪的明信片替我报了信。“橡木门”（the “oak”）敞开着，我在内门的小铜环上叩了一下，门里应声而出的便是我的同事本人，还有他万分热诚的欢迎。

===Original===
“At last,” said Thorndyke, “you have come forth from the house of bondage. I began to think that you had taken up your abode in Kennington for good.”

===Chinese===
“总算，”桑代克说，“你从那座为奴之家里脱身出来了。我起初还当你索性在肯宁顿长住下，再也不走了呢。”

===Original===
“I was beginning, myself, to wonder when I should escape. But here I am; and I may say at once that I am ready to shake the dust of general practice off my feet forever﻿—that is, if you are still willing to have me as your assistant.”

===Chinese===
“我自己也正纳闷几时才能逃出来呢。不过我到底来了；而且不妨直说，我已准备把全科开业的尘土从脚上永远抖落——当然，前提是你仍旧肯收我当你的助手。”

===Original===
“Willing!” exclaimed Thorndyke, “Barkis himself was not more willing than I. You will be invaluable to me. Let us settle the terms of our comradeship forthwith, and tomorrow we will take measures to enter you as a student of the Inner Temple. Shall we have our talk in the open air and the spring sunshine?”

===Chinese===
“肯不肯！”桑代克失声道，“就连巴基斯（Barkis）本人也不见得比我更情愿。你于我将是个无价之宝。咱们这就把合伙的条件定下来，明天便去办手续，把你登记为内殿（Inner Temple）的学员。要不要到露天里去谈，就着春日的阳光？”

===Original===
I agreed readily to this proposal, for it was a bright, sunny day and warm for the time of year﻿—the beginning of April. We descended to the Walk and thence slowly made our way to the quiet court behind the church, where poor old Oliver Goldsmith lies, as he would surely have wished to lie, in the midst of all that had been dear to him in his chequered life. I need not record the matter of our conversation. To Thorndyke’s proposals I had no objections to offer but my own unworthiness and his excessive liberality. A few minutes saw our covenants fully agreed upon, and when Thorndyke had noted the points on a slip of paper, signed and dated it and handed it to me, the business was at an end.

===Chinese===
我欣然同意这一提议，因为那天天气晴朗，阳光灿然，就一年中这个时节而言已算和暖——其时正当四月初。我们下到那条步道，再从那里缓步走向教堂后面那座静谧的庭院，可怜的老奥利弗·哥尔德斯密斯（Oliver Goldsmith）便长眠在那里，恰如他自己必定希望的那样，安卧在他坎坷一生中最亲爱的一切事物中间。我们谈话的细目无须记录。对桑代克提出的条件，我除了自陈才不称职、怪他出手过于慷慨之外，别无异议。不出几分钟，我们的约定便全部谈妥；桑代克把要点记在一张纸条上，签了名，注了日期，交到我手里，事情便告完成。

===Original===
“There,” my colleague said with a smile as he put away his pocketbook, “if people would only settle their affairs in that way, a good part of the occupation of lawyers would be gone. Brevity is the soul of wit; and the fear of simplicity is the beginning of litigation.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，”我的同事收起皮夹，微笑道，“人们若都这样了结事务，律师们的营生便要丢掉一大半了。简洁是智慧的灵魂；而畏惧简明，便是诉讼的开端。”

===Original===
“And now,” I said, “I propose that we go and feed. I will invite you to lunch to celebrate our contract.”

===Chinese===
“现在，”我说，“我提议咱们去填填肚子。我请你吃午饭，庆贺咱们的合约。”

===Original===
“My learned junior is premature,” he replied. “I had already arranged a little festivity﻿—or rather had modified one that was already arranged. You remember Mr. Marchmont, the solicitor?”

===Chinese===
“我这位博学的后辈未免性急，”他答道，“一场小小的欢宴我已经安排好了——或者不如说，把一场早已安排好的欢宴改了改。你还记得那位事务律师（solicitor）马奇蒙特先生（Mr. Marchmont）吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“记得。”

===Original===
“He called this morning to ask me to lunch with him and a new client at the ‘Cheshire Cheese.’ I accepted and notified him that I should bring you.”

===Chinese===
“他今天上午来，邀我去‘柴郡奶酪’（Cheshire Cheese）与他本人以及一位新委托人共进午餐。我应下了，并且通知他说，我要带你同去。”

===Original===
“Why the ‘Cheshire Cheese’?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“为什么偏挑‘柴郡奶酪’？”我问。

===Original===
“Why not? Marchmont’s reasons for the selection were, first, that his client has never seen an old-fashioned London tavern, and second, that this is Wednesday and he, Marchmont, has a gluttonous affection for a really fine beefsteak pudding. You don’t object, I hope?”

===Chinese===
“为什么不呢？马奇蒙特选中那里，理由有二：其一，他的委托人从未见过一家老式伦敦酒馆；其二，今天是星期三，而他马奇蒙特对一块真正上好的牛排布丁，怀有一种饕餮式的深情。你不至于反对吧，我希望？”

===Original===
“Oh, not at all. In fact, now that you mention it, my own sensations incline me to sympathize with Marchmont. I breakfasted rather early.”

===Chinese===
“哦，毫不反对。说实在的，经你这一提，我自己的五脏庙也促使我同情马奇蒙特了。我早餐吃得颇早。”

===Original===
“Then come,” said Thorndyke. “The assignation is for one o’clock, and, if we walk slowly, we shall just hit it off.”

===Chinese===
“那就来吧，”桑代克说，“约定的是一点钟，咱们若走得慢些，时间便掐得刚刚好。”

===Original===
We sauntered up Inner Temple Lane, and, crossing Fleet Street, headed sedately for the tavern. As we entered the quaint old-world dining-room, Thorndyke looked round and a gentleman, who was seated with a companion at a table in one of the little boxes or compartments, rose and saluted us.

===Chinese===
我们缓步走上内殿巷（Inner Temple Lane），穿过舰队街（Fleet Street），从容不迫地朝那家酒馆走去。走进那间古雅而一派旧日风味的餐室时，桑代克环顾四周，一位绅士——正与一位同伴坐在一个小隔间里的桌旁——起身向我们致意。

===Original===
“Let me introduce you to my friend Mr. Stephen Blackmore,” he said as we approached. Then, turning to his companion, he introduced us by our respective names.

===Chinese===
“我来引见，这位是我的朋友斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔先生（Mr. Stephen Blackmore），”我们走近时他说道。随即又转向同伴，把我们二人按各自的名姓引见给对方。

===Original===
“I engaged this box,” he continued, “so that we might be private if we wished to have a little preliminary chat; not that beefsteak pudding is a great help to conversation. But when people have a certain business in view, their talk is sure to drift towards it, sooner or later.”

===Chinese===
“这个隔间是我订的，”他接着说，“为的是万一想先私下聊几句，可以不受打扰；倒不是说牛排布丁对交谈有多大助益。不过，人们心里既惦着某桩事务，话题迟早总会飘到那上头去。”

===Original===
