Thorndyke and I sat down opposite the lawyer and his client, and we mutually inspected one another. Marchmont I already knew; an elderly, professional-looking man, a typical solicitor of the old school; fresh-faced, precise, rather irascible, and conveying a not unpleasant impression of taking a reasonable interest in his diet. The other man was quite young, not more than five-and-twenty, and was a fine athletic-looking fellow with a healthy, out-of-door complexion and an intelligent and highly prepossessing face. I took a liking to him at the first glance, and so, I saw, did Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
桑代克和我在那位律师及其委托人对面落座，彼此打量了一番。马奇蒙特我本来就认识：一位上了年纪、一副专业人士气派的先生，老派事务律师的典型；面色红润，一丝不苟，脾气相当急躁，并且给人一个不坏的印象——他对自己的饮食，抱有一种合乎分寸的关切。另一位十分年轻，不过二十五岁上下，是个体格健美、富有运动家风度的青年，面色是久在户外的那种健康肤色，一张脸既聪慧又极为讨人喜欢。我第一眼便对他生出好感，而我看桑代克也是如此。

===Original===
“You two gentlemen,” said Blackmore, addressing us, “seem to be quite old acquaintances. I have heard so much about you from my friend, Reuben Hornby.”

===Chinese===
“二位先生，”布莱克莫尔对我们说道，“看来是老相识了。我从我的朋友鲁本·霍恩比（Reuben Hornby）那里，听了许多关于你们的事。”

===Original===
“Ah!” exclaimed Marchmont, “that was a queer case﻿—‘The Case of the Red Thumb Mark,’ as the papers called it. It was an eye-opener to old-fashioned lawyers like myself. We’ve had scientific witnesses before﻿—and bullied ’em properly, by Jove! when they wouldn’t give the evidence that we wanted. But the scientific lawyer is something new. His appearance in court made us all sit up, I can assure you.”

===Chinese===
“啊！”马奇蒙特叫道，“那是一桩离奇的案子——报纸称之为‘红拇指印案’。对我这样的老派律师而言，那真叫人大开眼界。科学证人，我们从前也请过——老实说，他们不肯给出我们想要的证词时，我们也没少收拾他们！可这位科学律师却是件新鲜事物。他一出庭，我们个个都坐直了身子，这话我可以向你担保。”

===Original===
“I hope we shall make you sit up again,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我倒希望，我们往后还能再教你坐直一回。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“You won’t this time,” said Marchmont. “The issues in this case of my friend Blackmore’s are purely legal; or rather, there are no issues at all. There is nothing in dispute. I tried to prevent Blackmore from consulting you, but he wouldn’t listen to reason. Here! Waiter! How much longer are we to be waiters? We shall die of old age before we get our victuals!”

===Chinese===
“这一回可不会了，”马奇蒙特说，“我这位朋友布莱克莫尔的这桩案子，争点纯属法律问题；或者不如说，根本没有什么争点。全无争议之处。我曾想劝阻布莱克莫尔，不叫他去请教你们，可他不肯听道理。喂！堂倌！还要让我们等多久，难不成要等到我们自己都变成堂倌？再不上菜，我们怕不都要老死在这儿了！”

===Original===
The waiter smiled apologetically. “Yessir!” said he. “Coming now, sir.” And at this very moment there was borne into the room a Gargantuan pudding in a great bucket of a basin, which being placed on a three-legged stool was forthwith attacked ferociously by the white-clothed, white-capped carver. We watched the process﻿—as did everyone present﻿—with an interest not entirely gluttonous, for it added a pleasant touch to the picturesque old room, with its sanded floor, its homely, pew-like boxes, its high-backed settles and the friendly portrait of the “great lexicographer” that beamed down on us from the wall.

===Chinese===
堂倌赔着笑。“好的，先生！”他说，“这就来，先生。”恰在此刻，一大盆高康大式的布丁被抬进屋来，那盆子大得像只吊桶，搁上三脚凳之后，随即被那位白衣白帽的切肉师傅凶猛地开膛剖挖。我们注视着这一过程——在座人人皆然——那份兴味倒不全在口腹之欲，因为它给这间如画的古老餐室平添了一抹惬意的色彩：沙土铺就的地面，教堂坐凳似的家常隔间，高背的长靠椅，还有墙上那位“大辞书家”和蔼可亲的画像，正俯视着我们，满面含笑。

===Original===
“This is a very different affair from your great, glittering modern restaurant,” Mr. Marchmont remarked.

===Chinese===
“这跟你们那些金碧辉煌的现代大餐馆，可是全然不同的两码事。”马奇蒙特先生评论道。

===Original===
“It is indeed,” said Blackmore, “and if this is the way in which our ancestors lived, it would seem that they had a better idea of comfort than we have.”

===Chinese===
“的确如此，”布莱克莫尔说，“倘若我们的祖先当年过的是这样的日子，那么看来他们对于安适之道，比我们懂得要多些。”

===Original===
There was a short pause, during which Mr. Marchmont glared hungrily at the pudding; then Thorndyke said:

===Chinese===
短短一阵静默，其间马奇蒙特先生饿狼似的死盯着那布丁；随后桑代克开口道：

===Original===
“So you refused to listen to reason, Mr. Blackmore?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你不肯听劝，布莱克莫尔先生？”

===Original===
“Yes. You see, Mr. Marchmont and his partner had gone into the matter and decided that there was nothing to be done. Then I happened to mention the affair to Reuben Hornby, and he urged me to ask your advice on the case.”

===Chinese===
“正是。您瞧，马奇蒙特先生和他的合伙人已把此事研究过，断定无计可施。后来我碰巧向鲁本·霍恩比提起这桩事，他便力劝我来向你们请教这个案子。”

===Original===
“Like his impudence,” growled Marchmont, “to meddle with my client.”

===Chinese===
“他可真放肆，”马奇蒙特咕哝道，“竟插手起我的委托人来。”

===Original===
“On which,” continued Blackmore, “I spoke to Mr. Marchmont and he agreed that it was worth while to take your opinion on the case, though he warned me to cherish no hopes, as the affair was not really within your specialty.”

===Chinese===
“于是，”布莱克莫尔接着说，“我便去同马奇蒙特先生谈，他同意不妨就这案子听取一下你们的意见，不过他告诫我别抱什么指望，因为这桩事其实不在你们的专业范围之内。”

===Original===
“So you understand,” said Marchmont, “that we expect nothing. This is quite a forlorn hope. We are taking your opinion as a mere formality, to be able to say that we have left nothing untried.”

===Chinese===
“所以你要明白，”马奇蒙特说，“我们不指望什么。这完全是一场渺茫无望的挣扎。我们来听取你的意见，纯属例行公事，只求能够说上一句：我们没有放过任何尝试。”

===Original===
“That is an encouraging start,” Thorndyke remarked. “It leaves me unembarrassed by the possibility of failure. But meanwhile you are arousing in me a devouring curiosity as to the nature of the case. Is it highly confidential? Because if not, I would mention that Jervis has now joined me as my permanent colleague.”

===Chinese===
“这倒是个令人鼓舞的开场，”桑代克说，“失败的可能既已预先豁免，我便毫无窘迫之感了。不过与此同时，你们正唤起我一种按捺不住的好奇，想知道这案子的底细。它是否高度机密？若不机密，我想提一句：杰维斯如今已正式加入，成了我的固定同事。”

===Original===
“It isn’t confidential at all,” said Marchmont. “The public are in full possession of the facts, and we should be only too happy to put them in still fuller possession, through the medium of the Probate Court, if we could find a reasonable pretext. But we can’t.”

===Chinese===
“一点也不机密，”马奇蒙特说，“公众早已把事实掌握得十足全面；倘若我们能找到一个像样的借口，我们巴不得借助遗嘱检验法院（Probate Court）的渠道，让他们知道得更详尽些。可惜找不到。”

===Original===
Here the waiter charged our table with the fussy rapidity of the overdue.

===Chinese===
这时堂倌朝我们的桌子发起冲锋，带着误了时限的人那种慌里慌张的麻利劲儿。

===Original===
“Sorry to keep you waiting, sir. Rather early, sir. Wouldn’t like it underdone, sir.”

===Chinese===
“对不起，让诸位久等了，先生。火候还嫩了点儿，先生。总不能半生不熟就端上来，先生。”

===Original===
Marchmont inspected his plate critically and remarked:

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特挑剔地审视了一阵自己盘中之物，说道：

===Original===
“I sometimes suspect these oysters of being mussels; and I’ll swear the larks are sparrows.”

===Chinese===
“我有时疑心，这些牡蛎是拿贻贝充数；至于那云雀么，我敢起誓，分明是麻雀。”

===Original===
“Let us hope so,” said Thorndyke. “The lark is better employed ‘at Heaven’s gate singing’ than garnishing a beefsteak pudding. But you were telling us about your case.”

===Chinese===
“但愿如此，”桑代克说，“云雀与其给牛排布丁当配头，不如‘在天门歌唱’，来得更得其所。不过，你方才正给我们讲你们的案子来着。”

===Original===
“So I was. Well it’s just a matter of﻿—ale or claret? Oh, claret, I know. You despise the good old British John Barleycorn.”

===Chinese===
“正是。这个嘛，那不过是件——要麦酒还是红葡萄酒？哦，红葡萄酒，我知道。你们瞧不上咱们英国老牌的约翰·巴利康（John Barleycorn）。”

===Original===
“He that drinks beer thinks beer,” retorted Thorndyke. “But you were saying that it is just a matter of﻿—?”

===Chinese===
“喝啤酒的人，心里想的也只有啤酒。”桑代克反唇相讥，“不过你方才说，那不过是件——？”

===Original===
“A matter of a perverse testator and an ill-drawn will. A peculiarly irritating case, too, because the defective will replaces a perfectly sound one, and the intentions of the testator were﻿—er﻿—were﻿—excellent ale, this. A little heady, perhaps, but sound. Better than your sour French wine, Thorndyke﻿—were﻿—er﻿—were quite obvious. What he evidently desired was﻿—mustard? Better have some mustard. No? Well, well! Even a Frenchman would take mustard. You can have no appreciation of flavour, Thorndyke, if you take your victuals in that crude, unseasoned state. And, talking of flavour, do you suppose that there is really any difference between that of a lark and that of a sparrow?”

===Chinese===
“一件任性固执的立遗嘱人和一份拟得蹩脚的遗嘱的事。这案子还格外叫人恼火，因为有缺陷的遗嘱取代了一份原本完好无缺的遗嘱，而立遗嘱人的意图嘛——呃——意图是——这麦酒真不赖。兴许有点儿上头，可是地道。比你们那酸溜溜的法国酒强，桑代克——这意图——呃——意图是一望而知的。他显然存心想要的是——芥末？来点芥末吧。不要？唉，唉！连法国人吃东西都就芥末的。桑代克，你若总这样原汁原味、不加佐料地进餐，就谈不上对滋味有什么鉴赏力。说到滋味，你当真以为，云雀的滋味和麻雀的滋味有什么分别吗？”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled grimly. “I should suppose,” said he, “that they were indistinguishable; but the question could easily be put to the test of experiment.”

===Chinese===
桑代克冷冷一笑。“依我想，”他说，“两者恐怕无从分辨；不过，这个问题不难付诸实验，一验便知。”

===Original===
“That is true,” agreed Marchmont, “and it would really be worth trying, for, as you say, sparrows are more easily obtainable than larks. But, about this will. I was saying﻿—er﻿—now, what was I saying?”

===Chinese===
“这话不假，”马奇蒙特附和道，“而且确实值得一试，因为正如你所说，麻雀比云雀容易弄到。可是，还是说回这份遗嘱。我方才说——呃——哦，我说到哪儿了？”

===Original===
“I understood you to say,” replied Thorndyke, “that the intentions of the testator were in some way connected with mustard. Isn’t that so, Jervis?”

===Chinese===
“照我领会，”桑代克答道，“你方才说的是：立遗嘱人的意图，与芥末有某种牵连。是不是这样，杰维斯？”

===Original===
“That was what I gathered,” said I.

===Chinese===
“我领会的正是这个。”我说。

===Original===
Marchmont gazed at us for a moment with a surprised expression and then, laughing good-humouredly, fortified himself with a draught of ale.

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特愕然望了我们片刻，随即爽朗地大笑起来，灌下一大口麦酒，给自己壮一壮声色。

===Original===
“The moral of which is,” Thorndyke added, “that testamentary dispositions should not be mixed up with beefsteak pudding.”

===Chinese===
“这件事的教训在于，”桑代克补上一句，“遗嘱处分万不可同牛排布丁搅在一处。”

===Original===
“I believe you’re right, Thorndyke,” said the unabashed solicitor. “Business is business and eating is eating. We had better talk over our case in my office or your chambers after lunch.”

===Chinese===
“我相信你说得对，桑代克，”这位面无愧色的事务律师说，“公事是公事，吃饭是吃饭。咱们最好饭后再到我的事务所，或是你的寓所去谈案子。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke, “come over to the Temple with me and I will give you a cup of coffee to clear your brain. Are there any documents?”

===Chinese===
“好，”桑代克说，“随我到坦普尔来，我请你们喝杯咖啡，清一清脑子。可有什么文书么？”

===Original===
“I have all the papers here in my bag,” replied Marchmont; and the conversation﻿—such conversation as is possible “when beards wag all” over the festive board﻿—drifted into other channels.

===Chinese===
“全部文件都在我这提包里。”马奇蒙特答道；于是谈话——宴席之上那种“酒酣耳热、胡须乱动”式的谈话——便漂进了别的河道。

===Original===
As soon as the meal was finished and the reckoning paid, we trooped out of Wine Office Court, and, insinuating ourselves through the line of empty hansoms that, in those days, crawled in a continuous procession on either side of Fleet Street, betook ourselves by way of Mitre Court to King’s Bench Walk. There, when the coffee had been requisitioned and our chairs drawn up around the fire, Mr. Marchmont unloaded from his bag a portentous bundle of papers, and we addressed ourselves to the business in hand.

===Chinese===
饭毕付账，我们一行人便涌出葡萄酒办公室巷（Wine Office Court），从当年在舰队街两侧鱼贯爬行、首尾相接的空双轮马车长队中侧身挤过，取道米特巷（Mitre Court），来到王座法庭步道。在那里，咖啡点了来，椅子围着壁炉摆开，马奇蒙特先生便从提包里卸下厚厚一大包煞有介事的文件，我们着手料理手头的事务。

===Original===
“Now,” said Marchmont, “let me repeat what I said before. Legally speaking, we have no case﻿—not the ghost of one. But my client wished to take your opinion, and I agreed on the bare chance that you might detect some point that we had overlooked. I don’t think you will, for we have gone into the case very thoroughly, but still, there is the infinitesimal chance and we may as well take it. Would you like to read the two wills, or shall I first explain the circumstances?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”马奇蒙特说，“请容我把先前的话再说一遍。从法律上讲，我们没有案子——连个案子的鬼影都没有。但我的委托人希望听取你们的意见，我也同意了，指望万一你们能察觉什么被我们漏掉的要点。我认为你们察觉不到，因为这案子我们已经研究得十分透彻；不过，机会虽微乎其微，不妨一试。你们是想先读那两份遗嘱，还是由我先把情形说明一番？”

===Original===
“I think,” replied Thorndyke, “a narrative of the events in the order of their occurrence would be most helpful. I should like to know as much as possible about the testator before I examine the documents.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”桑代克答道，“把各桩事件按先后原原本本叙来，最有帮助。在检视这些文书之前，我愿意尽量多了解立遗嘱人其人。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Marchmont. “Then I will begin with a recital of the circumstances, which, briefly stated, are these: My client, Stephen Blackmore, is the son of Mr. Edward Blackmore, deceased. Edward Blackmore had two brothers who survived him, John, the elder, and Jeffrey, the younger. Jeffrey is the testator in this case.

===Chinese===
“很好，”马奇蒙特说，“那我就从陈述情形讲起。扼要说来，情形是这样的：我的委托人斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔，是已故的爱德华·布莱克莫尔先生（Mr. Edward Blackmore）之子。爱德华·布莱克莫尔身后有两个兄弟：年长的约翰（John）与年幼的杰弗里（Jeffrey）。杰弗里便是本案的立遗嘱人。

===Original===
“Some two years ago, Jeffrey Blackmore executed a will by which he made his nephew Stephen his executor and sole legatee; and a few months later he added a codicil giving two hundred and fifty pounds to his brother John.”

===Chinese===
“大约两年前，杰弗里·布莱克莫尔立下一份遗嘱，指定他的侄子斯蒂芬为遗嘱执行人兼唯一受遗赠人；数月之后，又添了一纸遗嘱附录（codicil），赠他兄弟约翰二百五十镑。

===Original===
“What was the value of the estate?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“遗产总值多少？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“About three thousand five hundred pounds, all invested in Consols. The testator had a pension from the Foreign Office, on which he lived, leaving his capital untouched. Soon after having made his will, he left the rooms in Jermyn Street, where he had lived for some years, stored his furniture and went to Florence. From thence he moved on to Rome and then to Venice and other places in Italy, and so continued to travel about until the end of last September, when it appears that he returned to England, for at the beginning of October he took a set of chambers in New Inn, which he furnished with some of the things from his old rooms. As far as we can make out, he never communicated with any of his friends, excepting his brother, and the fact of his being in residence at New Inn or of his being in England at all became known to them only when he died.”

===Chinese===
“约三千五百镑，全部投在统一公债（Consols）上。立遗嘱人有一份外交部（Foreign Office）的养老金，日常便靠它过活，本金分文不动。立下遗嘱后不久，他便迁出了居住数年的杰明街（Jermyn Street）寓所，把家具贮存起来，去了佛罗伦萨（Florence）。此后又辗转迁往罗马（Rome），再到威尼斯（Venice）以及意大利（Italy）各地，就这样四处游历，直到去年九月底；看来他那时返回了英国，因为十月初，他便在新客栈（New Inn）租下一套寓所，用旧居的一部分物件布置起来。据我们所能查明，他从未与任何朋友通音信，只除他兄弟一人；而他住在新客栈这件事——乃至他人在英国这件事——他的朋友们都是到他死后才知晓的。”

===Original===
“Was this quite in accordance with his ordinary habits?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“这与他的平素习性完全相符吗？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“I should say not quite,” Blackmore answered. “My uncle was a studious, solitary man, but he was not formerly a recluse. He was not much of a correspondent but he kept up some sort of communication with his friends. He used, for instance, to write to me sometimes, and, when I came down from Cambridge for the vacations, he had me to stay with him at his rooms.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，不尽相符，”布莱克莫尔答道，“我叔父是个好学而孤僻的人，但从前并不做个隐士。他不勤于写信，却与朋友们保持着某种往来。譬如说，他从前偶尔还写信给我；我从剑桥（Cambridge）放假回来时，他也曾邀我到他寓所小住。”

===Original===
“Is there anything known that accounts for the change in his habits?”

===Chinese===
“可有什么已知的缘由，能解释他习性上的这一变化？”

===Original===
“Yes, there is,” replied Marchmont. “We shall come to that presently. To proceed with the narrative: On the fifteenth of last March he was found dead in his chambers, and a more recent will was then discovered, dated the twelfth of November of last year. Now no change had taken place in the circumstances of the testator to account for the new will, nor was there any appreciable alteration in the disposition of the property. As far as we can make out, the new will was drawn with the idea of stating the intentions of the testator with greater exactness and for the sake of doing away with the codicil. The entire property, with the exception of two hundred and fifty pounds, was, as before, bequeathed to Stephen, but the separate items were specified, and the testator’s brother, John Blackmore, was named as the executor and residuary legatee.”

===Chinese===
“有，”马奇蒙特答道，“这个我们稍后就讲到。且继续叙述：去年三月十五日，他被发现死在自己的寓所里，随而发现了一份订立更晚的遗嘱，日期为去年十一月十二日。而立遗嘱人的境况，在此期间并无任何变动足以解释这份新遗嘱，财产的处分也没有什么实质改动。据我们所知，新遗嘱的拟定，用意无非是把立遗嘱人的意图表述得更加确切，并借以撤销那纸附录。全部财产除去二百五十镑外，一如从前，遗赠给斯蒂芬；只是各项财产逐一开列分明，并指定立遗嘱人的兄弟约翰·布莱克莫尔（John Blackmore）为遗嘱执行人兼剩余遗产继承人。”

===Original===
“I see,” said Thorndyke. “So that your client’s interest in the will would appear to be practically unaffected by the change.”

===Chinese===
“我明白了，”桑代克说，“如此说来，这番变动对你委托人在遗嘱中的权益，看来几乎没有影响。”

===Original===
“Yes. There it is,” exclaimed the lawyer, slapping the table to add emphasis to his words. “That is the pity of it! If people who have no knowledge of law would only refrain from tinkering at their wills, what a world of trouble would be saved!”

===Chinese===
“正是。症结就在这里！”律师拍着桌子，给自己的话助威，“可惜就可惜在这上头！那些不懂法律的人，若肯少去摆弄自己的遗嘱，能省下多少麻烦啊！”

===Original===
“Oh, come!” said Thorndyke. “It is not for a lawyer to say that.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，得啦！”桑代克说，“这话可不该出自律师之口。”

===Original===
“No, I suppose not,” Marchmont agreed. “Only, you see, we like the muddle to be made by the other side. But, in this case, the muddle is on our side. The change, as you say, seems to leave our friend Stephen’s interests unaffected. That is, of course, what poor Jeffrey Blackmore thought. But he was mistaken. The effect of the change is absolutely disastrous.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，不该，”马奇蒙特承认，“只不过，你瞧，我们总喜欢把乱子出在对方那一头。可这一回，乱子偏偏出在我们自己这头。这番变动，如你所说，看来并未触动我们朋友斯蒂芬的权益。可怜的杰弗里·布莱克莫尔，当初自然也是这么想的。可是他错了。这番变动的后果，不折不扣是一场大祸。”

===Original===
“Indeed!”

===Chinese===
“此话怎讲！”

===Original===
“Yes. As I have said, no alteration in the testator’s circumstances had taken place at the time the new will was executed. *But* only two days before his death, his sister, Mrs. Edmund Wilson, died; and on her will being proved it appeared that she had bequeathed to him her entire personalty, estimated at about thirty thousand pounds.”

===Chinese===
“是这样。方才说过，新遗嘱订立之时，立遗嘱人的境况并无任何变动。*但是*，就在他去世前两天，他的姊妹埃德蒙·威尔逊太太（Mrs. Edmund Wilson）故去了；她的遗嘱一经检验，才发现她已把全部动产遗赠给他，估值约三万镑。”

===Original===
“Heigho!” exclaimed Thorndyke. “What an unfortunate affair!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”桑代克失声道，“多么不幸的一桩事！”

===Original===
“You are right,” said Mr. Marchmont; “it was a disaster. By the original will this great sum would have accrued to our friend Mr. Stephen, whereas now, of course, it goes to the residuary legatee, Mr. John Blackmore. And what makes it even more exasperating is the fact that this is obviously not in accordance with the wishes and intentions of Mr. Jeffrey, who clearly desired his nephew to inherit his property.”

===Chinese===
“你说得对，”马奇蒙特先生说，“那是一场灾祸。按原遗嘱，这笔巨款本该落到我们朋友斯蒂芬先生名下；而如今呢，自然归了剩余遗产继承人约翰·布莱克莫尔先生。更教人扼腕的是：这显然不合杰弗里先生的心愿与意图——他分明是要侄子继承他的财产的。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke; “I think you are justified in assuming that. But do you know whether Mr. Jeffrey was aware of his sister’s intentions?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克说，“我认为你这样推断，不无道理。不过你们可知道，杰弗里先生是否知晓他姊妹的打算？”

===Original===
“We think not. Her will was executed as recently as the third of September last, and it seems that there had been no communication between her and Mr. Jeffrey since that date. Besides, if you consider Mr. Jeffrey’s actions, you will see that they suggest no knowledge or expectation of this very important bequest. A man does not make elaborate dispositions in regard to three thousand pounds and then leave a sum of thirty thousand to be disposed of casually as the residue of the estate.”

===Chinese===
“我们想他并不知晓。她的遗嘱迟至去年九月三日才订立，而自那日期之后，她与杰弗里先生之间似乎再无音信往来。况且，只要细想杰弗里先生的行事，便知其中毫不见他对这笔重大遗赠有所知晓或有所期待的迹象。一个人断不会为三千镑的财产作一番周密的处分，然后听任三万镑之巨，随随便便作为遗产的剩余部分任人处置。”

===Original===
“No,” Thorndyke agreed. “And, as you have said, the manifest intention of the testator was to leave the bulk of his property to Mr. Stephen. So we may take it as virtually certain that Mr. Jeffrey had no knowledge of the fact that he was a beneficiary under his sister’s will.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克表示同意，“而且如你所言，立遗嘱人明白的意图，是把财产的大头留给斯蒂芬先生。因此我们几乎可以断定：杰弗里先生全然不知自己是姊妹遗嘱下的受益人。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mr. Marchmont, “I think we may take that as nearly certain.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”马奇蒙特先生说，“我想这一点差不多可以视为定论。”

===Original===
“With reference to the second will,” said Thorndyke, “I suppose there is no need to ask whether the document itself has been examined; I mean as to its being a genuine document and perfectly regular?”

===Chinese===
“至于第二份遗嘱，”桑代克说，“文书本身是否经过检验，我想无须多问；我指的是：它是否确属真件，且完全合乎程式？”

===Original===
Mr. Marchmont shook his head sadly.

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特先生黯然摇头。

===Original===
“No,” he said, “I am sorry to say that there can be no possible doubt as to the authenticity and regularity of the document. The circumstances under which it was executed establish its genuineness beyond any question.”

===Chinese===
“是的，检验过了，”他说，“我很遗憾，这份文书的真实性与合乎程式，不容丝毫怀疑。它订立时的种种情形，已使其真实性确凿无疑，更无疑义可言。”

===Original===
“What were those circumstances?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“那是些什么情形？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“They were these: On the morning of the twelfth of November last, Mr. Jeffrey came to the porter’s lodge with a document in his hand. ‘This,’ he said, ‘is my will. I want you to witness my signature. Would you mind doing so, and can you find another respectable person to act as the second witness?’ Now it happened that a nephew of the porter’s, a painter by trade, was at work in the Inn. The porter went out and fetched him into the lodge and the two men agreed to witness the signature. ‘You had better read the will,’ said Mr. Jeffrey. ‘It is not actually necessary, but it is an additional safeguard and there is nothing of a private nature in the document.’ The two men accordingly read the document, and, when Mr. Jeffrey had signed it in their presence, they affixed their signatures; and I may add that the painter left the recognizable impressions of three greasy fingers.”

===Chinese===
“情形是这样的：去年十一月十二日早晨，杰弗里先生手里拿着一份文书，来到门房。‘这个，’他说，‘是我的遗嘱。我想请你见证我的签名。可愿效劳？另外，你能否再寻一位正派人来充任第二见证人？’恰巧门房的一个侄子——以油漆匠为业——正在客栈里做工。门房出去把他叫进门房，两人便应允见证签名。‘你们最好把遗嘱读一遍，’杰弗里先生说，‘这并非必要手续，却多一重保障，况且文书里没有什么私密的内容。’两人于是读了那份文书；杰弗里先生当着他们的面签了字，两人也在上面签下自己的姓名；我还可以补充一句：那油漆匠留下了三根油手指头的清晰印痕。”

===Original===
“And these witnesses have been examined?”

===Chinese===
“这两位见证人都经过讯问了吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. They have both sworn to the document and to their own signatures, and the painter recognized his fingermarks.”

===Chinese===
“问过了。两人都已宣誓确认那份文书及他们自己的签名，那油漆匠也认出了自己的指印。”

===Original===
“That,” said Thorndyke, “seems to dispose pretty effectually of any question as to the genuineness of the will; and if, as I gather, Mr. Jeffrey came to the lodge alone, the question of undue influence is disposed of too.”

===Chinese===
“这样看来，”桑代克说，“遗嘱真伪的任何疑问，似乎都被干脆利落地了结了；而且，倘如我所料，杰弗里先生是独自一人来到门房的，那么不当影响的问题也就一并了结了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mr. Marchmont. “I think we must pass the will as absolutely flawless.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”马奇蒙特先生说，“我想我们必须承认，这份遗嘱无懈可击。”

===Original===
“It strikes me as rather odd,” said Thorndyke, “that Jeffrey should have known so little about his sister’s intentions. Can you explain it, Mr. Blackmore?”

===Chinese===
“我倒觉得颇为蹊跷，”桑代克说，“杰弗里对他姊妹的打算，竟知道得这样少。你能解释么，布莱克莫尔先生？”

===Original===
“I don’t think that it is very remarkable,” Stephen replied. “I knew very little of my aunt’s affairs and I don’t think my uncle Jeffrey knew much more, for he was under the impression that she had only a life interest in her husband’s property. And he may have been right. It is not clear what money this was that she left to my uncle. She was a very taciturn woman and made few confidences to anyone.”

===Chinese===
“我不觉得这有什么可怪，”斯蒂芬答道，“我对我姑母的家事知之甚少，我看杰弗里叔父所知也多不了多少，因为他始终以为，她对她丈夫的财产只享有终身权益。他或许并没有想错。她遗赠给我叔父的究竟是笔什么钱，眼下并不清楚。她是个极沉默寡言的女人，难得对谁吐露心事。”

===Original===
“So that it is possible,” said Thorndyke, “that she, herself, may have acquired this money recently by some bequest?”

===Chinese===
“如此说来，”桑代克说，“这钱有可能也是她自己新近从某笔遗赠得来的？”

===Original===
“It is quite possible,” Stephen answered.

===Chinese===
“很有可能。”斯蒂芬答道。

===Original===
“She died, I understand,” said Thorndyke, glancing at the notes that he had jotted down, “two days before Mr. Jeffrey. What date would that be?”

===Chinese===
“据我了解，”桑代克说着，瞥了一眼自己随手记下的笔记，“她在杰弗里先生之前两天去世。那是哪一天？”

===Original===
“Jeffrey died on the fourteenth of March,” said Marchmont.

===Chinese===
“杰弗里是三月十四日去世的。”马奇蒙特说。

===Original===
“So that Mrs. Wilson died on the twelfth of March?”

===Chinese===
“那么威尔逊太太是三月十二日去世的？”

===Original===
“That is so,” Marchmont replied; and Thorndyke then asked:

===Chinese===
“正是。”马奇蒙特答道。桑代克随即又问：

===Original===
“Did she die suddenly?”

===Chinese===
“她死得突然么？”

===Original===
“No,” replied Stephen; “she died of cancer. I understand that it was cancer of the stomach.”

===Chinese===
“不突然，”斯蒂芬答道，“她死于癌症。据我所知，是胃癌。”

===Original===
“Do you happen to know,” Thorndyke asked, “what sort of relations existed between Jeffrey and his brother John?”

===Chinese===
“你可知道，”桑代克问，“杰弗里和他兄弟约翰之间，平日关系如何？”

===Original===
“At one time,” said Stephen, “I know they were not very cordial; but the breach may have been made up later, though I don’t know that it actually was.”

===Chinese===
“有一阵子，我知道他们不甚融洽；不过嫌隙或许后来弥合了，虽然我并不清楚实际上弥合了没有。”

===Original===
“I ask the question,” said Thorndyke, “because, as I dare say you have noticed, there is, in the first will, some hint of improved relations. As it was originally drawn that will makes Mr. Stephen the sole legatee. Then, a little later, a codicil is added in favour of John, showing that Jeffrey had felt the necessity of making some recognition of his brother. This seems to point to some change in the relations, and the question arises: if such a change did actually occur, was it the beginning of a new and further improving state of feeling between the two brothers? Have you any facts bearing on that question?”

===Chinese===
“我之所以问，”桑代克说，“是因为——我想你们两位想必也注意到了——第一份遗嘱里，隐隐透出兄弟关系好转的迹象。遗嘱当初拟成之时，只立斯蒂芬先生一人为唯一受遗赠人；随后不久，又添了一纸有利于约翰的附录，可见杰弗里已觉得有必要对兄弟作出某种承认。这似乎表明两人的关系起了某种变化；于是问题来了：倘若这一变化确曾发生，它是否标志着兄弟二人之间一段日渐亲善的新情谊的开端？关于这个问题，你们可掌握什么事实？”

===Original===
Marchmont pursed up his lips with the air of a man considering an unwelcome suggestion, and, after a few moments of reflection, answered:

===Chinese===
马奇蒙特撮起嘴唇，一副掂量着某种不受欢迎提议的神气，沉吟片刻，答道：

===Original===
“I think we must say ‘yes’ to that. There is the undeniable fact that, of all Jeffrey’s friends, John Blackmore was the only one who knew that he was living in New Inn.”

===Chinese===
“这一点，我想我们必须答个‘是’字。有一件不容否认的事实：在杰弗里所有的朋友当中，唯独约翰·布莱克莫尔一人，知道他住在新客栈。”

===Original===
“Oh, John knew that, did he?”

===Chinese===
“哦，约翰是知道这个的，是么？”

===Original===
“Yes, he certainly did; for it came out in the evidence that he had called on Jeffrey at his chambers more than once. There is no denying that. But, mark you!” Mr. Marchmont added emphatically, “that does not cover the inconsistency of the will. There is nothing in the second will to suggest that Jeffrey intended materially to increase the bequest to his brother.”

===Chinese===
“是的，他确实知道；因为讯问中已经查明，他曾不止一次到杰弗里的寓所登门拜访。这是赖不掉的。可是，你听着！”马奇蒙特先生加重语气补充道，“这并不能掩盖遗嘱的自相矛盾之处。第二份遗嘱里，没有任何迹象表明，杰弗里打算实质性地增加他兄弟名下的遗赠。”

===Original===
“I quite agree with you, Marchmont. I think that is a perfectly sound position. You have, I suppose, fully considered the question as to whether it would be possible to set aside the second will on the ground that it fails to carry out the evident wishes and intentions of the testator?”

===Chinese===
“我完全同意你的看法，马奇蒙特。我认为这一立场无懈可击。那么，可否这样理解：你们已充分考虑过这个问题——能否以第二份遗嘱未能贯彻立遗嘱人明白的心愿与意图为由，将它推翻？”

===Original===
“Yes. My partner, Winwood, and I went into that question very carefully, and we also took counsel’s opinion﻿—Sir Horace Barnaby﻿—and he was of the same opinion as ourselves; that the court would certainly uphold the will.”

===Chinese===
“考虑过。我的合伙人温伍德（Winwood）和我，极仔细地研讨过这个问题；我们还征询了出庭律师的意见——霍勒斯·巴纳比爵士（Sir Horace Barnaby）——他与我等所见略同：法院必定维持这份遗嘱的效力。”

===Original===
“I think that would be my own view,” said Thorndyke, “especially after what you have told me. Do I understand that John Blackmore was the only person who knew that Jeffrey was in residence at New Inn?”

===Chinese===
“我想我自己也会持同样的看法，”桑代克说，“尤其是在听过你这番话之后。那么我可否理解为：约翰·布莱克莫尔是唯一知道杰弗里住在新客栈的人？”

===Original===
“The only one of his private friends. His bankers knew and so did the officials from whom he drew his pension.”

===Chinese===
“是他的私人朋友当中唯一的一个。他的银行知道，经手发放他养老金的官员们也知道。”

===Original===
“Of course he would have to notify his bankers of his change of address.”

===Chinese===
“他自然必须把地址的变更通知他的银行。”

===Original===
“Yes, of course. And apropos of the bank, I may mention that the manager tells me that, of late, they had noticed a slight change in the character of Jeffrey’s signature﻿—I think you will see the reason of the change when you hear the rest of his story. It was very trifling; not more than commonly occurs when a man begins to grow old, especially if there is some failure of eyesight.”

===Chinese===
“是的，当然。顺便说到银行，我不妨提一句：经理告诉我，近来他们注意到，杰弗里签名的笔性起了些微变化——我想，等你们听完他经历的后文，便明白这变化的原委了。变化极其轻微，不过是人到年老时常见的那种，尤其是视力有所衰退的时候。”

===Original===
“Was Mr. Jeffrey’s eyesight failing?” asked Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“杰弗里先生的视力在衰退么？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Yes, it was, undoubtedly,” said Stephen. “He was practically blind in one eye and, in the very last letter that I ever had from him, he mentioned that there were signs of commencing cataract in the other.”

===Chinese===
“是的，无疑在衰退，”斯蒂芬说，“他一只眼睛实际上已经全瞎；而在我收到他的最后一封信里，他还提到，另一只眼已现出白内障初起之象。”

===Original===
“You spoke of his pension. He continued to draw that regularly?”

===Chinese===
“方才说到他的养老金。他一直按期领取么？”

===Original===
“Yes; he drew his allowance every month, or rather, his bankers drew it for him. They had been accustomed to do so when he was abroad, and the authorities seem to have allowed the practice to continue.”

===Chinese===
“是的；他按月领取津贴，或者不如说，是由他的银行代领。他旅居国外期间，银行便一直这样代办，主管部门似乎也听任这一做法延续下去。”

===Original===
Thorndyke reflected a while, running his eye over the notes on the slips of paper in his hand, and Marchmont surveyed him with a malicious smile. Presently the latter remarked:

===Chinese===
桑代克沉吟了一会儿，目光在手中纸页的笔记上来回扫视；马奇蒙特则面带狡黠的微笑打量着他。片刻之后，后者说道：

===Original===
“Methinks the learned counsel is floored.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，这位博学的律师，是被难倒了。”

===Original===
Thorndyke laughed. “It seems to me,” he retorted, “that your proceedings are rather like those of the amiable individual who offered the bear a flint pebble, that he might crack it and extract the kernel. Your confounded will seems to offer no soft spot on which one could commence an attack. But we won’t give up. We seem to have sucked the will dry. Let us now have a few facts respecting the parties concerned in it; and, as Jeffrey is the central figure, let us begin with him and the tragedy at New Inn that formed the starting-point of all this trouble.”

===Chinese===
桑代克笑了。“依我看，”他反诘道，“你们这一手，倒很像那位好心的先生的做法：递给狗熊一块燧石卵石，好教它砸开取仁。你这份可恼的遗嘱，竟寻不出一处下口的软地方。但我们绝不罢手。这份遗嘱，我们似乎已经吮得点水不剩；那就再来一些与当事各方有关的事实吧。杰弗里既是全案的中心人物，就让我们从他本人讲起，从新客栈那场构成这一切祸端起点的悲剧讲起。”

## List of Illustrations / 插图目录

===Original===
1. The Inverted Inscription
2. The Track Chart


===Chinese===
1. 倒置的铭文
2. 轨迹图

## Preface / 序言

===Original===
Commenting upon one of my earlier novels, in respect of which I had claimed to have been careful to adhere to common probabilities and to have made use only of really practicable methods of investigation, a critic remarked that this was of no consequence whatever, so long as the story was amusing.

===Chinese===
有人评论我早先的一部小说——我曾声称在那本书里小心翼翼地只依循寻常的可能性、只采用真正切实可行的侦查方法——这位批评家说，这些统统无关紧要，只要故事有趣就行。

===Original===
Few people, I imagine, will agree with him. To most readers, and certainly to the kind of reader for whom an author is willing to take trouble, complete realism in respect of incidents and methods is an essential factor in maintaining the interest of a detective story. Hence it may be worth while to mention that Thorndyke’s method of producing the track chart, described in Chapters II and III, has been actually used in practice. It is a modification of one devised by me many years ago when I was crossing Ashanti to the city of Bontuku, the whereabouts of which in the far interior was then only vaguely known. My instructions were to fix the positions of all towns, villages, rivers and mountains as accurately as possible; but finding ordinary methods of surveying impracticable in the dense forest which covers the whole region, I adopted this simple and apparently rude method, checking the distances whenever possible by astronomical observation.

===Chinese===
我想，同意他的人寥寥无几。对大多数读者——尤其是对那种作者愿意为之劳心费力的读者——而言，情节与方法上的彻底写实，正是维系侦探小说趣味的一个根本要素。因此，或许值得一提：桑代克绘制路线图的方法（见第二、第三章所述）在实践中确实被用过。那是我多年前徒步穿越阿散蒂、前往深处腹地的城市邦图库时构想出的一种方法的改良——当年那座城的位置在遥远的内陆还只有个模糊的概略。我领受的任务是尽可能精确地测定所有城镇、村落、河流与山脉的位置；但眼见常规测量手段在这片覆盖全境的密林里行不通，我便采用了这种简单而看似粗糙的办法，并每逢可能便以天文观测核校距离。

===Original===
The resulting route-map was surprisingly accurate, as shown by the agreement of the outward and homeward tracks. It was published by the Royal Geographical Society, and incorporated in the map of this region compiled by the Intelligence Branch of the War Office, and it formed the basis of the map which accompanied my volume of *Travels in Ashanti and Jaman*. So that Thorndyke’s plan must be taken as quite a practicable one.

===Chinese===
由此绘成的路线图精确得惊人，往返两条路线的相互吻合便是明证。它由皇家地理学会出版，被并入陆军部情报处编制的该地区地图，并且构成了我的《阿散蒂与贾曼旅行记》一卷所附地图的底本。所以桑代克的方案必须视为完全切实可行。

===Original===
New Inn, the background of this story, and one of the last surviving inns of Chancery, has recently passed away after upwards of four centuries of newness. Even now, however, a few of the old, dismantled houses (including perhaps, the mysterious 31) may be seen from the Strand peeping over the iron roof of the skating rink which has displaced the picturesque hall, the pension-room and the garden. The postern gate, too, in Houghton Street still remains, though the arch is bricked up inside. Passing it lately, I made the rough sketch which appears on next page, and which shows all that is left of this pleasant old London backwater.

===Chinese===
作为本书故事背景的新客栈（New Inn），是残存至最后的几家律师学院客栈之一，在历经四百余年的“崭新”之后，近来终于谢世。不过时至今日，从河滨街仍可望见几幢拆剩的旧宅（其中或许就有那神秘的 31 号）越过旱冰场的铁皮屋顶探出身来——正是那座冰场，取代了如画的厅堂、年金室和花园。霍顿街的那扇后门也还在，只是门洞内侧已用砖封死。前些日子经过时，我画下了下一页那幅粗略的速写，它显示了这个宜人的旧伦敦僻静角落所余下的一切。

===Original===
R. A. F.
Gravesend

===Chinese===
R. A. F.
于格雷夫森德

## XI The Blackmore Case Reviewed / 布莱克莫尔案回顾

===Original===
One of the conditions of medical practice is the capability of transferring one’s attention at a moment’s notice from one set of circumstances to another equally important but entirely unrelated. At each visit on his round, the practitioner finds himself concerned with a particular, self-contained group of phenomena which he must consider at the moment with the utmost concentration, but which he must instantly dismiss from his mind as he moves on to the next case. It is a difficult habit to acquire; for an important, distressing or obscure case is apt to take possession of the consciousness and hinder the exercise of attention that succeeding cases demand; but experience shows the faculty to be indispensable, and the practitioner learns in time to forget everything but the patient with whose condition he is occupied at the moment.

===Chinese===
行医的条件之一，便是能够在一转念间，把注意力从一组境况挪到另一组同等重要却全不相干的境况上去。医生巡回出诊，每到一家，面对的都是一组自成一体的特定现象，此刻他必须以极度的专注加以考量；而一挪步到下一病例，又须立时把它们从心中撇开。这种习惯不易养成；因为一个重要、揪心或费解的病例，往往盘踞住意识，妨害后继病例所要求的注意的施展；但经验证明这种才能不可或缺，行医者到头来便学会了一件事：除了此刻正诊治着的病人，其余一概忘却。

===Original===
My first morning’s work on the Blackmore case showed me that the same faculty is demanded in legal practice; and it also showed me that I had yet to acquire it. For, as I looked over the depositions and the copy of the will, memories of the mysterious house in Kennington Lane continually intruded into my reflections, and the figure of Mrs. Schallibaum, white-faced, terrified, expectant, haunted me continually.

===Chinese===
我在布莱克莫尔案上头一个上午的工作使我明白：法律这一行同样需要这种才能；同时也使我明白，我尚且未能习得。因为，我翻阅那些证词笔录和遗嘱副本时，肯宁顿巷那座神秘宅子的种种记忆不断侵入我的思绪，而沙利鲍姆太太的身影——面无人色、惊恐万状、有所期待——更是不住地纠缠着我。

===Original===
In truth, my interest in the Blackmore case was little more than academic, whereas in the Kennington case I was one of the parties and was personally concerned. To me, John Blackmore was but a name, Jeffrey but a shadowy figure to which I could assign no definite personality, and Stephen himself but a casual stranger. Mr. Graves, on the other hand, was a real person. I had seen him amidst the tragic circumstances that had probably heralded his death, and had brought away with me, not only a lively recollection of him, but a feeling of profound pity and concern as to his fate. The villain Weiss, too, and the terrible woman who aided, abetted and, perhaps, even directed him, lived in my memory as vivid and dreadful realities. Although I had uttered no hint to Thorndyke, I lamented inwardly that I had not been given some work﻿—if there was any to do﻿—connected with this case, in which I was so deeply interested, rather than with the dry, purely legal and utterly bewildering case of Jeffrey Blackmore’s will.

===Chinese===
说实在的，我对布莱克莫尔案的兴趣，近乎只是书生式的；而在肯宁顿巷一案里，我自己便是当事的一方，利害切身。在我看来，约翰·布莱克莫尔不过是个名字，杰弗里不过是个影子般的人物，我无法给他安上任何鲜明的性格，连斯蒂芬本人，也不过萍水相逢的陌生人。格雷夫斯先生却不然，他是个活生生的人。我见过他，身处那些很可能预告了他的死亡的惨境之中；我不但带走了对他的鲜活记忆，还带走了对他的命运深切怜悯与挂念。恶棍韦斯，还有那个可怕的女人——她帮他作恶、教唆于他，说不定还在背后指使他——也都作为鲜明而骇人的实体，活在我的记忆里。尽管我对桑代克不曾吐露半句，心里却不免暗暗抱憾：分派给我的，竟是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔遗嘱这样一桩枯燥、纯乎法律而令人全然摸不着头脑的案子，而不是同我深为关切的那桩案子相关的工作——倘若那桩案子当真有工作可做的话。

===Original===
Nevertheless, I stuck loyally to my task. I read through the depositions and the will﻿—without getting a single glimmer of fresh light on the case﻿—and I made a careful digest of all the facts. I compared my digest with Thorndyke’s notes﻿—of which I also made a copy﻿—and found that, brief as they were, they contained several matters that I had overlooked. I also drew up a brief account of our visit to New Inn, with a list of the objects that we had observed or collected. And then I addressed myself to the second part of my task, the statement of my conclusions from the facts set forth.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，我仍忠实地守住我的任务。我把证词笔录和遗嘱通读了一遍——对本案未获得一线新的启示——又把全部事实细心摘编成一份提要。我拿自己的提要同桑代克的笔记对照——笔记我也抄了一份——发现它们虽简短，却包含好几处被我忽略了的要点。我还草拟了一份我们新客栈之行的简记，附上我们观察到或收集到的物件清单。然后我便着手任务的第二部分：陈述我从所列事实中得出的结论。

===Original===
It was only when I came to make the attempt that I realized how completely I was at sea. In spite of Thorndyke’s recommendation to study Marchmont’s statement as it was summarized in those notes which I had copied, and of his hint that I should find in that statement something highly significant, I was borne irresistibly to one conclusion, and one only﻿—and the wrong one at that, as I suspected: that Jeffrey Blackmore’s will was a perfectly regular, sound and valid document.

===Chinese===
直到着手尝试，我才发觉自己是何等茫然无措。桑代克曾叮嘱我研读马奇蒙特的陈述——我抄录的那些笔记里已把它概述出来——又暗示我说，我会在那份陈述里发现一样具有重大意义的东西；然而不顾这些，我还是身不由己地被推向一个结论，且仅此一个——而且我疑心还是个错结论：杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的遗嘱，是一份完全合乎规范、健全而有效的文书。

===Original===
I tried to attack the validity of the will from various directions, and failed every time. As to its genuineness, that was obviously not in question. There seemed to me only two conceivable respects in which any objection could be raised, viz. the competency of Jeffrey to execute a will and the possibility of undue influence having been brought to bear on him.

===Chinese===
我试着从几个方向攻击这份遗嘱的效力，回回都告失败。至于它的真实性，显然不成问题。在我看来，可以提出异议的无非两个方面：一是杰弗里是否具备订立遗嘱的行为能力，二是有无可能有人对他施加过不当影响。

===Original===
With reference to the first, there was the undoubted fact that Jeffrey was addicted to the opium habit, and this might, under some circumstances, interfere with a testator’s competency to make a will. But had any such circumstances existed in this case? Had the drug habit produced such mental changes in the deceased as would destroy or weaken his judgment? There was not a particle of evidence in favour of any such belief. Up to the very end he had managed his own affairs, and, if his habits of life had undergone a change, they were still the habits of a perfectly sane and responsible man.

===Chinese===
就第一点而言，无可置疑的事实是杰弗里沉溺于鸦片嗜好，而在某些情形下，这确实可能妨害遗嘱人订立遗嘱的能力。可本案当真存在这种情形吗？这种药瘾可曾使死者的精神发生足以摧毁或削弱其判断力的变化？支持这种想法的证据，一星半点也没有。直到最后，他都在亲自打理自己的事务；即便他的生活习性确曾改变，那也仍是一个神志完全清明、能负责任的人的习性。

===Original===
The question of undue influence was more difficult. If it applied to any person in particular, that person could be none other than John Blackmore. Now it was an undoubted fact that, of all Jeffrey’s acquaintance, his brother John was the only one who knew that he was in residence at New Inn. Moreover John had visited him there more than once. It was therefore possible that influence might have been brought to bear on the deceased. But there was no evidence that it had. The fact that the deceased man’s only brother should be the one person who knew where he was living was not a remarkable one, and it had been satisfactorily explained by the necessity of Jeffrey’s finding a reference on applying for the chambers. And against the theory of undue influence was the fact that the testator had voluntarily brought his will to the lodge and executed it in the presence of entirely disinterested witnesses.

===Chinese===
不当影响的问题就更难办了。倘若它落到某个具体的人头上，那人便只能是约翰·布莱克莫尔。而无可置疑的事实是：在杰弗里的全部相识当中，唯有兄弟约翰知道他住在新客栈。何况约翰还不止一次到那里看望过他。因此，死者是有可能受过旁人左右的。但并没有证据表明他确实受过。死者唯一的亲兄弟恰是唯一知晓他住处的人，这一事实本无足惊怪，况且杰弗里申请租那套寓所须觅一名保证人，这一点已把它解释得清清楚楚。再者，遗嘱人是自愿把遗嘱带到门房、当着一群全然无利害关系的证人订立的，这也与不当影响之说相抵触。

===Original===
In the end I had to give up the problem in despair, and, abandoning the documents, turned my attention to the facts elicited by our visit to New Inn.

===Chinese===
最后，我只得绝望地撇下这个问题，丢开那些文书，把注意力转向我们新客栈之行所获知的事实。

===Original===
What had we learned from our exploration? It was clear that Thorndyke had picked up some facts that had appeared to him important. But important in what respect? The only possible issue that could be raised was the validity or otherwise of Jeffrey Blackmore’s will; and since the validity of that will was supported by positive evidence of the most incontestable kind, it seemed that nothing that we had observed could have any real bearing on the case at all.

===Chinese===
我们那番查访究竟获知了些什么？桑代克显然拾得了一些在他看来十分重要的事实。可在哪方面重要？本案唯一可能提出的争点，就是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔那份遗嘱有效与否；而这份遗嘱的效力既有最无可争辩一类的确凿证据支撑，那么我们所观察到的一切，似乎同本案根本不可能有任何实际的关涉。

===Original===
But this, of course, could not be. Thorndyke was no dreamer nor was he addicted to wild speculation. If the facts observed by us seemed to him to be relevant to the case, I was prepared to assume that they were relevant, although I could not see their connection with it. And, on this assumption, I proceeded to examine them afresh.

===Chinese===
然而这当然说不通。桑代克不是空想家，也不惯于胡乱臆测。既然我们观察到的事实在他看来与本案有关，那么尽管我看不出其中的关联，我也愿意认定它们确乎有关。基于这一假定，我把它们重新检视了一遍。

===Original===
Now, whatever Thorndyke might have observed on his own account, I had brought away from the dead man’s chambers only a single fact; and a very extraordinary fact it was. The cuneiform inscription was upside down. That was the sum of the evidence that I had collected; and the question was, What did it prove? To Thorndyke it conveyed some deep significance. What could that significance be?

===Chinese===
且不说桑代克自己观察到了什么，我从死者的寓所里带走的只有一个事实；而这事实又极不寻常。那幅楔形文字铭文是倒挂的。这便是我收集到的全部证据；问题是，它证明了什么？在桑代克眼里，它含着某种深刻的意味。那意味又能是什么呢？

===Original===
The inverted position was not a mere temporary accident, as it might have been if the frame had been stood on a shelf or support. It was hung on the wall, and the plates screwed on the frame showed that its position was permanent and that it had never hung in any other. That it could have been hung up by Jeffrey himself was clearly inconceivable. But allowing that it had been fixed in its present position by some workman when the new tenant moved in, the fact remained that there it had hung, presumably for months, and that Jeffrey Blackmore, with his expert knowledge of the cuneiform character, had never noticed that it was upside down; or, if he had noticed it, that he had never taken the trouble to have it altered.

===Chinese===
那颠倒的位置并非一时的偶然——倘若镜框只是搁在架子或支架上，倒可能有此偶然。它是挂在墙上的，而用螺钉拧在框上的那几片挂片表明，这个位置是固定的，它从没在别的位置挂过。说它是杰弗里自己挂上去的，显然不可想象。但就算它是新住户搬入时由某个工匠装到如今这个位置上的，事实依旧是：它在那里挂了——推想起来——好几个月，而杰弗里·布莱克莫尔，凭他对楔形文字的专门学识，竟从未发觉它是倒的；或者纵然发觉了，也从不肯费神叫人改过。

===Original===
What could this mean? If he had noticed the error but had not troubled to correct it, that would point to a very singular state of mind, an inertness and indifference remarkable even in an opium-smoker. But assuming such a state of mind, I could not see that it had any bearing on the will, excepting that it was rather inconsistent with the tendency to make fussy and needless alterations which the testator had actually shown. On the other hand, if he had not noticed the inverted position of the photograph he must have been nearly blind or quite idiotic; for the photograph was over two feet long and the characters large enough to be read easily by a person of ordinary eyesight at a distance of forty or fifty feet. Now he obviously was not in a state of dementia, whereas his eyesight was admittedly bad; and it seemed to me that the only conclusion deducible from the photograph was that it furnished a measure of the badness of the deceased man’s vision﻿—that it proved him to have been verging on total blindness.

===Chinese===
这能说明什么呢？倘若他发觉了这一差错却懒得纠正，那便指向一种十分奇特的心境——一种麻木与冷漠，即便在吸鸦片的人当中也算罕见。可就算承认这种心境，我也看不出它同遗嘱有什么关涉；唯一说得上的倒是：它同遗嘱人实际表现出的那种小题大作、多此一举地改这改那的脾性，颇不相合。反过来说，倘若他竟没有察觉照片挂颠倒了，那他必定近乎全盲，或干脆是个白痴；因为那张照片长逾两英尺，上面的文字之大，视力寻常的人在四五十英尺开外也能毫不费力地读出。而他显然并无痴呆之症，目力之坏倒是有目共睹；于是在我看来，从这张照片能推出的唯一结论便是：它为死者目力之坏提供了一个量度——证明他已濒于全盲。

===Original===
But there was nothing startling new in this. He had, himself, declared that he was fast losing his sight. And again, what was the bearing of his partial blindness on the will? A totally blind man cannot draw up his will at all. But if he has eyesight sufficient to enable him to write out and sign a will, mere defective vision will not lead him to muddle the provisions. Yet something of this kind seemed to be in Thorndyke’s mind, for now I recalled the question that he had put to the porter: “When you read the will over in Mr. Blackmore’s presence, did you read it aloud?” That question could have but one significance. It implied a doubt as to whether the testator was fully aware of the exact nature of the document that he was signing. Yet, if he was able to write and sign it, surely he was able also to read it through, to say nothing of the fact that, unless he was demented, he must have remembered what he had written.

===Chinese===
但这又算不得什么惊人的新发现。他自己就说过，他的视力正在急速丧失。再者，他的半盲同遗嘱又有何干？全盲的人根本无法订立遗嘱。可他既然视力尚足以书写并签署一份遗嘱，单凭目力不济，总不至于把遗嘱的条款弄得一团糟。然而桑代克心里盘算的似乎正是这一类事情，因为此刻我想起了他向门房提的那个问题：“你在布莱克莫尔先生面前宣读遗嘱时，是读出声来的吗？”这一问题只可能有一种含义。它暗示一种疑问：遗嘱人对自己正在签署的文书究竟具有何种确切性质，是否全然知情。然而，他若能写能签，必也能把它通读一遍；更不必说，除非他已经痴呆，自己写下的内容总该记得。

===Original===
Thus, once more, my reasoning only led me into a blind alley at the end of which was the will, regular and valid and fulfilling all the requirements that the law imposed. Once again I had to confess myself beaten and in full agreement with Mr. Marchmont that “there was no case”; that “there was nothing in dispute.” Nevertheless, I carefully fixed in the pocket file that Thorndyke had given me the copy that I had made of his notes, together with the notes on our visit to New Inn, and the few and unsatisfactory conclusions at which I had arrived; and this brought me to the end of my first morning in my new capacity.

===Chinese===
就这样，我的推理再一次只把我引进一条死胡同，胡同尽头便是那份遗嘱——规规矩矩、有效无讹，法律所要求的一切无不齐备。我只得再次自认败下阵来，且完全赞同马奇蒙特先生的说法：“这案子不成立”；“没有任何可争之点”。尽管如此，我仍把抄录桑代克笔记的那份副本，连同关于新客栈之行的记录和我得出的那几条少而难以令人满意的结论，仔细收进桑代克给我的那个衣袋文件夹里；至此，我以新身份度过的第一个上午便到了头。

===Original===
“And how,” Thorndyke asked as we sat at lunch, “has my learned friend progressed? Does he propose that we advise Mr. Marchmont to enter a caveat?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”我们坐下吃午饭时，桑代克问道，“我这位博学的朋友进展如何？他是否打算建议我们劝马奇蒙特先生提出知会备忘呢？”

===Original===
“I’ve read all the documents and boiled all the evidence down to a stiff jelly; and I am in a worse fog than ever.”

===Chinese===
“我把全部文书都读完了，把全部证据熬成了一块硬实的冻子；可我陷的雾却比先前更浓了。”

===Original===
“There seems to be a slight mixture of metaphors in my learned friend’s remarks. But never mind the fog, Jervis. There is a certain virtue in fog. It serves, like a picture frame, to surround the essential with a neutral zone that separates it from the irrelevant.”

===Chinese===
“我这位博学的朋友的高论里，似乎略微掺和了两副比方。不过且不管那雾，杰维斯。雾自有雾的妙处。它就像画框，围着紧要之物布下一圈中性的地带，把它同不相干之物分隔开来。”

===Original===
“That is a very profound observation, Thorndyke,” I remarked ironically.

===Chinese===
“这可真是一句极高深的议论啊，桑代克。”我讥诮地说。

===Original===
“I was just thinking so myself,” he rejoined.

===Chinese===
“我也正这么想呢。”他应道。

===Original===
“And if you could contrive to explain what it means﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你要是能设法把这话说个明白——”

===Original===
“Oh, but that is unreasonable. When one throws off a subtly philosophic obiter dictum one looks to the discerning critic to supply the meaning. By the way, I am going to introduce you to the gentle art of photography this afternoon. I am getting the loan of all the cheques that were drawn by Jeffrey Blackmore during his residence at New Inn﻿—there are only twenty-three of them, all told﻿—and I am going to photograph them.”

===Chinese===
“噢，这就无理了。一个人随口抛出一句精微的哲学式附带议论（obiter dictum），本指望有眼光的批评家替他把含义补足呢。对了，今天下午我要领你入门，学一学摄影这门雅艺。我正要借取杰弗里·布莱克莫尔住在新客栈期间签发的全部支票——总共不过二十三张——打算把它们一一拍下来。”

===Original===
“I shouldn’t have thought the bank people would have let them go out of their possession.”

===Chinese===
“我原以为银行的人不会肯让支票脱手呢。”

===Original===
“They are not going to. One of the partners, a Mr. Britton, is bringing them here himself and will be present while the photographs are being taken; so they will not go out of his custody. But, all the same, it is a great concession, and I should not have obtained it but for the fact that I have done a good deal of work for the bank and that Mr. Britton is more or less a personal friend.”

===Chinese===
“他们并不打算让支票脱手。银行的一位合伙人，布里顿先生（Mr. Britton），要亲自把它们带到这儿来，拍摄时全程在场；这样，支票便不离他的保管。不过即便如此，这也是一次极大的通融；若不是我替这家银行做过不少工作，而布里顿先生又多少算是一位私交，这事本是办不成的。”

===Original===
“By the way, how comes it that the cheques are at the bank? Why were they not returned to Jeffrey with the passbook in the usual way?”

===Chinese===
“对了，那些支票怎么会在银行手里？为什么没有照惯例随存折一起退还给杰弗里呢？”

===Original===
“I understand from Britton,” replied Thorndyke, “that all Jeffrey’s cheques were retained by the bank at his request. When he was travelling he used to leave his investment securities and other valuable documents in his bankers’ custody, and, as he has never applied to have them returned, the bankers still have them and are retaining them until the will is proved, when they will, of course, hand over everything to the executors.”

===Chinese===
“我听布里顿说，”桑代克答道，“杰弗里的全部支票，都是应他本人的请求由银行留存的。他旅行期间，惯于把投资的证券及其他贵重文书交托银行保管；既然他从未申请领回，银行便一直持有，留存到遗嘱通过检验为止——到那时，自然会把一切移交遗嘱执行人。”

===Original===
“What is the object of photographing these cheques?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“把这些支票拍下来，用意何在？”我问。

===Original===
“There are several objects. First, since a good photograph is practically as good as the original, when we have the photographs we practically have the cheques for reference. Then, since a photograph can be duplicated indefinitely, it is possible to perform experiments on it which involve its destruction; which would, of course, be impossible in the case of original cheques.”

===Chinese===
“用意有好几层。第一，一张好照片实际上同原件一样顶用，照片在手，支票便等于是我们的备查之物。再者，照片可以无限复制，便能拿它去做那些须将它毁弃的实验；拿原票做，当然是不可能了。”

===Original===
“But the ultimate object, I mean. What are you going to prove?”

===Chinese===
“可我说的是最终目的。你打算证明什么？”

===Original===
“You are incorrigible, Jervis,” he exclaimed. “How should I know what I am going to prove? This is an investigation. If I knew the result beforehand, I shouldn’t want to perform the experiment.”

===Chinese===
“你真是无可救药，杰维斯。”他叫道，“我怎么会知道我要证明什么？这是查案。倘若结果我早已知晓，这实验也就不必做了。”

===Original===
He looked at his watch, and, as we rose from the table, he said:

===Chinese===
他看了看表，我们起身离席时，他说：

===Original===
“If we have finished, we had better go up to the laboratory and see that the apparatus is ready. Mr. Britton is a busy man, and, as he is doing us a great service, we mustn’t keep him waiting when he comes.”

===Chinese===
“若是吃完了，我们最好上实验室去，看看器材是否齐备。布里顿先生是个忙人，他这是帮我们大忙，他来的时候，我们可不能叫他久等。”

===Original===
We ascended to the laboratory, where Polton was already busy inspecting the massively built copying camera which﻿—with the long, steel guides on which the easel or copyholder travelled﻿—took up the whole length of the room on the side opposite to that occupied by the chemical bench. As I was to be inducted into the photographic art, I looked at it with more attention than I had ever done before.

===Chinese===
我们上到实验室，波尔顿已在里头忙着检视那架构造敦实的翻拍照相机；连同那两条长长的钢导轨——图版架（载稿架）便在轨上滑行——它占满了与化学工作台相对的一侧的整面长度。因为我即将被引入摄影这门技艺，便比以往任何时候都更用心地把它打量了一番。

===Original===
“We’ve made some improvements since you were here last, sir,” said Polton, who was delicately lubricating the steel guides. “We’ve fitted these steel runners instead of the blackleaded wooden ones that we used to have. And we’ve made two scales instead of one. Hallo! That’s the downstairs bell. Shall I go sir?”

===Chinese===
“您上次来过之后，我们又做了些改进，先生。”正给钢导轨细细上油的波尔顿说，“我们装上了这些钢滑轨，换掉了从前那副涂黑铅的木轨。标尺也做成了两副，不再是一副。嘿！是楼下的门铃。要我去吗，先生？”

===Original===
“Perhaps you’d better,” said Thorndyke. “It may not be Mr. Britton, and I don’t want to be caught and delayed just now.”

===Chinese===
“你还是去的好。”桑代克说，“来的未必是布里顿先生，这会儿我可不想被人绊住，耽搁工夫。”

===Original===
However, it was Mr. Britton; a breezy alert-looking middle-aged man, who came in escorted by Polton and shook our hands cordially, having been previously warned of my presence. He carried a small but solid handbag, to which he clung tenaciously up to the very moment when its contents were required for use.

===Chinese===
来的却正是布里顿先生；一个神情爽朗、精明干练的中年人，由波尔顿引进屋来，同我们热诚握手——他事先已得知我也在场。他携着一只小而结实的提包，紧抓不放，直到要用其中之物的那一刻，才肯撒手。

===Original===
“So that is the camera,” said he, running an inquisitive eye over the instrument. “Very fine one, too; I am a bit of a photographer myself. What is that graduation on the sidebar?”

===Chinese===
“这就是那架照相机喽，”他说着，用一双好打探的眼睛把这机器扫视了一遍，“还是顶好的一架；我自己也算得上半个摄影迷。侧板上那道刻度是什么？”

===Original===
“Those are the scales,” replied Thorndyke, “that shows the degree of magnification or reduction. The pointer is fixed to the easel and travels with it, of course, showing the exact size of the photograph. When the pointer is opposite 0 the photograph will be identical in size with the object photographed; when it points to, say, × 6, the photograph will be six times as long as the object, or magnified thirty-six times superficially, whereas if the pointer is at ÷ 6, the photograph will be a sixth of the length of the object, or one thirty-sixth superficial.”

===Chinese===
“那就是标尺，”桑代克答道，“它标示放大或缩小的倍率。指针固定在图版架上，随架一同移动，自然也就随时指出照片的准确尺寸。指针指到 0 时，照片与被摄物尺寸相同；指到——譬如说——× 6 时，照片的长度便是原物的六倍，按面积算是放大了三十六倍；而指针若在 ÷ 6，照片的长度便只有原物的六分之一，按面积算是三十六分之一。”

===Original===
“Why are there two scales?” Mr. Britton asked.

===Chinese===
“为什么要两副标尺？”布里顿先生问。

===Original===
“There is a separate scale for each of the two lenses that we principally use. For great magnification or reduction a lens of comparatively short focus must be used, but, as a long-focus lens gives a more perfect image, we use one of very long focus﻿—thirty-six inches﻿—for copying the same size or for slight magnification or reduction.”

===Chinese===
“我们主要使用的两只镜头，各配一副标尺。要作大幅的放大或缩小，必须用焦距较短的镜头；但长焦距镜头成像更为完美，所以我们用一只焦距极长的——三十六英寸——来翻拍原大，或作轻微的放大缩小。”

===Original===
“Are you going to magnify these cheques?” Mr. Britton asked.

===Chinese===
“这些支票要放大吗？”布里顿先生问。

===Original===
“Not in the first place,” replied Thorndyke. “For convenience and speed I am going to photograph them half-size, so that six cheques will go on one whole plate. Afterwards we can enlarge from the negatives as much as we like. But we should probably enlarge only the signatures in any case.”

===Chinese===
“第一步不放大。”桑代克答道，“为了省便快捷，我打算把它们拍成一半大小，这样一张整幅感光板能容下六张支票。事后我们尽可以从底片随意放大。不过无论如何，要放大的多半只是签名。”

===Original===
The precious bag was now opened and the twenty-three cheques brought out and laid on the bench in a consecutive series in the order of their dates. They were then fixed by tapes﻿—to avoid making pinholes in them﻿—in batches of six to small drawing boards, each batch being so arranged that the signatures were towards the middle. The first board was clamped to the easel, the latter was slid along its guides until the pointer stood at ÷ 2 on the long-focus scale and Thorndyke proceeded to focus the camera with the aid of a little microscope that Polton had made for the purpose. When Mr. Britton and I had inspected the exquisitely sharp image on the focusing-screen through the microscope, Polton introduced the plate and made the first exposure, carrying the dark-slide off to develop the plate while the next batch of cheques was being fixed in position.

===Chinese===
那只珍贵的提包这才打开，二十三张支票被取出来，按日期先后连成一列摊在工作台上。随后用细纸带把它们——以免戳出针孔——每六张一批固定到小画板上，每批都摆得让签名朝向中央。第一块画板夹上图版架，架身沿导轨推移，直到指针在长焦标尺上指到 ÷ 2，桑代克便借波尔顿为此特制的一具小显微镜对起焦来。待布里顿先生和我透过显微镜看清了对焦屏上那帧纤毫毕现的影像，波尔顿便装入感光板，作了第一次曝光，随即端着暗盒去显影，与此同时，下一批支票又给摆上了位置。

===Original===
In his photographic technique, as in everything else, Polton followed as closely as he could the methods of his principal and instructor; methods characterized by that unhurried precision that leads to perfect accomplishment. When the first negative was brought forth, dripping, from the darkroom, it was without spot or stain, scratch or pinhole; uniform in colour and of exactly the required density. The six cheques shown on it﻿—ridiculously small in appearance, though only reduced to half-length﻿—looked as clear and sharp as fine etchings; though, to be sure, my opportunity for examining them was rather limited, for Polton was uncommonly careful to keep the wet plate out of reach and so safe from injury.

===Chinese===
波尔顿的摄影手法，同他做别的所有事情一样，处处紧随他的主人兼师长；那套手法的特点，是从容不迫的精确，而从容不迫的精确自能臻于圆满。第一张底片从暗房里湿淋淋地捧出来时，无斑无渍，无划痕，无针孔；色调匀净，浓淡恰合所需。上面那六张支票——虽说只缩小到一半长度，看上去却小得可笑——清晰锐利，宛如精细的蚀刻画；不过说实在的，我得以细看它们的工夫颇为有限，因为波尔顿分外小心，总让那张湿底片离人远远的，以免受损。

===Original===
“Well,” said Mr. Britton, when, at the end of the séance, he returned his treasures to the bag, “you have now got twenty-three of our cheques, to all intents and purposes. I hope you are not going to make any unlawful use of them﻿—must tell our cashiers to keep a bright lookout; and”﻿—here he lowered his voice impressively and addressed himself to me and Polton﻿—“you understand that this is a private matter between Dr. Thorndyke and me. Of course, as Mr. Blackmore is dead, there is no reason why his cheques should not be photographed for legal purposes; but we don’t want it talked about; nor, I think, does Dr. Thorndyke.”

===Chinese===
“好了，”布里顿先生在拍摄结束时把他的宝贝收回包中，说道，“从一切实际的效用看，你们如今已握有我们这二十三张支票了。我希望你们不打算拿它们派什么不法用场——我得叮嘱我们的出纳们瞪大眼睛提防着；还有，”——说到这里他压低嗓音，郑重其事地对着我和波尔顿说——“你们明白，这是桑代克博士同我之间的一件私事。当然，布莱克莫尔先生既已去世，为法律上的目的翻拍他的支票，原无不可；只是这事我们不愿外传；我想，桑代克博士也不愿意。”

===Original===
“Certainly not,” Thorndyke agreed emphatically; “but you need not be uneasy, Mr. Britton. We are very uncommunicative people in this establishment.”

===Chinese===
“当然不愿。”桑代克斩钉截铁地表示赞同，“不过您尽管放心，布里顿先生。我们这宅子里的人，嘴都是很严的。”

===Original===
As my colleague and I escorted our visitor down the stairs, he returned to the subject of the cheques.

===Chinese===
我和我的同事送这位来客下楼时，他又提起了支票的事。

===Original===
“I don’t understand what you want them for,” he remarked. “There is no question turning on signatures in the case of Blackmore deceased, is there?”

===Chinese===
“我不明白你们要它们做什么用，”他说，“布莱克莫尔亡故一案，并没有什么系于签名的问题，是吧？”

===Original===
“I should say not,” Thorndyke replied rather evasively.

===Chinese===
“我看没有。”桑代克的回答颇有几分闪烁。

===Original===
“I should say very decidedly not,” said Mr. Britton, “if I understood Marchmont aright. And, even if there were, let me tell you, these signatures that you have got wouldn’t help you. I have looked them over very closely﻿—and I have seen a few signatures in my time, you know. Marchmont asked me to glance over them as a matter of form, but I don’t believe in matters of form; I examined them very carefully. There is an appreciable amount of variation; a very appreciable amount. *But* under the variation one can trace the personal character (which is what matters); the subtle, indescribable quality that makes it recognizable to the expert eye as Jeffrey Blackmore’s writing. You understand me. There is such a quality, which remains when the coarser characteristics vary; just as a man may grow old, or fat, or bald, or may take to drink, and become quite changed; and yet, through it all, he preserves a certain something which makes him recognizable as a member of a particular family. Well, I find that quality in all those signatures, and so will you, if you have had enough experience of handwriting. I thought it best to mention it in case you might be giving yourself unnecessary trouble.”

===Chinese===
“要是没会错马奇蒙特的意思，我就要说：断乎没有。”布里顿先生说，“而且，即便有，让我告诉你们，你们弄到的这些签名也帮不了你们的忙。我把它们非常仔细地看过一遍——要知道，我这些年头里见过的签名也不算少了。马奇蒙特请我照例过目一遍，可我不信什么例行公事；我对它们做了非常仔细的查验。变异是有的，看得出来的变异；*可是*在变异之下，你能追索出个人的特性（要紧的正是这个）；那是一种微妙而难以言传的气质，行家的眼睛凭它便能认出这是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的笔迹。您听懂我的意思了。是有这么一种气质，较粗浅的特征尽可变动，它依然留存；正如一个人可以变老、变胖、变秃，也可以沾上酒瘾，变得面目全非；然而自始至终，他总保有某种说不出的东西，使人一望而知他是某一家族的成员。喏，我在所有那些签名里都发现了这种气质；只要对笔迹有足够的阅历，你们也会发现。我想还是提一句的好，免得你们自寻烦恼。”

===Original===
“It is very good of you,” said Thorndyke, “and I need not say that the information is of great value, coming from such a highly expert source. As a matter of fact, your hint will be of great value to me.”

===Chinese===
“您真太好了，”桑代克说，“而且不必说，这番指教出自如此高明的行家，价值极大。实话说，您这一点拨，于我将大有裨益。”

===Original===
He shook hands with Mr. Britton, and, as the latter disappeared down the stairs, he turned into the sitting-room and remarked:

===Chinese===
他同布里顿先生握了手；待后者消失在楼梯下，他便转身进了起居室，说道：

===Original===
“There is a very weighty and significant observation, Jervis. I advise you to consider it attentively in all its bearings.”

===Chinese===
“这里头有一句极有分量、极有意味的议论，杰维斯。我劝你细细玩味，把它的方方面面都想到。”

===Original===
“You mean the fact that these signatures are undoubtedly genuine?”

===Chinese===
“你是说，这些签名确凿无疑都是真迹这一事实？”

===Original===
“I meant, rather, the very interesting general truth that is contained in Britton’s statement; that physiognomy is not a mere matter of facial character. A man carries his personal trademark, not in his face only, but in his nervous system and muscles﻿—giving rise to characteristic movements and gait; in his larynx﻿—producing an individual voice; and even in his mouth, as shown by individual peculiarities of speech and accent. And the individual nervous system, by means of these characteristic movements, transfers its peculiarities to inanimate objects that are the products of such movements; as we see in pictures, in carving, in musical execution and in handwriting. No one has ever painted quite like Reynolds or Romney; no one has ever played exactly like Liszt or Paganini; the pictures or the sounds produced by them, were, so to speak, an extension of the physiognomy of the artist. And so with handwriting. A particular specimen is the product of a particular set of motor centres in an individual brain.”

===Chinese===
“我指的是布里顿那番话里包含的一个非常有意味的普遍道理：观相之学，并非单在面部特征。一个人身上带着他个人的印记，不只在脸上，也在他的神经系统和肌肉里——由此生出独具特色的动作与步态；在他的喉头里——由此发出各人的嗓音；甚至也在他的口里，言语和腔调的个人特质便是明证。而各人的神经系统，正是通过这些特有的动作，把它的种种特性转印到这些动作所产出的无生命之物上；我们在绘画、雕刻、演奏和笔迹里都可见到。从没有人画得同雷诺兹（Reynolds）或罗姆尼（Romney）一模一样；也没有人演奏得同李斯特（Liszt）或帕格尼尼（Paganini）分毫不差；他们产出的画与音，不妨说是艺术家相貌的延展。笔迹也是一样。一件特定的笔迹样品，是一个特定头脑中一组特定运动中枢的产物。”

===Original===
“These are very interesting considerations, Thorndyke,” I remarked; “but I don’t quite see their present application. Do you mean them to bear in any special way on the Blackmore case?”

===Chinese===
“这些道理很有意思，桑代克，”我说，“可我不大明白它们眼下的用处。你是要让它们以某种特殊的方式，关涉到布莱克莫尔案吗？”

===Original===
“I think they do bear on it very directly. I thought so while Mr. Britton was making his very illuminating remarks.”

===Chinese===
“我认为它们同本案的关涉十分直接。布里顿先生发表那番极具启发性的议论时，我就这么想。”

===Original===
“I don’t see how. In fact I cannot see why you are going into the question of the signatures at all. The signature on the will is admittedly genuine, and that seems to me to dispose of the whole affair.”

===Chinese===
“我看不出怎么个直接法。说实话，我根本看不出你为什么要钻签名这个问题。遗嘱上的签名既已公认为真迹，在我看来，整个案子就此了结。”

===Original===
“My dear Jervis,” said he, “you and Marchmont are allowing yourselves to be obsessed by a particular fact﻿—a very striking and weighty fact, I will admit, but still, only an isolated fact. Jeffrey Blackmore executed his will in a regular manner, complying with all the necessary formalities and conditions. In the face of that single circumstance you and Marchmont would ‘chuck up the sponge,’ as the old pugilists expressed it. Now that is a great mistake. You should never allow yourself to be bullied and browbeaten by a single fact.”

===Chinese===
“我亲爱的杰维斯，”他说，“你和马奇蒙特正让一个特定的事实给魇住了——我承认，那是个非常醒目、非常有分量的事实，可它终究只是一个孤立的事实。杰弗里·布莱克莫尔以合乎规范的方式订立遗嘱，一切必要的形式与条件无不遵守。面对这一单独的情节，你和马奇蒙特就要——用老拳师的话说——‘把海绵扔出去’认输了。这就大错特错了。你绝不可容许自己被单独一个事实威逼恫吓住。”

===Original===
“But, my dear Thorndyke!” I protested, “this fact seems to be final. It covers all possibilities﻿—unless you can suggest any other that would cancel it.”

===Chinese===
“可是，我亲爱的桑代克！”我抗辩道，“这个事实看来是决定性的。它涵盖了所有可能——除非你能提出另一个能将它勾销的事实来。”

===Original===
“I could suggest a dozen,” he replied. “Let us take an instance. Supposing Jeffrey executed this will for a wager; that he immediately revoked it and made a fresh will, that he placed the latter in the custody of some person and that that person has suppressed it.”

===Chinese===
“我能提出一打来。”他答道，“且举一例。假设杰弗里订立这份遗嘱是为了一场赌赛；随即把它撤销，另立了一份新遗嘱；又假设他把新遗嘱交托某人保管，而那人把它隐匿了下来。”

===Original===
“Surely you do not make this suggestion seriously!” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“你总不会当真提出这种设想吧！”我叫道。

===Original===
“Certainly I do not,” he replied with a smile. “I merely give it as an instance to show that your final and absolute fact is really only conditional on there being no other fact that cancels it.”

===Chinese===
“当然不当真。”他含笑答道，“我不过拿它举个例子，说明你那个决定性的、绝对的事实，其实只是有条件的——条件便是不存在另一个能勾销它的事实。”

===Original===
“Do you think he might have made a third will?”

===Chinese===
“你认为他可能立过第三份遗嘱？”

===Original===
“It is obviously possible. A man who makes two wills may make three or more; but I may say that I see no present reason for assuming the existence of another will. What I want to impress on you is the necessity of considering all the facts instead of bumping heavily against the most conspicuous one and forgetting all the rest. By the way, here is a little problem for you. What was the object of which these are the parts?”

===Chinese===
“显然可能。立过两份遗嘱的人，自然也立得出三份或更多；不过我可以说，眼下我看不出有什么理由假定另有一份遗嘱存在。我要你铭记于心的是：必须把全部事实都考虑在内，而不是一头撞在最显眼的那个上面，把其余的忘个精光。对了，这儿有个小题目给你。这些碎片原本是件什么东西？”

===Original===
He pushed across the table a little cardboard box, having first removed the lid. In it were a number of very small pieces of broken glass, some of which had been cemented together by their edges.

===Chinese===
他先揭去盖子，把一只小纸板盒推过桌来。盒里是若干极小的碎玻璃片，有几片已由边缘粘合在一起。

===Original===
“These, I suppose,” said I, looking with considerable curiosity at the little collection, “are the pieces of glass that we picked up in poor Blackmore’s bedroom?”

===Chinese===
“我猜，”我说，一面怀着不小的好奇打量这小小的一堆，“这些就是我们在可怜的布莱克莫尔卧室里拾起的那些玻璃片吧？”

===Original===
“Yes. You see that Polton has been endeavouring to reconstitute the object, whatever it was; but he has not been very successful, for the fragments were too small and irregular and the collection too incomplete. However, here is a specimen, built up of six small pieces, which exhibits the general character of the object fairly well.”

===Chinese===
“正是。你看，波尔顿一直在设法把那件东西复原——不管它原是什么；可是不算太成功，因为碎片太小、太不规则，收得又不齐全。不过这里有一件样品，由六小片拼成，把那东西的大致模样显示得相当清楚。”

===Original===
He picked out the little irregularly shaped object and handed it to me; and I could not but admire the neatness with which Polton had joined the tiny fragments together.

===Chinese===
他挑出那件形状不规则的小东西，递给我；波尔顿把这些碎屑拼拢得如此齐整，我不由得暗暗佩服。

===Original===
I took the little “restoration,” and, holding it up before my eyes, moved it to and fro as I looked through it at the window.

===Chinese===
我接过那件小小的“复原品”，举在眼前，对着窗户一面来回移动，一面透过它观看。

===Original===
“It was not a lens,” I pronounced eventually.

===Chinese===
“这不是镜片。”我终于下了断语。

===Original===
“No,” Thorndyke agreed, “it was not a lens.”

===Chinese===
“不是，”桑代克同意道，“这不是镜片。”

===Original===
“And so cannot have been a spectacle-glass. But the surface was curved﻿—one side convex and the other concave﻿—and the little piece that remains of the original edge seems to have been ground to fit a bezel or frame. I should say that these are portions of a watch-glass.”

===Chinese===
“因此也不可能是眼镜片。可这表面是弧形的——一面凸，一面凹——而原先边缘残留的那一小片，似乎磨过，恰好嵌合表圈或框子。依我说，这些是表玻璃的碎块。”

===Original===
“That is Polton’s opinion,” said Thorndyke, “and I think you are both wrong.”

===Chinese===
“波尔顿也是这个意见，”桑代克说，“可我认为你们俩都错了。”

===Original===
“What do you say to the glass of a miniature or locket?”

===Chinese===
“那你看是微型画像或盒式坠饰上的玻璃呢？”

===Original===
“That is rather more probable, but it is not my view.”

===Chinese===
“这倒像样一些，但不是我的看法。”

===Original===
“What do you think it is?” I asked. But Thorndyke was not to be drawn.

===Chinese===
“那你觉得它是什么？”我问。可桑代克就是不肯吐口。

===Original===
“I am submitting the problem for solution by my learned friend,” he replied with an exasperating smile, and then added: “I don’t say that you and Polton are wrong; only that I don’t agree with you. Perhaps you had better make a note of the properties of this object, and consider it at your leisure when you are ruminating on the other data referring to the Blackmore case.”

===Chinese===
“本问题正提交我这位博学的朋友解答。”他带着令人恼火的笑容答道，随后又添了一句：“我没有说你和波尔顿错了；只是我不同意你们。或许你最好把这东西的特性记下来，等你反复寻思布莱克莫尔案其他材料的时候，得闲再慢慢推敲。”

===Original===
“My ruminations,” I said, “always lead me back to the same point.”

===Chinese===
“我的寻思，”我说，“回回都把我领回同一处。”

===Original===
“But you mustn’t let them,” he replied. “Shuffle your data about. Invent hypotheses. Never mind if they seem rather wild. Don’t put them aside on that account. Take the first hypothesis that you can invent and test it thoroughly with your facts. You will probably have to reject it, but you will be certain to have learned something new. Then try again with a fresh one. You remember what I told you of my methods when I began this branch of practice and had plenty of time on my hands?”

===Chinese===
“可你不能由着它们。”他答道，“把你的材料洗一洗牌。编造假设。哪怕显得相当离奇也不要紧。别因此把它们撇在一边。拿起你编得出的第一个假设，用你的事实把它彻底检验一遍。多半你得把它否掉，但你必定学到点新东西。然后换个新的再来。你可记得我初操这一行、手里时间充裕的时候，对你讲过我用的什么法子？”

===Original===
“I am not sure that I do.”

===Chinese===
“记不真切了。”

===Original===
“Well, I used to occupy my leisure in constructing imaginary cases, mostly criminal, for the purpose of study and for the acquirement of experience. For instance, I would devise an ingenious fraud and would plan it in detail, taking every precaution that I could think of against failure or detection, considering, and elaborately providing for, every imaginable contingency. For the time being, my entire attention was concentrated on it, making it as perfect and secure and undetectable as I could with the knowledge and ingenuity at my command. I behaved exactly as if I were proposing actually to carry it out, and my life or liberty depended on its success﻿—excepting that I made full notes of every detail of the scheme. Then when my plans were as complete as I could make them, and I could think of no way in which to improve them, I changed sides and considered the case from the standpoint of detection. I analysed the case, I picked out its inherent and unavoidable weaknesses, and, especially, I noted the respects in which a fraudulent proceeding of a particular kind differed from the bona fide proceeding that it simulated. The exercise was invaluable to me. I acquired as much experience from those imaginary cases as I should from real ones, and in addition, I learned a method which is the one that I practise to this day.”

===Chinese===
喏，我从前惯于把闲暇用来构造虚构案件——多是刑事案件——以作研习、以积阅历。譬如，我会设计一桩巧妙的骗局，把它的细枝末节一一规划停当，凡想得到的防败露、防侦破的手段无不用尽，凡想得到的意外情况无不考虑到，并为之周密布置。一时之间，我的全部注意力都凝聚其上，凭我掌握的知识和机巧，把它弄得尽量完善、稳妥、无迹可寻。我的行事，同我当真打算付诸实施、且性命与自由全系于其成败的人分毫不差——只有一点除外：我对方案的每一细节都做了详尽的笔记。待到方案在我手里完善得无可再完善、我想不出还有什么可改进之处，我便换到另一边，从侦破的立场来考量这桩案子。我把案子加以剖析，挑出它内在的、不可避免的破绽，尤其要记下：某一类诈伪的行径，同它所摹拟的真实行径（bona fide）究竟在哪些方面不同。这种练习于我弥足珍贵。我从那些虚构案件里取得的阅历，不下于从真实案件所能取得的；而且除此之外，我还学得了一种方法，至今奉行的仍是它。

===Original===
“Do you mean that you still invent imaginary cases as mental exercises?”

===Chinese===
“你是说，你如今仍以虚构案件作心智的练习？”

===Original===
“No; I mean that, when I have a problem of any intricacy, I invent a case which fits the facts and the assumed motives of one of the parties. Then I work at that case until I find whether it leads to elucidation or to some fundamental disagreement. In the latter case I reject it and begin the process over again.”

===Chinese===
“不；我是说，每当我遇到一桩错综复杂的问题，我便虚构一个案子，使它切合事实、切合某一方被假定的动机。然后我在这个案子上做工夫，直到弄清它究竟引向水落石出，还是碰上某种根本性的抵牾。若是后者，我便弃了它，把这道工序从头再来。”

===Original===
“Doesn’t that method sometimes involve a good deal of wasted time and energy?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这种方法难道不会有时白白耗去不少时间和精力吗？”我问。

===Original===
“No; because each time that you fail to establish a given case, you exclude a particular explanation of the facts and narrow down the field of inquiry. By repeating the process, you are bound, in the end, to arrive at an imaginary case which fits all the facts. Then your imaginary case is the real case and the problem is solved. Let me recommend you to give the method a trial.”

===Chinese===
“不会；因为你每失败于确立一个给定的案子，就排除掉对事实的一种特定解释，把查究的范围收窄一分。把这道工序反复下去，你到头来必定会达到一个切合全部事实的虚构案子。到那时，你的虚构案子便是真实案子，问题也就解了。我劝你把这方法试上一试。”

===Original===
I promised to do so, though with no very lively expectations as to the result, and with this, the subject was allowed, for the present, to drop.

===Chinese===
我答应照办，虽说对结果不抱多热烈期望；说到这里，这个话题便暂且搁下了。

## VII The Cuneiform Inscription / 楔形文字铭文

===Original===
The surprise which Thorndyke’s proceedings usually occasioned, especially to lawyers, was principally due, I think, to my friend’s habit of viewing occurrences from an unusual standpoint. He did not look at things quite as other men looked at them. He had no prejudices and he knew no conventions. When other men were cocksure, Thorndyke was doubtful. When other men despaired, he entertained hopes; and thus it happened that he would often undertake cases that had been rejected contemptuously by experienced lawyers, and, what is more, would bring them to a successful issue.

===Chinese===
桑代克的行事在旁人——尤其是律师们——心中每每引起的诧异，我想，主要归因于我这位朋友习惯从一个不同寻常的角度看待世事。他看事物，与旁人看的路数并不一样。他无所成见，也不识忌讳。旁人笃信不疑之时，桑代克却心存疑虑；旁人绝望之际，他却怀抱希望。正因如此，他往往接手那些被老练的律师们轻蔑地拒之门外的案子，而且，更了不得的是，居然能办得功德圆满。

===Original===
Thus it had been in the only other case in which I had been personally associated with him﻿—the so-called “Red Thumb Mark” case. There he was presented with an apparent impossibility; but he had given it careful consideration. Then, from the category of the impossible he had brought it to that of the possible; from the merely possible to the actually probable; from the probable to the certain; and in the end had won the case triumphantly.

===Chinese===
我亲身与他共事的唯一另一桩案子——即所谓“红拇指印案”——便是如此。那桩案子摆到他面前时，分明是一桩显然不可能的事；他却对它作了细致的推究。于是，他从“不可能”一类把它挪进了“可能”一类；从仅仅可能挪到确乎或然；从或然挪到确凿；到头来大获全胜。

===Original===
Was it conceivable that he could make anything of the present case? He had not declined it. He had certainly entertained it and was probably thinking it over at this moment. Yet could anything be more impossible? Here was the case of a man making his own will, probably writing it out himself, bringing it voluntarily to a certain place and executing it in the presence of competent witnesses. There was no suggestion of any compulsion or even influence or persuasion. The testator was admittedly sane and responsible; and if the will did not give effect to his wishes﻿—which, however, could not be proved﻿—that was due to his own carelessness in drafting the will and not to any unusual circumstances. And the problem﻿—which Thorndyke seemed to be considering﻿—was how to set aside that will.

===Chinese===
眼下这桩案子，他还能有所作为吗？这可以设想吗？他并没有拒绝受理。他分明已把它记挂在心，此刻多半正在反复思量。然而还有比这更不可能的案子吗？一个人自立遗嘱，多半还是亲笔写就，自愿把它带到某一处所，在合格的见证人面前签署生效。其中全无半点强迫的影子，甚至连影响、劝诱也谈不上。遗嘱人的神智与责任能力均无可置疑；倘若遗嘱未能落实他的心愿——不过这一点也无从证明——那也只能归咎于他本人起草遗嘱时的粗疏，而非任何异常的情由。而难题——桑代克眼下似乎正在思量的——正是如何把这纸遗嘱推翻。

===Original===
I reviewed the statements that I had heard, but turn them about as I would, I could get nothing out of them but confirmation of Mr. Marchmont’s estimate of the case. One fact that I had noted with some curiosity I again considered; that was Thorndyke’s evident desire to inspect Jeffrey Blackmore’s chambers. He had, it is true, shown no eagerness, but I had seen at the time that the questions which he put to Stephen were put, not with any expectation of eliciting information but for the purpose of getting an opportunity to look over the rooms himself.

===Chinese===
我把听到的那些陈述又过了一遍，可任凭我颠来倒去地琢磨，除了一再印证马奇蒙特先生对本案的估量之外，什么也琢磨不出来。有一个当时就令我略感好奇的事实，我又重新考虑了一遍：那就是桑代克对察看杰弗里·布莱克莫尔寓所的显而易见的愿望。诚然，他并未显出急切，可我当时就看出，他向斯蒂芬提出的那些问题，并非当真能问出什么消息，而是为了给自己博得一个亲自查看那几间屋子的机会。

===Original===
I was still cogitating on the subject when my colleague returned, followed by the watchful Polton with the tea-tray, and I attacked him forthwith.

===Chinese===
我正就这个题目苦思冥想，我的同事回来了，身后跟着警觉的波尔顿，端着茶盘，我便当即向他发起了攻势。

===Original===
“Well, Thorndyke,” I said, “I have been thinking about this Blackmore case while you have been gadding about.”

===Chinese===
“喂，桑代克，”我说，“你东跑西颠的这工夫，我一直在想这桩布莱克莫尔案。”

===Original===
“And may I take it that the problem is solved?”

===Chinese===
“那么我可否认为，难题已经解开了？”

===Original===
“No, I’m hanged if you may. I can make nothing of it.”

===Chinese===
“不行，见鬼，才没这么容易。我是一点门道也摸不出来。”

===Original===
“Then you are in much the same position as I am.”

===Chinese===
“那你我的处境倒差不太多。”

===Original===
“But, if you can make nothing of it, why did you undertake it?”

===Chinese===
“可是，既然你摸不出门道，当初为什么又接下它呢？”

===Original===
“I only undertook to think about it,” said Thorndyke. “I never reject a case offhand unless it is obviously fishy. It is surprising how difficulties, and even impossibilities, dwindle if you look at them attentively. My experience has taught me that the most unlikely case is, at least, worth thinking over.”

===Chinese===
“我只是答应考虑考虑，”桑代克说，“除非一桩案子分明散发着邪气，我从不一口回绝。只要你凝神细看，重重困难、乃至种种不可能，竟会怎样萎缩消退，实在令人惊奇。经验告诉我：最不像是能办的案子，至少也值得想一想。”

===Original===
“By the way, why do you want to look over Jeffrey’s chambers? What do you expect to find there?”

===Chinese===
“对了，你为什么要去查看杰弗里的寓所？指望在那儿找到什么？”

===Original===
“I have no expectations at all. I am simply looking for stray facts.”

===Chinese===
“我压根儿不指望什么。我不过是寻访零散的事实罢了。”

===Original===
“And all those questions that you asked Stephen Blackmore; had you nothing in your mind﻿—no definite purpose?”

===Chinese===
“那么你问斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔的那些问题，心里就毫无算盘——没有一定的目的？”

===Original===
“No purpose beyond getting to know as much about the case as I can.”

===Chinese===
“除了尽量多了解些案情之外，别无目的。”

===Original===
“But,” I exclaimed, “do you mean that you are going to examine those rooms without any definite object at all?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我失声道，“你的意思是，你就要去查勘那几间屋子，却全然没有明确的目标？”

===Original===
“I wouldn’t say that,” replied Thorndyke. “This is a legal case. Let me put an analogous medical case as being more within your present sphere. Supposing that a man should consult you, say, about a progressive loss of weight. He can give no explanation. He has no pain, no discomfort, no symptoms of any kind; in short, he feels perfectly well in every respect; *but* he is losing weight continuously. What would you do?”

===Chinese===
“那倒也不是，”桑代克答道，“这是桩法律案子。且让我举一个类似的医学病例，那更贴近你目前的行当。譬如有个男人来找你就医，诉说体重不断减轻。他说不出所以然。不疼，不胀，什么症状都没有；总而言之，自觉各方面都好端端的；*可是*体重却一个劲儿往下降。你会怎么办？”

===Original===
“I should overhaul him thoroughly,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“我会给他来一次彻底的大检查。”我答道。

===Original===
“Why? What would you expect to find?”

===Chinese===
“为什么？你指望查出什么来？”

===Original===
“I don’t know that I should start by expecting to find anything in particular. But I should overhaul him organ by organ and function by function, and if I could find nothing abnormal I should have to give it up.”

===Chinese===
“倒不是说一上来就指望查出什么特别的东西。但我会一个器官一个器官、一项功能一项功能地彻查下去，倘若查不出任何异常，那就只好罢手。”

===Original===
“Exactly,” said Thorndyke. “And that is just my position and my line of action. Here is a case which is perfectly regular and straightforward excepting in one respect. It has a single abnormal feature. And for that abnormality there is nothing to account.

===Chinese===
“正是，”桑代克说，“这恰恰就是我的处境和我的路数。眼前这桩案子，除了一点之外，处处规规矩矩、顺顺当当。它只有一处异常。而这处异常，无任何情由可加解释。

===Original===
