“Jeffrey Blackmore made a will. It was a well-drawn will and it apparently gave full effect to his intentions. Then he revoked that will and made another. No change had occurred in his circumstances or in his intentions. The provisions of the new will were believed by him to be identical with those of the old one. The new will differed from the old one only in having a defect in the drafting from which the first will was free, and of which he must have been unaware. Now why did he revoke the first will and replace it with another which he believed to be identical in its provisions? There is no answer to that question. It is an abnormal feature in the case. There must be some explanation of that abnormality and it is my business to discover it. But the facts in my possession yield no such explanation. Therefore it is my purpose to search for new facts which may give me a starting-point for an investigation.”

===Chinese===
“杰弗里·布莱克莫尔立了一份遗嘱。那是一份起草得很周密的遗嘱，显然充分落实了他的意愿。随后他废除了那份遗嘱，另立了一份。他的境况与意愿均无任何变化。他相信新遗嘱的条款与旧的那份完全一致。新遗嘱与旧遗嘱的区别，只在于起草上有一处旧遗嘱所无的缺陷，而这缺陷他必定毫不知情。那么，他为什么废除第一份遗嘱，另立一份他自认为条款全然相同的遗嘱？这个问题没有答案。这正是本案的一处异常。这异常必有某种解释，而发现这个解释正是我的本务。但我掌握的事实给不出这种解释。因此，我的目的便是搜寻新的事实，好为调查寻得一个起点。”

===Original===
This exposition of Thorndyke’s proposed conduct of the case, reasonable as it was, did not impress me as very convincing. I found myself coming back to Marchmont’s position, that there was really nothing in dispute. But other matters claimed our attention at the moment, and it was not until after dinner that my colleague reverted to the subject.

===Chinese===
桑代克对本案办理方略的这番阐述，尽管头头是道，在我听来却算不得多么折服。我发现自己又回到马奇蒙特的立场上去了：此事实在并无争点可言。但当时别的事情占据了我们的心神，直到晚饭之后，我的同事才重提这个话题。

===Original===
“How should you like to take a turn round to New Inn this evening?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“今晚陪我到新客栈转一趟，意下如何？”他问道。

===Original===
“I should have thought,” said I, “that it would be better to go by daylight. Those old chambers are not usually very well illuminated.”

===Chinese===
“我倒觉得，”我说，“不如白天去的好。那些老寓所里的光线，通常都不怎么充足。”

===Original===
“That is well thought of,” said Thorndyke. “We had better take a lamp with us. Let us go up to the laboratory and get one from Polton.”

===Chinese===
“这个想头不错，”桑代克说，“我们最好带盏灯去。上实验室找波尔顿要一盏吧。”

===Original===
“There is no need to do that,” said I. “The pocket-lamp that you lent me is in my overcoat pocket. I put it there to return it to you.”

===Chinese===
“用不着，”我说，“你借我的那盏随身小灯就在我大衣口袋里。我搁在那儿，正想还你。”

===Original===
“Did you have occasion to use it?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你用上它了吗？”他问道。

===Original===
“Yes. I paid another visit to the mysterious house and carried out your plan. I must tell you about it later.”

===Chinese===
“用上了。我又去了一趟那座神秘的宅子，把你的计划付诸实行。回头我得把经过讲给你听。”

===Original===
“Do. I shall be keenly interested to hear all about your adventures. Is there plenty of candle left in the lamp?”

===Chinese===
“一定讲。你的历险经过，我听得兴趣浓得很。灯里的蜡烛还剩不少吧？”

===Original===
“Oh yes. I only used it for about an hour.”

===Chinese===
“哦，还多。我只点了约莫一个钟头。”

===Original===
“Then let us be off,” said Thorndyke; and we accordingly set forth on our quest; and, as we went, I reflected once more on the apparent vagueness of our proceedings. Presently I reopened the subject with Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“那就动身吧，”桑代克说。于是我们便踏上这趟查访之途；一路走着，我又一次琢磨起我们此行表面上的漫无目的来。走了一程，我重新挑起这个话头。

===Original===
“I can’t imagine,” said I, “that you have absolutely nothing in view. That you are going to this place with no defined purpose whatever.”

===Chinese===
“我实在无法想象，”我说，“你心里竟全无成算，就这么到那地方去，不带半点明确的目的。”

===Original===
“I did not say exactly that,” replied Thorndyke. “I said that I was not going to look for any particular thing or fact. I am going in the hope that I may observe something that may start a new train of speculation. But that is not all. You know that an investigation follows a certain logical course. It begins with the observation of the conspicuous facts. We have done that. The facts were supplied by Marchmont. The next stage is to propose to oneself one or more provisional explanations or hypotheses. We have done that, too﻿—or, at least I have, and I suppose you have.”

===Chinese===
“我说的并不尽是那个意思，”桑代克答道，“我说的是，我不是去寻找任何特定的东西或特定的事实。我去，是盼望能观察到什么，好引出一串新的思索。但还不止于此。你是知道的，一项调查自有其一定的逻辑进程。它始于对显眼事实的观察。这一步我们已经做了——事实是马奇蒙特提供的。下一步，是给自己提出一个或多个暂时性的解释，亦即假说。这一步我们也做了——至少我做了，我想你也做了。”

===Original===
“I haven’t,” said I. “There is Jeffrey’s will, but why he should have made the change I cannot form the foggiest idea. But I should like to hear your provisional theories on the subject.”

===Chinese===
“我可没有，”我说，“杰弗里的遗嘱是有一份，可他为什么要那样更改，我连最模糊的影子也捉摸不出。不过，你那些暂时性的理论，我倒很愿意一听。”

===Original===
“You won’t hear them at present. They are mere wild conjectures. But to resume: what do we do next?”

===Chinese===
“眼下你还听不着。那不过是些胡乱的臆测罢了。言归正传：下一步我们做什么？”

===Original===
“Go to New Inn and rake over the deceased gentleman’s apartments.”

===Chinese===
“到新客栈去，把已故先生那几间屋子翻检一遍。”

===Original===
Thorndyke smilingly ignored my answer and continued﻿—

===Chinese===
桑代克含笑不理会我的回答，径自往下讲——

===Original===
“We examine each explanation in turn and see what follows from it; whether it agrees with all the facts and leads to the discovery of new ones, or, on the other hand, disagrees with some facts or leads us to an absurdity. Let us take a simple example.

===Chinese===
“我们逐一检验每一个解释，看看由它能推出什么：它是否与全部事实相符，能否引导我们发现新的事实；抑或相反，与某些事实相悖，或者把我们引向荒谬。且举一个浅近的例子。

===Original===
“Suppose we find scattered over a field a number of largish masses of stone, which are entirely different in character from the rocks found in the neighbourhood. The question arises, how did those stones get into that field? Three explanations are proposed. One: that they are the products of former volcanic action; two: that they were brought from a distance by human agency; three: that they were carried thither from some distant country by icebergs. Now each of those explanations involves certain consequences. If the stones are volcanic, then they were once in a state of fusion. But we find that they are unaltered limestone and contain fossils. Then they are not volcanic. If they were borne by icebergs, then they were once part of a glacier and some of them will probably show the flat surfaces with parallel scratches which are found on glacier-borne stones. We examine them and find the characteristic scratched surfaces. Then they have probably been brought to this place by icebergs. But this does not exclude human agency, for they might have been brought by men to this place from some other where the icebergs had deposited them. A further comparison with other facts would be needed.

===Chinese===
“假定我们在一片田野上发现散落着若干块颇大的石头，其性质与附近出产的岩石全然不同。问题来了：这些石头是怎么到那片田野里去的？现有三种解释。其一：它们是往昔火山作用的产物；其二：它们是人力从远处搬运来的；其三：它们是被冰山从某个遥远国度携带到此的。而这三种解释，各自都蕴含着某些必然的推论。倘若这些石头是火成的，它们便曾一度处于熔融状态。可我们发现它们是未经变动的石灰岩，里面还含着化石。那么它们便不是火山成因。倘若它们是由冰山载来的，那么它们曾经是冰川的一部分，其中若干块多半会带着扁平的擦面和平行的擦痕——冰川搬运的石头上常见这种特征。我们一查验，果然发现了这种特有的带擦痕的表面。那么它们多半是冰山带到此地来的了。但这并不能排除人力所为，因为也可能是人们从别处——即冰山早先抛下它们的地方——把它们搬运到此地的。这便有待进一步与其他事实比照参证了。

===Original===
“So we proceed in cases like this present one. Of the facts that are known to us we invent certain explanations. From each of those explanations we deduce consequences; and if those consequences agree with new facts, they confirm the explanation, whereas if they disagree they tend to disprove it. But here we are at our destination.”

===Chinese===
“办理眼下这样的案子，我们的步骤便是如此。就已知的事实，我们构想出若干解释；再从每个解释演绎出种种推论；这些推论若与新的事实相符，便印证该解释，若不相符，便倾向于驳倒它。好了，我们到地方了。”

===Original===
We turned out of Wych Street into the arched passage leading into New Inn, and, halting at the half-door of the lodge, perceived a stout, purple-faced man crouching over the fire, coughing violently. He held up his hand to intimate that he was fully occupied for the moment, and we accordingly waited for his paroxysm to subside. At length he turned towards us, wiping his eyes, and inquired our business.

===Chinese===
我们从维奇街（Wych Street）拐进通往新客栈的拱顶通道，在门房的半截门前站定，看见一个红紫脸膛的胖子正伏在炉火边咳得死去活来。他举手示意，表示这会儿实在腾不出空来，我们便等他那阵咳嗽平息下去。末了他抹着眼睛转过身来，问我们有何贵干。

===Original===
“Mr. Stephen Blackmore,” said Thorndyke, “has given me permission to look over his chambers. He said that he would mention the matter to you.”

===Chinese===
“斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔先生，”桑代克说，“已应允我查看他的寓所。他说会跟您打招呼的。”

===Original===
“So he has, sir,” said the porter; “but he has just taken the key himself to go to the chambers. If you walk across the Inn you’ll find him there; it’s on the farther side; number thirty-one, second floor.”

===Chinese===
“打招呼了，先生，”门房说，“不过他刚自己取了钥匙上寓所去了。您穿过客栈就能找到他；在对面那一侧；三十一号，二楼。”

===Original===
We made our way across to the house indicated, the ground floor of which was occupied by a solicitor’s offices and was distinguished by a good-sized brass plate. Although it had now been dark some time there was no light on the lower stairs, but we encountered on the first-floor landing a man who had just lit the lamp there. Thorndyke halted to address him.

===Chinese===
我们朝所指的那座宅子走去，其底层是一家律师事务所的办公处，门上以一块颇为像样的铜牌为记。这时天已黑了些时辰，下方几段楼梯并没有灯，但在一楼的楼梯平台上，我们碰见一个刚把那里的灯点亮的人。桑代克停下脚步，向他搭话。

===Original===
“Can you tell me who occupies the chambers on the third floor?”

===Chinese===
“请问三楼的寓所是谁住着？”

===Original===
“The third floor has been empty about three months,” was the reply.

===Chinese===
“三楼空了约莫三个月了。”对方答道。

===Original===
“We are going up to look at the chambers on the second floor,” said Thorndyke. “Are they pretty quiet?”

===Chinese===
“我们正要上楼去看二楼的寓所，”桑代克说，“那里清静吗？”

===Original===
“Quiet!” exclaimed the man. “Lord bless you the place is like a cemetery for the deaf and dumb. There’s the solicitors on the ground floor and the architects on the first floor. They both clear out about six, and when they’re gone the house is as empty as a blown hegg. I don’t wonder poor Mr. Blackmore made away with his-self. Livin’ up there all alone, it must have been like Robinson Crusoe without no man Friday and not even a blooming goat to talk to. Quiet! It’s quiet enough, if that’s what you want. Wouldn’t be no good to *me*.”

===Chinese===
“清静！”那人嚷道，“老天爷保佑，这地方就跟聋哑人坟场一个样。底层是律师们，一楼是绘图建筑师们。两拨人六点前后就走得精光，他们一走，这宅子空得就像个吹空了的鸡蛋壳。可怜的布莱克莫尔先生自寻了短见，我一点也不奇怪。一个人孤零零住在那上头，准跟没了星期五的鲁滨逊·克鲁索（Robinson Crusoe）似的，连只该死的山羊都没有，没个说话的伴儿。清静！要图清静，够清静的了。对*我*可没什么好处。”

===Original===
With a contemptuous shake of the head, he turned and retired down the next flight, and, as the echoes of his footsteps died away we resumed our ascent.

===Chinese===
他轻蔑地摇了摇头，转身退下一段楼梯去了；待他脚步声的回响消散，我们继续往上走。

===Original===
“So it would appear,” Thorndyke commented, “that when Jeffrey Blackmore came home that last evening, the house was empty.”

===Chinese===
“如此说来，”桑代克议论道，“杰弗里·布莱克莫尔最后一晚回到家时，这宅子是空的。”

===Original===
Arrived on the second-floor landing, we were confronted by a solid-looking door on the lintel of which the deceased man’s name was painted in white lettering which still looked new and fresh. Thorndyke knocked at the door, which was at once opened by Stephen Blackmore.

===Chinese===
到了二楼楼梯平台，一扇厚实牢固的门正对着我们，门楣上用白漆漆着死者的姓名，漆色犹新。桑代克敲了敲门，斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔应声把门打开。

===Original===
“I haven’t wasted any time before taking advantage of your permission, you see,” my colleague said as we entered.

===Chinese===
“你瞧，得了你的许可，我可一点工夫也没耽搁。”我们进屋时，我的同事说道。

===Original===
“No, indeed,” said Stephen; “you are very prompt. I have been rather wondering what kind of information you expect to gather from an inspection of these rooms.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，”斯蒂芬说，“您真够利索的。我倒一直在纳闷，您指望从察看这几间屋子里收集到什么样的消息。”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled genially, amused, no doubt, by the similarity of Stephen’s remarks to those of mine which he had so recently criticized.

===Chinese===
桑代克和蔼地笑了笑，多半是被斯蒂芬这话与我先前那些话的如出一辙逗乐了——我的那些话，他前不久才数落过。

===Original===
“A man of science, Mr. Blackmore,” he said, “expects nothing. He collects facts and keeps an open mind. As to me, I am a mere legal Autolycus, a snapper-up of unconsidered trifles of evidence. When I have accumulated a few facts, I arrange them, compare them and think about them. Sometimes the comparison yields new matter and sometimes it doesn’t; but in any case, believe me, it is a capital error to decide beforehand what data are to be sought for.”

===Chinese===
“科学家，布莱克莫尔先生，”他说，“是不抱任何预期的。他收集事实，并且不存成见。至于我嘛，不过是个法律界的奥托吕科斯（Autolycus），专捡无人在意的证据碎屑。待积累起若干事实，我便加以整理、比照、思量。有时比照之下能生出新的名堂，有时则不能；但无论如何，请相信我：预先限定该寻访哪些材料，乃是头等的大错。”

===Original===
“Yes, I suppose that is so,” said Stephen; “though, to me, it almost looks as if Mr. Marchmont was right; that there is nothing to investigate.”

===Chinese===
“是，我想是这么回事，”斯蒂芬说，“不过在我看来，这几乎就像马奇蒙特先生说的那样：根本没有什么可查的。”

===Original===
“You should have thought of that before you consulted me,” laughed Thorndyke. “As it is, I am engaged to look into the case and I shall do so; and, as I have said, I shall keep an open mind until I have all the facts in my possession.”

===Chinese===
“那你当初请我之前就该想到这一层才是，”桑代克笑道，“如今既然受托查究此案，我就要查下去；而且我说过，在我掌握全部事实之前，我将始终不存成见。”

===Original===
He glanced round the sitting-room, which we had now entered, and continued:

===Chinese===
他环顾我们此刻进入的起居室，继续说道：

===Original===
“These are fine, dignified old rooms. It seems a sin to have covered up all this oak panelling and that carved cornice and mantel with paint. Think what it must have been like when the beautiful figured wood was exposed.”

===Chinese===
“这些都是气派的、庄重的老屋子。把这些橡木护壁板，连同那雕花的檐口和壁炉架，统统用油漆遮盖起来，简直是造孽。想想看，当初这纹理美观的木料裸露在外时，该是何等光景。”

===Original===
“It would be very dark,” Stephen observed.

===Chinese===
“那会很昏暗的。”斯蒂芬说。

===Original===
“Yes,” Thorndyke agreed, “and I suppose we care more for light and less for beauty than our ancestors did. But now, tell me; looking round these rooms, do they convey to you a similar impression to that which the old rooms did? Have they the same general character?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克表示同意，“想来比起祖先，我们更重光线而轻美致了。不过现在请你告诉我：环顾这几间屋子，它们给你的印象，与旧居那几间相同吗？总的神味一致吗？”

===Original===
“Not quite, I think. Of course the rooms in Jermyn Street were in a different kind of house, but beyond that, I seem to feel a certain difference; which is rather odd, seeing that the furniture is the same. But the old rooms were more cosy, more homelike. I find something rather bare and cheerless, I was almost going to say squalid, in the look of these chambers.”

===Chinese===
“我想不尽相同。当然啦，杰明街那些屋子是在另一种宅子里，但撇开这层不谈，我似乎仍觉出某种不同；这就有点奇怪了，因为家具还是原来那套。可旧居更舒适些，更有家的味道。这几间屋子，我总觉得有几分空落、凄清，几乎想说寒碜了。”

===Original===
“That is rather what I should have expected,” said Thorndyke. “The opium habit alters a man’s character profoundly; and, somehow, apart from the mere furnishing, a room reflects in some subtle way, but very distinctly, the personality of its occupant, especially when that occupant lives a solitary life. Do you see any evidences of the activities that used to occupy your uncle?”

===Chinese===
“那倒正合我的预料，”桑代克说，“鸦片瘾会深刻地改变一个人的性情；而且，不知怎的，撇开陈设不谈，一间屋子总会以某种微妙却十分分明的方式，映照出居住者的为人，尤其当那居住者孑然独居的时候。令叔昔日所醉心的种种营生，你可看出什么痕迹？”

===Original===
“Not very much,” replied Stephen. “But the place may not be quite as he left it. I found one or two of his books on the table and put them back in the shelves, but I found no manuscript or notes such as he used to make. I noticed, too, that his ink-slab which he used to keep so scrupulously clean is covered with dry smears and that the stick of ink is all cracked at the end, as if he had not used it for months. It seems to point to a great change in his habits.”

===Chinese===
“不很多，”斯蒂芬答道，“不过这地方未必全是他离去时的原样。我在桌上发现了他一两本书，放回了架子上，可他惯常写的那种手稿或札记，却一页也没见着。我还注意到，他那方素来拭擦得一尘不染的砚台，如今糊满了干墨渍，那锭墨的墨头也龟裂斑驳，仿佛几个月不曾动用过。这似乎表明他的习性大变了。”

===Original===
“What used he to do with Chinese ink?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“他从前拿中国墨做什么用？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“He corresponded with some of his native friends in Japan, and he used to write in the Japanese character even if they understood English. That was what he chiefly used the Chinese ink for. But he also used to copy the inscriptions from these things.” Here Stephen lifted from the mantelpiece what looked like a fossil Bath bun, but was actually a clay tablet covered with minute indented writing.

===Chinese===
“他和日本几位当地朋友通信，哪怕对方通晓英文，他也惯用日文书写。中国墨主要是派这个用场的。不过他还惯常从这些东西上摹录铭文。”说到这里，斯蒂芬从壁炉架上拿起一件东西，看去像一只成了化石的巴斯圆面包（Bath bun），实则是一块泥板（clay tablet），上面布满细小的楔刻文字。

===Original===
“Your uncle could read the cuneiform character, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么，令叔认得楔形文字了？”

===Original===
“Yes; he was something of an expert. These tablets are, I believe, leases and other legal documents from Eridu and other Babylonian cities. He used to copy the inscriptions in the cuneiform writing and then translate them into English. But I mustn’t stay here any longer as I have an engagement for this evening. I just dropped in to get these two volumes﻿—*Thornton’s History of Babylonia*, which he once advised me to read. Shall I give you the key? You’d better have it and leave it with the porter as you go out.”

===Chinese===
“认得；他还算得上个行家呢。我相信这些泥板是埃利都（Eridu）等巴比伦城邦的租契和其他法律文书。他惯常先用楔形文字把铭文摹写下来，再译成英文。不过今晚我另有约会，不能在这儿多待了。我只是顺道来取这两卷书——《桑顿的巴比伦史》（Thornton’s History of Babylonia），他从前劝我读过。钥匙交给你们好吗？你们最好带着，出去时交给门房。”

===Original===
He shook hands with us and we walked out with him to the landing and stood watching him as he ran down the stairs. Glancing at Thorndyke by the light of the gas lamp on the landing, I thought I detected in his impassive face that almost imperceptible change of expression to which I have already alluded as indicating pleasure or satisfaction.

===Chinese===
他与我们握手道别，我们送他到楼梯平台上，站着目送他奔下楼梯去。借着平台上煤气灯的光，我瞟了桑代克一眼，觉得在他那张不动声色的脸上，又捕捉到了那种几不可察的表情变化——我先前已经提过，那是表示愉悦或满足的。

===Original===
“You are looking quite pleased with yourself,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“你瞧着可相当得意呢。”我说。

===Original===
“I am not displeased,” he replied calmly. “Autolycus has picked up a few crumbs; very small ones, but still crumbs. No doubt his learned junior has picked up a few likewise?”

===Chinese===
“谈不上不快，”他平静地答道，“奥托吕科斯捡到了几粒面包屑；屑是极小，终究是屑。想必他那位博学的后进也捡到了几粒吧？”

===Original===
I shook my head﻿—and inwardly suspected it of being rather a thick head.

===Chinese===
我摇了摇头——心里还暗自疑心，这颗脑袋未免太不开窍。

===Original===
“I did not perceive anything in the least degree significant in what Stephen was telling you,” said I. “It was all very interesting, but it did not seem to have any bearing on his uncle’s will.”

===Chinese===
“斯蒂芬方才讲的那些，我半点也没听出什么要紧的意思来，”我说，“都很有趣味，可似乎与他叔叔的遗嘱毫不相干。”

===Original===
“I was not referring only to what Stephen has told us, although that was, as you say, very interesting. While he was talking I was looking about the room, and I have seen a very strange thing. Let me show it to you.”

===Chinese===
“我指的并不单是斯蒂芬告诉我们的那些话，虽然那些话诚如你所说，很有趣味。他讲话的时候，我一直在四下打量这间屋子，而且看见了一件十分古怪的东西。待我指给你看。”

===Original===
He linked his arm in mine and, walking me back into the room, halted opposite the fireplace.

===Chinese===
他挽起我的手臂，把我带回屋里，在壁炉对面站定。

===Original===
“There,” said he, “look at that. It is a most remarkable object.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，”他说，“看那个。那可是件非同小可的东西。”

===Original===
I followed the direction of his gaze and saw an oblong frame enclosing a large photograph of an inscription in the weird and cabalistic arrowhead character. I looked at it in silence for some seconds and then, somewhat disappointed, remarked:

===Chinese===
我顺着他的目光望去，看见一个长方形相框，里面嵌着一幅放大的照片，照的是一篇铭文，用的正是那种古怪而如天书般的箭头文字。我默默端详了几秒钟，然后不无失望地说道：

===Original===
“I don’t see anything very remarkable in it, under the circumstances. In any ordinary room it would be, I admit; but Stephen has just told us that his uncle was something of an expert in cuneiform writing.”

===Chinese===
“就眼下的情形而论，我看不出它有什么非同小可之处。在随便哪间寻常屋子里，我承认它算得上；可斯蒂芬刚才告诉我们，他叔叔算是楔形文字的行家。”

===Original===
“Exactly,” said Thorndyke. “That is my point. That is what makes it so remarkable.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”桑代克说，“这正是我的着眼点。也正因此，它才如此非同小可。”

===Original===
“I don’t follow you at all,” said I. “That a man should hang upon his wall an inscription that is legible to him does not seem to me at all out of the way. It would be much more singular if he should hang up an inscription that he could *not* read.”

===Chinese===
“我完全跟不上你了，”我说，“一个人在自己墙上挂一幅自己认得的铭文，我看不出有任何反常之处。倘若他挂的是一幅自己*不*认得的铭文，那才蹊跷得多。”

===Original===
“No doubt,” replied Thorndyke. “But you will agree with me that it would be still more singular if a man should hang upon his wall an inscription that he *could* read﻿—and hang it upside down.”

===Chinese===
“那自然，”桑代克答道，“可是你会同意我的：倘若一个人在自己墙上挂一幅自己*认得*的铭文——却把它倒着挂——那才更加蹊跷。”

===Original===
I stared at Thorndyke in amazement.

===Chinese===
我惊愕地瞪着桑代克。

===Original===
“Do you mean to tell me,” I exclaimed, “that that photograph is really upside down?”

===Chinese===
“你是要告诉我，”我失声道，“那幅照片当真是倒挂着的？”

===Original===
“I do indeed,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“千真万确。”他答道。

===Original===
“But how do you know? Have we here yet another Oriental scholar?”

===Chinese===
“可你是怎么知道的？莫非我们这里又冒出一位东方学者？”

===Original===
Thorndyke chuckled. “Some fool,” he replied, “has said that ‘a little knowledge is a dangerous thing.’ Compared with much knowledge, it may be; but it is a vast deal better than no knowledge. Here is a case in point. I have read with very keen interest the wonderful history of the decipherment of the cuneiform writing, and I happen to recollect one or two of the main facts that seemed to me to be worth remembering. This particular inscription is in the Persian cuneiform, a much more simple and open form of the script than the Babylonian or Assyrian; in fact, I suspect that this is the famous inscription from the gateway at Persepolis﻿—the first to be deciphered; which would account for its presence here in a frame. Now this script consists, as you see, of two kinds of characters; the small, solid, acutely pointed characters which are known as wedges, and the larger, more obtuse characters, somewhat like our government broad arrows, and called arrowheads. The names are rather unfortunate, as both forms are wedge-like and both resemble arrowheads. The script reads from left to right, like our own writing, and unlike that of the Semitic peoples and the primitive Greeks; and the rule for the placing of the characters is that all the ‘wedges’ point to the right or downwards and the arrowhead forms are open towards the right. But if you look at this photograph you will see that all the wedges point upwards to the left and that the arrowhead characters are open towards the left. Obviously the photograph is upside down.”

===Chinese===
桑代克呵呵一笑。“有个蠢材，”他答道，“说过‘一知半解是件危险的事’。跟渊博相比，或许如此；可比一无所知又强出百倍。眼前正是一例。我怀着极浓的兴味读过楔形文字释读史的那段奇妙历程，其中一两个依我看值得记取的主要事实，恰好还记得。眼前这幅铭文，用的是波斯楔形文，一种比巴比伦文或亚述文简明疏朗得多的书体；实际上，我疑心这正是波斯波利斯（Persepolis）城门上那篇著名的铭文——最先被释读出来的那一篇；这也正好说明，它为何装了框挂在此处。这种文字，你瞧，由两类字符构成：一类细小、实心、锐角尖削，通称‘楔笔’（wedges）；另一类较大、较钝，略似我国政府器物上的宽箭标（broad arrow），称作‘箭头字’（arrowheads）。这两个名称起得都不大妥帖，因为两种形态其实都像楔子，也都像箭头。这种文字自左向右读，同我们自己的文字一样，而与闪米特各族及早期希腊人的文字相反；至于字符的布列规则，则是所有‘楔笔’一律朝右或朝下指，箭头形的字符一律向右开口。可你只要看看这幅照片，便会发现所有楔笔一律朝着左上方，箭头形字符一律向左开口。显然，这幅照片是倒挂的。”

===Original===
“But,” I exclaimed, “this is really most mysterious. What do you suppose can be the explanation?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我失声道，“这实在是玄之又玄了。你想可能会有什么解释？”

===Original===
“I think,” replied Thorndyke, “that we may perhaps get a suggestion from the back of the frame. Let us see.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”桑代克答道，“相框背面或许能给我们一点启发。且来看看。”

===Original===
He disengaged the frame from the two nails on which it hung, and, turning it round, glanced at the back; which he then presented for my inspection. A label on the backing paper bore the words, “J. Budge, Frame-maker and Gilder, 16, Gt. Anne Street, W.C.”

===Chinese===
他把相框从悬挂的两枚钉子上摘下，翻转过来，朝背面扫了一眼，随即递到我眼前查验。背板纸上贴着一张标签，写着“J. Budge, Frame-maker and Gilder, 16, Gt. Anne Street, W.C.”（J. 布杰，制框与镀金匠，大安妮街16号，西中区）。

===Original===
“Well?” I said, when I had read the label without gathering from it anything fresh.

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”我读罢标签，没从中读出什么新名堂，便问道。

===Original===
“The label, you observe, is the right way up as it hangs on the wall.”

===Chinese===
“你注意到没有，这框子挂在墙上时，标签是正的。”

===Original===
“So it is,” I rejoined hastily, a little annoyed that I had not been quicker to observe so obvious a fact. “I see your point. You mean that the frame-maker hung the thing upside down and Jeffrey never noticed the mistake?”

===Chinese===
“可不是，”我连忙应道，懊恼自己竟没更早点破这么明显的一层事实。“我明白你的意思了。你是说，制框匠把这东西装倒了，而杰弗里压根儿没觉察这个差错？”

===Original===
“That is a perfectly sound explanation,” said Thorndyke. “But I think there is something more. You will notice that the label is an old one; it must have been on some years, to judge by its dingy appearance, whereas the two mirror-plates look to me comparatively new. But we can soon put that matter to the test, for the label was evidently stuck on when the frame was new, and if the plates were screwed on at the same time, the wood that they cover will be clean and new-looking.”

===Chinese===
“这个解释完全站得住，”桑代克说，“不过我想还不止于此。你注意到没有，这张标签是张旧标签；瞧那暗旧的成色，贴上去总有些年头了，而那两片挂镜片（mirror-plates），我看倒是比较新的。不过这事很快就能验证：标签显然是相框簇新时贴上的，倘若挂片也是当时一并拧上去的，那么它们盖住的木头就该洁净如新。”

===Original===
He drew from his pocket a “combination” knife containing, among other implements, a screwdriver, with which he carefully extracted the screws from one of the little brass plates by which the frame had been suspended from the nails.

===Chinese===
他从口袋里掏出一柄“组合式”折刀（“combination” knife），各式家什里带着一把螺丝刀，他用它小心地旋出其中一枚小铜片上的螺丝——相框正是靠这铜片挂在钉子上的。

===Original===
“You see,” he said, when he had removed the plate and carried the photograph over to the gasjet, “the wood covered by the plate is as dirty and time-stained as the rest of the frame. The plates have been put on recently.”

===Chinese===
“你瞧，”他卸下铜片，把照片端到煤气灯口下，说道，“铜片盖住的木头，跟相框其余地方一样又脏又陈。这些挂片是近来才装上去的。”

===Original===
“And what are we to infer from that?”

===Chinese===
“由此我们又该推断出什么？”

===Original===
“Well, since there are no other marks of plates or rings upon the frame, we may safely infer that the photograph was never hung up until it came to these rooms.”

===Chinese===
“唔，框子上既没有旁的挂片或挂环的痕迹，我们尽可稳稳当当地推断：这幅照片在搬进这几间屋子之前，从未挂起过。”

===Original===
“Yes, I suppose we may. But what then? What inference does that lead to?”

===Chinese===
“是，我想可以这么说。可接下来呢？这又引出什么推论？”

===Original===
Thorndyke reflected for a few moments and I continued:

===Chinese===
桑代克沉吟了片刻，我接着说道：

===Original===
“It is evident that this photograph suggests more to you than it does to me. I should like to hear your exposition of its bearing on the case, if it has any.”

===Chinese===
“显然，这幅照片在你眼里暗示的东西比在我眼里多。它若与本案真有什么关涉，我倒愿听听你的阐说。”

===Original===
“Whether or no it has any real bearing on the case,” Thorndyke answered, “it is impossible for me to say at this stage. I told you that I had proposed to myself one or two hypotheses to account for and explain Jeffrey Blackmore’s will, and I may say that the curious misplacement of this photograph fits more than one of them. I won’t say more than that, because I think it would be profitable to you to work at this case independently. You have all the facts that I have and you shall have a copy of my notes of Marchmont’s statement of the case. With this material you ought to be able to reach some conclusion. Of course neither of us may be able to make anything of the case﻿—it doesn’t look very hopeful at present﻿—but whatever happens, we can compare notes after the event and you will be the richer by so much experience of actual investigation. But I will start you off with one hint, which is this: that neither you nor Marchmont seem to appreciate in the least the very extraordinary nature of the facts that he communicated to us.”

===Chinese===
“它究竟与本案有无真正的关涉，”桑代克答道，“眼下这个阶段我还说不上。我告诉过你，我已给自己提出了一两个假说，用以说明和解释杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的遗嘱；可以说，这幅照片古怪的倒挂，与不止一个假说都合得上。其余的我不再多讲，因为我觉得，让你独立钻研这桩案子，于你大有裨益。你已握有我掌握的全部事实，马奇蒙特案情陈述的笔记，你也将得到一份抄件。凭着这些材料，你理应能得出某种结论。当然，你我二人也可能谁都奈何不了这桩案子——眼下看去实在不怎么乐观——但无论结果如何，事后我们可以互对笔记，而你也将凭这许多实际查案的历练，多添一份身家。不过，我愿送你一个提示起步，那就是：对他传达给我们的那些事实，你也好，马奇蒙特也好，似乎都丝毫没有领会其何等异乎寻常。”

===Original===
“I thought Marchmont seemed pretty much alive to the fact that it was a very queer will.”

===Chinese===
“我还以为，对这是一份十分古怪的遗嘱这一点，马奇蒙特够警觉的了。”

===Original===
“So he did,” agreed Thorndyke. “But that is not quite what I mean. The whole set of circumstances, taken together and in relation to one another, impressed me as most remarkable; and that is why I am giving so much attention to what looks at first sight like such a very unpromising case. Copy out my notes, Jervis, and examine the facts critically. I think you will see what I mean. And now let us proceed.”

===Chinese===
“他确是警觉的，”桑代克表示同意，“可我的意思不尽在此。全部情形，合起来看、并就其相互关系而观，给我的印象是极不寻常；正因如此，我才对这桩乍看毫无指望的案子下这么大的功夫。杰维斯，把我的笔记抄一份，将那些事实严加审视。我想你会明白我的意思的。现在我们继续吧。”

===Original===
He replaced the brass plate and having reinserted the screws, hung up the frame, and proceeded to browse slowly round the room, stopping now and again to inspect the Japanese colour-prints and framed photographs of buildings and other objects of archaeological interest that formed the only attempts at wall-decoration. To one of the former he drew my attention.

===Chinese===
他把铜片装回原处，重新拧上螺丝，挂好相框，然后开始在屋里缓缓巡览，不时停下脚步，端详那些日本彩印版画和装框的照片——拍的是建筑物及其他考古趣味之物——它们便是这屋里仅有的壁面装饰了。他把我的注意力引到其中一幅版画上。

===Original===
“These things are of some value,” he remarked. “Here is one by Utamaro﻿—that little circle with the mark over it is his signature﻿—and you notice that the paper is becoming spotted in places with mildew. The fact is worth noting in more than one connection.”

===Chinese===
“这些东西有些价值，”他说，“这一幅是喜多川歌麿（Utamaro）的手笔——圈上带着花押记号的那只小圆圈，便是他的署名——你再留意，纸面已开始东一点西一点地生出霉斑。这一事实值得从不止一个方面记取。”

===Original===
I accordingly made a mental note and the perambulation continued.

===Chinese===
我便在心里记下这一条，巡览继续进行。

===Original===
“You observe that Jeffrey used a gas-stove, instead of a coal fire, no doubt to economize work, but perhaps for other reasons. Presumably he cooked by gas, too; let us see.”

===Chinese===
“你注意到，杰弗里用的是煤气炉，而不是煤炉，无疑是为省事，但或许另有缘故。想必他做饭也用煤气；我们去瞧瞧。”

===Original===
We wandered into the little cupboard-like kitchen and glanced round. A ring-burner on a shelf, a kettle, a frying-pan and a few pieces of crockery were its sole appointments. Apparently the porter was correct in his statement as to Jeffrey’s habits.

===Chinese===
我们踱进那间橱柜般的小厨房，环视一周。架上一只环形煤气燃烧嘴，一把水壶，一口煎锅，还有几件陶器餐具，便是它的全部陈设。看来门房关于杰弗里起居习性的说法不假。

===Original===
Returning to the sitting-room, Thorndyke resumed his inspection, pulling out the table drawers, peering inquisitively into cupboards and bestowing a passing glance on each of the comparatively few objects that the comfortless room contained.

===Chinese===
回到起居室，桑代克重新查勘起来：拉开桌子的抽屉，探头探脑地朝橱柜里张望，对这间凄凉的屋子里为数不多的几件器物，逐一流目一瞥。

===Original===
“I have never seen a more characterless apartment,” was his final comment. “There is nothing that seems to suggest any kind of habitual activity on the part of the occupant. Let us look at the bedroom.”

===Chinese===
“我还从没见过比这更没有个性的屋子，”他末了评论道，“这里没有一样东西，看去能叫人想见居住者有任何惯常的营生。我们看看卧室去吧。”

===Original===
We passed through into the chamber of tragic memories, and, when Thorndyke had lit the gas, we stood awhile looking about us in silence. It was a bare, comfortless room, dirty, neglected and squalid. The bed appeared not to have been remade since the catastrophe, for an indentation still marked the place where the corpse had lain, and even a slight powdering of ash could still be seen on the shabby counterpane. It looked to me a typical opium-smoker’s bedroom.

===Chinese===
我们穿过去，进了那间留存着惨痛记忆的卧室。桑代克点亮煤气灯后，我们默默地站了一会儿，四下环顾。这是一间空落、凄凉的屋子，肮脏、荒疏、寒碜。那张床自出事以来似乎不曾重新铺过，因为尸体躺过的地方仍留着一道凹痕，破旧的床罩上甚至还能看见一层薄薄的灰。在我眼里，这是间典型的鸦片烟客的卧室。

===Original===
“Well,” Thorndyke remarked at length, “there is character enough here﻿—of a kind. Jeffrey Blackmore would seem to have been a man of few needs. One could hardly imagine a bedroom in which less attention seemed to have been given to the comfort of the occupant.”

===Chinese===
“唔，”桑代克终于开口道，“这里的个性倒足得很——只是别具一格。杰弗里·布莱克莫尔看去是个欲求无几的人。一间卧室，对居住者的舒适照应得比如此更少的，恐怕叫人难以想象。”

===Original===
He looked about him keenly and continued: “The syringe and the rest of the lethal appliances and material have been taken away, I see. Probably the analyst did not return them. But there are the opium-pipe and the jar and the ash-bowl, and I presume those are the clothes that the undertakers removed from the body. Shall we look them over?”

===Chinese===
他目光锐利地四下扫视，继续说道：“注射器和其余那些致人死命的器具与物件，都已取走了，我看。多半是化验的人没有送还。不过鸦片烟枪、罐子和灰碗都还在，而我想，那边就是殡仪人员从尸体上除下来的衣裳。我们翻检一下如何？”

===Original===
He took up the clothes which lay, roughly folded, on a chair and held them up, garment by garment.

===Chinese===
他拿起胡乱叠放在椅上的衣裳，一件一件地提起来察看。

===Original===
“These are evidently the trousers,” he remarked, spreading them out on the bed. “Here is a little white spot on the middle of the thigh which looks like a patch of small crystals from a drop of the solution. Just light the lamp, Jervis, and let us examine it with a lens.”

===Chinese===
“这分明是那条裤子，”他说着，把它在床上摊开。大腿中段有一个小白点，看去像一滴溶液干后留下的一小片细晶。杰维斯，把灯点上，我们用放大镜查一查。”

===Original===
I lit the lamp, and when we had examined the spot minutely and identified it as a mass of minute crystals, Thorndyke asked:

===Chinese===
我点亮了灯。我们把那斑点细细查验一番，确认那是一小片细密的晶体之后，桑代克问道：

===Original===
“What do you make of those creases? You see there is one on each leg.”

===Chinese===
“那几道折痕，你怎么看？你瞧，每条裤腿上各有一道。”

===Original===
“It looks as if the trousers had been turned up. But if they have been they must have been turned up about seven inches. Poor Jeffrey couldn’t have had much regard for appearances, for they would have been right above his socks. But perhaps the creases were made in undressing the body.”

===Chinese===
“看样子这裤子曾被卷起过裤脚。可若真卷过，卷的幅度总得有七英寸上下。可怜的杰弗里未免太不顾体面，因为卷边正顶着他的袜口。不过，也许折痕是给尸体脱衣时弄出来的。”

===Original===
“That is possible,” said Thorndyke: “though I don’t quite see how it would have happened. I notice that his pockets seem to have been emptied﻿—no, wait; here is something in the waistcoat pocket.”

===Chinese===
“那也有可能，”桑代克说，“不过我不大明白这怎么会发生。我注意到他的口袋似乎都被掏空了——不，慢着；背心口袋里还有东西。”

===Original===
He drew out a shabby, pigskin card-case and a stump of lead pencil, at which latter he looked with what seemed to me much more interest than was deserved by so commonplace an object.

===Chinese===
他掏出一只寒碜的猪皮名片盒和一截铅笔头。对后者，他端详时的那副兴致，在我看去，远非这么一件平平无奇的东西所配消受。

===Original===
“The cards, you observe,” said he, “are printed from type, not from a plate. I would note that fact. And tell me what you make of that.”

===Chinese===
“这些名片，你注意到没有，”他说，“是铅字排印的，不是制版印的。这个事实我要记下。再告诉我，你从那件东西上看出了什么。”

===Original===
He handed me the pencil, which I examined with concentrated attention, helping myself even with the lamp and my pocket lens. But even with these aids I failed to discover anything unusual in its appearance. Thorndyke watched me with a mischievous smile, and, when I had finished, inquired:

===Chinese===
他把铅笔递给我。我凝神细察，连灯和随身放大镜都用上了。可即便有这些助力，也没能从它的模样里发现任何异样。桑代克挂着促狭的笑看着我，待我查完，问道：

===Original===
“Well; what is it?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样；是什么？”

===Original===
“Confound you!” I exclaimed. “It’s a pencil. Any fool can see that, and this particular fool can’t see any more. It’s a wretched stump of a pencil, villainously cut to an abominably bad point. It is coloured dark red on the outside and was stamped with some name that began with C﻿—O﻿—Cooperative Stores, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“该死的！”我嚷道，“这是支铅笔。随便哪个傻瓜都看得出来，而本傻瓜再看也看不出别的名堂了。一截可怜巴巴的铅笔头，笔尖削得惨不忍睹。外皮染成深红色，上头压印过一个以 C—O 开头的名字——兴许是合作商店（Cooperative Stores）的货色。”

===Original===
“Now, my dear Jervis,” Thorndyke protested, “don’t begin by confusing speculation with fact. The letters which remain are C﻿—O. Note that fact and find out what pencils there are which have inscriptions beginning with those letters. I am not going to help you, because you can easily do this for yourself. And it will be good discipline even if the fact turns out to mean nothing.”

===Chinese===
“得啦，亲爱的杰维斯，”桑代克抗议道，“别一上来就把臆测和事实搅在一处。留下的字母是 C—O。记下这个事实，再去查明有哪些铅笔，其铭文以这两个字母开头。我可不打算帮你，因为这事你自己轻而易举就能办到。纵然这事实到头来毫无意义，这对你也是一番好操练。”

===Original===
At this moment he stepped back suddenly, and, looking down at the floor, said:

===Chinese===
就在这当口，他猛地退了一步，低头望着地板，说道：

===Original===
“Give me the lamp, Jervis, I’ve trodden on something that felt like glass.”

===Chinese===
“把灯给我，杰维斯，我踩着一样什么东西，脚感像玻璃。”

===Original===
I brought the lamp to the place where he had been standing, close by the bed, and we both knelt on the floor, throwing the light of the lamp on the bare and dusty boards. Under the bed, just within reach of the foot of a person standing close by, was a little patch of fragments of glass. Thorndyke produced a piece of paper from his pocket and delicately swept the little fragments on to it, remarking:

===Chinese===
我把灯拿到他方才站的地方——床边近旁。我们双双跪在地板上，把灯光投向那裸露而积尘的木板。床底下、恰在一个紧挨床边站立之人伸脚可及之处，有一小摊碎玻璃。桑代克从口袋里掏出一张纸，把那些碎屑轻手轻脚地扫拢上去，一面说道：

===Original===
“By the look of things, I am not the first person who has trodden on that object, whatever it is. Do you mind holding the lamp while I inspect the remains?”

===Chinese===
“看这光景，在这件东西——管它是什么——上头踩上一脚的，我还不是头一个。劳驾替我举着灯，我来看看这些残骸。”

===Original===
I took the lamp and held it over the paper while he examined the little heap of glass through his lens.

===Chinese===
我接过灯，举在纸上方，他用放大镜检视那一小堆玻璃。

===Original===
“Well,” I asked. “What have you found?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，”我问，“发现了什么？”

===Original===
“That is what I am asking myself,” he replied. “As far as I can judge by the appearance of these fragments, they appear to be portions of a small watch-glass. I wish there were some larger pieces.”

===Chinese===
“这正是我在问自己的，”他答道，“就这些碎片的外观判断，它们像是一只小表玻璃（watch-glass）的残片。可惜没有更大块的。”

===Original===
“Perhaps there are,” said I. “Let us look about the floor under the bed.”

===Chinese===
“说不定有，”我说，“我们在床底下的地板上找找看。”

===Original===
We resumed our groping about the dirty floor, throwing the light of the lamp on one spot after another. Presently, as we moved the lamp about, its light fell on a small glass bead, which I instantly picked up and exhibited to Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
我们继续在龌龊的地板上摸索，把灯光一处接一处地照过去。不多时，随着我们移动灯，灯光落在一粒小小的玻璃珠上，我立刻把它拾起，呈给桑代克看。

===Original===
“Is this of any interest to you?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这个你可感兴趣？”我问。

===Original===
Thorndyke took the bead and examined it curiously.

===Chinese===
桑代克接过玻璃珠，好奇地察看。

===Original===
“It is certainly,” he said, “a very odd thing to find in the bedroom of an old bachelor like Jeffrey, especially as we know that he employed no woman to look after his rooms. Of course, it may be a relic of the last tenant. Let us see if there are any more.”

===Chinese===
“这东西，”他说，“出现在杰弗里这样的老单身汉的卧室里，实在是桩很蹊跷的事，尤其我们已知他并没雇女人打理屋子。当然，它也可能是上一房客的遗物。且看看还有没有。”

===Original===
We renewed our search, crawling under the bed and throwing the light of the lamp in all directions over the floor. The result was the discovery of three more beads, one entire bugle and the crushed remains of another, which had apparently been trodden on. All of these, including the fragments of the bugle that had been crushed, Thorndyke placed carefully on the paper, which he laid on the dressing-table the more conveniently to examine our find.

===Chinese===
我们重新搜查起来，钻到床底下，把灯光朝地板四面八方地照。结果又发现了三粒珠子，一枚完好的管珠（bugle）和另一枚显然被踩碎的管珠残骸。所有这些，连同那枚被踩碎的管珠的碎片，桑代克都小心地放到纸上，再把纸搁在梳妆台上，以便更顺手地查验我们的收获。

===Original===
“I am sorry,” said he, “that there are no more fragments of the watch-glass, or whatever it was. The broken pieces were evidently picked up, with the exception of the one that I trod on, which was an isolated fragment that had been overlooked. As to the beads, judging by their number and the position in which we found some of them﻿—that crushed bugle, for instance﻿—they must have been dropped during Jeffrey’s tenancy and probably quite recently.”

===Chinese===
“可惜，”他说，“表玻璃——或不管它原是什么——的碎片没有更多了。那些碎块显然被人捡拾过，只有我踩着的那一块是漏网的孤片。至于这些珠子，从它们的数目和我们发现其中几粒时的位置判断——譬如那枚被踩碎的管珠——它们必定是在杰弗里租住期间落下的，而且多半就在不久之前。”

===Original===
“What sort of garment do you suppose they came from?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你猜它们是从什么样的衣物上掉下来的？”我问。

===Original===
“They may have been part of a beaded veil or the trimming of a dress, but the grouping rather suggests to me a tag of bead fringe. The colour is rather unusual.”

===Chinese===
“它们可能是一幅珠饰面纱的一部分，或是一条衣裙上的缀饰，不过这堆珠子的分布格局，倒更使我想起一绺珠穗。这颜色也颇不寻常。”

===Original===
“I thought they looked like black beads.”

===Chinese===
“我看它们像黑珠子。”

===Original===
“So they do by this light, but I think that by daylight we shall find them to be a dark, reddish-brown. You can see the colour now if you look at the smaller fragments of the one that is crushed.”

===Chinese===
“在这灯光下确是如此，不过我想，到天亮再看，就会发觉它们是深红褐色的。你此刻细看那枚被踩碎的珠子的小碎片，便能辨出颜色来。”

===Original===
He handed me his lens, and, when I had verified his statement, he produced from his pocket a small tin box with a closely-fitting lid in which he deposited the paper, having first folded it up into a small parcel.

===Chinese===
他把放大镜递给我。待我证实了他的说法，他又从口袋里取出一只盖口严实的白铁小盒，先把那张纸折成一个小包，放了进去。

===Original===
“We will put the pencil in too,” said he; and, as he returned the box to his pocket he added: “you had better get one of these little boxes from Polton. It is often useful to have a safe receptacle for small and fragile articles.”

===Chinese===
“把铅笔也放进去，”他说；把盒子放回口袋时，又补了一句：“你最好也找波尔顿要一只这样的小盒。有个安稳妥帖的容器盛放细小易碎的东西，常常大有用处。”

===Original===
He folded up and replaced the dead man’s clothes as we had found them. Then, observing a pair of shoes standing by the wall, he picked them up and looked them over thoughtfully, paying special attention to the backs of the soles and the fronts of the heels.

===Chinese===
他把死者的衣裳照原样折好放回。随后，瞥见墙边立着一双鞋，他便拿起来细细察看，格外留意鞋底的后端和鞋跟的前面。

===Original===
“I suppose we may take it,” said he, “that these are the shoes that poor Jeffrey wore on the night of his death. At any rate there seem to be no others. He seems to have been a fairly clean walker. The streets were shockingly dirty that day, as I remember most distinctly. Do you see any slippers? I haven’t noticed any.”

===Chinese===
“我想我们可以认定，”他说，“这就是可怜的杰弗里死的那晚脚上穿的那双鞋。至少，别的鞋一双也没见着。他走路似乎相当爱干净。那天街道脏得吓人，这一点我记得再清楚不过。你见到拖鞋了吗？我一只也没留意到。”

===Original===
He opened and peeped into a cupboard in which an overcoat surmounted by a felt hat hung from a peg like an attenuated suicide; he looked in all the corners and into the sitting-room, but no slippers were to be seen.

===Chinese===
他打开一只小橱朝里张了张——橱里一件大衣顶着一顶毡帽，挂在挂钩上，活像一个瘦长条的自缢者；他又查看各个角落，还看了起居室，可拖鞋仍不见踪影。

===Original===
“Our friend seems to have had surprisingly little regard for comfort,” Thorndyke remarked. “Think of spending the winter evenings in damp boots by a gas fire!”

===Chinese===
“我们这位朋友对舒适之无所顾忌，实在出人意料，”桑代克说，“想想看，穿着潮湿的靴子，守着煤气炉火消磨冬夜！”

===Original===
“Perhaps the opium-pipe compensated,” said I; “or he may have gone to bed early.”

===Chinese===
“也许鸦片烟枪补偿了他，”我说，“再不，他也许睡得早。”

===Original===
“But he did not. The night porter used to see the light in his rooms at one o’clock in the morning. In the sitting-room, too, you remember. But he seems to have been in the habit of reading in bed﻿—or perhaps smoking﻿—for here is a candlestick with the remains of a whole dynasty of candles in it. As there is gas in the room, he couldn’t have wanted the candle to undress by. He used stearine candles, too; not the common paraffin variety. I wonder why he went to that expense.”

===Chinese===
“可他并没有。夜班门房过去常在凌晨一点钟望见他屋里的灯光。而且是在起居室里，这你还记得。不过他似乎惯于在床上读书——或者抽烟——因为这里有一支烛台，里头积着一朝接一朝蜡烛的残骸。屋里既有煤气灯，他总不会是脱衣就寝时用得着蜡烛。他用的还是硬脂蜡烛（stearine candles），不是寻常的石蜡货。我纳罕他为什么肯花这份钱。”

===Original===
“Perhaps the smell of the paraffin candle spoiled the aroma of the opium,” I suggested; to which Thorndyke made no reply but continued his inspection of the room, pulling out the drawer of the washstand﻿—which contained a single, worn-out nailbrush﻿—and even picking up and examining the dry and cracked cake of soap in the dish.

===Chinese===
“也许是石蜡烛的气味败了鸦片的香韵，”我猜道；对此桑代克未置一词，只顾继续查勘这间屋子：拉开洗脸架的抽屉——里面只有一把用秃了的指甲刷——甚至拿起碟子里那块干裂的皂块，端详了一番。

===Original===
“He seems to have had a fair amount of clothing,” said Thorndyke, who was now going through the chest of drawers, “though, by the look of it, he didn’t change very often, and the shirts have a rather yellow and faded appearance. I wonder how he managed about his washing. Why, here are a couple of pairs of boots in the drawer with his clothes! And here is his stock of candles. Quite a large box﻿—though nearly empty now﻿—of stearine candles, six to the pound.”

===Chinese===
“他的衣裳似乎颇为不少，”正在翻检五斗柜的桑代克说，“不过看这光景，他不常更换，衬衫都泛黄发旧了。我一直纳罕他的换洗衣服是怎么打发的。咦，抽屉里他的衣裳中间竟有两双靴子！这儿还有他存的蜡烛。好大一盒硬脂蜡烛——如今快空了——一磅六支的那种。”

===Original===
He closed the drawer and cast another inquiring look round the room.

===Chinese===
他关上抽屉，又用探询的目光把屋子环视了一遍。

===Original===
“I think we have seen all now, Jervis,” he said, “unless there is anything more that you would like to look into?”

===Chinese===
“我想现在都看遍了，杰维斯，”他说，“除非你还有什么想再查究的？”

===Original===
“No,” I replied. “I have seen all that I wanted to see and more than I am able to attach any meaning to. So we may as well go.”

===Chinese===
“没有了，”我答道，“想看的都看遍了，还多看出不少我没法赋予意义的东西。那我们就走吧。”

===Original===
I blew out the lamp and put it in my overcoat pocket, and, when we had turned out the gas in both rooms, we took our departure.

===Chinese===
我吹熄灯，把它放进大衣口袋，待两间屋子的煤气都关熄之后，我们便动身离开。

===Original===
As we approached the lodge, we found our stout friend in the act of retiring in favour of the night porter. Thorndyke handed him the key of the chambers, and, after a few sympathetic inquiries, about his health﻿—which was obviously very indifferent﻿—said:

===Chinese===
走近门房时，我们发觉我们那位胖子朋友正要下班，把位子让给夜班门房。桑代克把寓所钥匙交还给他，又体恤地问了几句他的健康——那健康分明很不济——然后说道：

===Original===
“Let me see; you were one of the witnesses to Mr. Blackmore’s will, I think?”

===Chinese===
“我想想；布莱克莫尔先生遗嘱的见证人，您是一位吧？”

===Original===
“I was, sir,” replied the porter.

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。”门房答道。

===Original===
“And I believe you read the document through before you witnessed the signature?”

===Chinese===
“而且我想，您在见证签名之前，把那份文件通读了一遍吧？”

===Original===
“I did, sir.”

===Chinese===
“读了，先生。”

===Original===
“Did you read it aloud?”

===Chinese===
“您是念出声的吗？”

===Original===
“Aloud, sir! Lor’ bless you, no, sir! Why should I? The other witness read it, and, of course, Mr. Blackmore knew what was in it, seeing that it was in his own handwriting. What should I want to read it aloud for?”

===Chinese===
“念出声，先生！老天爷，才不呢，先生！干吗要念？另一位见证人念过了，况且布莱克莫尔先生自然知道里面写的什么，那遗嘱本来就是他亲笔写的嘛。我要念出声做什么？”

===Original===
“No, of course you wouldn’t want to. By the way, I have been wondering how Mr. Blackmore managed about his washing.”

===Chinese===
“自然，您当然用不着念。对了，我一直纳闷，布莱克莫尔先生的换洗衣服是怎么打发的。”

===Original===
The porter evidently regarded this question with some disfavour, for he replied only with an interrogative grunt. It was, in fact, rather an odd question.

===Chinese===
门房显然不大乐意这个问题，因为他只回了一声狐疑的哼哼。说实话，这问题也确实有点古怪。

===Original===
“Did you get it done for him,” Thorndyke pursued.

===Chinese===
“洗衣的事，是他托您经手的吗？”桑代克追问。

===Original===
“No, certainly not, sir. He got it done for himself. The laundry people used to deliver the basket here at the lodge, and Mr. Blackmore used to take it in with him when he happened to be passing.”

===Chinese===
“不，当然不是，先生。那是他自己打理的。洗衣店的人从前常把篮子送到门房这儿，布莱克莫尔先生赶上路过，就自己捎进去。”

===Original===
“It was not delivered at his chambers, then?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，洗衣店不往他寓所送？”

===Original===
“No, sir. Mr. Blackmore was a very studious gentleman and he didn’t like to be disturbed. A studious gentleman would naturally not like to be disturbed.”

===Chinese===
“不送，先生。布莱克莫尔先生是位非常好学的绅士，不爱受人搅扰。好学的绅士嘛，自然不爱受人搅扰。”

===Original===
Thorndyke cordially agreed with these very proper sentiments and finally wished the porter “good night.” We passed out through the gateway into Wych Street, and, turning our faces eastward towards the Temple, set forth in silence, each thinking his own thoughts. What Thorndyke’s were I cannot tell, though I have no doubt that he was busily engaged in piecing together all that he had seen and heard and considering its possible application to the case in hand.

===Chinese===
桑代克对这番十分得体的见识热诚表示赞同，末了向门房道了“晚安”。我们穿过大门洞走进维奇街，掉头朝东往坦普尔而去，一路无言，各想各的心事。桑代克想的是什么，我说不上，不过我毫不怀疑，他正忙着把所见所闻缀合成片，思量它们对于手头这桩案子可能的用处。

===Original===
As to me, my mind was in a whirl of confusion. All this searching and examining seemed to be the mere flogging of a dead horse. The will was obviously a perfectly valid and regular will and there was an end of the matter. At least, so it seemed to me. But clearly that was not Thorndyke’s view. His investigations were certainly not purposeless; and, as I walked by his side trying to conceive some purpose in his actions, I only became more and more mystified as I recalled them one by one, and perhaps most of all by the cryptic questions that I had just heard him address to the equally mystified porter.

===Chinese===
至于我，脑子里乱作了一团。这一番搜检查看，在我看来不过是鞭打死马、徒劳无功。那份遗嘱分明是完全有效、完全合乎规矩的遗嘱，事情到此也就了结了。至少，我看来如此。可这显然不是桑代克的看法。他的种种查勘绝非无所为而为；而我走在他身边，力图构想出他这些举动背后的目的，却只是随着一件件回想起来，愈发如坠五里雾中——而最教人如坠云雾的，或许还是我方才听见他向那位同样一头雾水的门房提出的那些玄乎其玄的问题。

## IX The House of Mystery / 神秘之宅

===Original===
Half-past nine on the following morning found us spinning along the Albert Embankment in a hansom to the pleasant tinkle of the horse’s bell. Thorndyke appeared to be in high spirits, though the full enjoyment of the matutinal pipe precluded fluent conversation. As a precaution, he had put my notebook in his pocket before starting, and once or twice he took it out and looked over its pages; but he made no reference to the object of our quest, and the few remarks that he uttered would have indicated that his thoughts were occupied with other matters.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨九点半，我们乘着一辆双轮马车，沿着阿尔伯特堤岸轻快前行，马铃声叮当作响，煞是悦耳。桑代克看来兴致颇高，只是正尽兴享受着晨间那一斗烟，不便畅谈。为谨慎起见，出发前他已把我的笔记本揣进自己口袋，途中还取出来翻看过一两回；但他绝口不提我们此行的目标，偶尔冒出的几句闲话，倒像他的心思全在别的什么事上。

===Original===
Arrived at Vauxhall Station, we alighted and forthwith made our way to the bridge that spans Upper Kennington Lane near its junction with Harleyford Road.

===Chinese===
到了沃克斯霍尔车站，我们下车，径直朝那座凌跨上肯宁顿巷的桥走去——桥的位置，就在该巷与哈利福德路（Harleyford Road）交汇处附近。

===Original===
“Here is our starting point,” said Thorndyke. “From this place to the house is about three hundred yards﻿—say four hundred and twenty paces﻿—and at about two hundred paces we ought to reach our patch of new road-metal. Now, are you ready? If we keep step we shall average our stride.”

===Chinese===
“这就是我们的出发点，”桑代克说，“从这儿到那座宅子约三百码——就算四百二十步吧——走到约两百步处，就该踏上那段新铺的碎石路面。好了，准备好了吗？只要步调一致，咱俩的步长便可取个平均。”

===Original===
We started together at a good pace, stepping out with military regularity and counting aloud as we went. As we told out the hundred and ninety-fourth pace I observed Thorndyke nod towards the roadway a little ahead, and, looking at it attentively as we approached, it was easy to see by the regularity of surface and lighter colour, that it had recently been re-metalled.

===Chinese===
我们一齐迈步，速度不慢，踏着军人般的匀整节奏，边走边高声报数。数到第一百九十四步时，我见桑代克朝前方不远处的路面点了点头；待走近细看，那路面平整匀净、颜色较浅，一望便知是新近重铺过碎石的。

===Original===
Having counted out the four hundred and twenty paces, we halted, and Thorndyke turned to me with a smile of triumph.

===Chinese===
数满四百二十步，我们停了下来，桑代克带着得胜的微笑转向我。

===Original===
“Not a bad estimate, Jervis,” said he. “That will be your house if I am not much mistaken. There is no other mews or private roadway in sight.”

===Chinese===
“估得不坏，杰维斯，”他说，“要是我没大看错，那就是你那座宅子了。视野之内，再没有别的马厩巷或私家车道了。”

===Original===
He pointed to a narrow turning some dozen yards ahead, apparently the entrance to a mews or yard and closed by a pair of massive wooden gates.

===Chinese===
他指了指前方十几码外一处狭窄的拐口，看去像是某条马厩巷或院子的入口，两扇厚实的木门把它封得严严实实。

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered, “there can be no doubt that this is the place; but, by Jove!” I added, as we drew nearer, “the nest is empty! Do you see?”

===Chinese===
“对，”我答道，“准是这地方，毫无疑问；可是，天哪！”待走近了些我又补上一句，“巢已经空了！你看见了吗？”

===Original===
I pointed to a bill that was stuck on the gate, bearing, as I could see at this distance, the inscription “To Let.”

===Chinese===
我指了指贴在门上的一张招贴；隔着这么远，上面的字也认得出，写的是“招租”。

===Original===
“Here is a new and startling, if not altogether unexpected, development,” said Thorndyke, as we stood gazing at the bill; which set forth that “these premises, including stabling and workshops,” were “to be let on lease or otherwise,” and referred inquiries to Messrs. Ryebody Brothers, house-agents and valuers, Upper Kennington Lane. “The question is, should we make a few inquiries of the agent, or should we get the keys and have a look at the inside of the house? I am inclined to do both, and the latter first, if Messrs. Ryebody Brothers will trust us with the keys.”

===Chinese===
“事情有了新发展，虽算不上全然意外，却够惊人的，”桑代克说。我们站在那儿盯着那张招贴；上面写明“本处房产，连同马厩与作坊”“出租，订立租约或另议均可”，垂询请洽上肯宁顿巷的房地产代理兼估价行赖博迪兄弟公司（Messrs. Ryebody Brothers）。“问题是，我们是该向代理人打探几句，还是取了钥匙，进宅子里看一看？两样我都想做，而且先做后一样——只要赖博迪兄弟公司肯把钥匙放心交到我们手里。”

===Original===
We proceeded up the lane to the address given, and, entering the office, Thorndyke made his request﻿—somewhat to the surprise of the clerk; for Thorndyke was not quite the kind of person whom one naturally associates with stabling and workshops. However, there was no difficulty, but as the clerk sorted out the keys from a bunch hanging from a hook, he remarked:

===Chinese===
我们沿巷而上，来到招贴上给出的地址，走进事务所。桑代克提出了请求——这多少让那位文书感到意外；因为桑代克怎么看，也不像那种天然会同马厩和作坊打交道的角色。不过事情并无难处，只是当文书从挂在钩子上的一串钥匙里往外挑拣时，他说道：

===Original===
“I expect you will find the place in a rather dirty and neglected condition. The house has not been cleaned yet; it is just as it was left when the brokers took away the furniture.”

===Chinese===
“我看你们会觉得那地方又脏又荒。宅子还没打扫过；承揽人把家具搬走时是什么样，眼下就还是什么样。”

===Original===
“Was the last tenant sold up, then?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“那么，上一任房客是破了产、被查抄变卖家当了吗？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Oh, no. He had to leave rather unexpectedly to take up some business in Germany.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那倒没有。他走得相当仓促，说是去德国料理一桩生意。”

===Original===
“I hope he paid his rent,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“但愿他把房租付清了，”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Oh, yes. Trust us for that. But I should say that Mr. Weiss﻿—that was his name﻿—was a man of some means. He seemed to have plenty of money, though he always paid in notes. I don’t fancy he had a banking account in this country. He hadn’t been here more than about six or seven months and I imagine he didn’t know many people in England, as he paid us a cash deposit in lieu of references when he first came.”

===Chinese===
“哦，付清了。这上头您尽管信得过我们。不过要我说，韦斯先生——他姓这个——是个有些家底的人。他瞧着手头宽裕，只是付款向来用钞票。我看他在这个国家没有银行账户。他来这儿前后不过六七个月，我想他在英国也没几个相识，因为头一回来，他就付给我们一笔现金押金，顶了保荐之用。”

===Original===
“I think you said his name was Weiss. It wouldn’t be H. Weiss by any chance?”

===Chinese===
“我记得你说他姓韦斯。不会恰巧就是 H. 韦斯吧？”

===Original===
“I believe it was. But I can soon tell you.” He opened a drawer and consulted what looked like a book of receipt forms. “Yes; H Weiss. Do you know him, sir?”

===Chinese===
“我记得是。不过我马上就能告诉您。”他拉开一个抽屉，翻检一本看去像是收据簿的东西。“没错；H. 韦斯。先生认得他？”

===Original===
“I knew a Mr. H. Weiss some years ago. He came from Bremen, I remember.”

===Chinese===
“几年前我认得一位 H. 韦斯先生。我记得他是从不来梅（Bremen）来的。”

===Original===
“This Mr. Weiss has gone back to Hamburg,” the clerk observed.

===Chinese===
“这位韦斯先生回汉堡（Hamburg）去了，”文书说。

===Original===
“Ah,” said Thorndyke, “then it would seem not to be the same. My acquaintance was a fair man with a beard and a decidedly red nose and he wore spectacles.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”桑代克说，“那看来就不是同一个人了。我认得的那位，是个浅发白面的男子，蓄着胡子，鼻子红得扎眼，还戴一副眼镜。”

===Original===
“That’s the man. You’ve described him exactly,” said the clerk, who was apparently rather easily satisfied in the matter of description.

===Chinese===
“就是这位。您把他形容得分毫不差，”文书说——在核对相貌这件事上，这位显然是极容易满足的。

===Original===
“Dear me,” said Thorndyke; “what a small world it is. Do you happen to have a note of his address in Hamburg?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，”桑代克说，“这世界可真小。您手头可巧存着他汉堡住址的记录？”

===Original===
“I haven’t,” the clerk replied. “You see we’ve done with him, having got the rent, though the house is not actually surrendered yet. Mr. Weiss’s housekeeper still has the front-door key. She doesn’t start for Hamburg for a week or so, and meanwhile she keeps the key so that she can call every day and see if there are any letters.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”文书答道，“您想，房租既然收齐，我们同他就两讫了，虽说宅子其实还没正式交还。韦斯先生的管家妇还拿着前门钥匙。她约莫一星期后才动身去汉堡，这几天钥匙留在她手上，好让她每天过来一趟，看看有没有信。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” said Thorndyke. “I wonder if he still has the same housekeeper.”

===Chinese===
“原来如此，”桑代克说，“不知他用的还是不是原来那位管家妇。”

===Original===
“This lady is a German,” replied the clerk, “with a regular jaw-twisting name. Sounded like Shallybang.”

===Chinese===
“这位太太是德国人，”文书答道，“名字拗口得很，能把人的下巴都拧歪。听着像‘沙利邦’（Shallybang）。”

===Original===
“Schallibaum. That is the lady. A fair woman with hardly any eyebrows and a pronounced cast in the left eye.”

===Chinese===
“沙利鲍姆。正是这位太太。浅色头发的女人，眉毛几乎瞧不见，左眼斜视得厉害。”

===Original===
“Now that’s very curious, sir,” said the clerk. “It’s the same name, and this is a fair woman with remarkably thin eyebrows, I remember, now that you mention it. But it can’t be the same person. I have only seen her a few times and then only just for a minute or so; but I’m quite certain she had no cast in her eye. So, you see, sir, she can’t be the same person. You can dye your hair or you can wear a wig or you can paint your face; but a squint is a squint. There’s no faking a swivel eye.”

===Chinese===
“这可真怪了，先生，”文书说，“名字对得上；您这一提，我也想起来了，这位确实是个浅发女人，眉毛淡得出奇。可决不会是同一个人。我统共只见过她几面，每回也不过一分钟上下；可她眼睛不斜，这一点我有十足把握。所以您瞧，先生，她不可能是同一个人。头发可以染，假发可以戴，脸也可以涂脂抹粉；可斜眼就是斜眼。‘转轴眼’是作不得假的。”

===Original===
Thorndyke laughed softly. “I suppose not; unless, perhaps, someone might invent an adjustable glass eye. Are these the keys?”

===Chinese===
桑代克轻轻笑了。“我也想是作不得假；除非——兴许有朝一日，有谁发明出一种可以拨调的玻璃假眼来。这几把就是钥匙？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir. The large one belongs to the wicket in the front gate. The other is the latchkey belonging to the side door. Mrs. Shallybang has the key of the front door.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。大的那把开大门上的便门。另一把是弹簧锁钥匙，开边门。前门的钥匙在沙利邦太太手里。”

===Original===
“Thank you,” said Thorndyke. He took the keys, to which a wooden label was attached, and we made our way back towards the house of mystery, discussing the clerk’s statements as we went.

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，”桑代克说。他接过那几把系着木牌的钥匙，我们便转身朝那座神秘之宅走去，一路上讨论着文书的这番话。

===Original===
“A very communicable young gentleman, that,” Thorndyke remarked. “He seemed quite pleased to relieve the monotony of office work with a little conversation. And I am sure I was very delighted to indulge him.”

===Chinese===
“那位年轻先生真是健谈得很，”桑代克评论道，“能借几句闲话解解案牍的沉闷，他瞧着相当乐意；而我呢，自然也乐得成全他。”

===Original===
“He hadn’t much to tell, all the same,” said I.

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，他到底没说出多少东西，”我说。

===Original===
Thorndyke looked at me in surprise. “I don’t know what you would have, Jervis, unless you expect casual strangers to present you with a ready-made body of evidence, fully classified, with all the inferences and implications stated. It seemed to me that he was a highly instructive young man.”

===Chinese===
桑代克诧异地看着我。“我真不知道你还想怎么样，杰维斯，除非你指望萍水相逢的陌生人双手奉上一份现成的证据汇编，分门别类，连全部推论和含义都一并注明。在我看来，他可是位大有教益的年轻人。”

===Original===
“What did you learn from him?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“那你从他那儿听出了些什么？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, come, Jervis,” he protested; “is that a fair question, under our present arrangement? However, I will mention a few points. We learn that about six or seven months ago, Mr. H. Weiss dropped from the clouds into Kennington Lane and that he has now ascended from Kennington Lane into the clouds. That is a useful piece of information. Then we learn that Mrs. Schallibaum has remained in England; which might be of little importance if it were not for a very interesting corollary that it suggests.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，得了，杰维斯，”他提出抗议，“照咱们眼下这个约定，这么问公道吗？不过，我可以点出几条。我们得知，约莫六七个月前，H. 韦斯先生从云端跌进了肯宁顿巷，如今又从肯宁顿巷升上云端去了。这是一条有用的情报。其次，我们得知沙利鲍姆太太还留在英国；这事本身也许无关紧要，要不是它暗示出一个非常有意思的推论的话。”

===Original===
“What is that?”

===Chinese===
“什么推论？”

===Original===
“I must leave you to consider the facts at your leisure; but you will have noticed the ostensible reason for her remaining behind. She is engaged in puttying up the one gaping joint in their armour. One of them has been indiscreet enough to give this address to some correspondent﻿—probably a foreign correspondent. Now, as they obviously wish to leave no tracks, they cannot give their new address to the Post Office to have their letters forwarded, and, on the other hand, a letter left in the box might establish such a connection as would enable them to be traced. Moreover, the letter might be of a kind that they would not wish to fall into the wrong hands. They would not have given this address excepting under some peculiar circumstances.”

===Chinese===
“那些事实，我只好留给你自己从容去想；不过她滞留不走的表面理由，你总注意到了。她正忙着往那副盔甲上唯一豁开的接缝里抹油灰。他们两人里有位不够检点的，把这个地址给了某个通信人——多半还是个国外通信人。他们既显然不想留下任何踪迹，就不能把新地址交给邮局转信；反过来，一封信留在这信箱里，又可能坐实这样一层关联，教人顺藤摸到他们身上。再者，那封信或许正属于那种他们万万不愿落到旁人手里的东西。若非出于某种特殊的情况，他们原不会把这个地址给人。”

===Original===
“No, I should think not, if they took this house for the express purpose of committing a crime in it.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，我想是不会的——如果他们赁下这宅子，就是专为在里头作案的话。”

===Original===
“Exactly. And then there is one other fact that you may have gathered from our young friend’s remarks.”

===Chinese===
“正是。此外还有一桩事实，你或许已从我们那位年轻朋友的话里听出来了。”

===Original===
“What is that?”

===Chinese===
“什么事实？”

===Original===
“That a controllable squint is a very valuable asset to a person who wishes to avoid identification.”

===Chinese===
“那就是：一副收放自如的斜眼，对想要逃避身份指认的人来说，是一笔顶宝贵的资产。”

===Original===
“Yes, I did note that. The fellow seemed to think that it was absolutely conclusive.”

===Chinese===
“对，这一点我确实留意到了。那家伙似乎认定，它足以下绝对的确论。”

===Original===
“And so would most people; especially in the case of a squint of that kind. We can all squint towards our noses, but no normal person can turn his eyes away from one another. My impression is that the presence or absence, as the case might be, of a divergent squint would be accepted as absolute disproof of identity. But here we are.”

===Chinese===
“多数人也会这么想；尤其是那一类的斜视。人人都能把眼珠往鼻梁上斗，可没有哪个正常人能叫两颗眼珠各自朝外分飞。我的感想是：外斜视之有无——各随其例——都会被奉为对身份的绝对否证。好了，我们到了。”

===Original===
He inserted the key into the wicket of the large gate, and, when we had stepped through into the covered way, he locked it from the inside.

===Chinese===
他把钥匙插进大门的便门，等我们两人跨进那条带顶的廊道，他又从里面把它锁上了。

===Original===
“Why have you locked us in?” I asked, seeing that the wicket had a latch.

===Chinese===
“你把我们锁在里头做什么？”我问——我看见那便门原是带门闩的。

===Original===
“Because,” he replied, “if we now hear anyone on the premises we shall know who it is. Only one person besides ourselves has a key.”

===Chinese===
“因为这样，”他答道，“倘若此刻听见有人进宅，我们便知道来的是谁。除了我们，只有一个人手里有钥匙。”

===Original===
His reply startled me somewhat. I stopped and looked at him.

===Chinese===
他这话让我颇为吃惊。我停下脚步，望着他。

===Original===
“That is a quaint situation, Thorndyke. I hadn’t thought of it. Why she may actually come to the house while we are here; in fact, she may be in the house at this moment.”

===Chinese===
“这局面可真别致，桑代克。我先前竟没有想到。哎呀，说不定我们在这儿的时候，她当真会来；说不准，她这会儿就已经在宅子里了。”

===Original===
“I hope not,” said he. “We don’t particularly want Mr. Weiss to be put on his guard, for I take it, he is a pretty wide-awake gentleman under any circumstances. If she does come, we had better keep out of sight. I think we will look over the house first. That is of the most interest to us. If the lady does happen to come while we are here, she may stay to show us over the place and keep an eye on us. So we will leave the stables to the last.”

===Chinese===
“但愿没有，”他说，“我们并不怎么想让韦斯先生起戒心，因为据我判断，此公无论在什么境地，都是个相当警醒的角色。她若是真来了，我们最好避而不见。我想我们还是先把宅子查上一遍——那才是我们最感兴趣的。倘若这位太太真在我们盘桓期间来了，她多半会留下来，领我们到处看看，顺便盯住我们。所以马厩留到最后再查。”

===Original===
We walked down the entry to the side door at which I had been admitted by Mrs. Schallibaum on the occasion of my previous visits. Thorndyke inserted the latchkey, and, as soon as we were inside, shut the door and walked quickly through into the hall, whither I followed him. He made straight for the front door, where, having slipped up the catch of the lock, he began very attentively to examine the letter-box. It was a somewhat massive wooden box, fitted with a lock of good quality and furnished with a wire grille through which one could inspect the interior.

===Chinese===
我们沿着通道走到那扇边门前——先前几回，沙利鲍姆太太便是从这扇门迎我进去的。桑代克插入弹簧锁钥匙，一进屋便掩上门，快步穿行到门厅，我跟在他身后。他直奔前门，拨开锁上的搭扣，随即开始极仔细地检查那只信箱。那是一只颇为厚实的木箱，装着一把上好的锁，还配着一面铁丝格栅，透过格栅可以窥见箱内。

===Original===
“We are in luck, Jervis,” Thorndyke remarked. “Our visit has been most happily timed. There is a letter in the box.”

===Chinese===
“我们运气不坏，杰维斯，”桑代克说，“这一趟来得正是时候。箱子里有一封信。”

===Original===
“Well,” I said, “we can’t get it out; and if we could, it would be hardly justifiable.”

===Chinese===
“哼，”我说，“我们没法把它取出来；就算取得出，那样做也难说正当。”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” he replied, “that I am prepared to assent offhand to either of those propositions; but I would rather not tamper with another person’s letter, even if that person should happen to be a murderer. Perhaps we can get the information we want from the outside of the envelope.”

===Chinese===
“这两个论断是否都无可辩驳，”他答道，“我还不敢贸然附和；不过我宁可不擅动别人的信件，哪怕那人碰巧是个杀人犯。也许我们要的情报，从信封外面就能到手。”

===Original===
He produced from his pocket a little electric lamp fitted with a bull’s-eye, and, pressing the button, threw a beam of light in through the grille. The letter was lying on the bottom of the box face upwards, so that the address could easily be read.

===Chinese===
他从口袋里掏出一盏小小的电灯，配着牛眼式聚光镜；一按按钮，一束光便穿过格栅射了进去。那封信面朝上躺在箱底，地址一读即明。

===Original===
“Herrn Dr. H. Weiss,” Thorndyke read aloud. “German stamp, postmark apparently Darmstadt. You notice that the ‘Herrn Dr.’ is printed and the rest written. What do you make of that?”

===Chinese===
“‘Herrn Dr. H. Weiss’（H. 韦斯博士先生收），”桑代克朗声念道，“德国邮票，邮戳看去是达姆施塔特（Darmstadt）。你留意到‘Herrn Dr.’是印上去的，其余是手写的。这你怎么解释？”

===Original===
“I don’t quite know. Do you think he is really a medical man?”

===Chinese===
“我说不大好。你认为他当真是个行医的？”

===Original===
“Perhaps we had better finish our investigation, in case we are disturbed, and discuss the bearings of the facts afterwards. The name of the sender may be on the flap of the envelope. If it is not, I shall pick the lock and take out the letter. Have you got a probe about you?”

===Chinese===
“我们还是先把检查做完为好，免得半路被人搅扰，这些事实的干系留待之后再论。寄信人的名字或许就在封盖背面。若是没有，我就撬开这把锁，把信取出来。你身上带探针了吗？”

===Original===
“Yes; by force of habit I am still carrying my pocket case.”

===Chinese===
“带了；习惯成自然，我至今还揣着那只出诊小盒。”

===Original===
I took the little case from my pocket and extracting from it a jointed probe of thickish silver wire, screwed the two halves together and handed the completed instrument to Thorndyke; who passed the slender rod through the grille and adroitly turned the letter over.

===Chinese===
我从口袋里取出那只小盒，从里面抽出一根较粗银丝制的两截探针，把两截旋接起来，将配好的器械递给桑代克；他把那根细杆穿过格栅，利落地把信翻了个身。

===Original===
“Ha!” he exclaimed with deep satisfaction, as the light fell on the reverse of the envelope, “we are saved from the necessity of theft﻿—or rather, unauthorized borrowing﻿—‘Johann Schnitzler, Darmstadt.’ That is all that we actually want. The German police can do the rest if necessary.”

===Chinese===
“哈！”灯光落在信封背面时，他深为满意地叫出声来，“我们免了行窃之必要——或者说，免了‘擅自借用’之必要——‘约翰·施尼茨勒（Johann Schnitzler），达姆施塔特’。我们真正要得到的就这些。余下的事，必要时自有德国警方去办。”

===Original===
He handed me back my probe, pocketed his lamp, released the catch of the lock on the door, and turned away along the dark, musty-smelling hall.

===Chinese===
他把探针还我，把灯收回口袋，拨回门锁上的搭扣，转身沿着那昏暗而霉味扑鼻的门厅走去。

===Original===
“Do you happen to know the name of Johann Schnitzler?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你可巧认得约翰·施尼茨勒这个名字？”他问。

===Original===
I replied that I had no recollection of ever having heard the name before.

===Chinese===
我回答说，我不记得从前听见过这个名字。

===Original===
“Neither have I,” said he; “but I think we may form a pretty shrewd guess as to his avocation. As you saw, the words ‘Herrn Dr.’ were printed on the envelope, leaving the rest of the address to be written by hand. The plain inference is that he is a person who habitually addresses letters to medical men, and as the style of the envelope and the lettering﻿—which is printed, not embossed﻿—is commercial, we may assume that he is engaged in some sort of trade. Now, what is a likely trade?”

===Chinese===
“我也不认得，”他说，“不过对他的营生，我想我们不妨作个相当在行的猜测。你方才看见，信封上‘Herrn Dr.’几个字是印好的，地址的其余部分留待手写。明白的推论是：此人惯于给医生们寄信。再者，信封的样式和那些字样——是印的，不是压凸的——都带着买卖气，我们可以假定他从事某一门生意。那么，是哪一门生意的可能大呢？”

===Original===
“He might be an instrument maker or a drug manufacturer; more probably the latter, as there is an extensive drug and chemical industry in Germany, and as Mr. Weiss seemed to have more use for drugs than instruments.”

===Chinese===
“他也许是器械制造商，或者是药品制造商；后者的可能更大，因为德国的药品与化工产业极为庞大，而韦斯先生用得着药品的地方，瞧着总比器械多。”

===Original===
“Yes, I think you are right; but we will look him up when we get home. And now we had better take a glance at the bedroom; that is, if you can remember which room it was.”

===Chinese===
“不错，我想你说得对；到家后我们再查他一查。眼下我们最好去瞧一眼那间卧室——当然，前提是你还记得是哪一间。”

===Original===
“It was on the first floor,” said I, “and the door by which I entered was just at the head of the stairs.”

===Chinese===
“在二楼，”我说，“我进的那扇门，就在楼梯口的顶头。”

===Original===
We ascended the two flights, and, as we reached the landing, I halted.

===Chinese===
我们登上两段楼梯，一到楼梯口平台，我便停住了脚。

===Original===
“This was the door,” I said, and was about to turn the handle when Thorndyke caught me by the arm.

===Chinese===
“就是这扇门，”我说，正要转动把手，桑代克一把攥住了我的胳膊。

===Original===
“One moment, Jervis,” said he. “What do you make of this?”

===Chinese===
“且慢，杰维斯，”他说，“这个你怎么看？”

===Original===
He pointed to a spot near the bottom of the door where, on close inspection, four good-sized screw-holes were distinguishable. They had been neatly stopped with putty and covered with knotting, and were so nearly the colour of the grained and varnished woodwork as to be hardly visible.

===Chinese===
他指着门靠近下端的一处；细看之下，那里辨认得出四个不小的螺钉孔。孔用油灰齐整地填平，外罩一层虫胶漆，颜色同带木纹的清漆门板几乎无异，几乎看不出来。

===Original===
“Evidently,” I answered, “there has been a bolt there, though it seems a queer place to fix one.”

===Chinese===
“显然，”我答道，“那儿原先装过一道门闩，虽说这位置装闩，显得古怪。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” replied Thorndyke. “If you look up you will see that there was another at the top of the door, and, as the lock is in the middle, they must have been highly effective. But there are one or two other points that strike one. First, you will notice that the bolts have been fixed on quite recently, for the paint that they covered is of the same grimy tint as that on the rest of the door. Next, they have been taken off, which, seeing that they could hardly have been worth the trouble of removal, seems to suggest that the person who fixed them considered that their presence might appear remarkable, while the screw-holes, which have been so skilfully and carefully stopped, would be less conspicuous.

===Chinese===
“一点也不古怪，”桑代克答道，“你抬头看就知道，门顶上原本还有一道；锁又居中，这几样合起来，效用必定极高。不过另有一两处地方引人注目。第一，你会注意到门闩装上去为时未久，因为闩板遮盖着的那片漆，同门上其余各处的污暗色泽并无二致。第二，门闩又被拆了下来；拆下的旧闩本不值这点麻烦，这似乎说明，装闩的人认定，闩留在这儿会惹人注目，而塞堵得这般精工细心的螺钉孔，总归不那么显眼。

===Original===
“Then, they are on the outside of the door﻿—an unusual situation for bedroom bolts﻿—and were of considerable size. They were long and thick.”

===Chinese===
“再者，它们装在门外侧——卧室门闩装在外侧，可是罕见的情形——而且尺寸可观。又长又粗。”

===Original===
“I can see, by the position of the screw-holes, that they were long; but how do you arrive at their thickness?”

===Chinese===
“从螺钉孔的位置，我看得出它们长；可它们的粗细，你又是从何得知的？”

===Original===
“By the size of the counter-holes in the jamb of the door. These holes have been very carefully filled with wooden plugs covered with knotting; but you can make out their diameter, which is that of the bolts, and which is decidedly out of proportion for an ordinary bedroom door. Let me show you a light.”

===Chinese===
“从门框上闩孔的大小。那些孔都用木塞细细填堵，外罩虫胶漆；可孔径还分辨得出——那便是门闩的粗细，而且对一扇寻常卧室门来说，实在粗得不成比例。我给你照个亮。”

===Original===
He flashed his lamp into the dark corner, and I was able to see distinctly the portentously large holes into which the bolts had fitted, and also to note the remarkable neatness with which they had been plugged.

===Chinese===
他把灯光射进那个昏暗的角落，我于是分明看清了那几个供门闩插入的、大得骇人的孔，也注意到塞堵这些孔的手艺出奇地齐整。

===Original===
“There was a second door, I remember,” said I. “Let us see if that was guarded in a similar manner.”

===Chinese===
“我记得还有第二扇门，”我说，“去瞧瞧那扇是否也作了同样的防范。”

===Original===
We strode through the empty room, awakening dismal echoes as we trod the bare boards, and flung open the other door. At top and bottom, similar groups of screw-holes showed that this also had been made secure, and that these bolts had been of the same very substantial character as the others.

===Chinese===
我们大步穿过空屋，鞋底踏着光地板，激起一阵凄凉的回声，随后猛地推开另一扇门。门的上端和下端各有一组同样的螺钉孔，可见这扇门也曾被弄得牢不可破，门闩也同其余几道一般粗壮结实。

===Original===
Thorndyke turned away from the door with a slight frown.

===Chinese===
桑代克从那扇门前转过身，眉头微微一皱。

===Original===
“If we had any doubts,” said he, “as to what has been going on in this house, these traces of massive fastenings would be almost enough to settle them.”

===Chinese===
“倘若我们对这宅子里究竟干过什么还有怀疑，”他说，“这些笨重门闩留下的痕迹，差不多就足以打消了。”

===Original===
“They might have been there before Weiss came,” I suggested. “He only came about seven months ago and there is no date on the screw-holes.”

===Chinese===
“这些闩也可能是韦斯搬来之前就有的，”我提出异议，“他不过七个月前才来，螺钉孔上又没刻着日期。”

===Original===
“That is quite true. But when, with their recent fixture, you couple the facts that they have been removed, that very careful measures have been taken to obliterate the traces of their presence, and that they would have been indispensable for the commission of the crime that we are almost certain was being committed here, it looks like an excess of caution to seek other explanations.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假。可是，把它们安装之新近，同另外几桩事实放在一处看——它们被拆除了；有人极仔细地设法抹去它们存在过的痕迹；而要说那桩我们几乎断定曾在此地发生的罪行，这些门闩又是不可或缺——再另寻旁的解释，未免谨慎得过了头。”

===Original===
“But,” I objected, “if the man, Graves, was really imprisoned, could not he have smashed the window and called for help?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我反驳道，“倘若格雷夫斯那人是真被囚禁了，他难道不能砸破窗户呼救么？”

===Original===
“The window looks out on the yard, as you see; but I expect it was secured too.”

===Chinese===
“这窗你也看见了，正对着后院；不过我料想它也上了防范。”

===Original===
He drew the massive, old-fashioned shutters out of their recess and closed them.

===Chinese===
他把那厚重的老式百叶窗从壁槽里拉出，合上了。

===Original===
“Yes, here we are.” He pointed to four groups of screw-holes at the corners of the shutters, and, once more producing his lamp, narrowly examined the insides of the recesses into which the shutters folded.

===Chinese===
“果然，在这儿。”他指着百叶窗四角的四组螺钉孔，又掏出灯来，细细察看窗扇折合所入的壁槽内里。

===Original===
“The nature of the fastening is quite evident,” said he. “An iron bar passed right across at the top and bottom and was secured by a staple and padlock. You can see the mark the bar made in the recess when the shutters were folded. When these bars were fixed and padlocked and the bolts were shot, this room was as secure, for a prisoner unprovided with tools, as a cell in Newgate.”

===Chinese===
“紧固之物的性质一望而知，”他说，“上下各有一根铁条横贯，再用搭扣和挂锁锁死。百叶窗折起时铁条在壁槽里压出的印子，你都看得见。这几根铁条装好落锁、门闩插上之后，这间屋子对一个手无工具的囚徒而言，就同新门监狱（Newgate）的牢房一样牢不可破。”

===Original===
We looked at one another for awhile without speaking; and I fancy that if Mr. H. Weiss could have seen our faces he might have thought it desirable to seek some retreat even more remote than Hamburg.

===Chinese===
我们相顾无言，对视了片刻；我想，倘若 H. 韦斯先生那时看得见我们两张脸，他也许会认为，有必要寻一处比汉堡更偏远的地方藏身。

===Original===
“It was a diabolical affair, Jervis,” Thorndyke said at length, in an ominously quiet and even gentle tone. “A sordid, callous, cold-blooded crime of a type that is to me utterly unforgivable and incapable of extenuation. Of course, it may have failed. Mr. Graves may even now be alive. I shall make it my very especial business to ascertain whether he is or not. And if he is not, I shall take it to myself as a sacred duty to lay my hand on the man who has compassed his death.”

===Chinese===
“那是桩魔鬼般的勾当，杰维斯，”桑代克终于开口，语调阴沉地平静，甚至近乎温柔。“一桩卑污、冷酷、血冷的罪行；这一类的罪，在我全然不可饶恕，也无可宽贷。当然，它也可能没有得逞。格雷夫斯先生说不定此刻还活着。查明他到底活着与否，我将引为特别的要务。而倘若他不在了，我便把擒获那置他于死地之人，当作一桩神圣的职责，担在自己身上。”

===Original===
I looked at Thorndyke with something akin to awe. In the quiet unemotional tone of his voice, in his unruffled manner and the stony calm of his face, there was something much more impressive, more fateful, than there could have been in the fiercest threats or the most passionate denunciations. I felt that in those softly spoken words he had pronounced the doom of the fugitive villain.

===Chinese===
我望着桑代克，心头近乎敬畏。他那平静无波的语调，他那毫澜不惊的仪态和石像般沉静的面容，自有一种东西，比最凶狠的威吓、最激愤的痛斥所能有的，更慑人心魄，更带命运的分量。我感到，在这几句轻声吐出的话里，他已对那亡命的恶徒宣了判。

===Original===
He turned away from the window and glanced round the empty room. It seemed that our discovery of the fastenings had exhausted the information that it had to offer.

===Chinese===
他从窗前转过身，扫视一圈这间空屋。看来，紧固装置一经发现，这屋子所能供给的情报也就到头了。

===Original===
“It is a thousand pities,” I remarked, “that we were unable to look round before they moved out the furniture. We might have found some clue to the scoundrel’s identity.”

===Chinese===
“真是万分可惜，”我说，“我们没能在他们搬走家具之前来查看一番。说不定还能寻出那恶棍身份的线索呢。”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Thorndyke; “there isn’t much information to be gathered here, I am afraid. I see they have swept up the small litter from the floor and poked it under the grate. We will turn that over, as there seems to be nothing else, and then look at the other rooms.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克答道，“恐怕这儿已搜罗不出多少东西了。我看他们把地上的细碎垃圾扫作一堆，塞进了炉格底下。既然旁的没什么可查，我们就把它翻检一遍，然后再看看别的屋子。”

===Original===
He raked out the little heap of rubbish with his stick and spread it out on the hearth. It certainly looked unpromising enough, being just such a rubbish heap as may be swept up in any untidy room during a move. But Thorndyke went through it systematically, examining each item attentively, even to the local tradesmen’s bills and empty paper bags, before laying them aside. Another rake of his stick scattered the bulky masses of crumpled paper and brought into view an object which he picked up with some eagerness. It was a portion of a pair of spectacles, which had apparently been trodden on, for the sidebar was twisted and bent and the glass was shattered into fragments.

===Chinese===
他用手杖把那一小堆垃圾扒出来，摊在炉床上。这堆东西瞧着委实没什么指望，同任何一间邋遢屋子搬家时扫拢的垃圾并无二致。可桑代克有条不紊地翻检，每一样都细看一遍，连本地铺户的账单和空纸袋也不放过，然后才搁到一旁。他的手杖再一扒，成团的皱纸散开，露出一件事物，他颇为急切地拾了起来。那是一副眼镜的一部分，显然叫人踩过：镜腿拧弯折曲，镜片也碎裂成片。

===Original===
“This ought to give us a hint,” said he. “It will probably have belonged either to Weiss or Graves, as Mrs. Schallibaum apparently did not wear glasses. Let us see if we can find the remainder.”

===Chinese===
“这该能给我们一点提示，”他说，“它多半是韦斯或格雷夫斯之物，因为沙利鲍姆太太显然不戴眼镜。看看能不能找齐其余的部分。”

===Original===
We both groped carefully with our sticks amongst the rubbish, spreading it out on the hearth and removing the numerous pieces of crumpled paper. Our search was rewarded by the discovery of the second eyepiece of the spectacles, of which the glass was badly cracked but less shattered than the other. I also picked up two tiny sticks at which Thorndyke looked with deep interest before laying them on the mantelshelf.

===Chinese===
我们俩各持手杖，在垃圾堆里小心扒寻，把它摊开在炉床上，把一张张皱纸挪开。这番搜寻得了报偿：眼镜的另一只镜框寻到了，框中镜片裂得厉害，只是碎得比那一只轻些。我还拾起两根细小的秆儿，桑代克对它们深感兴趣地端详了一番，才搁上壁炉架。

===Original===
“We will consider them presently,” said he. “Let us finish with the spectacles first. You see that the left eyeglass is a concave cylindrical lens of some sort. We can make out that much from the fragments that remain, and we can measure the curvature when we get them home, although that will be easier if we can collect some more fragments and stick them together. The right eye is plain glass; that is quite evident. Then these will have belonged to your patient, Jervis. You said that the tremulous iris was in the right eye, I think?”

===Chinese===
“这两样稍后再谈，”他说，“先把眼镜了结。你瞧，左眼镜片是某种凹柱面透镜。单凭剩下的碎片，这一点已能断定；曲率等回家再量——当然，若能再多收几片碎片、拼拢起来，量起来更便当。右眼是平光镜，这毫无疑义。那么，这副眼镜就该是你那位病人的了，杰维斯。我记得你说过，虹膜震颤是在右眼？”

===Original===
“Yes,” I replied. “These will be his spectacles, without doubt.”

===Chinese===
“对，”我答道，“这准是他的眼镜，毫无疑问。”

===Original===
“They are peculiar frames,” he continued. “If they were made in this country, we might be able to discover the maker. But we must collect as many fragments of glass as we can.”

===Chinese===
“这镜框颇为特别，”他接着说，“倘若是在本国配制的，我们或许能查出制造者来。不过，镜片碎片得尽量多收些。”

===Original===
Once more we searched amongst the rubbish and succeeded, eventually, in recovering some seven or eight small fragments of the broken spectacle-glasses, which Thorndyke laid on the mantelshelf beside the little sticks.
