===Chinese===
我们又在垃圾堆里搜寻了一回，终于捡回七八小片碎镜片，桑代克把它们摆在壁炉架上，就在那两根小秆儿旁边。

===Original===
“By the way, Thorndyke,” I said, taking up the latter to examine them afresh, “what are these things? Can you make anything of them?”

===Chinese===
“对了，桑代克，”我说着拿起那两根秆儿重新察看，“这两样到底是什么东西？你能看出什么门道么？”

===Original===
He looked at them thoughtfully for a few moments and then replied:

===Chinese===
他对着它们沉思了片刻，然后答道：

===Original===
“I don’t think I will tell you what they are. You should find that out for yourself, and it will be well worth your while to do so. They are rather suggestive objects under the circumstances. But notice their peculiarities carefully. Both are portions of some smooth, stout reed. There is a long, thin stick﻿—about six inches long﻿—and a thicker piece only three inches in length. The longer piece has a little scrap of red paper stuck on at the end; apparently a portion of a label of some kind with an ornamental border. The other end of the stick has been broken off. The shorter, stouter stick has had its central cavity artificially enlarged so that it fits over the other to form a cap or sheath. Make a careful note of those facts and try to think what they probably mean; what would be the most likely use for an object of this kind. When you have ascertained that, you will have learned something new about this case. And now, to resume our investigations. Here is a very suggestive thing.” He picked up a small, wide-mouthed bottle and, holding it up for my inspection, continued: “Observe the fly sticking to the inside, and the name on the label, ‘Fox, Russell Street, Covent Garden.’ ”

===Chinese===
“它们是什么，我想还是不告诉你为好。这层道理你该自己去参透，而这番功夫也决计不会白费。在眼下这情形里，它们是颇能引人联想的东西。不过，它们的特性你要仔细记取。两根都是某种光滑粗实的芦秆的截段。一根细长——约六英寸——另一根较粗，只得三英寸长。长的一根末端贴着一小角红纸，看去像某种标签的一部分，带着花饰的边框；秆的另一端是折断的。短而粗的一根，中空被人为掏大，恰好能套在另一根上，构成一个帽套，或者说一根鞘管。把这几桩事实仔细记下，试着想想它们多半意味着什么；这一类物件，最可能的用途是什么。等你查明这一点，你对本案便又多知道了一层。现在，继续我们的检查。这儿有件很能启发人的东西。”他拾起一只小广口瓶，举到我眼前让我细看，接着说道：“注意内壁上粘着的这只苍蝇，再看标签上的名字——‘福克斯（Fox），拉塞尔街（Russell Street），科文特花园（Covent Garden）。’”

===Original===
“I don’t know Mr. Fox.”

===Chinese===
“福克斯先生这个人，我不认识。”

===Original===
“Then I will inform you that he is a dealer in the materials for ‘makeup,’ theatrical or otherwise, and will leave you to consider the bearing of this bottle on our present investigation. There doesn’t seem to be anything else of interest in this El Dorado excepting that screw, which you notice is about the size of those with which the bolts were fastened on the doors. I don’t think it is worth while to unstop any of the holes to try it; we should learn nothing fresh.”

===Chinese===
“那我就告诉你：此人是‘化装’材料的经销商——戏剧用也罢，别的用途也罢——这只瓶子同我们眼下这桩查案的干系，就留给你自己去参详。这座‘黄金国’（El Dorado）里，除了那枚螺钉，似乎再没有什么可看的了；你也注意到，那螺钉的尺寸，同门上固定门闩用的相仿。我看不值得捅开哪个孔去试它；试也试不出新东西。”

===Original===
He rose, and, having kicked the discarded rubbish back under the grate, gathered up his gleanings from the mantelpiece, carefully bestowing the spectacles and the fragments of glass in the tin box that he appeared always to carry in his pocket, and wrapping the larger objects in his handkerchief.

===Chinese===
他站起身，把翻检过的垃圾踢回炉格底下，再将壁炉架上的收获一一收拢：眼镜和玻璃碎片小心安置进那只他口袋里似乎常备的铁皮盒，大件的物件则用手帕裹起。

===Original===
“A poor collection,” was his comment, as he returned the box and handkerchief to his pocket, “and yet not so poor as I had feared. Perhaps, if we question them closely enough, these unconsidered trifles may be made to tell us something worth learning after all. Shall we go into the other room?”

===Chinese===
“收成寒碜，”他把铁皮盒和手帕放回口袋时评论道，“却也还不像我担心的那样寒碜。这些不入眼的小零碎，若是盘问得法，说不定终究能供出点值得一听的东西来。到隔壁屋里去看看？”

===Original===
We passed out on to the landing and into the front room, where, guided by experience, we made straight for the fireplace. But the little heap of rubbish there contained nothing that even Thorndyke’s inquisitive eye could view with interest. We wandered disconsolately round the room, peering into the empty cupboards and scanning the floor and the corners by the skirting, without discovering a single object or relic of the late occupants. In the course of my perambulations I halted by the window and was looking down into the street when Thorndyke called to me sharply:

===Chinese===
我们出到楼梯口平台，进了临街的前屋。有先例指路，我们直奔壁炉。可那一小堆垃圾里空空如也，连桑代克那双好窥探的眼睛也挑不出一件耐看的东西。我们恹恹地在屋里兜了一圈，把空碗橱挨个窥探，地板和踢脚板旁的角落也都扫视一遍，却未发现先前住户留下的一件东西、一件遗物。转悠之间，我在窗前站住，正朝楼下的街上望去，桑代克忽然厉声唤我：

===Original===
“Come away from the window, Jervis! Have you forgotten that Mrs. Schallibaum may be in the neighbourhood at this moment?”

===Chinese===
“离那扇窗户远些，杰维斯！你忘了沙利鲍姆太太这会儿可能就在左近？”

===Original===
As a matter of fact I had entirely forgotten the matter, nor did it now strike me as anything but the remotest of possibilities. I replied to that effect.

===Chinese===
实际上我确实把这事忘了个干净，而且此刻想来，这也不过是万分遥远的可能罢了。我便如此作答。

===Original===
“I don’t agree with you,” Thorndyke rejoined. “We have heard that she comes here to look for letters. Probably she comes every day, or even oftener. There is a good deal at stake, remember, and they cannot feel quite as secure as they would wish. Weiss must have seen what view you took of the case and must have had some uneasy moments thinking of what you might do. In fact, we may take it that the fear of you drove them out of the neighbourhood, and that they are mighty anxious to get that letter and cut the last link that binds them to this house.”

===Chinese===
“我可不同意，”桑代克应道，“我们已听说她来这儿查信。多半天天都来，甚至一天几趟。别忘了，他们押上的东西可不少，他们的心里决不像期望的那样踏实。韦斯必定看出了你对这案子所持的看法，一想到你可能采取的行动，必定有过不少坐立难安的时刻。事实上，我们不妨这样认定：正是对你的忌惮，把他们从这一带吓走了；此刻他们正急不可耐，想把那封信弄到手，斩断把他们同这座宅子拴在一起的最后一环。”

===Original===
“I suppose that is so,” I agreed; “and if the lady should happen to pass this way and should see me at the window and recognize me, she would certainly smell a rat.”

===Chinese===
“我想是这样，”我表示同意，“倘若那位太太碰巧打这儿经过，看见我立在窗口，又认出了我，她准会嗅出味儿不对。”

===Original===
“A rat!” exclaimed Thorndyke. “She would smell a whole pack of foxes, and Mr. H. Weiss would be more on his guard than ever. Let us have a look at the other rooms; there is nothing here.”

===Chinese===
“味儿不对！”桑代克叫道，“她嗅出的将是满满一窝狐狸，而 H. 韦斯先生的戒备，更会胜过以往任何时候。到其余各屋看看吧；这儿没什么可看的了。”

===Original===
We went up to the next floor and found traces of recent occupation in one room only. The garrets had evidently been unused, and the kitchen and ground-floor rooms offered nothing that appeared to Thorndyke worth noting. Then we went out by the side door and down the covered way into the yard at the back. The workshops were fastened with rusty padlocks that looked as if they had not been disturbed for months. The stables were empty and had been tentatively cleaned out, the coach-house was vacant, and presented no traces of recent use excepting a half-bald spoke-brush. We returned up the covered way and I was about to close the side door, which Thorndyke had left ajar, when he stopped me.

===Chinese===
我们上到再上一层，只有一间屋子留有近期住人的痕迹。顶楼显然一直闲置，厨房和底层的几个房间，也拿不出什么在桑代克看来值得记下的东西。随后我们从边门出去，沿带顶的廊道走进后院。几间作坊锁着锈挂锁，看去已数月无人动过。马厩空着，被草草清扫过；车房也空着，除了一把半秃的轮辐刷，再无近期用过的痕迹。我们折回廊道，我正要去关那扇桑代克虚掩着的边门，他拦住了我。

===Original===
“We’ll have another look at the hall before we go,” said he; and, walking softly before me, he made his way to the front door, where, producing his lamp, he threw a beam of light into the letter-box.

===Chinese===
“走之前，我们再到门厅看一眼，”他说；说罢在我前头放轻脚步，走到前门，掏出灯来，朝信箱里射进一束光。

===Original===
“Any more letters?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“又有信了吗？”我问。

===Original===
“Any more!” he repeated. “Look for yourself.”

===Chinese===
“还问有没有信！”他重复道，“你自己看吧。”

===Original===
I stooped and peered through the grille into the lighted interior; and then I uttered an exclamation.

===Chinese===
我俯下身，透过格栅往被照亮的箱内窥望；随即失声叫了出来。

===Original===
The box was empty.

===Chinese===
箱子空了。

===Original===
Thorndyke regarded me with a grim smile. “We have been caught on the hop, Jervis, I suspect,” said he.

===Chinese===
桑代克望着我，脸上挂着一丝狞笑。“我疑心咱们叫人打了个措手不及，杰维斯，”他说。

===Original===
“It is queer,” I replied. “I didn’t hear any sound of the opening or closing of the door; did you?”

===Chinese===
“怪了，”我答道，“开门关门的声音，我一点儿没听见；你听见了吗？”

===Original===
“No; I didn’t hear any sound; which makes me suspect that she did. She would have heard our voices and she is probably keeping a sharp lookout at this very moment. I wonder if she saw you at the window. But whether she did or not, we must go very warily. Neither of us must return to the Temple direct, and we had better separate when we have returned the keys and I will watch you out of sight and see if anyone is following you. What are you going to do?”

===Chinese===
“没有；我也一点儿没听见；这倒让我疑心是她听见了我们的动静。她想必听到了我们的说话声，此刻多半正严加瞭望呢。不知她可看见你在窗口没有。但无论看见与否，我们此后都得步步谨慎。你我二人都不得径直回坦普尔；还了钥匙之后，最好分头走，我会目送你走远，看看有没有人跟你。你打算做什么去？”

===Original===
“If you don’t want me, I shall run over to Kensington and drop in to lunch at the Hornbys’. I said I would call as soon as I had an hour or so free.”

===Chinese===
“你要是不需要我，我就跑一趟肯辛顿（Kensington），到霍恩比家蹭一顿午饭。我说过，一得着一两个钟头的空，就去拜访他们。”

===Original===
“Very well. Do so; and keep a lookout in case you are followed. I have to go down to Guildford this afternoon. Under the circumstances, I shall not go back home, but send Polton a telegram and take a train at Vauxhall and change at some small station where I can watch the platform. Be as careful as you can. Remember that what you have to avoid is being followed to any place where you are known, and, above all, revealing your connection with number Five A, King’s Bench Walk.”

===Chinese===
“很好。就这么办；路上留神，提防有人跟踪。今天下午我得去吉尔福德（Guildford）一趟。眼下这情形，我就不回家了，给波尔顿拍个电报，在沃克斯霍尔上车，再挑一个小站换车，好在站台上察看往来的人。你务必加倍小心。记住，你要避免的，是被人跟到任何认识你的地方去；而最要紧的，是切莫暴露你同王座法庭步道5A号（Five A）的干系。”

===Original===
Having thus considered our immediate movements, we emerged together from the wicket, and locking it behind us, walked quickly to the house-agents’, where an opportune office-boy received the keys without remark. As we came out of the office, I halted irresolutely and we both looked up and down the lane.

===Chinese===
眼下的行止既已计议停当，我们便一同从便门出来，回身落锁，快步走向房地产代理行；一个来得正巧的听差小厮一言不发地接过了钥匙。走出事务所时，我踌躇地站住，我们俩把那条巷子的两头都张望了一遍。

===Original===
“There is no suspicious looking person in sight at present,” Thorndyke said, and then asked: “Which way do you think of going?”

===Chinese===
“眼下视野里没有形迹可疑的人，”桑代克说，接着问：“你打算走哪条路？”

===Original===
“It seems to me,” I replied, “that my best plan would be to take a cab or an omnibus so as to get out of the neighbourhood as quickly as possible. If I go through Ravensden Street into Kennington Park Road, I can pick up an omnibus that will take me to the Mansion House, where I can change for Kensington. I shall go on the top so that I can keep a lookout for any other omnibus or cab that may be following.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，”我答道，“最稳妥的办法是雇一辆马车，或者搭一辆公共马车，尽快离开这一带。穿过拉文斯登街（Ravensden Street）到肯宁顿公园路（Kennington Park Road），就能搭上一辆开往市长官邸（Mansion House）去的公共马车，到了那儿再换车去肯辛顿。我要坐在顶层，好留意后面有没有别的公共马车或出租马车跟上来。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke, “that seems a good plan. I will walk with you and see that you get a fair start.”

===Chinese===
“好，”桑代克说，“这主意不错。我陪你走一程，看着你顺当地脱身。”

===Original===
We walked briskly along the lane and through Ravensden Street to the Kennington Park Road. An omnibus was approaching from the south at a steady jog-trot and we halted at the corner to wait for it. Several people passed us in different directions, but none seemed to take any particular notice of us, though we observed them rather narrowly, especially the women. Then the omnibus crawled up. I sprang on the footboard and ascended to the roof, where I seated myself and surveyed the prospect to the rear. No one else got on the omnibus﻿—which had not stopped﻿—and no cab or other passenger vehicle was in sight. I continued to watch Thorndyke as he stood sentinel at the corner, and noted that no one appeared to be making any effort to overtake the omnibus. Presently my colleague waved his hand to me and turned back towards Vauxhall, and I, having satisfied myself once more that no pursuing cab or hurrying foot-passenger was in sight, decided that our precautions had been unnecessary and settled myself in a rather more comfortable position.

===Chinese===
我们脚步轻快地沿巷而行，穿过拉文斯登街，到了肯宁顿公园路。一辆公共马车正从南边颠儿颠儿地稳步驶来，我们便在街角停步候它。好几拨行人打我们身旁各奔东西，没有一个看来对我们有什么特别的留意，尽管我们把他们也瞧得相当仔细，尤其是那些女的。随后公共马车慢腾腾地挨近。我跃上踏板，登到顶层坐下，朝后方的景况环顾。再没有别人上车——马车并没有停——视野里也不见任何出租马车或其他载客的车辆。我继续望着站在街角放哨的桑代克，注意到没有人显出要赶超这辆马车的意思。不多时，我的同事向我挥了挥手，转身朝沃克斯霍尔回去了；而我，在再次确信视野里并无追踵的出租马车或疾行的步行客之后，认定我们那番提防原是多余，便把身子挪得更舒坦了些。

## X The Hunter Hunted / 猎者反被猎

===Original===
The omnibus of those days was a leisurely vehicle. Its ordinary pace was a rather sluggish trot, and in a thickly populated thoroughfare its speed was further reduced by frequent stoppages. Bearing these facts in mind, I gave an occasional backward glance as we jogged northward, though my attention soon began to wander from the rather remote possibility of pursuit to the incidents of our late exploration.

===Chinese===
那个年月的公共马车是种从容的车辆。它寻常的步速不过是颇为迟缓的小跑，到了人烟稠密的大街上，又因频频停靠而愈发缓慢。心里存着这几层，马车载着我们徐徐北行，我不时回头张望一眼；不过没过多久，我的注意力便从追踪那桩相当渺茫的可能，游移到了我们方才那番查探的种种情节上去。

===Original===
It had not been difficult to see that Thorndyke was very well pleased with the results of our search, but excepting the letter﻿—which undoubtedly opened up a channel for further inquiry and possible identification﻿—I could not perceive that any of the traces that we had found justified his satisfaction. There were the spectacles, for instance. They were almost certainly the pair worn by Mr. Graves. But what then? It was exceedingly improbable that we should be able to discover the maker of them, and if we were, it was still more improbable that he would be able to give us any information that would help us. Spectacle-makers are not usually on confidential terms with their customers.

===Chinese===
不难看出，桑代克对我们这趟搜查的收获十分满意；只是除那封信外——它无疑为进一步查访乃至查明身份开出了一条门路——我看不出我们寻到的任何其他痕迹当得起他这份满意。就拿那副眼镜来说。它几乎肯定就是格雷夫斯先生戴的那一副。可那又怎样？我们竟能寻出它的制造商，此事微乎其微；纵然寻得出，他能给我们什么帮衬，可能性就更其渺茫。眼镜制造商同主顾之间，向来不至深交到互吐机密。

===Original===
As to the other objects, I could make nothing of them. The little sticks of reed evidently had some use that was known to Thorndyke and furnished, by inference, some kind of information about Weiss, Graves, or Mrs. Schallibaum. But I had never seen anything like them before and they conveyed nothing whatever to me. Then the bottle that had seemed so significant to Thorndyke was to me quite uninforming. It did, indeed, suggest that some member of the household might be connected with the stage, but it gave no hint as to which one. Certainly that person was not Mr. Weiss, whose appearance was as remote from that of an actor as could well be imagined. At any rate, the bottle and its label gave me no more useful hint than it might be worth while to call on Mr. Fox and make inquiries; and something told me very emphatically that this was not what it had conveyed to Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
至于另外几样物件，我便全然参不透了。那几截小芦秆显然自有其用途，只是桑代克心中有数，而且据理推来，它们提供了某种关于韦斯、格雷夫斯或沙利鲍姆太太的消息。可我从前不曾见过类似的东西，它们对我说来毫无意味。再有那只瓶子，在桑代克眼里如此意味深长，在我看来却全无信息可言。它确实暗示这一户人家里或有哪一位同戏剧界有些瓜葛，却点不出究竟是哪一位。当然那人决计不是韦斯先生——他的相貌离一个演员有多远，简直想象不尽。总而言之，那只瓶子和它的标签给我的有用提示，充其量是值得去拜访一趟福克斯先生打听打听；可有什么在斩钉截铁地告诉我，它向桑代克所传递的决不止这一点。

===Original===
These reflections occupied me until the omnibus, having rumbled over London Bridge and up King William Street, joined the converging streams of traffic at the Mansion House. Here I got down and changed to an omnibus bound for Kensington; on which I travelled westward pleasantly enough, looking down into the teeming streets and whiling away the time by meditating upon the very agreeable afternoon that I promised myself, and considering how far my new arrangement with Thorndyke would justify me in entering into certain domestic engagements of a highly interesting kind.

===Chinese===
这些念头一直陪着我，直到公共马车隆隆驶过伦敦桥（London Bridge），上了国王威廉街（King William Street），汇入市长官邸处交汇合流的车马。我在这里下车，换搭一辆开往肯辛顿的公共马车；乘着它一路西行倒也惬意：俯望底下熙熙攘攘的街市，一面盘算着我给自己许下的那个极愉快的下午，一面思量我和桑代克的新安排，够不够叫我放手去缔结某种意味深长的家庭之约。

===Original===
What might have happened under other circumstances it is impossible to tell and useless to speculate; the fact is that my journey ended in a disappointment. I arrived, all agog, at the familiar house in Endsley Gardens only to be told by a sympathetic housemaid that the family was out; that Mrs. Hornby had gone into the country and would not be home until night, and﻿—which mattered a good deal more to me﻿—that her niece, Miss Juliet Gibson, had accompanied her.

===Chinese===
换了别的情形会怎样，既说不得，也犯不着瞎猜；事实是我这一趟以失望收场。我满心热望地赶到恩斯利花园（Endsley Gardens）那所熟悉的宅子，却只听一位好心的女仆说，一家人都出门了；霍恩比太太下乡去了，到夜里才会回来；还有——这一位于我更要紧得多——她的外甥女朱丽叶·吉布森小姐（Juliet Gibson）也随同去了。

===Original===
Now a man who drops into lunch without announcing his intention or previously ascertaining those of his friends has no right to quarrel with fate if he finds an empty house. Thus philosophically I reflected as I turned away from the house in profound discontent, demanding of the universe in general why Mrs. Hornby need have perversely chosen my first free day to go gadding into the country, and above all, why she must needs spirit away the fair Juliet. This was the crowning misfortune (for I could have endured the absence of the elder lady with commendable fortitude), and since I could not immediately return to the Temple it left me a mere waif and stray for the time being.

===Chinese===
一个不先打招呼、也不问问朋友有无空暇就闯去吃午饭的人，撞上一座空宅，是无权跟命运怄气的。我便如此这般以哲人风度自宽自解，怏怏转身离开那宅子，一面泛泛地质问苍天：霍恩比太太何苦偏偏拣我头一个闲暇的日子任性地跑乡下闲逛，更要紧的是，何苦非把美丽的朱丽叶一并摄了去。这才是最要命的一桩（因为老太太不在家，我原可以可嘉的坚忍熬将过去）；何况我又不能即刻回坦普尔去，一时间竟成了个无处栖身的游魂。

===Original===
Instinct﻿—of the kind that manifests itself especially about one o’clock in the afternoon﻿—impelled me in the direction of Brompton Road, and finally landed me at a table in a large restaurant apparently adjusted to the needs of ladies who had come from a distance to engage in the feminine sport of shopping. Here, while waiting for my lunch, I sat idly scanning the morning paper and wondering what I should do with the rest of the day; and presently it chanced that my eye caught the announcement of a matinee at the theatre in Sloane Square. It was quite a long time since I had been at a theatre, and, as the play﻿—light comedy﻿—seemed likely to satisfy my not very critical taste, I decided to devote the afternoon to reviving my acquaintance with the drama. Accordingly as soon as my lunch was finished, I walked down the Brompton Road, stepped on to an omnibus, and was duly deposited at the door of the theatre. A couple of minutes later I found myself occupying an excellent seat in the second row of the pit, oblivious alike of my recent disappointment and of Thorndyke’s words of warning.

===Chinese===
本能——尤其是下午一点钟前后格外活跃的那一种——把我推向布朗普顿路（Brompton Road），末了安置在一家大餐馆的桌旁；那馆子看去专为远道而来、从事购物这项女性运动的女客而设。在这里，我一边等午饭，一边懒懒地翻着晨报，盘算这一天余下的时光如何打发；不一会儿，恰好瞧见一则广告：斯隆广场（Sloane Square）的剧院有一场日场戏。我已经很久没进过剧院了，而这出戏——轻喜剧——想来合得了我那不算挑剔的口味，于是决计把下午用来重温同戏剧的旧交。于是午饭一毕，我便沿布朗普顿路而下，跨上一辆公共马车，顺顺当当地给放在了剧院门口。两分钟后，我已坐进正厅后排第二排的一个上好座位，方才的失意也罢，桑代克的警告之言也罢，一古脑儿都忘了。

===Original===
I am not an enthusiastic playgoer. To dramatic performances I am disposed to assign nothing further than the modest function of furnishing entertainment. I do not go to a theatre to be instructed or to have my moral outlook elevated. But, by way of compensation, I am not difficult to please. To a simple play, adjusted to my primitive taste, I can bring a certain bucolic appreciation that enables me to extract from the performance the maximum of enjoyment; and when, on this occasion, the final curtain fell and the audience rose, I rescued my hat from its insecure resting-place and turned to go with the feeling that I had spent a highly agreeable afternoon.

===Chinese===
我不是个热心看戏的人。对戏剧演出，我至多肯派给它一项本分的功用——供人消遣。我进剧院不为受教，也不为让道德境界升华。不过作为补偿，我也不难取悦。对一出合乎我原始口味的简单戏文，我能拿出几分田园式的鉴赏力，从演出里榨出最大限度的乐趣；所以这一回，终幕落下、观众起立时，我从那搁得不大牢靠的地方抢救出帽子，转身外行，自觉这一下午过得着实愉快。

===Original===
Emerging from the theatre, borne on the outgoing stream, I presently found myself opposite the door of a teashop. Instinct﻿—the five o’clock instinct this time﻿—guided me in; for we are creatures of habit, especially of the tea habit. The unoccupied table to which I drifted was in a shady corner not very far from the pay-desk; and here I had been seated less than a minute when a lady passed me on her way to the farther table. The glimpse that I caught of her as she approached﻿—it was but a glimpse, since she passed behind me﻿—showed that she was dressed in black, that she wore a beaded veil and hat, and in addition to the glass of milk and the bun that she carried, she was encumbered by an umbrella and a small basket, apparently containing some kind of needlework. I must confess that I gave her very little attention at the time, being occupied in anxious speculation as to how long it would be before the fact of my presence would impinge on the consciousness of the waitress.

===Chinese===
随着散场的人流涌出剧院，不多时我便站到了一家茶室门口的对面。本能——这一回是五点钟的本能——引我进去；因为我们都是习惯的造物，尤其是喝茶的习惯。我漂到一张空桌旁；桌子在个背阴的角落，离收银台不远。我在这里坐下还不到一分钟，一位女士打我身边经过，往更靠里的桌子去。她走近时我瞥了那一眼——也不过是瞥一眼，因为她自我背后绕行——看得出她一身黑衣，戴着缀珠的面纱和帽子；除了一杯牛奶和一只小圆面包，手里还累赘着一把雨伞和一只小提篮，篮里瞧着像装着什么针线活计。我必须承认，当时我几乎没把她放在心上，因为正忙着焦灼地揣测：还要多久，我这位来客的事实才能撞进那女侍的意识里去。

===Original===
The exact time by the clock on the wall was three minutes and a quarter, at the expiration of which an anaemic young woman sauntered up to the table and bestowed on me a glance of sullen interrogation, as if mutely demanding what the devil I wanted. I humbly requested that I might be provided with a pot of tea; whereupon she turned on her heel (which was a good deal worn down on the offside) and reported my conduct to a lady behind a marble-topped counter.

===Chinese===
照墙上挂钟的准确计时，历时三分又一刻（十五秒）；这一期限届满时，一个脸色贫血的年轻女人踱到桌前，赏了我一眼忿忿的质问，仿佛不出声地问我到底要什么鬼名堂。我谦卑地请求赏一壶茶；于是她靴跟一转（那只跟子的外侧已磨去了好一截），把我的行径呈报给大理石面柜台后面的一位女士。

===Original===
It seemed that the counter lady took a lenient view of the case, for in less than four minutes the waitress returned and gloomily deposited on the table before me a teapot, a milk-jug, a cup and saucer, a jug of hot water, and a small pool of milk. Then she once more departed in dudgeon.

===Chinese===
看来柜台后那位女士对此案持宽仁之见，因为不出四分钟，女侍回来了，阴沉沉地在我桌上放下茶壶、奶罐、带托的茶杯、一罐热水，和一小汪牛奶。随后她又一次忿然而去。

===Original===
I had just given the tea in the pot a preliminary stir and was about to pour out the first cup when I felt someone bump lightly against my chair and heard something rattle on the floor. I turned quickly and perceived the lady, whom I had seen enter, stooping just behind my chair. It seemed that having finished her frugal meal she was on her way out when she had dropped the little basket that I had noticed hanging from her wrist; which basket had promptly disgorged its entire contents on the floor.

===Chinese===
我刚把壶里的茶先搅了一遭，正要斟头一杯，忽觉有人轻轻撞上我的椅子，又听见什么东西在地板上哗啦啦一响。我急忙回头，看见我进来时见过的那位女士，正俯身在我椅后。原来她用完了那顿清简的饭食，正要出去，却把我先前留意到挂在她腕上的那只小提篮失手掉了；那篮子登时把满腹的内容尽数吐在了地板上。

===Original===
Now everyone must have noticed the demon of agility that seems to enter into an inanimate object when it is dropped, and the apparently intelligent malice with which it discovers, and rolls into, the most inaccessible places. Here was a case in point. This particular basket had contained materials for Oriental bead-work; and no sooner had it reached the floor than each item of its contents appeared to become possessed of a separate and particular devil impelling it to travel at headlong speed to some remote and unapproachable corner as distant as possible from its fellows.

===Chinese===
想来人人都不难留意到：一件无生命的东西一旦落地，就仿佛有股机灵的邪灵附了体，带着那种显然富于心智的歹毒，专会寻着最够不着的旮旯滚进去。眼前正是一例。这只提篮里装的是东方串珠活计的材料；篮子一沾地板，里头的每一件东西便仿佛各各着了一个专门附体的魔鬼，驱着它没命地奔向某个遥远难近的角落，离它的同伴们越远越好。

===Original===
As the only man﻿—and almost the only person﻿—near, the duty of salvage-agent manifestly devolved upon me; and down I went, accordingly, on my hands and knees, regardless of a nearly new pair of trousers, to grope under tables, chairs and settles in reach of the scattered treasure. A ball of the thick thread or twine I recovered from a dark and dirty corner after a brief interview with the sharp corner of a settle, and a multitude of the large beads with which this infernal industry is carried on I gathered from all parts of the compass, coming forth at length (quadrupedally) with a double handful of the treasure-trove and a very lively appreciation of the resistant qualities of a cast-iron table-stand when applied to the human cranium.

===Chinese===
作为近旁唯一的男人——而且几乎是唯一在场的人——打捞代理人之责显然落到了我头上；于是我不顾一条近乎全新的裤子，就此双手双膝着地，在够得着的桌椅和高背长椅底下摸索那散落的珍宝。那团粗线绳是我在一个阴暗肮脏的角落里、同高背长椅的尖角短暂会晤之后捞回来的；至于这门地狱行当所用的那许多大珠子，我从四面八方一一拾拢，最后（四肢着地地）钻将出来，双手捧满这批拾获之物，同时对铸铁桌柱施加于人类颅骨时的抗撞性能，获得了非常鲜活的体会。

===Original===
The owner of the lost and found property was greatly distressed by the accident and the trouble it had caused me; in fact she was quite needlessly agitated about it. The hand which held the basket into which I poured the rescued trash trembled visibly, and the brief glance that I bestowed on her as she murmured her thanks and apologies﻿—with a very slight foreign accent﻿—showed me that she was excessively pale. That much I could see plainly in spite of the rather dim light in this part of the shop and the beaded veil that covered her face; and I could also see that she was a rather remarkable looking woman, with a great mass of harsh, black hair and very broad black eyebrows that nearly met above her nose and contrasted strikingly with the dead white of her skin. But, of course, I did not look at her intently. Having returned her property and received her acknowledgments, I resumed my seat and left her to go on her way.

===Chinese===
这位失物复得之物的物主，为这桩意外和给我添的麻烦深感不安；事实上她的激动全无必要。她一手提着篮子，我往里倾倒那些抢救回来的破烂时，那只手看得见地发着抖；她低声道谢致歉时——带着极轻微的外国口音——我投去的那匆匆一瞥告诉我，她苍白得厉害。尽管店堂这一带光线相当昏暗，又有缀珠面纱罩着脸，这一点我还是看得分明；我还看得出她是个相貌颇为触目的女人，一头浓密粗硬的黑发，两道极宽的黑眉毛几乎在鼻梁上方连成一片，同她死白的皮肤相衬，格外醒目。不过，我自然没有紧盯细看。归还了她的物件、领受了她的谢意之后，我便回到座位上，任她自去。

===Original===
I had once more grasped the handle of the teapot when I made a rather curious discovery. At the bottom of the teacup lay a single lump of sugar. To the majority of persons it would have meant nothing. They would have assumed that they had dropped it in and forgotten it and would have proceeded to pour out the tea. But it happened that, at this time, I did not take sugar in my tea; whence it followed that the lump had not been put in by me. Assuming, therefore, that it had been carelessly dropped in by the waitress, I turned it out on the table, filled the cup, added the milk, and took a tentative draught to test the temperature.

===Chinese===
我重新握住茶壶柄时，有了一个颇为古怪的发现。茶杯底上躺着一块方糖。对多数人来说，这什么也算不上。他们会以为是自己放进去忘了，然后照旧斟茶不误。可偏偏这时候我喝茶是不放糖的；由此推论，那块糖不是我放的。于是我便认定是女侍粗心失手掉的，把它倒在桌上，斟满茶，兑上奶，试探地呷了一口试试温度。

===Original===
The cup was yet at my lips when I chanced to look into the mirror that faced my table. Of course it reflected the part of the shop that was behind me, including the cashier’s desk; at which the owner of the basket now stood paying for her refreshment. Between her and me was a gas chandelier which cast its light on my back but full on her face; and her veil notwithstanding, I could see that she was looking at me steadily; was, in fact, watching me intently and with a very curious expression﻿—an expression of expectancy mingled with alarm. But this was not all. As I returned her intent look﻿—which I could do unobserved, since my face, reflected in the mirror, was in deep shadow﻿—I suddenly perceived that that steady gaze engaged her right eye only; the other eye was looking sharply towards her left shoulder. In short, she had a divergent squint of the left eye.

===Chinese===
茶杯尚在唇边，我碰巧朝我桌上正对的那面镜子里望了一眼。镜中自然映出我身后的那片店堂，包括收银台；提篮的主人此刻正站在台前付她的茶点钱。她与我之间悬着一盏煤气枝形吊灯，灯光照在我的背上，却正正照在她脸上；虽隔着面纱，我看得出她正定定地望着我；实际上是在凝神注视我，带着一种极古怪的神情——一种期待与惊惶交织的神情。还不止于此。当我回敬她那凝注的目光时——我可以这样而不被察觉，因为我的脸映在镜中，正处在深影里——我突然发觉，那道定定的凝视只用她的右眼；另一只眼正锐利地斜向她自己的左肩。一言以蔽之，她的左眼有散开性斜视。

===Original===
I put down my cup with a thrill of amazement and a sudden surging up of suspicion and alarm. An instant’s reflection reminded me that when she had spoken to me a few moments before, both her eyes had looked into mine without the slightest trace of a squint. My thoughts flew back to the lump of sugar, to the unguarded milk-jug and the draught of tea that I had already swallowed; and, hardly knowing what I intended, I started to my feet and turned to confront her. But as I rose, she snatched up her change and darted from the shop. Through the glass door, I saw her spring on to the footboard of a passing hansom and give the driver some direction. I saw the man whip up his horse, and, by the time I reached the door, the cab was moving off swiftly towards Sloane Street.

===Chinese===
我放下杯子，一阵惊愕窜过全身，疑心与警觉也骤然涌起。转念一想便记起：方才片刻之前她同我说话时，那双眼睛是直直望进我眼里的，没有丝毫斜视的痕迹。我的思绪飞回那块方糖，飞向那只无人看守的奶罐和我已经下咽的那口茶；于是我霍地起身，转向她去——自己也几乎不知要做什么。可我刚站起来，她已一把抓起找头，疾奔出店。隔着玻璃门，我看见她跃上一辆路过的双轮马车的踏板，向车夫吩咐了一句去处。我看见那人扬鞭催马；待我赶到门口，马车已朝斯隆街（Sloane Street）飞快驶远了。

===Original===
I stood irresolute. I had not paid and could not run out of the shop without making a fuss, and my hat and stick were still on the rail opposite my seat. The woman ought to be followed, but I had no fancy for the task. If the tea that I had swallowed was innocuous, no harm was done and I was rid of my pursuer. So far as I was concerned, the incident was closed. I went back to my seat, and picking up the lump of sugar which still lay on the table where I had dropped it, put it carefully in my pocket. But my appetite for tea was satisfied for the present. Moreover it was hardly advisable to stay in the shop lest some fresh spy should come to see how I fared. Accordingly I obtained my check, handed it in at the cashier’s desk and took my departure.

===Chinese===
我站着，进退两难。账还没付，不闹出动静就没法冲出店去；帽子手杖还搁在我座位对面的衣帽杆上。照理这女人该跟踪下去，可我实在无心担这差事。倘若我咽下的那口茶无毒，便没造成任何伤害，我也就此甩掉了跟踪我的人。就我而论，这场事变算是了结了。我回到座位上，拾起那块方糖——它还躺在我倒它的地方——小心收进口袋。不过眼下我的茶兴已经足矣。再说，留在店里也殊非明智，免得再来一个探子瞧瞧我的情形。于是我取了账单，交到收银台，离店而去。

===Original===
All this time, it will be observed, I had been taking it for granted that the lady in black had followed me from Kensington to this shop; that, in fact, she was none other than Mrs. Schallibaum. And, indeed, the circumstances had rendered the conclusion inevitable. In the very instant when I had perceived the displacement of the left eye, complete recognition had come upon me. When I had stood facing the woman, the brief glance at her face had conveyed to me something dimly reminiscent of which I had been but half conscious and had instantly forgotten. But the sight of that characteristic squint had at once revived and explained it. That the woman was Mrs. Schallibaum I now felt no doubt whatever.

===Chinese===
可以注意到，自始至终我都理所当然地认定：那黑衣女士是从肯辛顿一路跟到这家茶室来的；换言之，她不是别人，正是沙利鲍姆太太。事实上，种种情形也使这个结论无可逃避。就在我察觉那只左眼错位的同一瞬，完整的认出已整个袭上心头。方才我面对她站着时，对她脸上那匆匆一瞥曾传递给我某种隐隐约约似曾相识的东西，我当时只半意识地觉着，随即忘却了。可一看到那标志性的斜视，它顿时复活了，也得到了解释。这女人就是沙利鲍姆太太——如今我对此再无半点怀疑。

===Original===
Nevertheless, the whole affair was profoundly mysterious. As to the change in the woman’s appearance, there was little in that. The coarse, black hair might be her own, dyed, or it might be a wig. The eyebrows were made-up; it was a simple enough proceeding and made still more simple by the beaded veil. But how did she come to be there at all? How did she happen to be made-up in this fashion at this particular time? And, above all, how came she to be provided with a lump of what I had little doubt was poisoned sugar?

===Chinese===
尽管如此，这整桩事仍旧深不可解。至于这女人外貌上的变化，倒算不得什么。那粗硬的黑发也许是她本来的，染过色，也许是假发。眉毛是画出来的；这手续够简单的了，隔着缀珠面纱就更简单。可她究竟怎么会出现在那里？又怎么偏偏在这个时辰化好了这副装束？而最要紧的是，她身边怎么会备着一块几乎可以断定下了毒的方糖？

===Original===
I turned over the events of the day, and the more I considered them the less comprehensible they appeared. No one had followed the omnibus either on foot or in a vehicle, as far as I could see; and I had kept a careful lookout, not only at starting but for some considerable time after. Yet, all the time, Mrs. Schallibaum must have been following. But how? If she had known that I was intending to travel by the omnibus she might have gone to meet it and entered before I did. But she could not have known: and moreover she did not meet the omnibus, for we watched its approach from some considerable distance. I considered whether she might not have been concealed in the house and overheard me mention my destination to Thorndyke. But this failed to explain the mystery, since I had mentioned no address beyond “Kensington.” I had, indeed, mentioned the name of Mrs. Hornby, but the supposition that my friends might be known by name to Mrs. Schallibaum, or even that she might have looked the name up in the directory, presented a probability too remote to be worth entertaining.

===Chinese===
我把这一天的种种事情翻来覆去地想，越想越不可解。据我所见，无论步行还是乘车，都没有人跟在那辆公共马车后头；而我一直留着神，不光动身时，此后好一段工夫也一样。然而沙利鲍姆太太必然全程缀在后面。可她是怎样缀上的？倘若她知道我打算乘公共马车，大可以赶去迎它，先我一步上车。可她不可能知道：何况她也没有去迎那辆马车，因为我们隔着老远就望着它驶来了。我又想，她会不会是藏在宅子里，偷听到我向桑代克报了去处。可这解释不了这个谜，因为我除了“肯辛顿”这个字眼，没有提到任何地址。我确实提过霍恩比太太的名字，可是设想我的朋友们会被沙利鲍姆太太闻知名姓、甚至设想她会翻着姓名录查到这个名字，其可能性渺茫得不值一顾。

===Original===
But, if I reached no satisfactory conclusion, my cogitations had one useful effect; they occupied my mind to the exclusion of that unfortunate draught of tea. Not that I had been seriously uneasy after the first shock. The quantity that I had swallowed was not large﻿—the tea being hotter than I cared for﻿—and I remembered that, when I had thrown out the lump of sugar, I had turned the cup upside down on the table; so there could have been nothing solid left in it. And the lump of sugar was in itself reassuring, for it certainly would not have been used in conjunction with any less conspicuous but more incriminating form of poison. That lump of sugar was now in my pocket, reserved for careful examination at my leisure; and I reflected with a faint grin that it would be a little disconcerting if it should turn out to contain nothing but sugar after all.

===Chinese===
不过，我虽没有得出一个满意的结论，这番苦思却有一桩好处：它占满了我的心神，把那倒霉的一口茶挤了出去。倒不是说我受了最初那一惊之后还当真不安。我咽下的量并不大——那茶比我爱喝的烫了些——而且我记得，把那块方糖倒出来之后，我曾把杯子倒扣在桌上；所以杯里不可能再剩下什么固体的东西。再者，那块方糖本身也令人宽心，因为下毒的人决不会拿它同某种不这么显眼、却更容易定罪的毒药并用。那块方糖此刻就在我口袋里，留着得空细细检验；我略带苦笑地想道：万一到头来它里头除了糖什么也没有，那可就有点叫人下不来台了。

===Original===
On leaving the teashop, I walked up Sloane Street with the intention of doing what I ought to have done earlier in the day. I was going to make perfectly sure that no spy was dogging my footsteps. But for my ridiculous confidence I could have done so quite easily before going to Endsley Gardens; and now, made wiser by a startling experience, I proceeded with systematic care. It was still broad daylight﻿—for the lamps in the teashop had been rendered necessary only by the faulty construction of the premises and the dullness of the afternoon﻿—and in an open space I could see far enough for complete safety. Arriving at the top of Sloane Street, I crossed Knightsbridge, and, entering Hyde Park, struck out towards the Serpentine. Passing along the eastern shore, I entered one of the long paths that lead towards the Marble Arch and strode along it at such a pace as would make it necessary for any pursuer to hurry in order to keep me in sight. Halfway across the great stretch of turf, I halted for a few moments and noted the few people who were coming in my direction. Then I turned sharply to the left and headed straight for the Victoria Gate, but again, halfway, I turned off among a clump of trees, and, standing behind the trunk of one of them, took a fresh survey of the people who were moving along the paths. All were at a considerable distance and none appeared to be coming my way.

===Chinese===
出了茶室，我便沿斯隆街而上，打算补做一件本该在白天早些时候做的事：确切查明没有探子在缀着我的脚步。若不是我那份可笑的自信，去恩斯利花园之前本可轻易办到；如今经这场惊心动魄的历练长了智慧，我便有条不紊地谨慎行事。此刻仍是白昼大亮——茶室里点灯，只因房舍构造欠佳、加上午后天色阴沉的缘故——在开阔地里，我放眼望去，尽可望得足够远，保得周全。走到斯隆街尽头，我穿过骑士桥（Knightsbridge），进了海德公园（Hyde Park），朝蛇形湖（the Serpentine）方向进发。沿东岸走了一程，我踏上一条通往大理石拱门（Marble Arch）的长径，迈开大步，走得叫任何跟踪者若想不丢了我，就非得赶急不可。走到那大片草坪中途，我停了片刻，留意了寥寥几个迎面而来的人。随后我猛地左转，直奔维多利亚门（Victoria Gate）而去；但行至半途，又折进一丛树木，站到其中一棵树干后面，重新打量径上来来往往的人。人人都离得相当远，没有一个像是朝我这边来的。

===Original===
I now moved cautiously from one tree to another and passed through the wooded region to the south, crossed the Serpentine bridge at a rapid walk and hurrying along the south shore left the Park by Apsley House. From hence I walked at the same rapid pace along Piccadilly, insinuating myself among the crowd with the skill born of long acquaintance with the London streets, crossed amidst the seething traffic at the Circus, darted up Windmill Street and began to zigzag amongst the narrow streets and courts of Soho. Crossing the Seven Dials and Drury Lane I passed through the multitudinous back-streets and alleys that then filled the area south of Lincoln’s Inn, came out by Newcastle Street, Holywell Street and Half-Moon Alley into the Strand, which I crossed immediately, ultimately entering the Temple by Devereux Court.

===Chinese===
这时我小心翼翼地从一棵树挪到另一棵树，穿过南面的林地，快步跨过蛇形湖桥，又沿南岸疾行，从阿普斯利宅（Apsley House）旁出了公园。由此我照旧疾行，沿皮卡迪利大街（Piccadilly）走去，凭着熟稔伦敦街巷练出的功夫，见缝插针地混进人流，在皮卡迪利广场（the Circus）沸反盈天的车马中人缝里穿过，一头窜上风磨街（Windmill Street），开始在那索霍区（Soho）的窄街小巷之间穿来绕去。横过七晷区（Seven Dials）和德鲁里巷（Drury Lane），我又穿行于当时挤满林肯律师学院（Lincoln’s Inn）以南一带的无数后街小巷，从纽卡斯尔街（Newcastle Street）、霍利韦尔街（Holywell Street）和半月巷（Half-Moon Alley）口出来到了河滨街，随即将它横穿过去，最后由德弗罗巷（Devereux Court）进了坦普尔。

===Original===
Even then I did not relax my precautions. From one court to another I passed quickly, loitering in those dark entries and unexpected passages that are known to so few but the regular Templars, and coming out into the open only at the last where the wide passage of King’s Bench Walk admits of no evasion. Halfway up the stairs, I stood for some time in the shadow, watching the approaches from the staircase window; and when, at length, I felt satisfied that I had taken every precaution that was possible, I inserted my key and let myself into our chambers.

===Chinese===
即便那时我也不敢松懈。我从一个小院疾行到另一个小院，在那些除了坦普尔的常客少有人识的暗门道和出人意料的过廊里逡巡逗留，直到最后才走到明处——王座法庭步道那条宽宽的过道，已容不得闪避藏身。上楼到一半，我在阴影里站了好一会儿，从楼梯窗监看各处通道；直到终于自觉已尽了一切可能的提防，才把钥匙插进锁里，进了我们的寓所。

===Original===
Thorndyke had already arrived, and, as I entered, he rose to greet me with an expression of evident relief.

===Chinese===
桑代克已经回来了；我进门时，他起身相迎，一脸如释重负的神情。

===Original===
“I am glad to see you, Jervis,” he said. “I have been rather anxious about you.”

===Chinese===
“见到你真高兴，杰维斯，”他说，“我一直为你有些挂心。”

===Original===
“Why?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“为什么？”我问。

===Original===
“For several reasons. One is that you are the sole danger that threatens these people﻿—as far as they know. Another is that we made a most ridiculous mistake. We overlooked a fact that ought to have struck us instantly. But how have you fared?”

===Chinese===
“有好几层缘故。一是就他们所知，你是威胁这伙人的唯一危险。二是我们犯了一个荒唐透顶的错误，漏想了一件本该一下子就撞进眼里的事实。你这一路怎么样？”

===Original===
“Better than I deserved. That good lady stuck to me like a burr﻿—at least I believe she did.”

===Chinese===
“比我应得的好。那位好太太像牛蒡果一样粘住了我——至少我相信是如此。”

===Original===
“I have no doubt she did. We have been caught napping finely, Jervis.”

===Chinese===
“她必然如此，毫无疑问。咱们可是结结实实地打了回盹，杰维斯。”

===Original===
“How?”

===Chinese===
“怎么说？”

===Original===
“We’ll go into that presently. Let us hear about your adventures first.”

===Chinese===
“这个回头再谈。先听听你的历险。”

===Original===
I gave him a full account of my movements from the time when we parted to that of my arrival home, omitting no incident that I was able to remember and, as far as I could, reconstituting my exceedingly devious homeward route.

===Chinese===
我从我们分手讲起，一直讲到抵家，把自己的行踪原原本本说了一遍，凡记得起的事一桩不落，又尽力把那条极其迂回的回家路线重新拼了出来。

===Original===
“Your retreat was masterly,” he remarked with a broad smile. “I should think that it would have utterly defeated any pursuer; and the only pity is that it was probably wasted on the desert air. Your pursuer had by that time become a fugitive. But you were wise to take these precautions, for, of course, Weiss might have followed you.”

===Chinese===
“你这场撤退堪称大师手笔，”他咧嘴笑道，“我想它足以叫任何跟踪者一败涂地；唯一可惜的是，多半是对着荒漠白费了口舌——到那时，追你的人早已成了逃亡的人。不过你这些提防是明智的，因为韦斯当然也可能缀着你。”

===Original===
“But I thought he was in Hamburg?”

===Chinese===
“可我以为他在汉堡呢？”

===Original===
“Did you? You are a very confiding young gentleman, for a budding medical jurist. Of course we don’t know that he is not; but the fact that he has given Hamburg as his present whereabouts establishes a strong presumption that he is somewhere else. I only hope that he has not located you, and, from what you tell me of your later methods, I fancy that you would have shaken him off even if he had started to follow you from the teashop.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？对一个初出茅庐的医学法学家来说，你这位年轻绅士实在轻信得可以。我们当然不知道他不在汉堡；但他把汉堡报作眼下的落脚处，这一事实本身就强烈地预示他人不在此地。我只盼他没有查到你的下落；而照你讲的后半程那些手段，我想即便他从茶室就缀上你，你也能把他甩脱。”

===Original===
“I hope so too. But how did that woman manage to stick to me in that way? What was the mistake we made?”

===Chinese===
“我也希望如此。可那女人究竟怎么钉上我的？我们犯的又是什么错？”

===Original===
Thorndyke laughed grimly. “It was a perfectly asinine mistake, Jervis. You started up Kennington Park Road on a leisurely, jog-trotting omnibus, and neither you nor I remembered what there is underneath Kennington Park Road.”

===Chinese===
桑代克冷冷一笑。“那是个十足的蠢驴式错误，杰维斯。你乘着一辆慢吞吞颠儿颠儿的公共马车沿肯宁顿公园路出发，你我竟然都没想起来肯宁顿公园路的底下有什么。”

===Original===
“Underneath!” I exclaimed, completely puzzled for the moment. Then, suddenly realizing what he meant, “Of course!” I exclaimed. “Idiot that I am! You mean the electric railway?”

===Chinese===
“底下！”我叫出声来，一时全然懵住。随即恍然大悟：“可不是嘛！”我又叫道，“我这蠢材！你是说那条电气铁路？”

===Original===
“Yes. That explains everything. Mrs. Schallibaum must have watched us from some shop and quietly followed us up the lane. There were a good many women about and several were walking in our direction. There was nothing to distinguish her from the others unless you had recognized her, which you would hardly have been able to do if she had worn a veil and kept at a fair distance. At least I think not.”

===Chinese===
“正是。这一下全说通了。沙利鲍姆太太必定是从哪家铺子里监视着我们，又悄悄跟进了巷子。那一带女人不少，有几个正朝我们这个方向走。你若不曾认出她，便无从把她从旁人里分辨出来；而她若罩着面纱、隔着一段稳妥的距离，你也几乎无从认出她。至少我想是认不出的。”

===Original===
“No,” I agreed, “I certainly should not. I had only seen her in a half-dark room. In outdoor clothes and with a veil, I should never have been able to identify her without very close inspection. Besides there was the disguise or makeup.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我表示同意，“我确实认不出。我只在半明半暗的房间里见过她。换了出门的装束，再加上面纱，不经贴近细看，我决计辨认不出她来。何况还有那化装易容呢。”

===Original===
“Not at that time. She would hardly come disguised to her own house, for it might have led to her being challenged and asked who she was. I think we may take it that there was no actual disguise, although she would probably wear a shady hat and a veil; which would have prevented either of us from picking her out from the other women in the street.”

===Chinese===
“那时还没有。她总不至于化着装回自己家去，那可能招人盘问她的身份。我想我们可以认定她当时并未真的化装，只是多半戴了顶遮阴的帽子、罩了面纱；单是这两样，就足以叫我们谁也无法把她从街上其他女人里挑出来了。”

===Original===
“And what do you think happened next?”

===Chinese===
“那你觉得接下来如何？”

===Original===
“I think that she simply walked past us﻿—probably on the other side of the road﻿—as we stood waiting for the omnibus, and turned up Kennington Park Road. She probably guessed that we were waiting for the omnibus and walked up the road in the direction in which it was going. Presently the omnibus would pass her, and there were you in full view on top keeping a vigilant lookout in the wrong direction. Then she would quicken her pace a little and in a minute or two would arrive at the Kennington Station of the South London Railway. In a minute or two more she would be in one of the electric trains whirling along under the street on which your omnibus was crawling. She would get out at the Borough Station, or she might take a more risky chance and go on to the Monument; but in any case she would wait for your omnibus, hail it and get inside. I suppose you took up some passengers on the way?”

===Chinese===
“我想，我们站在那里等公共马车时，她索性从我们身边走了过去——多半在街对面——然后拐上了肯宁顿公园路。她多半猜到了我们是在等公共马车，便顺着马车要去的方向沿路走去。不多时，公共马车就会从她身旁驶过，而你正在顶层一览无余，朝着错误的方向警惕瞭望。于是她稍稍加快脚步，一两分钟后便到了南伦敦铁路（South London Railway）的肯宁顿站。再过一两分钟，她已坐上一列电气火车，在你那辆公共马车爬行的街道底下飞驰。她会在鲍勒站（Borough Station）下车，也可能冒个更大的险，一直坐到纪念碑站（the Monument）；但无论如何，她都会等来你的公共马车，招手拦下，走进车厢里去。我想你们路上捎过一些乘客吧？”

===Original===
“Oh dear, yes. We were stopping every two or three minutes to take up or set down passengers; and most of them were women.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛。每过两三分钟就停一停，接客的下客的都有；而且大部分是女的。”

===Original===
“Very well; then we may take it that when you arrived at the Mansion House, Mrs. Schallibaum was one of your inside passengers. It was a rather quaint situation, I think.”

===Chinese===
“很好；那么可以认定，你到达市长官邸时，沙利鲍姆太太正是你车厢里的乘客之一。这局面想想也着实妙。”

===Original===
“Yes, confound her! What a couple of noodles she must have thought us!”

===Chinese===
“可不是，真该死！她心里必定把咱们当成一对十足的傻瓜！”

===Original===
“No doubt. And that is the one consoling feature in the case. She will have taken us for a pair of absolute greenhorns. But to continue. Of course she travelled in your omnibus to Kensington﻿—you ought to have gone inside on both occasions, so that you could see everyone who entered and examine the inside passengers; she will have followed you to Endsley Gardens and probably noted the house you went to. Thence she will have followed you to the restaurant and may even have lunched there.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问。而这正是本案唯一令人宽慰的一点。她必是把咱们当成一双地道的外行嫩角了。接着往下说。她自然乘着你的公共马车到了肯辛顿——两趟车你都该坐进车厢里去，好看着每一个上车的人、把车厢里的乘客端详个遍——她会跟着你到恩斯利花园，多半还记下了你进去的那所宅子。然后她会跟你到那家餐馆，说不定还在那儿用过午饭。”

===Original===
“It is quite possible,” said I. “There were two rooms and they were filled principally with women.”

===Chinese===
“大有可能，”我说，“那里头有两间厅，坐的主要是女客。”

===Original===
“Then she will have followed you to Sloane Street, and, as you persisted in riding outside, she could easily take an inside place in your omnibus. As to the theatre, she must have taken it as a veritable gift of the gods; an arrangement made by you for her special convenience.”

===Chinese===
“然后她会跟你到斯隆街；既然你执意坐在外面车顶，她尽可以轻而易举地在你那辆公共马车车厢里占个座。至于那家剧院，她必定把它看作诸神实实在在的恩赐——你专为她的方便作出的安排。”

===Original===
“Why?”

===Chinese===
“为什么？”

===Original===
“My dear fellow! consider. She had only to follow you in and see you safely into your seat and there you were, left till called for. She could then go home, make up for her part; draw out a plan of action, with the help, perhaps, of Mr. Weiss, provide herself with the necessary means and appliances and, at the appointed time, call and collect you.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的伙计！想想看。她只消跟着你进场，亲眼看你安稳落座，你就此存局待取了。她随后便可以回家，为她的角色上妆；或许还在韦斯先生相助之下拟一套行动计划，备齐必需的手段与器具，到了约定时刻，再来把你领取。”

===Original===
“That is assuming a good deal,” I objected. “It is assuming, for instance, that she lives within a moderate distance of Sloane Square. Otherwise it would have been impossible.”

===Chinese===
“这假定未免太多，”我提出异议，“比如，这等于假定她住得离斯隆广场不远。不然就绝无可能。”

===Original===
“Exactly. That is why I assume it. You don’t suppose that she goes about habitually with lumps of prepared sugar in her pocket. And if not, then she must have got that lump from somewhere. Then the beads suggest a carefully prepared plan, and, as I said just now, she can hardly have been made-up when she met us in Kennington Lane. From all of which it seems likely that her present abode is not very far from Sloane Square.”

===Chinese===
“正是。所以我才这样假定。你总不至于想，她平素出门，口袋里就揣着调制好的方糖吧。倘若不然，那她那块糖必是从某个地方弄来的。再者，那些珠子也说明这是一套精心预备的计划；而且方才说过，她在肯宁顿巷迎着我们时，多半还没上妆。综观种种，看来她如今的落脚处离斯隆广场不会太远。”

===Original===
“At any rate,” said I, “it was taking a considerable risk. I might have left the theatre before she came back.”

===Chinese===
“无论如何，”我说，“这风险可不小。她说不定还没回来，我就已经离了剧院。”

===Original===
“Yes,” Thorndyke agreed. “But it is like a woman to take chances. A man would probably have stuck to you when once he had got you off your guard. But she was ready to take chances. She chanced the railway, and it came off; she chanced your remaining in the theatre, and that came off too. She calculated on the probability of your getting tea when you came out, and she hit it off again. And then she took one chance too many; she assumed that you probably took sugar in your tea, and she was wrong.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克同意道，“可女人就是爱赌运气。换了男人，一旦把你诱离了戒心，多半会钉住你不放。她却随时肯赌。她赌了那趟铁路，赢了；赌你留在剧院里，又赢了。她算准你出场后多半要喝茶，又给她猜中。随后她多赌了一把；她假定你喝茶多半放糖——这一把却赌输了。”

===Original===
“We are taking it for granted that the sugar was prepared,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“咱们这是把那糖认定为预先调制过的了，”我说。

===Original===
“Yes. Our explanation is entirely hypothetical and may be entirely wrong. But it all hangs together, and if we find any poisonous matter in the sugar, it will be reasonable to assume that we are right. The sugar is the Experimentum Crucis. If you will hand it over to me, we will go up to the laboratory and make a preliminary test or two.”

===Chinese===
“不错。我们的解释纯属假设，也可能全盘皆错。但它各个环节互相咬合；倘若我们在糖里查出任何毒质，那么认定我们没错就是合理的了。这块糖就是那“判决性实验”（Experimentum Crucis）。你把它交给我，咱们上实验室去，做上一两项初步检验。”

===Original===
I took the lump of sugar from my pocket and gave it to him, and he carried it to the gas-burner, by the light of which he examined it with a lens.

===Chinese===
我从口袋里取出那块方糖交给他；他把它拿到煤气灯下，就着灯光用放大镜检视。

===Original===
“I don’t see any foreign crystals on the surface,” said he; “but we had better make a solution and go to work systematically. If it contains any poison we may assume that it will be some alkaloid, though I will test for arsenic too. But a man of Weiss’s type would almost certainly use an alkaloid, on account of its smaller bulk and more ready solubility. You ought not to have carried this loose in your pocket. For legal purposes that would seriously interfere with its value as evidence. Bodies that are suspected of containing poison should be carefully isolated and preserved from contact with anything that might lead to doubt in the analysis. It doesn’t matter much to us, as this analysis is only for our own information and we can satisfy ourselves as to the state of your pocket. But bear the rule in mind another time.”

===Chinese===
“表面上看不见什么异样的晶体，”他说，“不过咱们最好把它溶成溶液，按部就班地做。倘若它含有毒物，我们可以假定那会是某种生物碱，尽管砷我也要验一验。但韦斯这一路的人，几乎必然会用生物碱，因为它体积小，又易溶。你不该把它松松地揣在口袋里。若为法律用途，这将严重损害它作为证据的价值。凡疑含毒物之物，都应仔细隔离保存，避免接触任何可能在分析中引起疑问的东西。对咱们关系不大，因为这次分析只供我们自己参考，你口袋里原先的情形我们自己也能查个明白。不过这条规矩，下回要记取。”

===Original===
We now ascended to the laboratory, where Thorndyke proceeded at once to dissolve the lump of sugar in a measured quantity of distilled water by the aid of gentle heat.

===Chinese===
这时我们上楼到实验室去；桑代克当即动手，借文火把那块方糖溶进量好的蒸馏水里。

===Original===
“Before we add any acid,” said he, “or introduce any fresh matter, we will adopt the simple preliminary measure of tasting the solution. The sugar is a disturbing factor, but some of the alkaloids and most mineral poisons excepting arsenic have a very characteristic taste.”

===Chinese===
“在加任何酸、或引入任何新东西之前，”他说，“咱们先采用尝味这一简单的初步手段。糖是个干扰因素，但有些生物碱和除砷以外的大多数矿物毒物，都有极特征的味道。”

===Original===
He dipped a glass rod in the warm solution and applied it gingerly to his tongue.

===Chinese===
他把一根玻璃棒浸进温热的溶液里，小心翼翼地点到自己舌上。

===Original===
“Ha!” he exclaimed, as he carefully wiped his mouth with his handkerchief, “simple methods are often very valuable. There isn’t much doubt as to what is in that sugar. Let me recommend my learned brother to try the flavour. But be careful. A little of this will go a long way.”

===Chinese===
“哈！”他一面用手帕仔细拭嘴，一面叫道，“简单的方法往往极有价值。那糖里是什么，已没有多少疑问了。容我向本人这位博学的同仁荐一荐此味。不过小心，此物一点点就够受的。”

===Original===
He took a fresh rod from the rack, and, dipping it in the solution, handed it to me. I cautiously applied it to the tip of my tongue and was immediately aware of a peculiar tingling sensation accompanied by a feeling of numbness.

===Chinese===
他从架上取了一根新玻璃棒，蘸了溶液，递给我。我谨慎地点到舌尖，立刻觉出一种奇异的刺麻感，伴着一阵麻木。

===Original===
“Well,” said Thorndyke; “what is it?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，”桑代克说，“是什么？”

===Original===
“Aconite,” I replied without hesitation.

===Chinese===
“乌头，”我毫不迟疑地答道。

===Original===
“Yes,” he agreed; “aconite it is, or more probably aconitine. And that, I think, gives us all the information we want. We need not trouble now to make a complete analysis, though I shall have a quantitative examination made later. You note the intensity of the taste and you see what the strength of the solution is. Evidently that lump of sugar contained a very large dose of the poison. If the sugar had been dissolved in your tea, the quantity that you drank would have contained enough aconitine to lay you out within a few minutes; which would account for Mrs. Schallibaum’s anxiety to get clear of the premises. She saw you drink from the cup, but I imagine she had not seen you turn the sugar out.”

===Chinese===
“对，”他附和道，“是乌头，或者更可能是乌头碱。我想，这已给了我们想问的全部消息。完整的分析现在不必麻烦做了，尽管日后我还要做一次定量检验。你注意到味道之烈，也看得到溶液的浓淡。显然，那块方糖里含着极大的一剂毒。倘若那糖溶进了你的茶里，你喝下的那点东西所含的乌头碱，已足以叫你在几分钟之内直挺挺躺倒；这也就解释了沙利鲍姆太太何以急急要脱离现场。她看见你举杯喝了，可我想她没看见你把糖倒了出来。”

===Original===
“No, I should say not, to judge by her expression. She looked terrified. She is not as hardened as her rascally companion.”

===Chinese===
“是没有，瞧她那神色就知道。她一脸惊骇。她还不像她那恶棍同伴那样心硬。”

===Original===
“Which is fortunate for you, Jervis. If she had not been in such a fluster, she would have waited until you had poured out your tea, which was what she probably meant to do, or have dropped the sugar into the milk-jug. In either case you would have got a poisonous dose before you noticed anything amiss.”

===Chinese===
“这于你是运气，杰维斯。她若不是那样慌作一团，本会等你把茶斟出来——她原本多半就打算这样——或者把糖投进奶罐。无论哪样，不等你察觉不对，你早已吞下了足以中毒的一剂。”

===Original===
“They are a pretty pair, Thorndyke,” I exclaimed. “A human life seems to be no more to them than the life of a fly or a beetle.”

===Chinese===
“真是一对宝贝，桑代克，”我叫道，“一条人命在他们眼里，竟同一只苍蝇甲虫的命没什么两样。”

===Original===
“No; that is so. They are typical poisoners of the worst kind; of the intelligent, cautious, resourceful kind. They are a standing menace to society. As long as they are at large, human lives are in danger, and it is our business to see that they do not remain at large a moment longer than is unavoidable. And that brings us to another point. You had better keep indoors for the next few days.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，正是如此。他们是最恶劣一类的典型毒手；是有头脑、有谨慎、有手段的一类。他们是社会常年的祸患。只要他们一天逍遥法外，人命便一天处在危险之中；让他们多逍遥一刻，都是我们职责所不容。这就引出另一件事：今后几天，你最好闭门不出。”

===Original===
“Oh, nonsense,” I protested. “I can take care of myself.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，说得什么话，”我抗议道，“我自会当心自己。”

===Original===
“I won’t dispute that,” said Thorndyke, “although I might. But the matter is of vital importance and we can’t be too careful. Yours is the only evidence that could convict these people. They know that and will stick at nothing to get rid of you﻿—for by this time they will almost certainly have ascertained that the teashop plan has failed. Now your life is of some value to you and to another person whom I could mention; but apart from that, you are the indispensable instrument for ridding society of these dangerous vermin. Moreover, if you were seen abroad and connected with these chambers, they would get the information that their case was really being investigated in a businesslike manner. If Weiss has not already left the country he would do so immediately, and if he has, Mrs. Schallibaum would join him at once, and we might never be able to lay hands on them. You must stay indoors, out of sight, and you had better write to Miss Gibson and ask her to warn the servants to give no information about you to anyone.”

===Chinese===
“这我不来争，”桑代克说，“尽管我本可以争一争。但此事性命攸关，再怎么小心也不为过。能定这两人罪的证据，唯有你一人。他们明白这一点，为除掉你什么手段都使得出来——因为到这时，茶室之计已然败露，他们几乎必然已经查知了。你的性命，于你自己、于我还可以点出姓名的另一位，都有些价值；但撇开这个不谈，你是为社会除掉这两只危险害虫的必不可少的工具。再者，你若被人看见在外走动、又同这处寓所挂上钩，他们便会得到消息：他们那案子正有人按部就班地真查。韦斯倘若尚未离境，会立刻就走；倘若已走，沙利鲍姆太太也会立刻去同他会合，那咱们也许永远抓不着他们了。你必须待在屋里，避开人眼；最好还写封信给吉布森小姐，请她叮嘱仆役们，任何人问起你都不得透露消息。”

===Original===
“And how long,” I asked, “am I to be held on parole?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”我问，“我这假释在外的日子要熬到几时？”

===Original===
“Not long, I think. We have a very promising start. If I have any luck, I shall be able to collect all the evidence I want in about a week. But there is an element of chance in some of it which prevents me from giving a date. And it is just possible that I may have started on a false track. But that I shall be able to tell you better in a day or two.”

===Chinese===
“我想不长。咱们的起点大有可为。若运气尚可，一周上下我就能把需要的证据收齐。只是其中几分要凭机缘，使我无法给你个日子。也说不定我起头就走错了道。这个，过一两天我更能告诉你。”

===Original===
“And I suppose,” I said gloomily, “I shall be out of the hunt altogether?”

===Chinese===
“那我猜想，”我郁郁地说，“这趟追猎是要把我整个儿排除在外了？”

===Original===
“Not at all,” he replied. “You have got the Blackmore case to attend to. I shall hand you over all the documents and get you to make an orderly digest of the evidence. You will then have all the facts and can work out the case for yourself. Also I shall ask you to help Polton in some little operations which are designed to throw light into dark places and which you will find both entertaining and instructive.”

===Chinese===
“绝非如此，”他答道，“你还有布莱克莫尔案要料理。我会把全部案卷移交给你，请你把证据编成一份条理分明的摘要。这样你便掌握了全部事实，可以自己去推演这个案子。另外我还要请你帮波尔顿做几样小小的操作——都是设计来照亮暗处的玩意儿——你会发觉它们既好玩，又长学问。”

===Original===
“Supposing Mrs. Hornby should propose to call and take tea with us in the gardens?” I suggested.

===Chinese===
“倘若霍恩比太太提议来拜访，同我们在花园里一道用茶呢？”我提了一句。

===Original===
“And bring Miss Gibson with her?” Thorndyke added dryly. “No, Jervis, it would never do. You must make that quite clear to her. It is more probable than not that Mrs. Schallibaum made a careful note of the house in Endsley Gardens, and as that would be the one place actually known to her, she and Weiss﻿—if he is in England﻿—would almost certainly keep a watch on it. If they should succeed in connecting that house with these chambers, a few inquiries would show them the exact state of the case. No; we must keep them in the dark if we possibly can. We have shown too much of our hand already. It is hard on you, but it cannot be helped.”

===Chinese===
“还带着吉布森小姐一起来？”桑代克干巴巴地补了一句，“不成，杰维斯，万万不成。这一点你必须向她说个透彻。沙利鲍姆太太多半把恩斯利花园那所宅子仔细记下了；那既是她唯一确实知道的地方，她和韦斯——他若还在英国——几乎必定会盯着它。他们若当真把那所宅子同这处寓所挂上了钩，稍一打探便会明白案子的实在底细。不成；能瞒多久是多久，务必让他们蒙在鼓里。咱们的底牌已经亮得太多。这于你是苦了些，可是没法子。”

===Original===
“Oh, don’t think I am complaining,” I exclaimed. “If it is a matter of business, I am as keen as you are. I thought at first that you were merely considering the safety of my vile body. When shall I start on my job?”

===Chinese===
“嗐，别以为我在抱怨，”我叫道，“既然是正经公事，我的劲头不输你。起初我只道你惦记的是我这副贱躯的安全。我这差事几时上工？”

===Original===
“Tomorrow morning. I shall give you my notes on the Blackmore case and the copies of the will and the depositions, from which you had better draw up a digest of the evidence with remarks as to the conclusions that it suggests. Then there are our gleanings from New Inn to be looked over and considered; and with regard to this case, we have the fragments of a pair of spectacles which had better be put together into a rather more intelligible form in case we have to produce them in evidence. That will keep you occupied for a day or two, together with some work appertaining to other cases. And now let us dismiss professional topics. You have not dined and neither have I, but I dare say Polton has made arrangements for some sort of meal. We will go down and see.”

===Chinese===
“明早。我会把我关于布莱克莫尔案的笔记交给你，还有遗嘱和口供笔录的抄本；你最好据此拟一份证据摘要，附注它所提示的结论。此外还有咱们从新客栈搜罗的零星收获要过目斟酌；就本案而言，还有一副眼镜的碎片，最好把它们拼合成一个较能辨认的形态，以防日后须当证据提出。这够你忙上一两天了，另外还有几桩别的案子的活计。现在，公事的话题搁下吧。你没用晚饭，我也没有，不过我想波尔顿多半已备下了什么吃食。咱们下去看看。”

===Original===
We descended to the lower floor, where Thorndyke’s anticipations were justified by a neatly laid table to which Polton was giving the finishing touches.

===Chinese===
我们下到楼下；一张铺陈齐整的餐桌证实了桑代克的预见，波尔顿正在做最后的点缀。

## I The Mysterious Patient / 神秘的病人

===Original===
As I look back through the years of my association with John Thorndyke, I am able to recall a wealth of adventures and strange experiences such as falls to the lot of very few men who pass their lives within hearing of Big Ben. Many of these experiences I have already placed on record; but it now occurs to me that I have hitherto left unrecorded one that is, perhaps, the most astonishing and incredible of the whole series; an adventure, too, that has for me the added interest that it inaugurated my permanent association with my learned and talented friend, and marked the close of a rather unhappy and unprosperous period of my life.

===Chinese===
回顾我与约翰·桑代克（John Thorndyke）相识共事的那些年头，我能忆起的奇遇与怪异经历之多，在毕生生活于大本钟钟声可闻之处的人当中，怕是很少有人能及。这些经历，我已有许多笔之于书；但如今我想起来，尚有一桩至今未曾记录——它或许是整个系列中最令人骇异、最难以置信的一桩；而且这桩奇遇于我还有另一层兴味：它开启了我与那位博学多才的朋友的永久结缘，也标志着我一生中一段相当失意、相当不顺的时期就此落幕。

===Original===
Memory, retracing the journey through the passing years to the starting-point of those strange events, lands me in a shabby little ground-floor room in a house near the Walworth end of Lower Kennington Lane. A couple of framed diplomas on the wall, a card of Snellen’s test-types and a stethoscope lying on the writing-table, proclaim it a doctor’s consulting-room; and my own position in the round-backed chair at the said table, proclaims me the practitioner in charge.

===Chinese===
记忆循着流年把这段旅程回溯到那些怪事的起点，把我安放在下肯宁顿巷（Lower Kennington Lane）近沃尔沃思（Walworth）一端的一所房子底层，一间寒酸的小屋里。墙上两幅装框的文凭，写字台上一张斯内伦视力表（Snellen’s test-types）和一只听诊器，宣告这是一间医生的诊室；而我本人端坐在前述桌旁的圆背椅里，则宣告我就是主持诊务的那位医生。

===Original===
It was nearly nine o’clock. The noisy little clock on the mantelpiece announced the fact, and, by its frantic ticking, seemed as anxious as I to get the consultation hours over. I glanced wistfully at my mud-splashed boots and wondered if I might yet venture to assume the slippers that peeped coyly from under the shabby sofa. I even allowed my thoughts to wander to the pipe that reposed in my coat pocket. Another minute and I could turn down the surgery gas and shut the outer door. The fussy little clock gave a sort of preliminary cough or hiccup, as if it should say: “Ahem! ladies and gentlemen, I am about to strike.” And at that moment, the bottle-boy opened the door and, thrusting in his head, uttered the one word: “Gentleman.”

===Chinese===
将近九点钟了。壁炉台上那只聒噪的小钟播报了这个事实，而且听它那阵发狂似的滴答声，仿佛同我一样急盼着应诊时间赶紧熬完。我恋恋地瞥了一眼自己溅满泥浆的靴子，盘算着能不能斗胆换上那双正从破旧沙发底下羞答答探出头的拖鞋。我甚至任凭思绪溜到安卧在外衣口袋里的烟斗上。再有一分钟，我就可以调暗诊疗室的煤气灯，闩上临街的大门了。那只急性子的小钟发出一声类似开嗓的咳嗽或打嗝，仿佛要说：“咳嗯！女士们，先生们，本人就要鸣钟报时了。”就在这一刻，药童（bottle-boy）推开门，探进脑袋，吐出一个词：“绅士。”

===Original===
Extreme economy of words is apt to result in ambiguity. But I understood. In Kennington Lane, the race of mere men and women appeared to be extinct. They were all gentlemen﻿—unless they were ladies or children﻿—even as the Liberian army was said to consist entirely of generals. Sweeps, labourers, milkmen, costermongers﻿—all were impartially invested by the democratic bottle-boy with the rank and title of *armigeri*. The present nobleman appeared to favour the aristocratic recreation of driving a cab or job-master’s carriage, and, as he entered the room, he touched his hat, closed the door somewhat carefully, and then, without remark, handed me a note which bore the superscription “Dr. Stillbury.”

===Chinese===
措辞俭省到极处，就难免生出歧义。可我懂。在肯宁顿巷，平平常常的“男人”和“女人”这一种人类，似乎已经绝迹了。来客个个都是绅士——除非她们是位夫人小姐，或者是个孩子——正像人们说的，利比里亚的军队清一色全由将军组成。扫烟囱的、卖苦力的、送奶的、推车叫卖的小贩——一概由那位奉行民主的药童不分彼此地授予 *armigeri*（拉丁文，“缙绅”）的阶位与头衔。眼下这位贵人偏爱的贵族式消遣，看来是赶车——驾一辆出租马车，或是马车行放租的轿车。他走进屋来，碰了碰帽子，把门相当仔细地带上，然后一言不发，递给我一封短简，封皮上写的收信人是“斯蒂尔伯里医生（Dr. Stillbury）”。

===Original===
“You understand,” I said, as I prepared to open the envelope, “that I am not Dr. Stillbury. He is away at present and I am looking after his patients.”

===Chinese===
“您得知道，”我一边准备拆信一边说，“我并不是斯蒂尔伯里医生。他眼下不在，由我代为照看他的病人。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t signify,” the man replied. “You’ll do as well.”

===Chinese===
“那不要紧，”来人答道，“您也一样顶用。”

===Original===
On this, I opened the envelope and read the note, which was quite brief, and, at first sight, in no way remarkable.

===Chinese===
于是我拆开信封，读起便条来。它十分简短，乍看毫无出奇之处。

===Original===
“Dear Sir,” it ran, “Would you kindly come and see a friend of mine who is staying with me? The bearer of this will give you further particulars and convey you to the house. Yours truly, H. Weiss.”

===Chinese===
信上写道：“敬启者：可否劳驾来舍下一晤正暂住在敝处的友人？持信人将向您细述详情，并带您到那所宅子。您忠实的，H. 韦斯（H. Weiss）。”

===Original===
There was no address on the paper and no date, and the writer was unknown to me.

===Chinese===
纸上没有地址，没有日期，写信人我也不认识。

===Original===
“This note,” I said, “refers to some further particulars. What are they?”

===Chinese===
“这便条，”我说，“提到了详情。都是些什么？”

===Original===
The messenger passed his hand over his hair with a gesture of embarrassment. “It’s a ridicklus affair,” he said, with a contemptuous laugh. “If I had been Mr. Weiss, I wouldn’t have had nothing to do with it. The sick gentleman, Mr. Graves, is one of them people what can’t abear doctors. He’s been ailing now for a week or two, but nothing would induce him to see a doctor. Mr. Weiss did everything he could to persuade him, but it was no go. He wouldn’t. However, it seems Mr. Weiss threatened to send for a medical man on his own account, because, you see, he was getting a bit nervous; and then Mr. Graves gave way. But only on one condition. He said the doctor was to come from a distance and was not to be told who he was or where he lived or anything about him; and he made Mr. Weiss promise to keep to that condition before he’d let him send. So Mr. Weiss promised, and, of course, he’s got to keep his word.”

===Chinese===
送信人抬手抹了抹头发，神情局促。“这档子事实在可笑，”他说，还轻蔑地笑了笑，“我要是韦斯先生，才不沾这档子事呢。生病的那位绅士，格雷夫斯（Graves）先生，就是那种见不得大夫的人。他身子不爽利也有一两个礼拜了，可怎么劝都不肯见大夫。韦斯先生好话说尽，全不顶事。他就是不去。不过嘛，看样子韦斯先生放了话，说要不管三七二十一自个儿去请位医生，因为您知道，他有点沉不住气了；这么着，格雷夫斯先生才让了步。但只有一个条件。他说，大夫得从老远的地方请来，还不许知道他是谁、住在哪儿、他的任何底细；他要韦斯先生先应承死守这个条件，才准他派人去请。韦斯先生应承了，那自然就得守这个话。”

===Original===
“But,” I said, with a smile, “you’ve just told me his name﻿—if his name really is Graves.”

===Chinese===
“不过，”我含笑说道，“你方才已经把他的名字告诉我了——倘若他的名字果真是格雷夫斯的话。”

===Original===
“You can form your own opinion on that,” said the coachman.

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，您自己琢磨，”马车夫说。

===Original===
“And,” I added, “as to not being told where he lives, I can see that for myself. I’m not blind, you know.”

===Chinese===
“还有，”我补了一句，“说到不告诉我他住在哪儿，这个我自己就看得出来。您知道，我眼睛可不瞎。”

===Original===
“We’ll take the risk of what you see,” the man replied. “The question is, will you take the job on?”

===Chinese===
“您看见什么，风险归我们担，”那人答道，“问题是，这活儿您接不接？”

===Original===
Yes; that was the question, and I considered it for some time before replying. We medical men are pretty familiar with the kind of person who “can’t abear doctors,” and we like to have as little to do with him as possible. He is a thankless and unsatisfactory patient. Intercourse with him is unpleasant, he gives a great deal of trouble and responds badly to treatment. If this had been my own practice, I should have declined the case offhand. But it was not my practice. I was only a deputy. I could not lightly refuse work which would yield a profit to my principal, unpleasant though it might be.

===Chinese===
不错；这就是问题所在，我掂量了好一阵才作答。我们行医的，对那种“见不得大夫”的人再熟悉不过，也都巴不得少沾为妙。这是一种吃力不讨好的病人。同他打交道不痛快，他麻烦极多，疗效又坏。这若是我自己的诊所，我会一口回绝这个病例。但这不是我的诊所。我不过是个代理。虽说不情愿，我却不能轻慢地推掉一桩能给我的东家生利的活计。

===Original===
As I turned the matter over in my mind, I half unconsciously scrutinized my visitor﻿—somewhat to his embarrassment﻿—and I liked his appearance as little as I liked his mission. He kept his station near the door, where the light was dim﻿—for the illumination was concentrated on the table and the patient’s chair﻿—but I could see that he had a somewhat sly, unprepossessing face and a greasy, red moustache that seemed out of character with his rather perfunctory livery; though this was mere prejudice. He wore a wig, too﻿—not that there was anything discreditable in that﻿—and the thumbnail of the hand that held his hat bore disfiguring traces of some injury﻿—which, again, though unsightly, in no wise reflected on his moral character. Lastly, he watched me keenly with a mixture of anxiety and sly complacency that I found distinctly unpleasant. In a general way, he impressed me disagreeably. I did not like the look of him at all; but nevertheless I decided to undertake the case.

===Chinese===
我在心里翻来覆去掂量这件事，一面半不觉察地把来客端详了一番——端详得他颇为局促——而他的相貌，同他的差事一样不合我的意。他守在门边他的岗位上，那里光线昏暗——因为照明都聚在诊桌和病人座椅那里——但我仍看得出，他生着一张颇为狡黠、不讨喜的脸，唇上一抹油亮的红髭，同他那身敷衍塞责的号衣颇不相称；不过这纯属成见。他还戴着假发——这倒没什么可訾议的——而那只持帽的手，拇指指甲上带着毁伤形迹的疤痕——这一条虽说不雅观，却也决不有损他的品行。末了，他目光灼灼地盯着我，那里面焦灼与狡黠的自得兼而有之，令我很不痛快。总而言之，他给我的印象很坏。他那副相貌我一点也不喜欢；可尽管如此，我还是决定接下来这个病例。

===Original===
“I suppose,” I answered, at length, “it is no affair of mine who the patient is or where he lives. But how do you propose to manage the business? Am I to be led to the house blindfolded, like the visitor to the bandit’s cave?”

===Chinese===
“我想，”我终于答道，“病人是谁、住在哪里，都不干我的事。可你们打算怎样经办这件事？难不成要我蒙上眼睛，像进强盗洞府的客人那样，被领到那所宅子去？”

===Original===
The man grinned slightly and looked very decidedly relieved.

===Chinese===
那人微微咧嘴一笑，神色分明松快了不少。

===Original===
“No, sir,” he answered; “we ain’t going to blindfold you. I’ve got a carriage outside. I don’t think you’ll see much out of that.”

===Chinese===
“不用，先生，”他答道，“我们不至于给您蒙眼。外头备着一辆马车呢。我想您从那车里头也瞧不见什么。”

===Original===
“Very well,” I rejoined, opening the door to let him out, “I’ll be with you in a minute. I suppose you can’t give me any idea as to what is the matter with the patient?”

===Chinese===
“很好，”我应道，开门放他出去，“我马上就来。病人的病症，你总该能给我个大概吧？”

===Original===
“No, sir, I can’t,” he replied; and he went out to see to the carriage.

===Chinese===
“不成了，先生，我给不了，”他答道；说罢便出去照看马车了。

===Original===
I slipped into a bag an assortment of emergency drugs and a few diagnostic instruments, turned down the gas and passed out through the surgery. The carriage was standing at the kerb, guarded by the coachman and watched with deep interest by the bottle-boy. I viewed it with mingled curiosity and disfavour. It was a kind of large brougham, such as is used by some commercial travellers, the usual glass windows being replaced by wooden shutters intended to conceal the piles of sample-boxes, and the doors capable of being locked from outside with a railway key.

===Chinese===
我往提包里塞了一应应急药品和几件诊断器械，调暗煤气灯，穿过诊疗室出了门。马车停在路缘，由马车夫看守着，药童则兴致勃勃地盯着它瞧。我怀着好奇与反感参半的心情把它打量了一番。那是一种大型的布鲁厄姆式四轮马车（brougham），有些行商就使用这种车：寻常的玻璃窗换成了木质窗板，本是为了遮掩成堆的样品箱，车门还能用铁路锁钥从外面锁上。

===Original===
As I emerged from the house, the coachman unlocked the door and held it open.

===Chinese===
我从宅子里出来，马车夫便开锁拉开车门，扶住不动。

===Original===
“How long will the journey take?” I asked, pausing with my foot on the step.

===Chinese===
“路上得走多久？”我问道，一只脚停在踏凳上。

===Original===
The coachman considered a moment or two and replied:

===Chinese===
马车夫寻思了一两秒，答道：

===Original===
“It took me, I should say, nigh upon half an hour to get here.”

===Chinese===
“要我说，我赶车到这儿，花了差不多半个钟头。”

===Original===
This was pleasant hearing. A half an hour each way and a half an hour at the patient’s house. At that rate it would be half-past ten before I was home again, and then it was quite probable that I should find some other untimely messenger waiting on the doorstep. With a muttered anathema on the unknown Mr. Graves and the unrestful life of a locum tenens, I stepped into the uninviting vehicle. Instantly the coachman slammed the door and turned the key, leaving me in total darkness.

===Chinese===
这可真是悦耳的消息。来回各半个钟头，病人宅上再耗半个钟头。照这样，等我回到家就十点半了，而且大有可能在门阶上撞见另一位不知趣的信使等着我。我低声咒骂了一句那位素昧平生的格雷夫斯先生，又咒骂了一句代理医生（locum tenens）这种不得安生的生活，便一步跨进那辆毫不招人待见的马车。马车夫立刻砰地摔上车门，一拧锁钥，把我撂进了一团漆黑里。

===Original===
One comfort was left to me; my pipe was in my pocket. I made shift to load it in the dark, and, having lit it with a wax match, took the opportunity to inspect the interior of my prison. It was a shabby affair. The moth-eaten state of the blue cloth cushions seemed to suggest that it had been long out of regular use; the oilcloth floor-covering was worn into holes; ordinary internal fittings there were none. But the appearances suggested that the crazy vehicle had been prepared with considerable forethought for its present use. The inside handles of the doors had apparently been removed; the wooden shutters were permanently fixed in their places; and a paper label, stuck on the transom below each window, had a suspicious appearance of having been put there to cover the painted name and address of the job-master or livery-stable keeper who had originally owned the carriage.

===Chinese===
我只剩下一件安慰：烟斗还在口袋里。我摸着黑勉强把它装上，用一支蜡杆火柴点着，趁便把我这座囚笼的内里察看了一番。真是件寒酸东西。蓝呢坐垫蛀得斑斑驳驳，似乎早已长久不在正经役使之中；铺地的油布磨出了窟窿；寻常的车内设施一概没有。但种种迹象表明，这辆破车为着眼下的用途，是经过相当深谋远虑的准备的。车门的内把手显然已被拆去；木质窗板被牢牢固定在原位；而贴在每扇车窗下横档上的一张纸标签，形迹可疑，很像是用来遮盖原车主——某位马车行或车马出租行老板——漆在车上的姓名地址的。

===Original===
These observations gave me abundant food for reflection. This Mr. Weiss must be an excessively conscientious man if he had considered that his promise to Mr. Graves committed him to such extraordinary precautions. Evidently no mere following of the letter of the law was enough to satisfy his sensitive conscience. Unless he had reasons for sharing Mr. Graves’s unreasonable desire for secrecy﻿—for one could not suppose that these measures of concealment had been taken by the patient himself.

===Chinese===
这些观察给了我充裕的思考材料。这位韦斯先生，倘若认为他对格雷夫斯先生的承诺竟连这样的非常防范都责无旁贷，那良心必定是敏感得过了分。显然，单是遵从承诺的字面，还不足以餍足他那份纤细的良心——除非他另有缘由，也要分有格雷夫斯先生那份无理的保密之心——因为总不能设想，这些遮掩的措施是出自病人本人的布置。

===Original===
The further suggestions that evolved themselves from this consideration were a little disquieting. Whither was I being carried and for what purpose? The idea that I was bound for some den of thieves where I might be robbed and possibly murdered, I dismissed with a smile. Thieves do not make elaborately concerted plans to rob poor devils like me. Poverty has its compensations in that respect. But there were other possibilities. Imagination backed by experience had no difficulty in conjuring up a number of situations in which a medical man might be called upon, with or without coercion, either to witness or actively to participate in the commission of some unlawful act.

===Chinese===
顺着这层思考自行演化出来的进一步联想，就有点叫人不安了。我这是被载往何处，所为何来？若说我此行是要奔什么贼窝，在那儿遭劫、没准还送命——这个念头我一笑置之。贼才不会煞费苦心定下这等周密的计谋，来抢我这种穷措大。贫穷在这上头自有它的补偿。但别的可能性是有的。有经验撑腰的想象力，不费什么事就能召来一连串的境况：一个医生可能被人召去——用强也罢，不用强也罢——或亲见、或亲手参与某种不法行径的施行。

===Original===
Reflections of this kind occupied me pretty actively if not very agreeably during this strange journey. And the monotony was relieved, too, by other distractions. I was, for example, greatly interested to notice how, when one sense is in abeyance, the other senses rouse into a compensating intensity of perception. I sat smoking my pipe in darkness which was absolute save for the dim glow from the smouldering tobacco in the bowl, and seemed to be cut off from all knowledge of the world without. But yet I was not. The vibrations of the carriage, with its hard springs and iron-tired wheels, registered accurately and plainly the character of the roadway. The harsh rattle of granite setts, the soft bumpiness of macadam, the smooth rumble of wood-pavement, the jarring and swerving of crossed tramlines; all were easily recognizable and together sketched the general features of the neighbourhood through which I was passing. And the sense of hearing filled in the details. Now the hoot of a tug’s whistle told of proximity to the river. A sudden and brief hollow reverberation announced the passage under a railway arch (which, by the way, happened several times during the journey); and, when I heard the familiar whistle of a railway-guard followed by the quick snorts of a skidding locomotive, I had as clear a picture of a heavy passenger-train moving out of a station as if I had seen it in broad daylight.

===Chinese===
在这趟古怪的旅途中，这类思虑颇为活跃地——虽说谈不上惬意地——占据着我。旅途的沉闷，也有别的调剂来纾解。譬如说，我发现了一件颇有意思的事：一种感官既经停用，其余的感官便会奋起，把知觉强化到足以补偿的地步。我坐在绝对的黑暗里抽着烟斗——唯有斗钵中闷燃的烟草透出一点暗红的微光——仿佛与外界的一切音讯完全隔绝。然而并没有。这辆弹簧梆硬、车轮包铁的马车的震动，把路面的性状精确而明白地记录下来。花岗岩铺路石的刺耳哐啷，碎石路的绵软颠簸，木铺路面的平滑轰隆，横穿电车轨道时的震撞与偏滑；这些声响无一难以辨认，合起来便勾勒出我所途经街区的大致面貌。而听觉又补足了细部。时而，拖船汽笛的一声长鸣，报知已行近河畔；一阵突然而短促的空洞回响，宣告正从铁路拱桥下穿行（顺便说一句，这一路竟穿了好几回）；待听到列车员那熟悉的哨音，继之以一列刹闸打滑的机车喷出的一连串短促鼻息，一列笨重的客车正驶出车站的图景便清晰浮现，恍如大白天亲眼看见一般。

===Original===
I had just finished my pipe and knocked out the ashes on the heel of my boot, when the carriage slowed down and entered a covered way﻿—as I could tell by the hollow echoes. Then I distinguished the clang of heavy wooden gates closed behind me, and a moment or two later the carriage door was unlocked and opened. I stepped out blinking into a covered passage paved with cobbles and apparently leading down to a mews; but it was all in darkness, and I had no time to make any detailed observations, as the carriage had drawn up opposite a side door which was open and in which stood a woman holding a lighted candle.

===Chinese===
我刚抽完一斗，正把烟灰磕在靴跟上，马车便放慢下来，驶进一条带顶棚的过道——那空空的回声告诉了我。接着我听出沉重的木门在我身后哐啷合拢，一两分钟后，车门开锁、打开。我眨着眼跨下车来，站到一条铺着鹅卵石的顶棚过道里，它看样子是通向后面的马厩巷的；但四下里一片漆黑，我也来不及细看，因为马车已在一扇敞开的边门对面停住，门口立着一个端着点亮的蜡烛的女人。

===Original===
“Is that the doctor?” she asked, speaking with a rather pronounced German accent and shading the candle with her hand as she peered at me.

===Chinese===
“是医生吗？”她问，说话带着相当重的德国口音，一边抬手拢住烛光打量我。

===Original===
I answered in the affirmative, and she then exclaimed:

===Chinese===
我作了肯定的回答，她随即说道：

===Original===
“I am glad you have come. Mr. Weiss will be so relieved. Come in, please.”

===Chinese===
“您来了我很高兴。韦斯先生这下可以放心了。请进。”

===Original===
I followed her across a dark passage into a dark room, where she set the candle down on a chest of drawers and turned to depart. At the door, however, she paused and looked back.

===Chinese===
我跟着她穿过一条漆黑的过道，走进一间漆黑的屋子。她把蜡烛搁在一只五斗柜上，转身要走。但到了门口，却又停步回望。

===Original===
“It is not a very nice room to ask you into,” she said. “We are very untidy just now, but you must excuse us. We have had so much anxiety about poor Mr. Graves.”

===Chinese===
“请您进这么一间屋子，实在不成样子，”她说，“我们这阵子乱得很，您得多担待。为了可怜的格雷夫斯先生，我们愁得没了法子。”

===Original===
“He has been ill some time, then?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，他已经病了些日子了？”

===Original===
“Yes. Some little time. At intervals, you know. Sometimes better, sometimes not so well.”

===Chinese===
“是的。有些日子了。时发时止的，您知道。有时好些，有时又差些。”

===Original===
As she spoke, she gradually backed out into the passage but did not go away at once. I accordingly pursued my inquiries.

===Chinese===
她一边说，一边慢慢退到过道里，却没有立刻走开。我便接着盘问。

===Original===
“He has not been seen by any doctor, has he?”

===Chinese===
“他一直没请任何医生看过，是吗？”

===Original===
“No,” she answered, “he has always refused to see a doctor. That has been a great trouble to us. Mr. Weiss has been very anxious about him. He will be so glad to hear that you have come. I had better go and tell him. Perhaps you will kindly sit down until he is able to come to you,” and with this she departed on her mission.

===Chinese===
“没有，”她答道，“他从来就死活不肯见医生。这一直是我们的一桩大心事。韦斯先生为他担心得不得了。听说您来了，他准高兴。我这就去告诉他。请您好意坐一坐，等他得空过来，”说罢，她便自去了。

===Original===
It struck me as a little odd that, considering his anxiety and the apparent urgency of the case, Mr. Weiss should not have been waiting to receive me. And when several minutes elapsed without his appearing, the oddness of the circumstance impressed me still more. Having no desire, after the journey in the carriage, to sit down, I whiled away the time by an inspection of the room. And a very curious room it was; bare, dirty, neglected and, apparently, unused. A faded carpet had been flung untidily on the floor. A small, shabby table stood in the middle of the room; and beyond this, three horsehair-covered chairs and a chest of drawers formed the entire set of furniture. No pictures hung on the mouldy walls, no curtains covered the shuttered windows, and the dark drapery of cobwebs that hung from the ceiling to commemorate a long and illustrious dynasty of spiders hinted at months of neglect and disuse.

===Chinese===
这就有点蹊跷了：韦斯先生既是那般焦心，病情看去又那样紧迫，竟没有候着迎接我。而几分钟过去，他仍不露面，这情形的蹊跷便愈发压在我心上。坐了一路马车，我全无落座之意，便借着打量屋子消磨时间。这真是一间古怪的屋子：空落、肮脏、无人经管，而且看样子久弃不用。一张褪色的地毯胡乱撂在地板上。屋子当中立着一张寒酸的小桌；此外，三把马鬃面子的椅子和一只五斗柜，便是全部的家具陈设。发霉的墙上不挂一幅画，上了窗板的窗上不挂一幅帘，从天花板垂下的那一幅幅蛛网黑纱——足为蜘蛛家族悠久而显赫的王朝志念——暗示着数月的弃置与冷落。

===Original===
The chest of drawers﻿—an incongruous article of furniture for what seemed to be a dining-room﻿—as being the nearest and best lighted object received most of my attention. It was a fine old chest of nearly black mahogany, very battered and in the last stage of decay, but originally a piece of some pretensions. Regretful of its fallen estate, I looked it over with some interest and had just observed on its lower corner a little label bearing the printed inscription “Lot 201” when I heard footsteps descending the stairs.
