A moment later the door opened and a shadowy figure appeared standing close by the threshold.

===Chinese===
那只五斗柜——搁在这间看模样本是餐室的屋子里，它显得不伦不类——因为离我最近、受光最足，便得了我的大半注意。那是一只近乎乌黑的桃花心木老柜，破旧不堪、朽坏到了极处，但当年原是件颇有几分讲究的家什。我惋惜它的败落，饶有兴味地把它检看了一遍，刚在柜子下角发现一张小标签，印着“第201号拍品”（Lot 201）的字样，便听见楼梯上有脚步声下来。片刻之后，房门开了，一条幽暗的人影出现在门槛近旁。

===Original===
“Good evening, doctor,” said the stranger, in a deep, quiet voice and with a distinct, though not strong, German accent. “I must apologize for keeping you waiting.”

===Chinese===
“晚上好，医生，”那陌生人说道，嗓音低沉平静，带着明显但不重的德国口音，“让您久等，实在过意不去。”

===Original===
I acknowledged the apology somewhat stiffly and asked: “You are Mr. Weiss, I presume?”

===Chinese===
我多少带着点生硬领了他的歉意，问道：“想必您就是韦斯先生？”

===Original===
“Yes, I am Mr. Weiss. It is very good of you to come so far and so late at night and to make no objection to the absurd conditions that my poor friend has imposed.”

===Chinese===
“是的，我就是韦斯。您肯在夜里这么晚赶这么远的路，又对我那可怜的朋友定下的荒唐条件毫无异议，实在难为您了。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” I replied. “It is my business to go when and where I am wanted, and it is not my business to inquire into the private affairs of my patients.”

===Chinese===
“哪里的话，”我答道，“人家何时何地需要我，赶去应诊便是我的本分；探问病人的私事，却不是我的本分。”

===Original===
“That is very true, sir,” he agreed cordially, “and I am much obliged to you for taking that very proper view of the case. I pointed that out to my friend, but he is not a very reasonable man. He is very secretive and rather suspicious by nature.”

===Chinese===
“这话一点不错，先生，”他诚恳地应和道，“您对这件事持这样端正的看法，我很感激。这层道理我也向他指出过，可他不是个怎么讲得通道理的人。他天性深藏不露，又相当多疑。”

===Original===
“So I inferred. And as to his condition; is he seriously ill?”

===Chinese===
“我也这么推断。那么他的状况呢？病得重吗？”

===Original===
“Ah,” said Mr. Weiss, “that is what I want you to tell me. I am very much puzzled about him.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”韦斯先生说，“这正是我要请您告诉我的。我让他弄得一头雾水。”

===Original===
“But what is the nature of his illness? What does he complain of?”

===Chinese===
“可他这病是什么性质？他有什么自诉？”

===Original===
“He makes very few complaints of any kind although he is obviously ill. But the fact is that he is hardly ever more than half awake. He lies in a kind of dreamy stupor from morning to night.”

===Chinese===
“他病容明明摆着，却几乎什么都不诉说。可实情是，他难得有完全醒转的时候。他从早到晚躺着，陷在一种迷迷糊糊的木然里。”

===Original===
This struck me as excessively strange and by no means in agreement with the patient’s energetic refusal to see a doctor.

===Chinese===
这话我觉得反常到了极处，同病人当初那么起劲地拒绝见医生，全然对不上榫。

===Original===
“But,” I asked, “does he never rouse completely?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”我问，“他就从来没有完全清醒的时候？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” Mr. Weiss answered quickly; “he rouses from time to time and is then quite rational, and, as you may have gathered, rather obstinate. That is the peculiar and puzzling feature in the case; this alternation between a state of stupor and an almost normal and healthy condition. But perhaps you had better see him and judge for yourself. He had a rather severe attack just now. Follow me, please. The stairs are rather dark.”

===Chinese===
“哦，有的，”韦斯先生应声而答，“他时不时醒转过来，那时神志相当清楚，而且——您想必也听出来了——相当执拗。这桩病例又古怪又费解的地方就在这里：木然状态与近乎正常健康的状态交替更迭。不过，您最好还是亲眼见见他，自己来判断。他方才发作得相当重。请跟我来。楼梯挺黑的。”

===Original===
The stairs were very dark, and I noticed that they were without any covering of carpet, or even oilcloth, so that our footsteps resounded dismally as if we were in an empty house. I stumbled up after my guide, feeling my way by the handrail, and on the first floor followed him into a room similar in size to the one below and very barely furnished, though less squalid than the other. A single candle at the farther end threw its feeble light on a figure in the bed, leaving the rest of the room in a dim twilight.

===Chinese===
楼梯果真黑得很，我又注意到梯级上没有铺地毯，连油布也没有，于是我们的脚步声凄凄地回响，仿佛置身空屋。我手扶栏杆摸着道，跌跌撞撞地跟在引路人身后上楼，在二楼随他走进一间屋子，大小与楼下那间相仿，陈设也是寥寥，只是不像那间那么不堪。远端一支孤烛，把微弱的光投在床上一条人影上，屋中其余处便都浸在昏暝里。

===Original===
As Mr. Weiss tiptoed into the chamber, a woman﻿—the one who had spoken to me below﻿—rose from a chair by the bedside and quietly left the room by a second door. My conductor halted, and looking fixedly at the figure in the bed, called out:

===Chinese===
韦斯先生蹑手蹑脚走进卧房时，一个女人——正是方才在楼下同我说话的那位——从床边的椅子上起身，从另一扇门悄然出去了。我的引路人停住脚，凝望着床上的人影，唤道：

===Original===
“Philip! Philip! Here is the doctor come to see you.”

===Chinese===
“菲利普（Philip）！菲利普！医生来看你来了。”

===Original===
He paused for a moment or two, and, receiving no answer, said: “He seems to be dozing as usual. Will you go and see what you can make of him?”

===Chinese===
他停了一两秒，听不见应声，便说：“看来他照例又打盹了。请您过去瞧瞧，能看出点什么。”

===Original===
I stepped forward to the bedside, leaving Mr. Weiss at the end of the room near the door by which we had entered, where he remained, slowly and noiselessly pacing backwards and forwards in the semi-obscurity. By the light of the candle I saw an elderly man with good features and a refined, intelligent and even attractive face, but dreadfully emaciated, bloodless and sallow. He lay quite motionless except for the scarcely perceptible rise and fall of his chest; his eyes were nearly closed, his features relaxed, and, though he was not actually asleep, he seemed to be in a dreamy, somnolent, lethargic state, as if under the influence of some narcotic.

===Chinese===
我举步来到床边，把韦斯先生留在屋尾我们进门的那扇门近旁；他就留在那儿，在半暗不明中缓缓地、悄无声息地来回踱步。就着烛光，我看见一个上了年纪的男人，眉目端正，面容文雅、聪慧，甚至称得上有几分动人，只是枯瘦得吓人，面无血色，肤色蜡黄。他一动不动地躺着，唯有胸口一起一伏，微弱得几乎察觉不出；双眼半阖，五官松弛，虽说并未真正睡着，却像是陷在一种迷离困倦、昏沉呆滞的境地里，仿佛受了某种麻醉剂的影响。

===Original===
I watched him for a minute or so, timing his slow breathing by my watch, and then suddenly and sharply addressed him by name; but the only response was a slight lifting of the eyelids, which, after a brief, drowsy glance at me, slowly subsided to their former position.

===Chinese===
我守了他约莫一分钟，对着表数他那缓慢的呼吸，随后突然厉声唤他的名字；可唯一的反应只是眼皮微微一抬，惺忪地朝我瞥了一眼，又慢慢落回原处。

===Original===
I now proceeded to make a physical examination. First, I felt his pulse, grasping his wrist with intentional brusqueness in the hope of rousing him from his stupor. The beats were slow, feeble and slightly irregular, giving clear evidence, if any were needed, of his generally lowered vitality. I listened carefully to his heart, the sounds of which were very distinct through the thin walls of his emaciated chest, but found nothing abnormal beyond the feebleness and uncertainty of its action. Then I turned my attention to his eyes, which I examined closely with the aid of the candle and my ophthalmoscope lens, raising the lids somewhat roughly so as to expose the whole of the irises. He submitted without resistance to my rather ungentle handling of these sensitive structures, and showed no signs of discomfort even when I brought the candle-flame to within a couple of inches of his eyes.

===Chinese===
接着我着手做体格检查。先诊脉：我故意十分粗鲁地一把攥住他的手腕，指望把他从昏沉里惊醒。脉来迟缓、虚弱，还略有不匀，倘若还需要什么明证来证明他周身生机之委顿，这便是了。我细听他的心音——他那瘦削的胸壁单薄，心音听来格外清楚——但除了搏动的虚弱与不稳，别无异常。随后我把注意力转向他的眼睛，借着烛光和我的检眼镜镜片凑近细察，还相当粗暴地掀起眼睑，好让两侧虹膜整个显露出来。我对这些娇嫩组织这番并不温柔的摆弄，他全然顺受；即便我把烛火移到离他双眼不过两英寸上下，也不见他有半点不适的神色。

===Original===
But this extraordinary tolerance of light was easily explained by closer examination; for the pupils were contracted to such an extreme degree that only the very minutest point of black was visible at the centre of the grey iris. Nor was this the only abnormal peculiarity of the sick man’s eyes. As he lay on his back, the right iris sagged down slightly towards its centre, showing a distinctly concave surface; and, when I contrived to produce a slight but quick movement of the eyeball, a perceptible undulatory movement could be detected. The patient had, in fact, what is known as a tremulous iris, a condition that is seen in cases where the crystalline lens has been extracted for the cure of cataract, or where it has become accidentally displaced, leaving the iris unsupported. In the present case, the complete condition of the iris made it clear that the ordinary extraction operation had not been performed, nor was I able, on the closest inspection with the aid of my lens, to find any trace of the less common “needle operation.” The inference was that the patient had suffered from the accident known as “dislocation of the lens”; and this led to the further inference that he was almost or completely blind in the right eye.

===Chinese===
不过，这种异乎寻常的耐光，稍加细察便轻易有了交代：瞳孔缩得极小，灰色虹膜正中只余一针尖大的黑点。而且病人眼睛的异常还不止这一桩。他仰卧着，右眼虹膜向中心微微塌陷，表面明显内凹；而当我设法让眼球产生一下轻微而急促的转动时，还能察觉虹膜一阵可辨的波动。这位病人所患的，其实就是所谓的“虹膜震颤”，这种情形见于为治白内障而摘除了晶状体的病例，或晶状体因意外脱位、虹膜失去支撑之时。而在眼下这例，虹膜完好无损，可见并未做过寻常的白内障摘除术；我又借助镜片极力细察，也寻不出较罕见的“针拨术”的丝毫痕迹。由此推断，病人遭遇过那种名为“晶状体脱位”的意外；而进一步推论下去：他的右眼已近乎失明，或已全盲。

===Original===
This conclusion was, indeed, to some extent negatived by a deep indentation on the bridge of the nose, evidently produced by spectacles, and by marks which I looked for and found behind the ears, corresponding to the hooks or “curl sides” of the glasses. For those spectacles which are fitted with curl sides to hook over the ears are usually intended to be worn habitually, and this agreed with the indentation on the nose; which was deeper than would have been accounted for by the merely occasional use of spectacles for reading. But if only one eye was useful, a single eyeglass would have answered the purpose; not that there was any weight in this objection, for a single eyeglass worn constantly would be much less convenient than a pair of hook-sided spectacles.

===Chinese===
不过，鼻梁上一道深陷的压痕——显系眼镜压出来的——多少否定了这个结论；我又在耳后寻找，果真找到了与镜腿挂钩、也就是所谓“卷边镜腿”相对应的印痕。凡装着卷边镜腿、可挂上耳朵的眼镜，通常都是预备常年佩戴的，这与鼻梁上的压痕正相吻合；那压痕之深，绝非偶尔读书才戴戴眼镜所能造成。但倘若他只有一只眼睛可用，单片眼镜本该足以济事——倒不是说这一驳有多少分量，因为单片眼镜常年佩戴，远不如一副卷腿眼镜来得方便。

===Original===
As to the nature of the patient’s illness, only one opinion seemed possible. It was a clear and typical case of opium or morphine poisoning. To this conclusion all his symptoms seemed to point with absolute certainty. The coated tongue, which he protruded slowly and tremulously in response to a command bawled in his ear; his yellow skin and ghastly expression; his contracted pupils and the stupor from which he could hardly be roused by the roughest handling and which yet did not amount to actual insensibility; all these formed a distinct and coherent group of symptoms, not only pointing plainly to the nature of the drug, but also suggesting a very formidable dose.

===Chinese===
至于病人的病症是什么性质，看来只容一种意见。这是鸦片或吗啡中毒，一例清楚而典型的病案。他的一切症状仿佛都斩钉截铁地指向这个结论。那舌苔厚腻的舌头——我在他耳边大喝一声，他才迟缓而颤颤地伸出来；蜡黄的皮肤和死人似的面容；缩得极小的瞳孔；还有那种任凭最粗鲁的摆弄也几乎唤不醒、却又未到全然不省人事地步的木然——这一切构成了一个分明而连贯的症状群，不但明明白白指向毒物的种类，还提示着剂量之大，相当可畏。

===Original===
But this conclusion in its turn raised a very awkward and difficult question. If a large﻿—a poisonous﻿—dose of the drug had been taken, how, and by whom had that dose been administered? The closest scrutiny of the patient’s arms and legs failed to reveal a single mark such as would be made by a hypodermic needle. This man was clearly no common morphinomaniac; and in the absence of the usual sprinkling of needlemarks, there was nothing to show or suggest whether the drug had been taken voluntarily by the patient himself or administered by someone else.

===Chinese===
但这个结论又反过来引出一个十分尴尬、十分难办的疑问。倘若服下的剂量很大——大到中毒的地步——这剂量是怎样用下的，又是谁用下的？我把病人的臂腿翻检到至细至尽，也没能找出一处皮下注射的针头会留下的斑痕。此人显然不是寻常的吗啡瘾君子；而既然寻常惯见的那一片针眼不见踪影，便毫无凭据显示、也无从暗示：这药是病人自己甘愿服下的，还是旁人替他用下的。

===Original===
And then there remained the possibility that I might, after all, be mistaken in my diagnosis. I felt pretty confident. But the wise man always holds a doubt in reserve. And, in the present case, having regard to the obviously serious condition of the patient, such a doubt was eminently disturbing. Indeed, as I pocketed my stethoscope and took a last look at the motionless, silent figure, I realized that my position was one of extraordinary difficulty and perplexity. On the one hand my suspicions﻿—aroused, naturally enough, by the very unusual circumstances that surrounded my visit﻿—inclined me to extreme reticence; while, on the other, it was evidently my duty to give any information that might prove serviceable to the patient.

===Chinese===
此外还剩下一种可能：毕竟是我的诊断错了。我自觉颇有把握。可智者总留着几分疑。而在此案，念及病人显而易见的危境，这一分疑就格外令人心扰。真的，当我把听诊器收回口袋，对那静止无声的人影投去最后一瞥时，我明白自己的处境真是难乎其难、疑窦丛生。一方面，我的疑心——此番出诊四周那极不寻常的境遇引它起来，原属自然——倾向于让我三缄其口；另一方面，凡有消息可能于病人有益，据实以告又分明是我的本分。

===Original===
As I turned away from the bed Mr. Weiss stopped his slow pacing to and fro and faced me. The feeble light of the candle now fell on him, and I saw him distinctly for the first time. He did not impress me favourably. He was a thickset, round-shouldered man, a typical fair German with tow-coloured hair, greased and brushed down smoothly, a large, ragged, sandy beard and coarse, sketchy features. His nose was large and thick with a bulbous end, and inclined to a reddish purple, a tint which extended to the adjacent parts of his face as if the colour had run. His eyebrows were large and beetling, overhanging deep-set eyes, and he wore a pair of spectacles which gave him a somewhat owlish expression. His exterior was unprepossessing, and I was in a state of mind that rendered me easily receptive of an unfavourable impression.

===Chinese===
我转身离开病床，韦斯先生便停下缓慢的来回踱步，面向我。烛光此刻正照在他身上，我头一回把他看得分明。他没有给我好印象。他是个敦实的、溜肩膀的男人，一个典型的浅发德国人：淡黄色的头发抹了油，梳得服服帖帖；一部大而蓬乱的沙色胡子；五官粗陋，像是寥寥几笔勾成的。鼻子又大又厚，鼻头圆鼓，颜色偏向红紫，而这颜色还漫到脸上邻近的部位，仿佛颜色洇了开去。眉毛又粗又浓，高高凸起，罩着深陷的双眼，鼻上又架着一副眼镜，给他添了几分类似猫头鹰的神气。他这副外表本就引不起人的好感，而我当时的心境，又极容易领受恶劣的印象。

===Original===
“Well,” he said, “what do you make of him?” I hesitated, still perplexed by the conflicting necessities of caution and frankness, but at length replied:

===Chinese===
“怎么样，”他说，“您看他这病是怎么回事？”我踌躇了，还在谨慎与坦白两难之间彷徨，但终于答道：

===Original===
“I think rather badly of him, Mr. Weiss. He is in a very low state.”

===Chinese===
“我看不大妙，韦斯先生。他的境况非常衰弱。”

===Original===
“Yes, I can see that. But have you come to any decision as to the nature of his illness?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，这我也看得出来。可对他的病是什么性质，您有了决断没有？”

===Original===
There was a tone of anxiety and suppressed eagerness in the question which, while it was natural enough in the circumstances, by no means allayed my suspicions, but rather influenced me on the side of caution.

===Chinese===
这句问话里含着焦虑和压住的急切；这在此情此景原也够自然，却丝毫没能平息我的疑心，反倒把我更推向谨慎一边。

===Original===
“I cannot give a very definite opinion at present,” I replied guardedly. “The symptoms are rather obscure and might very well indicate several different conditions. They might be due to congestion of the brain, and, if no other explanation were possible, I should incline to that view. The alternative is some narcotic poison, such as opium or morphia.”

===Chinese===
“眼下我还给不出十分明确的意见，”我带着保留地答道，“症状相当隐晦，很可能表示几种不同的病症。也许是脑充血所致；倘若没有别的解释可能，我便会倾向于那一说。另一种可能，是某种麻醉性毒物，比如鸦片或吗啡。”

===Original===
“But that is quite impossible. There is no such drug in the house, and as he never leaves his room now, he could not get any from outside.”

===Chinese===
“可那是万万不可能的。家里没有这类药，而他如今从不出房门，也没法从外头弄到。”

===Original===
“What about the servants?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“那么仆人们呢？”我问。

===Original===
“There are no servants excepting my housekeeper, and she is absolutely trustworthy.”

===Chinese===
“除了我的管家妇，家里没有仆人，而她是绝对靠得住的。”

===Original===
“He might have some store of the drug that you are not aware of. Is he left alone much?”

===Chinese===
“他也许私藏着药，您并不知情。他常独自一人待着吗？”

===Original===
“Very seldom indeed. I spend as much time with him as I can, and when I am not able to be in the room, Mrs. Schallibaum, my housekeeper, sits with him.”

===Chinese===
“实在难得有这种时候。我尽量多陪着他；我腾不出工夫守在屋里时，就由我的管家妇沙利鲍姆太太（Mrs. Schallibaum）守着他。”

===Original===
“Is he often as drowsy as he is now?”

===Chinese===
“他常像现在这样昏昏欲睡吗？”

===Original===
“Oh, very often; in fact, I should say that is his usual condition. He rouses up now and again, and then he is quite lucid and natural for, perhaps, an hour or so; but presently he becomes drowsy again and doses off, and remains asleep, or half asleep, for hours on end. Do you know of any disease that takes people in that way?”

===Chinese===
“哦，常是这样；说实在的，我看这就是他的常态。他时不时清醒过来，那时神志相当清明、自然，兴许能撑上一个钟头上下；可不多时又困意上来，打起盹，便一觉睡去，或半睡半醒，一连好几个钟头。您可知道有什么病是这么拿捏人的？”

===Original===
“No,” I answered. “The symptoms are not exactly like those of any disease that is known to me. But they are much very like those of opium poisoning.”

===Chinese===
“不知道，”我答道，“这些症状同我所知的任何疾病都不完全相像。但同鸦片中毒的症状，却像极了。”

===Original===
“But, my dear sir,” Mr. Weiss retorted impatiently, “since it is clearly impossible that it can be opium poisoning, it must be something else. Now, what else can it be? You were speaking of congestion of the brain.”

===Chinese===
“可是，亲爱的先生，”韦斯先生不耐烦地驳道，“鸦片中毒既然分明不可能，那就必然是别的什么了。那么，还能是什么呢？您方才说到脑充血来着。”

===Original===
“Yes. But the objection to that is the very complete recovery that seems to take place in the intervals.”

===Chinese===
“是的。但这一说有个难处：间歇期里他似乎恢复得十分彻底。”

===Original===
“I would not say very complete,” said Mr. Weiss. “The recovery is rather comparative. He is lucid and fairly natural in his manner, but he is still dull and lethargic. He does not, for instance, show any desire to go out, or even to leave his room.”

===Chinese===
“我却不敢说十分彻底，”韦斯先生说，“那恢复只是比出来的。他神志清明，举止也相当自然，可仍旧迟钝、倦怠。比方说，他毫无出门的意思，连房门都懒得迈出。”

===Original===
I pondered uncomfortably on these rather contradictory statements. Clearly Mr. Weiss did not mean to entertain the theory of opium poisoning; which was natural enough if he had no knowledge of the drug having been used. But still﻿—

===Chinese===
这些前后抵牾的说法，我越琢磨越不安。显然，韦斯先生根本无意考虑鸦片中毒一说；倘若他对药物之被使用当真毫不知情，这态度原也够自然。可是——

===Original===
“I suppose,” said Mr. Weiss, “you have experience of sleeping sickness?”

===Chinese===
“您想必，”韦斯先生说，“对昏睡病有过经验吧？”

===Original===
The suggestion startled me. I had not. Very few people had. At that time practically nothing was known about the disease. It was a mere pathological curiosity, almost unheard of excepting by a few practitioners in remote parts of Africa, and hardly referred to in the textbooks. Its connection with the trypanosome-bearing insects was as yet unsuspected, and, to me, its symptoms were absolutely unknown.

===Chinese===
这一问叫我吃了一惊。我没有。有过的也寥寥无几。那时候，这病实际上还几乎无人知晓。它不过是病理学上的一桩珍奇，除了非洲僻远地区的少数开业医生几乎无人听闻，教科书里也难得提起。它同携带锥虫的昆虫之间的干系，当时还无人疑及；至于它的症状，于我是全然陌生。

===Original===
“No, I have not,” I replied. “The disease is nothing more than a name to me. But why do you ask? Has Mr. Graves been abroad?”

===Chinese===
“没有，”我答道，“这病于我不过是个名目。可您为什么问这个？格雷夫斯先生出国去过？”

===Original===
“Yes. He has been travelling for the last three or four years, and I know that he spent some time recently in West Africa, where this disease occurs. In fact, it was from him that I first heard about it.”

===Chinese===
“去过。这三四年他一直在外旅行，我知道他前不久还在西非待过一阵，这病就在那一带发生。说起来，我还是先从他那儿听说这病的。”

===Original===
This was a new fact. It shook my confidence in my diagnosis very considerably, and inclined me to reconsider my suspicions. If Mr. Weiss was lying to me, he now had me at a decided disadvantage.

===Chinese===
这是个新情况。它大大动摇了我对诊断的自信，也让我倾向于把疑心重新掂量一遍。倘若韦斯先生是在骗我，那此刻他已把我拿捏得死死的了。

===Original===
“What do you think?” he asked. “Is it possible that this can be sleeping sickness?”

===Chinese===
“您看呢？”他问，“这有没有可能是昏睡病？”

===Original===
“I should not like to say that it is impossible,” I replied. “The disease is practically unknown to me. I have never practised out of England and have had no occasion to study it. Until I have looked the subject up, I should not be in a position to give an opinion. Of course, if I could see Mr. Graves in one of what we may call his ‘lucid intervals’ I should be able to form a better idea. Do you think that could be managed?”

===Chinese===
“我不愿说不可能，”我答道，“这病我实际上毫无所知。我从未在英格兰以外行医，也无缘研习过它。等我把这问题查考一番之后，才谈得上拿出意见。当然，倘若我能趁格雷夫斯先生——我们姑且称之为——‘清醒间歇’的时候见他一面，便能形成更清楚的看法。您看这事办得到吗？”

===Original===
“It might. I see the importance of it and will certainly do my best; but he is a difficult man; a very difficult man. I sincerely hope it is not sleeping sickness.”

===Chinese===
“兴许办得到。我知道这事要紧，一定尽力；可他是个难对付的人；非常难对付。我衷心希望这不是昏睡病。”

===Original===
“Why?”

===Chinese===
“为什么？”

===Original===
“Because﻿—as I understood from him﻿—that disease is invariably fatal, sooner or later. There seem to be no cure. Do you think you will be able to decide when you see him again?”

===Chinese===
“因为——据我向他了解——那病迟早一律致命。似乎没有治法。您下次见他时，能够拿定主意吗？”

===Original===
“I hope so,” I replied. “I shall look up the authorities and see exactly what the symptoms are﻿—that is, so far as they are known; but my impression is that there is very little information available.”

===Chinese===
“希望如此，”我答道，“我要去查各家权威，看看症状究竟是些什么——就目前已知的而言；不过我的印象是，现成的资料少得很。”

===Original===
“And in the meantime?”

===Chinese===
“那么眼下呢？”

===Original===
“We will give him some medicine and attend to his general condition, and you had better let me see him again as soon as possible.” I was about to say that the effect of the medicine itself might throw some light on the patient’s condition, but, as I proposed to treat him for morphine poisoning, I thought it wiser to keep this item of information to myself. Accordingly, I confined myself to a few general directions as to the care of the patient, to which Mr. Weiss listened attentively. “And,” I concluded, “we must not lose sight of the opium question. You had better search the room carefully and keep a close watch on the patient, especially during his intervals of wakefulness.”

===Chinese===
“我们会给他用些药，调养他的整体状况，您最好尽早安排我再见他一面。”我本想补上一句：药物本身的效果或许也能给病人的病情投下一线光亮；但因我打算按吗啡中毒为他施治，我想，这一条消息还是由我自己藏着更为稳妥。于是我只交代了几条有关病人护理的大体嘱咐，韦斯先生听得很是用心。“还有，”末了我说道，“鸦片这个问题咱们不能撒手。您最好把这屋子细细搜查一遍，对病人严密看守，尤其是在他清醒的间歇里。”

===Original===
“Very well, doctor,” Mr. Weiss replied, “I will do all that you tell me and I will send for you again as soon as possible, if you do not object to poor Graves’s ridiculous conditions. And now, if you will allow me to pay your fee, I will go and order the carriage while you are writing the prescription.”

===Chinese===
“好的，医生，”韦斯先生答道，“您吩咐的我一概照办，我也会尽早再请您来，只要您不嫌弃可怜的格雷夫斯那些荒唐条件。现在，请容我付您诊金；趁您开处方的工夫，我去吩咐套车。”

===Original===
“There is no need for a prescription,” I said. “I will make up some medicine and give it to the coachman.”

===Chinese===
“不必开处方了，”我说，“我配好药，交给马车夫就是。”

===Original===
Mr. Weiss seemed inclined to demur to this arrangement, but I had my own reasons for insisting on it. Modern prescriptions are not difficult to read, and I did not wish Mr. Weiss to know what treatment the patient was having.

===Chinese===
韦斯先生对这安排似乎有些异议，但我坚持如此，自有我的道理。现代的处方并不难读懂，我可不愿让韦斯先生知道病人接受的是什么疗法。

===Original===
As soon as I was left alone, I returned to the bedside and once more looked down at the impassive figure. And as I looked, my suspicions revived. It was very like morphine poisoning; and, if it was morphine, it was no common, medicinal dose that had been given. I opened my bag and took out my hypodermic case from which I extracted a little tube of atropine tabloids. Shaking out into my hand a couple of the tiny discs, I drew down the patient’s underlip and slipped the little tablets under his tongue. Then I quickly replaced the tube and dropped the case into my bag; and I had hardly done so when the door opened softly and the housekeeper entered the room.

===Chinese===
屋里一剩我独自一人，我便回到床边，再低头端详那毫无反应的身形。这一看，我的疑心又活了过来。这太像吗啡中毒了；而且倘若真是吗啡，那用下的就绝非寻常的药用剂量。我打开提包，取出皮下注射盒，从里面抽出一小管阿托品药片。我把两粒小小的药片抖进掌心，翻下病人的下唇，把药片塞进他舌头底下。随即我赶快把小管放回，把盒子丢回提包；刚收拾停当，门轻轻开了，管家妇走进屋来。

===Original===
“How do you find Mr. Graves?” she asked in what I thought a very unnecessarily low tone, considering the patient’s lethargic state.

===Chinese===
“您看格雷夫斯先生怎么样？”她问，嗓音压得极低——念及病人这副昏沉光景，我觉得全无必要。

===Original===
“He seems to be very ill,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“他病得似乎很重，”我答道。

===Original===
“So!” she rejoined, and added: “I am sorry to hear that. We have been anxious about him.”

===Chinese===
“这样啊！”她应了一声，又道：“听您这么说真难过。我们一直为他悬着心呢。”

===Original===
She seated herself on the chair by the bedside, and, shading the candle from the patient’s face﻿—and her own, too﻿—produced from a bag that hung from her waist a half-finished stocking and began to knit silently and with the skill characteristic of the German housewife. I looked at her attentively (though she was so much in the shadow that I could see her but indistinctly) and somehow her appearance prepossessed me as little as did that of the other members of the household. Yet she was not an ill-looking woman. She had an excellent figure, and the air of a person of good social position; her features were good enough and her colouring, although a little unusual, was not unpleasant. Like Mr. Weiss, she had very fair hair, greased, parted in the middle and brushed down as smoothly as the painted hair of a Dutch doll. She appeared to have no eyebrows at all﻿—owing, no doubt, to the light colour of the hair﻿—and the doll-like character was emphasized by her eyes, which were either brown or dark grey, I could not see which. A further peculiarity consisted in a “habit spasm,” such as one often sees in nervous children; a periodical quick jerk of the head, as if a cap-string or dangling lock were being shaken off the cheek. Her age I judged to be about thirty-five.

===Chinese===
她在床边的椅子上坐下，抬手替病人挡住烛光——也顺带挡住自己的——从挂在腰间的一只袋子里摸出一只织了一半的袜子，无声地织了起来，那份手艺，正是德国家庭主妇特有的娴熟。我留神打量她（虽说她大半隐在暗影里，我只能看个依稀），不知怎么，她的相貌同这一家其余的人一样引不起我的好感。其实她并非是个相貌难看的女人。她身段极好，有股大家出身的气度；五官端正，那种发色虽略嫌少见，倒也不惹人厌。同韦斯先生一样，她有一头极淡的金发，抹了油，从中间分开，梳理得像荷兰娃娃画上去的头发一样溜光。她看上去简直像全然没有眉毛——不用说，是发色太浅之故——而那双眼睛——究竟是棕色还是深灰，我分辨不出——又把那股娃娃气添得更足。再有一桩特别处，是她有一种“习惯性痉挛”，就是神经质的孩子身上常见的那种：脑袋每隔一阵猛地一抽，仿佛要把帽带或一绺垂发从脸颊上甩脱。她的年纪，我估摸在三十五岁上下。

===Original===
The carriage, which one might have expected to be waiting, seemed to take some time in getting ready. I sat, with growing impatience, listening to the sick man’s soft breathing and the click of the housekeeper’s knitting-needles. I wanted to get home, not only for my own sake; the patient’s condition made it highly desirable that the remedies should be given as quickly as possible. But the minutes dragged on, and I was on the point of expostulating when a bell rang on the landing.

===Chinese===
那辆按理早该候着的马车，备办起来却似乎要费些时候。我坐着，越来越按捺不住，耳里听着病人轻缓的呼吸和管家妇毛线针的嗒嗒声。我想回家，倒不只是为着自己；照病人的情形，药物越早用下越好，这可是头等要紧。可是一分一秒磨蹭过去，我正要开口催问，楼梯平台上铃响了。

===Original===
“The carriage is ready,” said Mrs. Schallibaum. “Let me light you down the stairs.”

===Chinese===
“马车备好了，”沙利鲍姆太太说，“我来掌烛送您下楼。”

===Original===
She rose, and, taking the candle, preceded me to the head of the stairs, where she stood holding the light over the baluster-rail as I descended and crossed the passage to the open side door. The carriage was drawn up in the covered way as I could see by the faint glimmer of the distant candle; which also enabled me dimly to discern the coachman standing close by in the shadow. I looked round, rather expecting to see Mr. Weiss, but, as he made no appearance, I entered the carriage. The door was immediately banged to and locked, and I then heard the heavy bolts of the gates withdrawn and the loud creaking of hinges. The carriage moved out slowly and stopped; the gates slammed to behind me; I felt the lurch as the coachman climbed to his seat and we started forward.

===Chinese===
她起身端起蜡烛，引我走到楼梯口，便举烛倚着栏杆，照我下楼、穿过过道，走向敞开的边门。借那点远远的微弱烛光，我看出马车还停在顶棚过道里；那点光也让我隐约辨出紧傍在暗处的马车夫。我环顾四周，颇盼能见到韦斯先生，但他没有露面，我便上了车。车门立时砰地合上、落了锁，接着我听见大门沉重的门闩抽开，还有铰链响亮的吱嘎。马车缓缓挪出，停了停；大门在我身后哐当合拢；车身一晃，是马车夫爬上了驭座——我们启程前行。

===Original===
My reflections during the return journey were the reverse of agreeable. I could not rid myself of the conviction that I was being involved in some very suspicious proceedings. It was possible, of course, that this feeling was due to the strange secrecy that surrounded my connection with this case; that, had I made my visit under ordinary conditions, I might have found in the patient’s symptoms nothing to excite suspicion or alarm. It might be so, but that consideration did not comfort me.

===Chinese===
归途中的种种思绪决计说不上愉快。我甩不掉一个念头：自己正被卷进某桩形迹十分可疑的勾当里去。当然，这种感觉也可能出自这桩案子笼罩在我头上的那层诡秘；倘若我是在寻常境况下出的诊，说不定在病人的症状里根本寻不出什么足以起疑、足以告警的东西。也许是这样吧；可这层思量并没有安慰到我。

===Original===
Then, my diagnosis might be wrong. It might be that this was, in reality, a case of some brain affection accompanied by compression, such as slow haemorrhage, abscess, tumour or simple congestion. These cases were very difficult at times. But the appearances in this one did not consistently agree with the symptoms accompanying any of these conditions. As to sleeping sickness, it was, perhaps a more hopeful suggestion, but I could not decide for or against it until I had more knowledge; and against this view was the weighty fact that the symptoms did exactly agree with the theory of morphine poisoning.

===Chinese===
再者，我的诊断也可能有误。实情也许是一宗脑疾伴压迫的病例，比如慢性出血、脓肿、瘤子，或者单纯的充血。这类病例有时极难断。但此例的种种表现，跟上述任何一种情形相随的症状都对不拢。至于昏睡病，那设想或许更叫人存几分指望，可我须得有了更多识见才能下一断语；而与这一说相抗的，是一个沉甸甸的事实：这些症状恰恰同吗啡中毒之说严丝合缝。

===Original===
But even so, there was no conclusive evidence of any criminal act. The patient might be a confirmed opium-eater, and the symptoms heightened by deliberate deception. The cunning of these unfortunates is proverbial and is only equalled by their secretiveness and mendacity. It would be quite possible for this man to feign profound stupor so long as he was watched, and then, when left alone for a few minutes, to nip out of bed and help himself from some secret store of the drug. This would be quite in character with his objection to seeing a doctor and his desire for secrecy. But still, I did not believe it to be the true explanation. In spite of all the various alternative possibilities, my suspicions came back to Mr. Weiss and the strange, taciturn woman, and refused to budge.

===Chinese===
但纵然如此，也没有任何确凿证据表明存在犯罪行为。病人也许是个积习已深的鸦片鬼，症状又被存心的装假添了几分颜色。这类可怜人的狡黠是有口皆碑的，唯一能与之匹敌的，是他们的讳莫如深和满口谎话。这个人尽可以一面受人监视、一面佯作深度的昏迷，待到独处几分钟的空当，一骨碌溜下床，从私藏的药里自行取用。这同他反对见医生、渴望隐姓埋名的做派倒是十分相符。可我还是不信这就是真解。任凭种种旁的可能性如何摆列，我的怀疑仍旧回到韦斯先生和那个古怪缄默的女人身上，寸步不移。

===Original===
For all the circumstances of the case were suspicious. The elaborate preparations implied by the state of the carriage in which I was travelling; the makeshift appearance of the house; the absence of ordinary domestic servants, although a coachman was kept; the evident desire of Mr. Weiss and the woman to avoid thorough inspection of their persons; and, above all, the fact that the former had told me a deliberate lie. For he had lied, beyond all doubt. His statement as to the almost continuous stupor was absolutely irreconcilable with his other statement as to the patient’s wilfulness and obstinacy and even more irreconcilable with the deep and comparatively fresh marks of the spectacles on the patient’s nose. That man had certainly worn spectacles within twenty-four hours, which he would hardly have done if he had been in a state bordering on coma.

===Chinese===
因为这桩案子的所有情形都可疑。我乘坐的那辆马车的状况所暗含的周密布置；宅子那副将就凑合的模样；明明养着马车夫，却没有寻常的家仆；韦斯先生和那女人显然不愿让人细看他们的相貌；而尤其是，前者分明对我说了一个存心的谎。因为他确确实实撒了谎。他那一套几于不间断的昏迷之说，同他另一套关于病人任性执拗的说法绝对无法两立，而同病人鼻上那道又深、又比较新鲜的镜架压痕，就更无法两立了。那人二十四小时之内必定戴过眼镜；倘若真处在一种濒于昏迷的状态，他是决计不会戴的。

===Original===
My reflections were interrupted by the stopping of the carriage. The door was unlocked and thrown open, and I emerged from my dark and stuffy prison opposite my own house.

===Chinese===
马车一停，我的思绪也断了。车门开了锁、被拉开，我从那黑暗憋闷的囚笼里钻出来，正对着自家的家门。

===Original===
“I will let you have the medicine in a minute or two,” I said to the coachman; and, as I let myself in with my latchkey, my mind came back swiftly from the general circumstances of the case to the very critical condition of the patient. Already I was regretting that I had not taken more energetic measures to rouse him and restore his flagging vitality; for it would be a terrible thing if he should take a turn for the worse and die before the coachman returned with the remedies. Spurred on by this alarming thought, I made up the medicines quickly and carried the hastily wrapped bottles out to the man, whom I found standing by the horse’s head.

===Chinese===
“过一两分钟我就把药给你拿来，”我对马车夫说；用弹簧钥匙开门进屋时，我的心思已从案子的种种情由，倏地转回到病人万分危急的境况上。我已经在懊悔，方才没有采取更有力的措施唤醒他、挽回他委顿的生机；倘若他病情一转恶化，在马车夫带回药物之前便死去了，那可就可怕了。被这个吓人的念头鞭策着，我飞快配好药，抱起胡乱裹住的药瓶出去交给那人——只见他正立在马头旁边。

===Original===
“Get back as quickly as you can,” I said, “and tell Mr. Weiss to lose no time in giving the patient the draught in the small bottle. The directions are on the labels.”

===Chinese===
“尽快赶回去，”我说，“告诉韦斯先生，立刻把小瓶里的药水给病人服下。用法都写在标签上。”

===Original===
The coachman took the packages from me without reply, climbed to his seat, touched the horse with his whip and drove off at a rapid pace towards Newington Butts.

===Chinese===
马车夫一言不发地接过药包，爬上驭座，一鞭挥去，朝着纽因顿巴茨（Newington Butts）方向疾驰而去。

===Original===
The little clock in the consulting-room showed that it was close on eleven; time for a tired G.P. to be thinking of bed. But I was not sleepy. Over my frugal supper I found myself taking up anew the thread of my meditations, and afterwards, as I smoked my last pipe by the expiring surgery fire, the strange and sinister features of the case continued to obtrude themselves on my notice. I looked up Stillbury’s little reference library for information on the subject of sleeping sickness, but learned no more than that it was “a rare and obscure disease of which very little was known at present.” I read up morphine poisoning and was only further confirmed in the belief that my diagnosis was correct; which would have been more satisfactory if the circumstances had been different.

===Chinese===
诊室里的小钟指着快十一点了；一个困乏的开业医生，也该想着上床了。可我毫无睡意。就着俭省的晚饭，我发觉自己又把沉思的线头重新拾起；饭后，我偎着诊疗室里将熄的炉火抽当日最后一斗烟时，这案子种种古怪而不祥的面目仍旧一径往我的注意里闯。我在斯蒂尔伯里那小小的参考书库里查寻昏睡病的资料，结果所得不过一句：“一种罕见而晦暗之疾，目前所知甚少。”我又翻阅了吗啡中毒的文献，结果只是愈发确信我的诊断无误——倘若种种情形不是这般，这本该是件更叫人称心的事。

===Original===
For the interest of the case was not merely academic. I was in a position of great difficulty and responsibility and had to decide on a course of action. What ought I to do? Should I maintain the professional secrecy to which I was tacitly committed, or ought I to convey a hint to the police?

===Chinese===
因为这案子的干系并不止于学问。我正处在一个艰难而责任重大的位置上，必须定出一条行动的路数。我该怎么办？是恪守我已默认承下的职业秘密，还是该向警方透个风声？

===Original===
Suddenly, and with a singular feeling of relief, I bethought myself of my old friend and fellow-student, John Thorndyke, now an eminent authority on Medical Jurisprudence. I had been associated with him temporarily in one case as his assistant, and had then been deeply impressed by his versatile learning, his acuteness and his marvellous resourcefulness. Thorndyke was a barrister in extensive practice, and so would be able to tell me at once what was my duty from a legal point of view; and, as he was also a doctor of medicine, he would understand the exigencies of medical practice. If I could find time to call at the Temple and lay the case before him, all my doubts and difficulties would be resolved.

===Chinese===
忽然，怀着一种异样的宽慰，我想起了我的老友、当年的同窗约翰·桑代克，他如今已是医学法学（Medical Jurisprudence）方面鼎鼎大名的权威。我曾在一件案子里短期做过他的助手，那时便深深折服于他学识的渊博驳杂、他的明察秋毫，和他那惊人的随机应变之才。桑代克是一位业务繁盛的出庭律师，立刻便能告诉我，从法律的观点看，我的义务何在；而他同时又是一位医学博士，也能体察行医的种种难处。倘若我能抽空去一趟坦普尔（the Temple），把这桩案子摆在他面前，我的一切疑虑与为难便都消解了。

===Original===
Anxiously, I opened my visiting-list to see what kind of day’s work was in store for me on the morrow. It was not a heavy day, even allowing for one or two extra calls in the morning, but yet I was doubtful whether it would allow of my going so far from my district, until my eye caught, near the foot of the page, the name of Burton. Now Mr. Burton lived in one of the old houses on the east side of Bouverie Street, less than five minutes’ walk from Thorndyke’s chambers in King’s Bench Walk; and he was, moreover, a “chronic” who could safely be left for the last. When I had done with Mr. Burton I could look in on my friend with a very good chance of catching him on his return from the hospital. I could allow myself time for quite a long chat with him, and, by taking a hansom, still get back in good time for the evening’s work.

===Chinese===
我急切地翻开出诊簿，看看明天等着我的将是怎样一天的工作。那天不算繁重，即便把上午多出的一两处出诊也算上；可我仍拿不准它是否容得我跑出辖区这么远，直到目光落在近页脚处的“伯顿（Burton）”这个名字上。这位伯顿先生住在布弗里街（Bouverie Street）东侧的一幢老宅里，离桑代克在王座法庭步道（King’s Bench Walk）的寓所，步行不过五分钟；何况他又是个“老病号”，尽可安心留到最后再看。料理完伯顿先生，我就能顺道去瞧我那位朋友，十拿九稳正撞上他从医院回来。我尽可以从容同他畅谈一番，再雇一辆双轮马车（hansom），仍旧赶得及回来做晚间的活计。

===Original===
This was a great comfort. At the prospect of sharing my responsibilities with a friend on whose judgment I could so entirely rely, my embarrassments seemed to drop from me in a moment. Having entered the engagement in my visiting-list, I rose, in greatly improved spirits, and knocked out my pipe just as the little clock banged out impatiently the hour of midnight.

===Chinese===
这真是莫大的安慰。一想到能把肩上的担子分给一位其判断我可以全然信赖的朋友，我的种种窘困霎时间仿佛纷纷落地。我在出诊簿里记下这项安排，便起了身，心绪大为宽舒，把烟斗磕空——恰在此时，那只小钟不耐烦地铛铛敲出了午夜十二点。

## IV The Official View / 官方见解

===Original===
I rose on the following morning still possessed by the determination to make some oportunity during the day to call on Thorndyke and take his advice on the now urgent question as to what I was to do. I use the word “urgent” advisedly; for the incidents of the preceding evening had left me with the firm conviction that poison was being administered for some purpose to my mysterious patient, and that no time must be lost if his life was to be saved. Last night he had escaped only by the narrowest margin﻿—assuming him to be still alive﻿—and it was only my unexpectedly firm attitude that had compelled Mr. Weiss to agree to restorative measures.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨起身时，我心里仍揣着那个念头：当天无论如何要寻个机会去拜访桑代克，请教他的意见，好解一解眼下这个迫在眉睫的难题——我究竟该怎么办。“迫在眉睫”一词，我用得并非随便；因为前一晚的种种遭遇已使我深信，有人正出于某种目的，给我的神秘病人下毒，若要保住他的性命，就一刻也耽搁不得。昨夜他已是九死一生——姑且假定他眼下还活着——而若不是我那出乎意料的强硬态度，韦斯先生断不会同意采取救治措施。

===Original===
That I should be sent for again I had not the slightest expectation. If what I so strongly suspected was true, Weiss would call in some other doctor, in the hope of better luck, and it was imperative that he should be stopped before it was too late. This was my view, but I meant to have Thorndyke’s opinion, and act under his direction, but

===Chinese===
至于他们再来请我，我丝毫不抱指望。倘若我那强烈的怀疑属实，韦斯便会另请一位医生，指望换个运气；必须赶在太迟之前把他截住。这便是我的看法，不过我仍想听听桑代克的意见，在他的指点之下行事，可是——

===Original===
> “The best laid plans of mice and men
 

 Gang aft agley.”

===Chinese===
> “老鼠与人的万全之计，
> 到头来也常常落空。”

===Original===
When I came downstairs and took a preliminary glance at the rough memorandum-book, kept by the bottle-boy, or, in his absence, by the housemaid, I stood aghast. The morning’s entries looked already like a sample page of the Post Office directory. The new calls alone were more than equal to an ordinary day’s work, and the routine visits remained to be added. Gloomily wondering whether the Black Death had made a sudden reappearance in England, I hurried to the dining-room and made a hasty breakfast, interrupted at intervals by the apparition of the bottle-boy to announce new messages.

===Chinese===
我下楼来，先朝那本粗陋的备忘簿扫了一眼——那簿子由药童记录，他不在时便由女仆代管——顿时惊得说不出话。单是早晨的登记，看去已像《邮政地址录》（Post Office directory）的样张。光是新增的召唤，便抵得上一整天的寻常工作量，例行出诊还没算在内。我满心晦暗，直疑心黑死病在英格兰猝然复起，一面匆匆赶到餐室，仓促用早餐，其间一次次被药童的鬼影打断，他来通报新的口信。

===Original===
The first two or three visits solved the mystery. An epidemic of influenza had descended on the neighbourhood, and I was getting not only our own normal work but a certain amount of overflow from other practices. Further, it appeared that a strike in the building trade had been followed immediately by a widespread failure of health among the bricklayers who were members of a certain benefit club; which accounted for the remarkable suddenness of the outbreak.

===Chinese===
头两三趟出诊便解开了这个谜。一场流行性感冒降临这一带，我接下的不但是自家的正常诊务，还有别家诊所溢出的一份。此外还听说，建筑行业刚闹过一场罢工，紧随其后，某个互助会的砌砖工人便成片病倒；这便解释了这场疫病何以来得如此突兀。

===Original===
Of course, my contemplated visit to Thorndyke was out of the question. I should have to act on my own responsibility. But in the hurry and rush and anxiety of the work﻿—for some of the cases were severe and even critical﻿—I had no opportunity to consider any course of action, nor time to carry it out. Even with the aid of a hansom which I chartered, as Stillbury kept no carriage, I had not finished my last visit until near on midnight, and was then so spent with fatigue that I fell asleep over my postponed supper.

===Chinese===
当然，我原打算去拜访桑代克一事，是断无可能了。我只得自作主张，自负其责。但在诊务的匆遽、奔忙与焦灼之中——有几个病例沉重，甚至危急——我既无暇考虑任何行动方案，也没有工夫付诸实行。斯蒂尔伯里不备马车，我雇了一辆双轮马车代步；饶是如此，看完最后一趟出诊也已近午夜，而我早已筋疲力尽，对着推迟至今的晚饭，坐着便睡着了。

===Original===
As the next day opened with a further increase of work, I sent a telegram to Dr. Stillbury at Hastings, whither he had gone, like a wise man, to recruit after a slight illness. I asked for authority to engage an assistant, but the reply informed me that Stillbury himself was on his way to town; and to my relief, when I dropped in at the surgery for a cup of tea, I found him rubbing his hands over the open daybook.

===Chinese===
第二天一开张，诊务更是有增无减，我便给远在黑斯廷斯（Hastings）的斯蒂尔伯里医生发去一封电报——他小恙之后，像个明白人似的，到那里休养去了。我请求准许我雇一名助手，回电却说斯蒂尔伯里本人已在回城途中；待我顺道进诊室喝杯茶，果见他对着摊开的日记簿（daybook）搓手，我这才放了心。

===Original===
“It’s an ill wind that blows nobody good,” he remarked cheerfully as we shook hands. “This will pay the expenses of my holiday, including you. By the way, you are not anxious to be off, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
“这阵邪风，到底也有人沾光，”握手时他喜滋滋地说，“这一下，我休假的盘缠全有了着落，连带你那份也在内。对了，你不至于急着要走吧，我想？”

===Original===
As a matter of fact, I was; for I had decided to accept Thorndyke’s offer, and was now eager to take up my duties with him. But it would have been shabby to leave Stillbury to battle alone with this rush of work or to seek the services of a strange assistant.

===Chinese===
事实上我急着呢；因为我已决意接受桑代克的邀约，此刻巴不得早早到他手下就任。可若把斯蒂尔伯里一个人撇下，让他独力应付这阵忙乱，或是另去寻一位生疏的助手，未免太不地道。

===Original===
“I should like to get off as soon as you can spare me,” I replied, “but I’m not going to leave you in the lurch.”

===Chinese===
“您几时腾得开手，我就想几时脱身，”我答道，“可我也不会把您撂在难处不管。”

===Original===
“That’s a good fellow,” said Stillbury. “I knew you wouldn’t. Let us have some tea and divide up the work. Anything of interest going?”

===Chinese===
“这才是好伙计，”斯蒂尔伯里说，“我就知道你不会的。来，咱们喝点茶，把活计分一分。近来可有什么有意思的事？”

===Original===
There were one or two unusual cases on the list, and, as we marked off our respective patients, I gave him the histories in brief synopsis. And then I opened the subject of my mysterious experiences at the house of Mr. Weiss.

===Chinese===
单子上有一两例不寻常的病例，我们各自圈定病人的当口，我把病史向他作了简明扼要的概述。随后我便提起话头，讲起我在韦斯先生那所宅子里的神秘经历。

===Original===
“There’s another affair that I want to tell you about; rather an unpleasant business.”

===Chinese===
“还有一桩事我想告诉您；是件相当不痛快的事。”

===Original===
“Oh, dear!” exclaimed Stillbury. He put down his cup and regarded me with quite painful anxiety.

===Chinese===
“哦，天哪！”斯蒂尔伯里失声喊道。他放下茶杯，带着一脸几乎叫人看了难受的焦虑望着我。

===Original===
“It looks to me like an undoubted case of criminal poisoning,” I continued.

===Chinese===
“依我看，这是一桩确凿无疑的投毒害人案。”我接着说。

===Original===
Stillbury’s face cleared instantly. “Oh, I’m glad it’s nothing more than that,” he said with an air of relief. “I was afraid, it was some confounded woman. There’s always that danger, you know, when a locum is young and happens﻿—if I may say so, Jervis﻿—to be a good-looking fellow. Let us hear about this case.”

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔伯里的脸色霎时放晴。“哦，幸亏不过是这么档子事，”他一副如释重负的口气说，“我还当是哪个可恶的女人闹出来的呢。您知道，代理医生但凡年轻，又碰巧——恕我直言，杰维斯——生得仪表堂堂，这种风险总是在所难免。来，说说这个案子吧。”

===Original===
I gave him a condensed narrative of my connection with the mysterious patient, omitting any reference to Thorndyke, and passing lightly over my efforts to fix the position of the house, and wound up with the remark that the facts ought certainly to be communicated to the police.

===Chinese===
我把自己同那位神秘病人的牵连，向他作了一番扼要的叙述，只字不提桑代克，对我设法确定那所宅子位置的种种努力也一笔带过，末了说，这些情况理应报告警方。

===Original===
“Yes,” he admitted reluctantly, “I suppose you’re right. Deuced unpleasant though. Police cases don’t do a practice any good. They waste a lot of time, too; keep you hanging about to give evidence. Still, you are quite right. We can’t stand by and see the poor devil poisoned without making some effort. But I don’t believe the police will do anything in the matter.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”他不甚情愿地承认道，“我想你是对的。只是糟心得很。闹出警务案子，对诊所的名声没有半点好处。还白白糟蹋工夫，把你吊在那儿随时等着作证。不过，你确实说得对。总不能眼睁睁看着那可怜虫被毒死而袖手不管。可我不信警方会为此有什么举动。”

===Original===
“Don’t you really?”

===Chinese===
“您真不信？”

===Original===
“No, I don’t. They like to have things pretty well cut and dried before they act. A prosecution is an expensive affair, so they don’t care to prosecute unless they are fairly sure of a conviction. If they fail they get hauled over the coals.”

===Chinese===
“不信，我真不信。他们喜欢把事情弄得十拿九稳、成竹在胸，才肯动手。起诉是桩花钱的事，所以除非有相当把握定罪，他们轻易不肯起诉。一旦失手，就得挨上头一顿好训。”

===Original===
“But don’t you think they would get a conviction in this case?”

===Chinese===
“可您不觉得，这桩案子他们一告便能定罪吗？”

===Original===
“Not on your evidence, Jervis. They might pick up something fresh, but, if they didn’t they would fail. You haven’t got enough hard-baked facts to upset a capable defence. Still, that isn’t our affair. You want to put the responsibility on the police and I entirely agree with you.”

===Chinese===
“凭你这份证词定不了，杰维斯。他们或许能另挖出些新的东西，可若挖不出，便要败诉。你手里的铁板钉钉的事实还不够，扳不倒一个有能耐的辩护人。话虽如此，那不是咱们的事。你想把责任推给警方，我完全赞成。”

===Original===
“There ought not to be any delay,” said I.

===Chinese===
“这事不该有任何耽搁。”我说。

===Original===
“There needn’t be. I shall look in on Mrs. Wackford and you have to see the Rummel children; we shall pass the station on our way. Why shouldn’t we drop in and see the inspector or superintendent?”

===Chinese===
“也无需耽搁。我要去看沃克福德太太（Mrs. Wackford），你呢，得去看拉梅尔家（Rummel）的孩子们；咱们路上正好经过警察局。何不顺道进去，见见巡官或者督察？”

===Original===
The suggestion met my views exactly. As soon as we had finished tea, we set forth, and in about ten minutes found ourselves in the bare and forbidding office attached to the station.

===Chinese===
这提议正合我意。茶一用完，我们便出发，约莫十分钟后，已置身于警察局附设的那间空落落、冷面无情的办公室里。

===Original===
The presiding officer descended from a high stool, and, carefully laying down his pen, shook hands cordially.

===Chinese===
主事警官从一张高脚凳上下来，小心地搁下笔，热络地同我们握手。

===Original===
“And what can I do for you gentlemen?” he asked, with an affable smile.

===Chinese===
“两位先生，我能为你们效什么劳？”他挂着一副和蔼的笑容问道。

===Original===
Stillbury proceeded to open our business.

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔伯里便开始道明我们的来意。

===Original===
“My friend here, Dr. Jervis, who has very kindly been looking after my work for a week or two, has had a most remarkable experience, and he wants to tell you about it.”

===Chinese===
“我这位朋友，杰维斯医生，承他好意，替我照看了一两个星期的诊务，最近有了一段非同寻常的经历，他想来说给您听听。”

===Original===
“Something in my line of business?” the officer inquired.

===Chinese===
“是我分内的事吗？”警官问道。

===Original===
“That,” said I, “is for you to judge. I think it is, but you may think otherwise”; and hereupon, without further preamble, I plunged into the history of the case, giving him a condensed statement similar to that which I had already made to Stillbury.

===Chinese===
“这，”我说，“得由您来判断。我看是，不过您也许另有看法。”说罢我便不再多作开场白，一头讲起案子的始末，向他作了份简明陈述，同我方才对斯蒂尔伯里讲的相仿。

===Original===
He listened with close attention, jotting down from time to time a brief note on a sheet of paper; and, when I had finished, he wrote out in a black-covered notebook a short précis of my statement.

===Chinese===
他凝神谛听，不时在一页纸上草草记下寥寥数语；待我讲完，他又在一本黑皮笔记本里，把我的陈述写成一篇简短的摘要（précis）。

===Original===
“I have written down here,” he said, “the substance of what you have told me. I will read the deposition over to you, and, if it is correct, I will ask you to sign it.”

===Chinese===
“我在这里记下的，”他说，“是您向我陈述的要点。我把这份笔录念给您听，若没有出入，就要请您签个字。”

===Original===
He did so, and, when I had signed the document, I asked him what was likely to be done in the matter.

===Chinese===
他照着念了；我在文书上签过字，便问他对这事打算如何处置。

===Original===
“I am afraid,” he replied, “that we can’t take any active measures. You have put us on our guard and we shall keep our eyes open. But I think that is all we can do, unless we hear something further.”

===Chinese===
“恐怕，”他答道，“我们没法采取任何主动措施。您已让我们有了提防，我们会睁大眼睛留心。但除非再听到什么新的动静，我想我们能做的就只有这些了。”

===Original===
“But,” I exclaimed, “don’t you think that it is a very suspicious affair?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我嚷道，“您不觉得这桩事非常可疑吗？”

===Original===
“I do,” he replied. “A very fishy business indeed, and you were quite right to come and tell us about it.”

===Chinese===
“我觉得，”他答道，“这实在是一桩腥气十足的勾当，您来向我们报告，做得一点不错。”

===Original===
“It seems a pity not to take some measures,” I said. “While you are waiting to hear something further, they may give the poor wretch a fresh dose and kill him.”

===Chinese===
“一点措施不采取，未免可惜，”我说，“趁你们在这儿等着听新的动静，他们兴许又给那可怜人喂上一剂，把他就此结果了。”

===Original===
“In which case we should hear something further, unless some fool of a doctor were to give a death certificate.”

===Chinese===
“真到那一步，我们自然就听到新的动静了——除非有哪个糊涂蛋医生肯给他开死亡证明。”

===Original===
“But that is very unsatisfactory. The man ought not to be allowed to die.”

===Chinese===
“可这也太不能令人满意了。总不该容这个人就这么死掉。”

===Original===
“I quite agree with you, sir. But we’ve no evidence that he is going to die. His friends sent for you, and you treated him skilfully and left him in a fair way to recovery. That’s all that we really know about it. Yes, I know,” the officer continued as I made signs of disagreement, “you think that a crime is possibly going to be committed and that we ought to prevent it. But you overrate our powers. We can only act on evidence that a crime has actually been committed or is actually being attempted. Now we have no such evidence. Look at your statement, and tell me what you can swear to.”

===Chinese===
“我完全同意您，先生。可我们没有任何证据表明他会死。他的朋友们派人来请您，您施治得当，离开时他已处在颇有指望康复的境况。我们真正知道的，就这么多。是，我知道，”见我连连做出不以为然的神色，警官接着说，“您是想说，一桩罪案可能即将发生，认为我们理应加以阻止。但您把我们的权能估量得太高了。我们只能凭这样的证据行事：一桩罪案业已发生，或正在实际施行。而眼下我们没有这样的证据。请您瞧瞧您那份陈述，告诉我，哪几条是您能起誓作保的。”

===Original===
“I think I could swear that Mr. Graves had taken a poisonous dose of morphine.”

===Chinese===
“我想我能起誓：格雷夫斯先生服下过致毒剂量的吗啡。”

===Original===
“And who gave him that poisonous dose?”

===Chinese===
“那么，是谁给他服下那致毒剂量的呢？”

===Original===
“I very strongly suspect﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我极其强烈地怀疑是——”

===Original===
“That’s no good, sir,” interrupted the officer. “Suspicion isn’t evidence. We should want you to swear an information and give us enough facts to make out a prima facie case against some definite person. And you couldn’t do it. Your information amounts to this: that a certain person has taken a poisonous dose of morphine and apparently recovered. That’s all. You can’t swear that the names given to you are real names, and you can’t give us any address or even any locality.”

===Chinese===
“那没有用，先生，”警官打断道，“怀疑算不得证据。我们得请您立一份宣誓控状，拿出足够的事实，对某个确指之人构成表面成立的案件（prima facie case）。而这您做不到。您的控状拢共是说：某人服下过致毒剂量的吗啡，而且显然已经康复。仅此而已。报给您的那些姓名是否是真名，您起不了誓；任何地址您也给不出，连个大致的地段都指不出来。”

===Original===
“I took some compass bearings in the carriage,” I said. “You could locate the house, I think, without much difficulty.”

===Chinese===
“我在马车里记下了几个罗盘方位，”我说，“要定位那所宅子，我想你们不费什么难。”

===Original===
The officer smiled faintly and fixed an abstracted gaze on the clock.

===Chinese===
警官淡淡一笑，把一种心不在焉的目光钉在时钟上。

===Original===
“*You* could, sir,” he replied. “I have no doubt whatever that *you* could. *I* couldn’t. But, in any case, we haven’t enough to go upon. If you learn anything fresh, I hope you will let me know; and I am very much obliged to you for taking so much trouble in the matter. Good evening sir. Good evening, Dr. Stillbury.”

===Chinese===
“*您*能，先生，”他答道，“您能定位，这一点我毫不怀疑。*我*不能。不过无论如何，我们手头可凭的线索太少了。您若再打听到什么新的消息，望您让我知道；这事劳您费了这么多心，我不胜感激。晚安，先生。晚安，斯蒂尔伯里医生。”

===Original===
He shook hands with us both genially, and, accepting perforce this very polite but unmistakable dismissal, we took our departure.

===Chinese===
他和蔼可亲地同我们俩一一握手；我们只得领受这道礼貌至极却明白无误的逐客令，起身告辞。

===Original===
Outside the station, Stillbury heaved a comfortable sigh. He was evidently relieved to find that no upheavals were to take place in his domain.

===Chinese===
警察局外头，斯蒂尔伯里舒舒服服地叹了口气。眼见自己这一亩三分地里掀不起什么风波，他显然如释重负。

===Original===
“I thought that would be their attitude,” he said, “and they are quite right, you know. The function of law is to prevent crime, it is true; but prophylaxis in the sense in which we understand it is not possible in legal practice.”

===Chinese===
“我料到他们便是这个态度，”他说，“而且他们一点没错，您知道。法律的职能确乎在于防止犯罪；但照我们行医人理解的那层意义上的预防，在法律实务里是办不到的。”

===Original===
I assented without enthusiasm. It was disappointing to find that no precautionary measures were to be taken. However, I had done all that I could in the matter. No further responsibility lay upon me, and, as it was practically certain that I had seen and heard the last of Mr. Graves and his mysterious household, I dismissed the case from my mind. At the next corner Stillbury and I parted to go our respective ways; and my attention was soon transferred from the romance of crime to the realities of epidemic influenza.

===Chinese===
我有气无力地应了一声是。得知不会采取任何防范措施，实在叫人泄气。不过，这事我已尽了人事。进一步的干系已不在我身上；而且，我同格雷夫斯先生及其神秘宅院的缘分，实际上已到头了——再不会相见，也再不会听闻——我便把这案子从心里打发了。到了下一个街角，我和斯蒂尔伯里分手，各奔各路；我的心思也很快从罪案的传奇，转到了流行性感冒的现实上头。

===Original===
The plethora of work in Dr. Stillbury’s practice continued longer than I had bargained for. Day after day went by and still found me tramping the dingy streets of Kennington or scrambling up and down narrow stairways; turning in at night dead tired, or turning out half awake to the hideous jangle of the night bell.

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔伯里医生诊所里这过剩的活计，持续得比我预想的久。一天天过去，我依旧在肯宁顿那些灰暗的街上奔波，或在窄楼梯上爬上爬下；夜里归来，筋疲力尽；或半夜被夜铃那可憎的叮当催起，半睡半醒地出门应诊。

===Original===
It was very provoking. For months I had resisted Thorndyke’s persuasion to give up general practice and join him. Not from lack of inclination, but from a deep suspicion that he was thinking of my wants rather than his own; that his was a charitable rather than a business proposal. Now that I knew this not to be the case, I was impatient to join him; and, as I trudged through the dreary thoroughfares of this superannuated suburb, with its once rustic villas and its faded gardens, my thoughts would turn enviously to the quiet dignity of the Temple and my friend’s chambers in King’s Bench Walk.

===Chinese===
这实在恼人。几个月来，我一直拗着不肯依从桑代克的劝——放弃全科开业，去与他共事。倒不是心不愿，而是深深疑心他惦记的是我的窘境，而非他自己的需要；疑心那与其说是一桩生意上的提议，不如说是一番好心的周济。如今既知并非如此，我便急不可耐地想去投他；当我拖着步子，走过这处老朽郊区沉闷的大街——两旁是昔日的村舍别墅和褪了色的园圃——我的思绪便一次次艳羡地飞向坦普尔那静穆的气象，飞向朋友的寓所所在的王座法庭步道。

===Original===
The closed carriage appeared no more; nor did any whisper either of good or evil reach me in connection with the mysterious house from which it had come. Mr. Graves had apparently gone out of my life forever.

===Chinese===
那辆封得严严实实的马车再没露面；与它所来自的那所神秘宅子相关的一切，也无论吉凶，再无半点风声传到我耳中。格雷夫斯先生显然已从我的生活里永远消失了。

===Original===
But if he had gone out of my life, he had not gone out of my memory. Often, as I walked my rounds, would the picture of that dimly-lit room rise unbidden. Often would I find myself looking once more into that ghastly face, so worn, so wasted and haggard, and yet so far from repellent. All the incidents of that last night would reconstitute themselves with a vividness that showed the intensity of the impression that they had made at the time. I would have gladly forgotten the whole affair, for every incident of it was fraught with discomfort. But it clung to my memory; it haunted me; and ever as it returned it bore with it the disquieting questions: Was Mr. Graves still alive? And, if he was not, was there really nothing which could have been done to save him?

===Chinese===
可他虽走出了我的生活，却始终没走出我的记忆。多少回，我在出诊途中，那间灯光昏暗的屋子的图景会不召自来。多少回，我发现自己又在端详那张死人般的脸——那样憔悴，那样枯槁瘦削，却又不带半点叫人憎厌之处。最后一夜的种种情事，会自行一一重组，鲜活如昨，足见它们当时给我的印象何等深刻。我巴不得把整桩事忘个干净，因为其中每一幕都浸透着不安。可它偏黏在记忆里，缠住我不放；而且每当它重来，总捎来那几个扰人的疑问：格雷夫斯先生还活着吗？倘若不在了，当真就无计可施、救不了他了吗？

===Original===
Nearly a month passed before the practice began to show signs of returning to its normal condition. Then the daily lists became more and more contracted and the day’s work proportionately shorter. And thus the term of my servitude came to an end. One evening, as we were writing up the daybook, Stillbury remarked:

===Chinese===
将近一个月过去，诊所才显出回复常态的迹象。每日的诊务单于是越来越短，一天的活计也随之递减。我这段苦役的期限，就这样到了头。一天晚上，我们正补记日记簿，斯蒂尔伯里开口道：

===Original===
“I almost think, Jervis, I could manage by myself now. I know you are only staying on for my sake.”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯，我简直觉得，如今我一个人也应付得过来了。我知道，你留下纯粹是为了我。”

===Original===
“I am staying on to finish my engagement, but I shan’t be sorry to clear out if you can do without me.”

===Chinese===
“我留下，是为了做满聘期；不过您若离得开我，我走便是了，绝无不舍。”

===Original===
“I think I can. When would you like to be off?”

===Chinese===
“我想离得开。你想几时动身？”

===Original===
“As soon as possible. Say tomorrow morning, after I have made a few visits and transferred the patients to you.”

===Chinese===
“越快越好。就说明天上午吧，等我跑完几趟出诊，把病人移交给您。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Stillbury. “Then I will give you your cheque and settle up everything tonight, so that you shall be free to go off when you like tomorrow morning.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”斯蒂尔伯里说，“那我今晚就把支票开给你，把一切账目结清，明天早晨你爱几时走就几时走。”

===Original===
Thus ended my connection with Kennington Lane. On the following day at about noon, I found myself strolling across Waterloo Bridge with the sensations of a newly liberated convict and a cheque for twenty-five guineas in my pocket. My luggage was to follow when I sent for it. Now, unhampered even by a handbag, I joyfully descended the steps at the north end of the bridge and headed for King’s Bench Walk by way of the Embankment and Middle Temple Lane.

===Chinese===
我与肯宁顿巷的这一段瓜葛，就此了结。第二天中午时分，我正踱过滑铁卢桥（Waterloo Bridge），心情宛如一名新获释放的囚犯，口袋里揣着一张二十五几尼的支票。行李等我去信再随后运来。此刻连一只手提包的拖累也没有，我欢欢喜喜走下桥北端的台阶，取道河滨大道（the Embankment）与中殿巷（Middle Temple Lane），朝王座法庭步道而去。

## XII The Portrait / 肖像

===Original===
The state of mind which Thorndyke had advised me to cultivate was one that did not come easily. However much I endeavoured to shuffle the facts of the Blackmore case, there was one which inevitably turned up on the top of the pack. The circumstances surrounding the execution of Jeffrey Blackmore’s will intruded into all my cogitations on the subject with hopeless persistency. That scene in the porter’s lodge was to me what King Charles’s head was to poor Mr. Dick. In the midst of my praiseworthy efforts to construct some intelligible scheme of the case, it would make its appearance and reduce my mind to instant chaos.

===Chinese===
桑代克劝我养成的那种心境，实在不易养成。无论我怎样竭力洗布莱克莫尔案这副事实之牌，总有一张无可避免地翻上牌堆的顶端。围绕杰弗里·布莱克莫尔遗嘱订立过程的种种情形，以无可救药的执拗，侵入我在这题目上的一切思索。门房里的那一幕之于我，犹如查理王的头（King Charles’s head）之于可怜的狄克先生（Mr. Dick）。每当我正本着可嘉的干劲，为这案子构想某种说得通的方案时，它便要登场，霎时把我的心智搅成一团混沌。

===Original===
For the next few days, Thorndyke was very much occupied with one or two civil cases, which kept him in court during the whole of the sitting; and when he came home, he seemed indisposed to talk on professional topics. Meanwhile, Polton worked steadily at the photographs of the signatures, and, with a view to gaining experience, I assisted him and watched his methods.

===Chinese===
此后几天，桑代克为一两桩民事诉讼忙得脱不开身，整个庭期都守在法庭里；回到家来，似乎也不愿谈本行的话题。其间，波尔顿不紧不慢地做那些签名照片的活计；我为了长些经验，从旁协助，观察他的手法。

===Original===
In the present case, the signatures were enlarged from their original dimensions﻿—rather less than an inch and a half in length﻿—to a length of four and a half inches; which rendered all the little peculiarities of the handwriting surprisingly distinct and conspicuous. Each signature was eventually mounted on a slip of card bearing a number and the date of the cheque from which it was taken, so that it was possible to place any two signatures together for comparison. I looked over the whole series and very carefully compared those which showed any differences, but without discovering anything more than might have been expected in view of Mr. Britton’s statement. There were some trifling variations, but they were all very much alike, and no one could doubt, on looking at them, that they were all written by the same hand.

===Chinese===
在本案里，那些签名从原先的尺寸——长约不到一英寸半——放大到四英寸半长；这一来，笔迹上种种细小的特点便出奇地分明醒目。每个签名最后都裱贴在一张卡片纸条上，编有号码，并注明所取自的那张支票的日期，如此便可把任意两个签名摆到一处比较。我把整个系列检视了一遍，又把凡显出差异的都极仔细地比较过，却除了布里顿先生那番话所能预期的之外，并没有发现什么。有少许零星的变化，但它们全都十分相像，任何人一看便不会怀疑：它们全出自同一只手。

===Original===
As this, however, was apparently not in dispute, it furnished no new information. Thorndyke’s object﻿—for I felt certain that he had something definite in his mind﻿—must be to test something apart from the genuineness of the signatures. But what could that something be? I dared not ask him, for questions of that kind were anathema, so there was nothing for it but to lie low and see what he would do with the photographs.

===Chinese===
然而这一点既然显然没有争议，也就提供不出什么新的信息。桑代克的目的——因为我确信他心里已装着某种明确的东西——必是要检验签名真伪以外的什么。可那会是什么呢？我不敢问他，因为那一类问题是他深恶的禁忌，于是别无他法，只好蛰伏不动，看他对这些照片作何处置。

===Original===
The whole series was finished on the fourth morning after my adventure at Sloane Square, and the pack of cards was duly delivered by Polton when he brought in the breakfast tray. Thorndyke took up the pack somewhat with the air of a whist player, and, as he ran through them, I noticed that the number had increased from twenty-three to twenty-four.

===Chinese===
整套照片在我斯隆广场那番历险之后的第四天早晨完工，那一叠卡片由波尔顿端着早餐托盘进来时一并郑重交付。桑代克拿起这叠卡片，神气颇有几分像一位惠斯特牌（whist）玩家；他一张张翻检时，我注意到数目已从二十三张增加到二十四张。

===Original===
“The additional one,” Thorndyke explained, “is the signature to the first will, which was in Marchmont’s possession. I have added it to the collection as it carries us back to an earlier date. The signature of the second will presumably resembles those of the cheques drawn about the same date. But that is not material, or, if it should become so, we could claim to examine the second will.”

===Chinese===
“添上的这一张，”桑代克解释道，“是第一份遗嘱上的签名，那遗嘱一直在马奇蒙特手里。我把它添进这套收藏，因为它把我们带回到一个更早的日期。第二份遗嘱上的签名，想必同前后日期所开的那几张支票相像。但这无关紧要；倘若日后竟紧要起来，我们尽可要求查验第二份遗嘱。”

===Original===
He laid the cards out on the table in the order of their dates and slowly ran his eye down the series. I watched him closely and ventured presently to ask:

===Chinese===
他把卡片按日期先后摊在桌上，目光顺着这一列缓缓看下去。我紧盯着他，过了一会儿才斗胆发问：

===Original===
“Do you agree with Mr. Britton as to the general identity of character in the whole set of signatures?”

===Chinese===
“整套签名总体特征出于一手这一点，你同意布里顿先生的看法吗？”

===Original===
“Yes,” he replied. “I should certainly have put them down as being all the signatures of one person. The variations are very slight. The later signatures are a little stiffer, a little more shaky and indistinct, and the *B’s* and *k’s* are both appreciably different from those in the earlier ones. But there is another fact which emerges when the whole series is seen together, and it is so striking and significant a fact, that I am astonished at its not having been remarked on by Mr. Britton.”

===Chinese===
“同意，”他答道，“我当然会把它们认作同一个人的一批签名。变化甚微。较晚的签名略显僵硬，略显颤抖、模糊，*B* 与 *k* 的写法同早先签名里的相比，也都有可觉察的差异。但把整个系列放在一起看，还会显出另一个事实；而这事实如此醒目、如此意味深长，布里顿先生竟没有一句提到它，实在令我诧异。”

===Original===
“Indeed!” said I, stooping to examine the photographs with fresh interest; “what is that?”

===Chinese===
“真的吗！”我说着，俯身带着新的兴致细看那些照片；“那是什么？”

===Original===
“It is a very simple fact and very obvious, but yet, as I have said, very significant. Look carefully at number one, which is the signature of the first will, dated three years ago, and compare it with number three, dated the eighteenth of September last year.”

===Chinese===
“这是一个非常简单、也非常明显的事实，但正如我方才所说，非常意味深长。细看第一号，那是第一份遗嘱的签名，日期在三年前；再把它同第三号比较，第三号日期是去年九月十八日。”

===Original===
“They look to me identical,” said I, after a careful comparison.

===Chinese===
仔细比较一番之后，我说：“依我看，它们一模一样。”

===Original===
“So they do to me,” said Thorndyke. “Neither of them shows the change that occurred later. But if you look at number two, dated the sixteenth of September, you will see that it is in the later style. So is number four, dated the twenty-third of September; but numbers five and six, both at the beginning of October, are in the earlier style, like the signature of the will. Thereafter all the signatures are in the new style; but, if you compare number two, dated the sixteenth of September with number twenty-four, dated the fourteenth of March of this year﻿—the day of Jeffrey’s death﻿—you see that they exhibit no difference. Both are in the ‘later style,’ but the last shows no greater change than the first. Don’t you consider these facts very striking and significant?”

===Chinese===
“我看也是，”桑代克说。“两张都显不出后来发生的那个变化。但你看第二号，日期是九月十六日，你会发现它已是较晚的风格。第四号也是，日期是九月二十三日；可是第五号和第六号，都在十月初，却是较早的风格，同遗嘱上的签名一样。此后所有签名便都是新风格了；不过，你若把九月十六日的第二号，同今年三月十四日的第二十四号——杰弗里去世那一天——比较一下，便看出它们显不出任何差别。两张都是‘较晚的风格’，但末一张比起头一张来，变化并不更深一层。你不觉得这些事实非常醒目、非常意味深长吗？”

===Original===
I reflected a few moments, trying to make out the deep significance to which Thorndyke was directing my attention﻿—and not succeeding very triumphantly.

===Chinese===
我思索了片刻，想参透桑代克引我注意的那层深意——却参透得不太成功。

===Original===
“You mean,” I said, “that the occasional reversions to the earlier form convey some material suggestion?”

===Chinese===
“你的意思是，”我说，“偶尔回退到较早笔形的那些签名，传达出某种重要的暗示？”

===Original===
“Yes; but more than that. What we learn from an inspection of this series is this: that there was a change in the character of the signature; a very slight change, but quite recognizable. Now that change was not gradual or insidious nor was it progressive. It occurred at a certain definite time. At first there were one or two reversions to the earlier form, but after number six the new style continued to the end; and you notice that it continued without any increase in the change and without any variation. There are no intermediate forms. Some of the signatures are in the ‘old style’ and some in the ‘new,’ but there are none that are half and half. So that, to repeat: We have here two types of signature, very much alike, but distinguishable. They alternate, but do not merge into one another to produce intermediate forms. The change occurs abruptly, but shows no tendency to increase as time goes on; it is not a progressive change. What do you make of that, Jervis?”

===Chinese===
“是；但不止于此。检视这一系列使我们明白的是：签名的特征发生过一次变化；变化很轻微，却完全认得出来。而这变化既非渐次而来，也非潜移默化，更不是进行性的。它发生在某一确定的时刻。起初有一两次回退到较早的笔形，但第六号之后，新风格便一路持续到底；而且你注意到，它持续下来，变化既不加深，也不波动。没有中间形态。有些签名是‘旧风格’，有些是‘新风格’，却没有一张半新半旧。所以，再说一遍：我们这里有两种类型的签名，非常相像，却分辨得出。它们交替出现，却不互相交融而生出中间形态。这变化突然而至，却不随时间推移而有加剧之势；它不是一种进行性的变化。杰维斯，你由这事得出什么结论？”

===Original===
“It is very remarkable,” I said, poring over the cards to verify Thorndyke’s statements. “I don’t quite know what to make of it. If the circumstances admitted of the idea of forgery, one would suspect the genuineness of some of the signatures. But they don’t﻿—at any rate, in the case of the later will, to say nothing of Mr. Britton’s opinion on the signatures.”

===Chinese===
“真是非常奇特，”我说，一面伏在卡片上核对桑代克的话。“我说不上该作何解。倘若情势容得下伪造的念头，人们就会怀疑其中某些签名的真伪。但情势不容——至少就后一份遗嘱而言是如此，更不必说布里顿先生对这批签名的意见了。”

===Original===
“Still,” said Thorndyke, “there must be some explanation of the change in the character of the signatures, and that explanation cannot be the failing eyesight of the writer; for that is a gradually progressive and continuous condition, whereas the change in the writing is abrupt and intermittent.”

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，”桑代克说，“签名特征的这一变化总得有个解释，而这解释不可能是书写人视力的衰退；因为视力衰退是一种渐进而连续的状况，笔迹的变化却是突发的、间歇的。”

===Original===
I considered Thorndyke’s remark for a few moments; and then a light﻿—though not a very brilliant one﻿—seemed to break on me.

===Chinese===
我把桑代克这番话琢磨了片刻；随后一线光亮——虽说不太明亮——仿佛照上了我的心头。

===Original===
“I think I see what you are driving at,” said I. “You mean that the change in the writing must be associated with some new condition affecting the writer, and that that condition existed intermittently?”

===Chinese===
“我想我明白你的用意了，”我说。“你是说，笔迹的变化必定关联着影响书写人的某种新状况，而这状况是间歇性存在的？”

===Original===
Thorndyke nodded approvingly, and I continued:

===Chinese===
桑代克赞许地点头，我接着说：

===Original===
“The only intermittent condition that we know of is the effect of opium. So that we might consider the clearer signatures to have been made when Jeffrey was in his normal state, and the less distinct ones after a bout of opium-smoking.”

===Chinese===
“我们所知道的唯一间歇性状况，就是鸦片的作用。因此可以认为，较为清晰的那些签名，是杰弗里处于正常状态时写下的；较为模糊的那些，则是在过足一阵鸦片瘾之后写的。”

===Original===
“That is perfectly sound reasoning,” said Thorndyke. “What further conclusion does it lead to?”

===Chinese===
“这推理十分稳妥，”桑代克说。“由此还能引出什么进一步的结论？”

===Original===
“It suggests that the opium habit had been only recently acquired, since the change was noticed only about the time he went to live at New Inn; and, since the change in the writing is at first intermittent and then continuous, we may infer that the opium-smoking was at first occasional and later became a confirmed habit.”

===Chinese===
“它提示，鸦片嗜好是晚近才染上的，因为那变化差不多是在他搬去新客栈居住的时候才被注意到的；而且，既然笔迹的变化起初间歇、后来持续，我们可以推断：抽鸦片起初只是偶一为之，后来才积习成癖。”

===Original===
“Quite a reasonable conclusion and very clearly stated,” said Thorndyke. “I don’t say that I entirely agree with you, or that you have exhausted the information that these signatures offer. But you have started in the right direction.”

===Chinese===
“相当合理的结论，表述也很清楚，”桑代克说。“我并不说我完全同意你，也不说你已把这批签名所能提供的信息用尽了。但你起步的方向是对的。”

===Original===
“I may be on the right road,” I said gloomily; “but I am stuck fast in one place and I see no chance of getting any farther.”

===Chinese===
“我或许是在正路上，”我怏怏地说；“可是我在一处给卡得死死的，看不出再往前走的半点指望。”

===Original===
“But you have a quantity of data,” said Thorndyke. “You have all the facts that I had to start with, from which I constructed the hypothesis that I am now busily engaged in verifying. I have a few more data now, for ‘as money makes money’ so knowledge begets knowledge, and I put my original capital out to interest. Shall we tabulate the facts that are in our joint possession and see what they suggest?”

===Chinese===
“可你手里已有一大批材料，”桑代克说。“我起步时掌握的全部事实，如今都在你手里，我就是由它们构筑出眼下正忙着验证的那个假设的。如今我又多了几项数据，因为正如‘钱能生钱’，知识也孳生知识，我把原有的本钱放出去生息了。我们把共同掌握的事实开列成表，看看它们能提示些什么，怎么样？”

===Original===
I grasped eagerly at the offer, though I had conned over my notes again and again.

===Chinese===
我热切地抓住这一提议，尽管我的笔记早已一遍又一遍温熟了。

===Original===
Thorndyke produced a slip of paper from a drawer, and, uncapping his fountain-pen, proceeded to write down the leading facts, reading each aloud as soon as it was written.

===Chinese===
桑代克从抽屉里取出一张纸条，拧开自来水笔，动手写下主要事实，每写好一条，随即朗读出来。

===Original===
“1. The second will was unnecessary since it contained no new matter, expressed no new intentions and met no new conditions, and the first will was quite clear and efficient.

===Chinese===
“1. 第二份遗嘱并无必要，因为它不含任何新的内容，未表达任何新的意愿，也不应付任何新的情况，而第一份遗嘱十分明确，也完全有效。

===Original===
“2. The evident intention of the testator was to leave the bulk of his property to Stephen Blackmore.

===Chinese===
“2. 遗嘱人显而易见的意向，是把财产的绝大部分留给斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔。

===Original===
“3. The second will did not, under existing circumstances, give effect to this intention, whereas the first will did.

===Chinese===
“3. 在现有情形下，第二份遗嘱并未落实这一意向，第一份遗嘱反而落实了。

===Original===
“4. The signature of the second will differs slightly from that of the first, and also from what had hitherto been the testator’s ordinary signature.

===Chinese===
“4. 第二份遗嘱上的签名，同第一份的略有出入，也同遗嘱人此前一贯的寻常签名有出入。

===Original===
“And now we come to a very curious group of dates, which I will advise you to consider with great attention.

===Chinese===
“现在我们来到一组十分古怪的日期，我劝你极为用心地加以考量。

===Original===
“5. Mrs. Wilson made her will at the beginning of September last year, without acquainting Jeffrey Blackmore, who seems to have been unaware of the existence of this will.

===Chinese===
“5. 威尔逊太太去年九月初立下遗嘱，并未知会杰弗里·布莱克莫尔，后者似乎一直不晓得这份遗嘱的存在。

===Original===
“6. His own second will was dated the twelfth of November of last year.

===Chinese===
“6. 他自己的第二份遗嘱，日期是去年十一月十二日。

===Original===
“7. Mrs. Wilson died of cancer on the twelfth of March this present year.

===Chinese===
“7. 威尔逊太太于今年三月十二日死于癌症。

===Original===
“8. Jeffrey Blackmore was last seen alive on the fourteenth of March.

===Chinese===
“8. 杰弗里·布莱克莫尔最后一次被人见到还活着，是三月十四日。

===Original===
“9. His body was discovered on the fifteenth of March.

===Chinese===
“9. 他的尸体于三月十五日被发现。

===Original===
“10. The change in the character of his signature began about September last year and became permanent after the middle of October.

===Chinese===
“10. 他签名特征的变化始于去年九月前后，十月中旬以后便固定不变了。

===Original===
“You will find that collection of facts repay careful study, Jervis, especially when considered in relation to the further data:

===Chinese===
“杰维斯，你会发现这批事实值得细细玩味，尤其是同下面的补充数据对照起来看时：

===Original===
“11. That we found in Blackmore’s chambers a framed inscription of large size, hung upside down, together with what appeared to be the remains of a watch-glass and a box of stearine candles and some other objects.”

===Chinese===
“11. 我们在布莱克莫尔的寓所里发现一幅装框的大幅铭文，倒挂着；同它一起的，还有看似表玻璃残片的东西、一盒硬脂蜡烛，以及另外一些物件。”

===Original===
He passed the paper to me and I pored over it intently, focusing my attention on the various items with all the power of my will. But, struggle as I would, no general conclusion could be made to emerge from the mass of apparently disconnected facts.

===Chinese===
他把纸条递给我，我凝神细读，以全副意志力把注意力集注在各个条目上。然而任凭我怎样挣扎，也无法使一个总括性的结论，从这堆显然互不关联的事实里浮现出来。

===Original===
“Well?” Thorndyke said presently, after watching with grave interest my unavailing efforts; “what do you make of it?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”桑代克说。他一直神情凝重而饶有兴味地看着我那阵徒劳，这时才开腔：“你作何解？”

===Original===
“Nothing!” I exclaimed desperately, slapping the paper down on the table. “Of course, I can see that there are some queer coincidences. But how do they bear on the case? I understand that you want to upset this will; which we know to have been signed without compulsion or even suggestion in the presence of two respectable men, who have sworn to the identity of the document. That is your object, I believe?”

===Chinese===
“什么也作不出！”我绝望地嚷道，一掌把纸条拍在桌上。“当然，我看得出来有那么几个古怪的巧合。可它们同这案子有什么相干？据我理解，你要推翻这份遗嘱；而我们明知，它是在两位体面人面前、不受强迫甚至没有暗示的情况下签下的，那两位且已宣誓证明这份文书确凿同一。这就是你的目的，我想没弄错吧？”

===Original===
“Certainly it is.”

===Chinese===
“一点不错。”

===Original===
“Then I am hanged if I see how you are going to do it. Not, I should say, by offering a group of vague coincidences that would muddle any brain but your own.”

===Chinese===
“那么，我真是见鬼了，看不出你打算怎么做到。我料想，总不能靠端出一组含含糊糊的巧合——那种东西除了你这副脑袋，搁谁的脑子都得搅成一团糨糊。”

===Original===
Thorndyke chuckled softly but pursued the subject no farther.

===Chinese===
桑代克低声一笑，却没有把话头再往前引。

===Original===
“Put that paper in your file with your other notes,” he said, “and think it over at your leisure. And now I want a little help from you. Have you a good memory for faces?”

===Chinese===
“把这张纸归入你的卷宗，同你其余的笔记放在一处，”他说，“得空时细细想想。现在我要你帮我一点小忙。你对人脸的记忆力好不好？”

===Original===
“Fairly good, I think. Why?”

===Chinese===
“还算不坏，我想。问这个做什么？”

===Original===
“Because I have a photograph of a man whom I think you may have met. Just look at it and tell me if you remember the face.”

===Chinese===
“因为我这里有一张男人的照片，我想你也许见过他。看一看，然后告诉我，你还记不记得这张脸。”

===Original===
He drew a cabinet size photograph from an envelope that had come by the morning’s post and handed it to me.

===Chinese===
他从早晨邮班送来的一只信封里抽出一张六寸幅面的照片，递到我手里。

===Original===
“I have certainly seen this face somewhere,” said I, taking the portrait over to the window to examine it more thoroughly, “but I can’t, at the moment, remember where.”

===Chinese===
“这张脸我准在什么地方见过，”我说，一面把照片拿到窗前，想看得更透彻些，“只是眼下想不起是在哪儿了。”

===Original===
“Try,” said Thorndyke. “If you have seen the face before, you should be able to recall the person.”

===Chinese===
“试试看，”桑代克说。“你要是从前见过这张脸，就该能把那个人回忆起来。”

===Original===
I looked intently at the photograph, and the more I looked, the more familiar did the face appear. Suddenly the identity of the man flashed into my mind and I exclaimed in astonishment:

===Chinese===
我凝神细看那张照片，越看，这张脸越显得眼熟。忽然，此人的身份电光般闪入我的脑海，我失声惊呼：

===Original===
“It can’t be that poor creature at Kennington, Mr. Graves?”

===Chinese===
“这不会是肯宁顿那个可怜的人吧——格雷夫斯先生？”

===Original===
“I think it can,” replied Thorndyke, “and I think it is. But could you swear to the identity in a court of law?”

===Chinese===
“我想会是，”桑代克答道，“而且我想正是。可你能在法庭上宣誓指认这一身份吗？”

===Original===
“It is my firm conviction that the photograph is that of Mr. Graves. I would swear to that.”

===Chinese===
“我坚信这张照片就是格雷夫斯先生的。这一点我愿意宣誓。”

===Original===
“No man ought to swear to more,” said Thorndyke. “Identification is always a matter of opinion or belief. The man who will swear unconditionally to identity from memory only is a man whose evidence should be discredited. I think your sworn testimony would be sufficient.”

===Chinese===
“谁也不该宣誓担保比这更多的东西，”桑代克说。“辨认身份，永远属于意见或信念的范畴。单凭记忆就肯无条件宣誓认定同一身份的人，他的证词便不该被采信。我想，你的宣誓证词尽够了。”

===Original===
It is needless to say that the production of this photograph filled me with amazement and curiosity as to how Thorndyke had obtained it. But, as he replaced it impassively in its envelope without volunteering any explanation, I felt that I could not question him directly. Nevertheless, I ventured to approach the subject in an indirect manner.

===Chinese===
不必说，这张照片一亮相，我便满心惊诧，好奇桑代克是从哪里弄到它的。但他不动声色地把照片放回信封，丝毫不作解释，我便觉得不便直接动问。尽管如此，我还是绕着弯儿去探这个题目。

===Original===
“Did you get any information from those Darmstadt people?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你从达姆施塔特那些人那里得到什么消息没有？”我问。

===Original===
“Schnitzler? Yes. I learned, through the medium of an official acquaintance, that Dr. H. Weiss was a stranger to them; that they knew nothing about him excepting that he had ordered from them, and been supplied with, a hundred grams of pure hydrochlorate of morphine.”

===Chinese===
“施尼茨勒那儿？得到了。我经由一位官方相识居间打听到：H·韦斯医生于他们是全然陌生的；除了知道他曾向他们订购、并已由他们供给他一百克纯净盐酸吗啡之外，对他一无所知。”

===Original===
“All at once?”

===Chinese===
“一次全订的？”

===Original===
“No. In separate parcels of twenty-five grams each.”

===Chinese===
“不是。分作数包，每包二十五克。”

===Original===
“Is that all you know about Weiss?”

===Chinese===
“关于韦斯，你所知道的就这些？”

===Original===
“It is all that I actually know; but it is not all that I suspect﻿—on very substantial grounds. By the way, what did you think of the coachman?”

===Chinese===
“我确然知道的就这些；但我疑心的却不止于此——而且有相当扎实的根据。对了，你对那个车夫有什么想法？”

===Original===
“I don’t know that I thought very much about him. Why?”

===Chinese===
“说不上对他想过很多。怎么？”

===Original===
“You never suspected that he and Weiss were one and the same person?”

===Chinese===
“你从没起过疑心，疑心他和韦斯是同一个人？”

===Original===
“No. How could they be? They weren’t in the least alike. And one was a Scotchman and the other a German. But perhaps you know that they were the same?”

===Chinese===
“没有。他们怎么可能是一个人？两人毫无相似之处。况且一个是苏格兰人，一个是德国人。不过，兴许你知道他们就是同一个？”

===Original===
“I only know what you have told me. But considering that you never saw them together, that the coachman was never available for messages or assistance when Weiss was with you; that Weiss always made his appearance some time after you arrived, and disappeared some time before you left; it has seemed to me that they might have been the same person.”

===Chinese===
“我只知道你告诉我的那些。但你想想：你从未同时见过他们两人；每逢韦斯同你在一起，那车夫便总是唤不来、也帮不上；韦斯总在你到达之后好一阵才露面，又在你离开之前好一阵就隐没。在我看来，他们本可能是同一个人。”

===Original===
“I should say it was impossible. They were so very different in appearance. But supposing that they were the same; would the fact be of any importance?”

===Chinese===
“我看不可能。两人的相貌差别太大了。不过，姑且假定他们是同一个；这一事实又有什么要紧？”

===Original===
“It would mean that we could save ourselves the trouble of looking for the coachman. And it would suggest some inferences, which will occur to you if you think the matter over. But being only a speculative opinion, at present, it would not be safe to infer very much from it.”

===Chinese===
“那意味着，我们可以省下寻找那车夫的麻烦。而且它会提示一些推论，你把这层细细想过，推论自会浮现。但这眼下只是个臆测性的看法，从它推得太多并不稳妥。”

===Original===
“You have rather taken me by surprise,” I remarked. “It seems that you have been working at this Kennington case, and working pretty actively I imagine, whereas I supposed that your entire attention was taken up by the Blackmore affair.”

===Chinese===
“你可真让我吃了一惊，”我说。“看来你一直在办这桩肯宁顿案，而且我料想办得相当起劲；我还以为你的全部心思都叫布莱克莫尔那桩事占去了呢。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t do,” he replied, “to allow one’s entire attention to be taken up by any one case. I have half a dozen others﻿—minor cases, mostly﻿—to which I am attending at this moment. Did you think I was proposing to keep you under lock and key indefinitely?”

===Chinese===
“任凭一桩案子占去全部注意力，”他答道，“是要不得的。我手上还有半打别的案子——多半是小案——此刻都在照应。你总不至于以为，我打算把你无限期地锁在这儿吧？”

===Original===
“Well, no. But I thought the Kennington case would have to wait its turn. And I had no idea that you were in possession of enough facts to enable you to get any farther with it.”

===Chinese===
“呃，倒没有。只是我以为，肯宁顿案总得慢慢轮到它。而且我全然没有想到，你手里掌握的事实，已经足够叫它再往前走了。”

===Original===
