“But you knew all the very striking facts of the case, and you saw the further evidence that we extracted from the empty house.”

===Chinese===
“可是这案子里所有非常触目的事实，你都已经知道；我们从那座空宅里搜出的进一步证据，你也亲眼见了。”

===Original===
“Do you mean those things that we picked out from the rubbish under the grate?”

===Chinese===
“你是说我们从炉格底下那堆垃圾里捡出来的那些东西？”

===Original===
“Yes. You saw those curious little pieces of reed and the pair of spectacles. They are lying in the top drawer of that cabinet at this moment, and I should recommend you to have another look at them. To me they are most instructive. The pieces of reed offered an extremely valuable suggestion, and the spectacles enabled me to test that suggestion and turn it into actual information.”

===Chinese===
“正是。你见过那几截古怪的小芦秆，还有那副眼镜。它们此刻就躺在那边柜子的顶抽屉里，我劝你再看上一眼。在我看来，它们极有教益。那几截芦秆提供了一个极其宝贵的提示，那副眼镜则使我能检验这一提示，把它变成确凿的信息。”

===Original===
“Unfortunately,” said I, “the pieces of reed convey nothing to me. I don’t know what they are or of what they have formed a part.”

===Chinese===
“可惜的是，”我说，“那几截芦秆对我说不出什么。我不知道它们是什么，也不知道它们原是何物的一部分。”

===Original===
“I think,” he replied, “that if you examine them with due consideration, you will find their use pretty obvious. Have a good look at them and the spectacles too. Think over all that you know of that mysterious group of people who lived in that house, and see if you cannot form some coherent theory of their actions. Think, also, if we have not some information in our possession by which we might be able to identify some of them, and infer the identity of the others. You will have a quiet day, as I shall not be home until the evening; set yourself this task. I assure you that you have the material for identifying﻿—or rather for testing the identity of﻿—at least one of those persons. Go over your material systematically, and let me know in the evening what further investigations you would propose.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”他答道，“你只要以应有的审慎检视它们，就会发现它们的用途相当明显。把它们连同那副眼镜都好好看一看。把你所知道的、住在那座宅子里那伙神秘人物的一切都想一遍，看你能否为他们的行为构成某种连贯的理论。再想一想，我们手里是否已有一些信息，凭它也许能辨认出其中某些人，并推断出其余人的身份。今天你会清静一天，因为我要到晚上才回家；就给自己立下这桩功课吧。我向你保证，你手里已有辨认——不如说检验——那伙人中至少一人身份的材料。把你的材料系统地过上一遍，晚上告诉我，你提议再作哪些调查。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said I. “It shall be done according to your word. I will addle my brain afresh with the affair of Mr. Weiss and his patient, and let the Blackmore case rip.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”我说。“谨遵台命。我就再用韦斯先生和他病人那桩事，把脑子搅上一搅，布莱克莫尔案且由它去吧。”

===Original===
“There is no need to do that. You have a whole day before you. An hour’s really close consideration of the Kennington case ought to show you what your next move should be, and then you could devote yourself to the consideration of Jeffrey Blackmore’s will.”

===Chinese===
“倒也不必如此。你面前还有整整一天。对肯宁顿案真下一番贴切的推敲，个把钟头便该让你看出下一步该怎么走，然后你尽可以专心去考虑杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的遗嘱。”

===Original===
With this final piece of advice, Thorndyke collected the papers for his day’s work, and, having deposited them in his brief bag, took his departure, leaving me to my meditations.

===Chinese===
丢下这最后一句忠告，桑代克收拾起他当日工作需用的文件，装进提包，动身出门去了，留下我独自沉思。

## XIII The Statement of Samuel Wilkins / 塞缪尔·威尔金斯的证词

===Original===
As soon as I was alone, I commenced my investigations with a rather desperate hope of eliciting some startling and unsuspected facts. I opened the drawer and taking from it the two pieces of reed and the shattered remains of the spectacles, laid them on the table. The repairs that Thorndyke had contemplated in the case of the spectacles, had not been made. Apparently they had not been necessary. The battered wreck that lay before me, just as we had found it, had evidently furnished the necessary information; for, since Thorndyke was in possession of a portrait of Mr. Graves, it was clear that he had succeeded in identifying him so far as to get into communication with someone who had known him intimately.

===Chinese===
一等到独处，我便着手查究，怀着一种近乎孤注一掷的希望，指望引出几桩惊人的、出人意料的事实。我拉开抽屉，取出那两截芦苇秆和眼镜的残骸，把它们摆在桌上。桑代克原打算把那副眼镜修理一番，这修理并没有做。看来也并无必要。摆在我面前的这副砸烂的残骸——正如我们发现它时的原样——显然已经提供了必要的信息；因为，桑代克手里既然有了一幅格雷夫斯先生的肖像，可见他已把此人的身份查到了这一步：同某个与此人熟识的人接上了联系。

===Original===
The circumstance should have been encouraging. But somehow it was not. What was possible to Thorndyke was, theoretically, possible to me﻿—or to anyone else. But the possibility did not realize itself in practice. There was the personal equation. Thorndyke’s brain was not an ordinary brain. Facts of which his mind instantly perceived the relation remained to other people unconnected and without meaning. His powers of observation and rapid inference were almost incredible, as I had noticed again and again, and always with undiminished wonder. He seemed to take in everything at a single glance and in an instant to appreciate the meaning of everything that he had seen.

===Chinese===
这一情况本该令人鼓舞。可不知怎么，并不然。桑代克办得到的事，照道理说，我也办得到——或者随便谁都办得到。但这一可能性在实践中并不自行兑现。这里有个“人差”（personal equation）的问题。桑代克的头脑不是寻常的头脑。在他心里一瞥便见出关系来的那些事实，落在旁人眼里仍是互不相连、毫无意义。他观察与速断的本领几乎令人难以置信，这一点我一次又一次地注意到，而且每次都不减惊叹。他仿佛一瞥之间便把一切尽收眼底，而且转瞬便领会了他所见的一切的含义。

===Original===
Here was a case in point. I had myself seen all that he had seen, and, indeed, much more; for I had looked on the very people and witnessed their actions, whereas he had never set eyes on any of them. I had examined the little handful of rubbish that he had gathered up so carefully, and would have flung it back under the grate without a qualm. Not a glimmer of light had I perceived in the cloud of mystery, nor even a hint of the direction in which to seek enlightenment. And yet Thorndyke had, in some incomprehensible manner, contrived to piece together facts that I had probably not even observed, and that so completely that he had already, in these few days, narrowed down the field of inquiry to quite a small area.

===Chinese===
眼前正是一例。他所见过的，我全都亲自见过，而且实在说，见得比他多得多；因为我亲眼看过那些人，目睹了他们的行动，他却连一个也没见过。他那么细心拾起来的一小撮破烂，我也检视过了，若是依着我，会毫无芥蒂地把它们扔回炉栅底下去。在这一团疑云里，我连一线光亮也没有看出来，甚至连该往哪个方向寻求启示的一点暗示也没有。然而桑代克却以某种不可理解的方式，把我多半根本不曾留意的事实拼合了起来，而且拼合得如此完整，以至于在这短短几天里，他已把查究的范围缩小到很小的一块了。

===Original===
From these reflections I returned to the objects on the table. The spectacles, as things of which I had some expert knowledge, were not so profound a mystery to me. A pair of spectacles might easily afford good evidence for identification; that I perceived clearly enough. Not a ready-made pair, picked up casually at a shop, but a pair constructed by a skilled optician to remedy a particular defect of vision and to fit a particular face. And such were the spectacles before me. The build of the frames was peculiar; the existence of a cylindrical lens﻿—which I could easily make out from the remaining fragments﻿—showed that one glass had been cut to a prescribed shape and almost certainly ground to a particular formula, and also that the distance between centres must have been carefully secured. Hence these spectacles had an individual character. But it was manifestly impossible to inquire of all the spectacle-makers in Europe﻿—for the glasses were not necessarily made in England. As confirmation the spectacles might be valuable; as a starting-point they were of no use at all.

===Chinese===
从这些沉思里，我回到桌上的物件上来。眼镜，作为我略具专门知识的东西，对我来说并不是那么高深的谜。一副眼镜很容易为鉴定提供良好的证据；这一点我看得够清楚。不是那种从铺子里随手买来的现成货，而是由熟练的验光配镜师为矫正某种特定的视力缺陷、为贴合某一张特定的脸而制的一副。摆在我面前的正是这样一副。镜架的构造很特别；有一片柱面镜——这从残存的碎片上很容易辨认出来——说明其中一片镜片曾按规定的形状切割过，而且几乎可以肯定按某个特定的配方研磨过，还说明两镜片的中心距必定经过仔细的校定。因此这副眼镜具有一种个人的特性。但是，要遍访欧洲所有的眼镜制造商去查询，显然是办不到的——因为这些镜片未必是在英国配制的。作为佐证，这副眼镜也许很有价值；作为出发点，它们却毫无用处。

===Original===
From the spectacles I turned to the pieces of reed. These were what had given Thorndyke his start. Would they give me a leading hint too? I looked at them and wondered what it was that they had told Thorndyke. The little fragment of the red paper label had a dark-brown or thin black border ornamented with a fret-pattern, and on it I detected a couple of tiny points of gold like the dust from leaf-gilding. But I learned nothing from that. Then the shorter piece of reed was artificially hollowed to fit on the longer piece. Apparently it formed a protective sheath or cap. But what did it protect? Presumably a point or edge of some kind. Could this be a pocketknife of any sort, such as a small stencil-knife? No; the material was too fragile for a knife-handle. It could not be an etching-needle for the same reason; and it was not a surgical appliance﻿—at least it was not like any surgical instrument that was known to me.

===Chinese===
从眼镜我转向那两截芦苇秆。正是它们给了桑代克入手的线索。它们也会给我一个关键的提示吗？我望着它们，纳闷它们究竟告诉了桑代克什么。那一小片红色纸标签的残角，边缘有一道深褐或近乎黑色的窄边，饰以回纹图案；我还从上面辨出两三个金色的细点，像是贴金箔时蹭下的金屑。但从中我一无所获。再看那截较短的芦苇秆，经过人为的掏挖，正好能套在较长的那截上。显然它是个护套或护帽。可它护的是什么呢？想来是某种尖头或刃口。会不会是哪种随身小刀，比如一种小小的镂花刻刀（stencil-knife）？不；这材料做刀柄太脆了。出于同样的道理，它也不会是蚀刻针（etching-needle）；它更不是外科器械——至少不像我所知道的任何外科器械。

===Original===
I turned it over and over and cudgelled my brains; and then I had a brilliant idea. Was it a reed pen of which the point had been broken off? I knew that reed pens were still in use by draughtsmen of decorative leanings with an affection for the “fat line.” Could any of our friends be draughtsmen? This seemed the most probable solution of the difficulty, and the more I thought about it the more likely it seemed. Draughtsmen usually sign their work intelligibly, and even when they use a device instead of a signature their identity is easily traceable. Could it be that Mr. Graves, for instance, was an illustrator, and that Thorndyke had established his identity by looking through the works of all the well-known thick-line draughtsmen?

===Chinese===
我把它翻来覆去，绞尽脑汁；忽然灵光一现。它会不会是一支折断了笔尖的芦苇笔（reed pen）？我知道，有些好作装饰、偏爱“粗肥线条”（fat line）的画师至今仍用芦苇笔。我们那几位“朋友”里头，会不会有谁是画师？这似乎是这个难题最可能的解答，而且我越想越觉得像。画师们作画通常署的是一看便知的名字，即便用画记代替签名，其身份也容易追索。会不会是，比方说，格雷夫斯先生本是一位插图画家，而桑代克正是把知名的粗线条画家的作品挨个翻检过，才坐实了他的身份？

===Original===
This problem occupied me for the rest of the day. My explanation did not seem quite to fit Thorndyke’s description of his methods; but I could think of no other. I turned it over during my solitary lunch; I meditated on it with the aid of several pipes in the afternoon; and having refreshed my brain with a cup of tea, I went forth to walk in the Temple gardens﻿—which I was permitted to do without breaking my parole﻿—to think it out afresh.

===Chinese===
这个问题占据了我这一天余下的全部时间。我的解释似乎同桑代克对他方法的描述不太吻合；但我想不出别的来。独自午餐时我把它掂量来掂量去；下午又借几斗烟对它反复参详；再用一杯茶提了提神，我便出门到坦普尔的花园里去散步——这是获准的，不算破坏我的假释——从头再把它想一遍。

===Original===
The result was disappointing. I was basing my reasoning on the assumption that the pieces of reed were parts of a particular appliance, appertaining to a particular craft; whereas they might be the remains of something quite different, appertaining to a totally different craft or to no craft at all. And in no case did they point to any known individual or indicate any but the vaguest kind of search. After pacing the pleasant walks for upwards of two hours, I at length turned back towards our chambers, where I arrived as the lamplighter was just finishing his round.

===Chinese===
结果是失望。我的推理建立在这样一个假定上：这两截芦苇秆是某件特定器具的部件，属于某一门特定的行当；而它们也可能是全然另一种东西的残余，属于完全另一门行当，或者根本不属于任何行当。而且无论如何，它们都指不到任何已知的个人身上，至多提示一种最漫无边际的搜索。在那些宜人的步道上来回踱了两个多钟头之后，我终于转身回我们的寓所去，到家时点灯人刚好巡完他这一轮。

===Original===
My fruitless speculations had left me somewhat irritable. The lighted windows that I had noticed as I approached had given me the impression that Thorndyke had returned. I had intended to press him for a little further information. When, therefore, I let myself into our chambers and found, instead of my colleague, a total stranger﻿—and only a back view at that﻿—I was disappointed and annoyed.

===Chinese===
我这番劳而无功的冥想弄得我有些烦躁。走近时看见那几扇亮着的窗子，我以为是桑代克回来了。我本想逼他再透露一点消息。因此，当我开门进屋，发现等在那里的不是我的同事，而是一个彻头彻尾的陌生人——而且还只看得见个背影——我便又失望又气恼。

===Original===
The stranger was seated by the table, reading a large document that looked like a lease. He made no movement when I entered, but when I crossed the room and wished him “Good evening,” he half rose and bowed silently. It was then that I first saw his face, and a mighty start he gave me. For one moment I actually thought he was Mr. Weiss, so close was the resemblance, but immediately I perceived that he was a much smaller man.

===Chinese===
那陌生人坐在桌旁，读一份像租约的大文件。我进来时他毫无动静，可等我穿过房间向他道“晚上好”，他便半抬起身，默默一躬。就在那时我头一回看清他的脸，好家伙，着实吓了我一大跳。有一瞬间我竟当他是韦斯先生，像得实在逼真；但我随即看出，他的个头要小得多。

===Original===
I sat down nearly opposite and stole an occasional furtive glance at him. The resemblance to Weiss was really remarkable. The same flaxen hair, the same ragged beard and a similar red nose, with the patches of acne rosacea spreading to the adjacent cheeks. He wore spectacles, too, through which he took a quick glance at me now and again, returning immediately to his document.

===Chinese===
我在近乎对面坐下，不时偷眼觑他。他同韦斯的相像真可谓惊人。同样的淡黄头发，同样的蓬乱胡子，同样一只红鼻子，玫瑰痤疮（acne rosacea）的红斑同样蔓延到近旁的双颊。他也戴着一副眼镜，不时透过镜片飞快地瞥我一眼，随即又回到他的文件上。

===Original===
After some moments of rather embarrassing silence, I ventured to remark that it was a mild evening; to which he assented with a sort of Scotch “Hm﻿—hm” and nodded slowly. Then came another interval of silence, during which I speculated on the possibility of his being a relative of Mr. Weiss and wondered what the deuce he was doing in our chambers.

===Chinese===
在相当难堪的沉默里过了片刻，我冒昧说了句今晚天气温和；他应了一声苏格兰式的“唔——唔”，慢慢点头。接着又是一阵沉默，其间我揣测他会不会是韦斯先生的亲戚，又纳闷他究竟跑到我们寓所里来搞什么名堂。

===Original===
“Have you an appointment with Dr. Thorndyke?” I asked, at length.

===Chinese===
“您同桑代克博士有约吗？”我终于问道。

===Original===
He bowed solemnly, and by way of reply﻿—in the affirmative, as I assumed﻿—emitted another “hm﻿—hm.”

===Chinese===
他郑重地一躬，算是答复——我且当它是肯定——又发出一声“唔——唔”。

===Original===
I looked at him sharply, a little nettled by his lack of manners; whereupon he opened out the lease so that it screened his face, and as I glanced at the back of the document, I was astonished to observe that it was shaking rapidly.

===Chinese===
我狠狠地瞪着他，为他这副没礼貌的样子微微着恼；于是他把那份租约摊开，遮住了脸；我朝文件的背面瞥了一眼，惊愕地发现它正飞快地抖动着。

===Original===
The fellow was actually laughing! What he found in my simple question to cause him so much amusement I was totally unable to imagine. But there it was. The tremulous movements of the document left me in no possible doubt that he was for some reason convulsed with laughter.

===Chinese===
这家伙竟然在笑！我那句平平常常的问话里到底有什么东西让他乐成这样，我全然无法想象。可事实摆在眼前。那份文件抖个不停，使我毫不怀疑：他不知为了什么正笑得浑身直颤。

===Original===
It was extremely mysterious. Also, it was rather embarrassing. I took out my pocket file and began to look over my notes. Then the document was lowered and I was able to get another look at the stranger’s face. He was really extraordinarily like Weiss. The shaggy eyebrows, throwing the eye-sockets into shadow, gave him, in conjunction with the spectacles, the same owlish, solemn expression that I had noticed in my Kennington acquaintance; and which, by the way, was singularly out of character with the frivolous behaviour that I had just witnessed.

===Chinese===
这真是极其神秘。同时也相当叫人难堪。我取出衣袋里的活页夹，开始翻看我的笔记。这时那份文件放了下来，我又得以看一眼那陌生人的脸。他实在像韦斯像得出奇。那两道蓬乱的浓眉把眼窝罩进阴影里，加上一副眼镜，便给了他一副我在肯宁顿那位相识脸上见过的同样的猫头鹰式的庄重神情——顺便说一句，这神情同我方才目睹的那种轻浮举止，可谓格格不入到了极点。

===Original===
From time to time as I looked at him, he caught my eye and instantly averted his own, turning rather red. Apparently he was a shy, nervous man, which might account for his giggling; for I have noticed that shy or nervous people have a habit of smiling inopportunely and even giggling when embarrassed by meeting an over-steady eye. And it seemed my own eye had this disconcerting quality, for even as I looked at him, the document suddenly went up again and began to shake violently.

===Chinese===
我不时朝他看，一撞上我的目光，他便立即把自己的眼光移开，脸涨得相当红。看来他是个害羞的、怯生生的男人，这倒可以解释他为何吃吃地笑；因为我注意到，害羞或胆怯的人有个习惯：被一双过于定住的眼睛盯得窘了，便会不合时宜地微笑，甚至咯咯地笑。而看来我自己的目光正有这种乱人心神的本事，因为就在我望着他的时候，那份文件突然又竖了起来，剧烈地抖动起来。

===Original===
I stood it for a minute or two, but, finding the situation intolerably embarrassing, I rose, and brusquely excusing myself, went up to the laboratory to look for Polton and inquire at what time Thorndyke was expected home. To my surprise, however, on entering, I discovered Thorndyke himself just finishing the mounting of a microscopical specimen.

===Chinese===
我忍了一两分钟，但觉得这局面尴尬得叫人受不了，便站起身来，生硬地告了个便，上楼到实验室去找波尔顿，问问桑代克预计几时回来。不料一进门，却发现桑代克本人正在给一件显微镜标本封片，眼看就要完工。

===Original===
“Did you know that there is someone below waiting to see you?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“您知道楼下有个人等着见您吗？”我问。

===Original===
“Is it anyone you know?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“是你认识的人吗？”他问。

===Original===
“No,” I answered. “It is a red-nosed, sniggering fool in spectacles. He has got a lease or a deed or some other sort of document which he has been using to play a sort of idiotic game of Peep-Bo! I couldn’t stand him, so I came up here.”

===Chinese===
“不认识，”我答道，“那是个红鼻子的、戴着眼镜、动辄窃笑的蠢货。他手里拿着一份租约还是契据还是别的什么文书，一直在拿它玩一种白痴式的躲猫猫（Peep-Bo）！我实在受不了他，就上来了。”

===Original===
Thorndyke laughed heartily at my description of his client.

===Chinese===
桑代克听我这样形容他的委托人，放怀大笑。

===Original===
“What are you laughing at?” I asked sourly; at which he laughed yet more heartily and added to the aggravation by wiping his eyes.

===Chinese===
“您笑什么？”我酸溜溜地问；他闻言笑得更欢，还抹起眼睛来，愈发火上浇油。

===Original===
“Our friend seems to have put you out,” he remarked.

===Chinese===
“咱们这位朋友似乎把你惹得不轻。”他说。

===Original===
“He put me out literally. If I had stayed much longer I should have punched his head.”

===Chinese===
“岂止是惹——他是货真价实地把我撵出来了。再待久一点，我就要敲他的脑袋了。”

===Original===
“In that case,” said Thorndyke, “I am glad you didn’t stay. But come down and let me introduce you.”

===Chinese===
“那样的话，”桑代克说，“你没待下去我倒高兴。不过下来吧，让我来给你们引见。”

===Original===
“No, thank you. I’ve had enough of him for the present.”

===Chinese===
“不了，谢谢。眼下我受够他了。”

===Original===
“But I have a very special reason for wishing to introduce you. I think you will get some information from him that will interest you very much; and you needn’t quarrel with a man for being of a cheerful disposition.”

===Chinese===
“可我有一层很特别的理由，想引见你们相识。我想你会从他那里听到一些使你极感兴趣的情况；再说，一个人天性开朗，你也犯不着同他过不去。”

===Original===
“Cheerful be hanged!” I exclaimed. “I don’t call a man cheerful because he behaves like a gibbering idiot.”

===Chinese===
“乐观见鬼去吧！”我嚷道，“一个人行事像个傻笑个不停的白痴，我可不称之为开朗。”

===Original===
To this Thorndyke made no reply but a broad and appreciative smile, and we descended to the lower floor. As we entered the room, the stranger rose, and, glancing in an embarrassed way from one of us to the other, suddenly broke out into an undeniable snigger. I looked at him sternly, and Thorndyke, quite unmoved by his indecorous behaviour, said in a grave voice:

===Chinese===
对这话，桑代克只报以一个开怀而会心的微笑，于是我们下楼来到下面一层。我们进屋时，那陌生人站起身来，窘迫地在我们两人之间扫来扫去，忽然迸出一声赖不掉的窃笑。我严厉地瞪着他，桑代克对他的失仪毫不动容，用庄重的声音说道：

===Original===
“Let me introduce you, Jervis; though I think you have met this gentleman before.”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯，让我来给你们引见；不过我想，这位先生你是从前见过的。”

===Original===
“I think not,” I said stiffly.

===Chinese===
“我看没有。”我生硬地说。

===Original===
“Oh yes, you have, sir,” interposed the stranger; and, as he spoke, I started; for the voice was uncommonly like the familiar voice of Polton.

===Chinese===
“哦，见过的，先生，”那陌生人插话道；他一开口，我不由得一怔；因为这声音同波尔顿那熟悉的嗓音像得出奇。

===Original===
I looked at the speaker with sudden suspicion. And now I could see that the flaxen hair was a wig; that the beard had a decidedly artificial look, and that the eyes that beamed through the spectacles were remarkably like the eyes of our factotum. But the blotchy face, the bulbous nose and the shaggy, overhanging eyebrows were alien features that I could not reconcile with the personality of our refined and aristocratic-looking little assistant.

===Chinese===
我怀着陡然而起的疑心打量这个说话的人。这一来我看出来了：那淡黄的头发是一顶假发；那部胡子一副人造的样子再明白不过；还有那双从镜片后面湛然有光的眼睛，同我们那位万能听差的眼睛像极了。可是那张斑块斑驳的脸、那个球茎般的大鼻子、那两道垂覆的浓眉，却都是格格不入的特征，我怎么也无法把它们同我们这位斯文而相貌贵气的小助手的人格调和起来。

===Original===
“Is this a practical joke?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这是在拿我开玩笑吗？”我问。

===Original===
“No,” replied Thorndyke; “it is a demonstration. When we were talking this morning it appeared to me that you did not realize the extent to which it is possible to conceal identity under suitable conditions of light. So I arranged, with Polton’s rather reluctant assistance, to give you ocular evidence. The conditions are not favourable﻿—which makes the demonstration more convincing. This is a very well-lighted room and Polton is a very poor actor; in spite of which it has been possible for you to sit opposite him for several minutes and look at him, I have no doubt, very attentively, without discovering his identity. If the room had been lighted only with a candle, and Polton had been equal to the task of supporting his makeup with an appropriate voice and manner, the deception would have been perfect.”

===Chinese===
“不是开玩笑，”桑代克答道，“是一次演示。今早我们谈话的时候，我觉得你没有意识到：在合适的光线条件下，身份可以被掩蔽到什么地步。于是我作了安排——多亏波尔顿相当勉强的协助——给你一个亲眼可见的证据。现在的条件并不有利——这反倒使这次演示更有说服力。这是一间光线很亮的房间，波尔顿又是个很蹩脚的演员；尽管如此，你仍旧能够在他对面坐上好几分钟，而且我毫不怀疑，看得相当仔细，却没有识破他的身份。倘若这间屋子只用一支蜡烛照明，波尔顿又有本事用相称的嗓音和举止撑住他的化装，这骗局便可算天衣无缝了。”

===Original===
“I can see that he has a wig on, quite plainly,” said I.

===Chinese===
“他戴着假发，这我看得明明白白。”我说。

===Original===
“Yes; but you would not in a dimly lighted room. On the other hand, if Polton were to walk down Fleet Street at midday in this condition, the makeup would be conspicuously evident to any moderately observant passerby. The secret of making up consists in a careful adjustment to the conditions of light and distance in which the makeup is to be seen. That in use on the stage would look ridiculous in an ordinary room; that which would serve in an artificially lighted room would look ridiculous out of doors by daylight.”

===Chinese===
“看得明白；可要是在一间光线昏暗的屋子里，你就看不出来了。反过来，波尔顿若是这副模样在正午沿着舰队街走一遭，任何一个稍微留意的过路人都会一眼看穿那化装。化装的秘诀，在于仔细调校，使之适应化装将要呈现于其中的光线与距离条件。舞台上用的那套，摆在普通房间里会显得荒唐；适合人工照明房间用的那套，在户外日光下也会显得荒唐。”

===Original===
“Is any effective makeup possible out of doors in ordinary daylight?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“在户外寻常的日光底下，可能化出奏效的装来吗？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” replied Thorndyke. “But it must be on a totally different scale from that of the stage. A wig, and especially a beard or moustache, must be joined up at the edges with hair actually stuck on the skin with transparent cement and carefully trimmed with scissors. The same applies to eyebrows; and alterations in the colour of the skin must be carried out much more subtly. Polton’s nose has been built up with a small covering of toupee-paste, the pimples on the cheeks produced with little particles of the same material; and the general tinting has been done with greasepaint with a very light scumble of powder colour to take off some of the shine. This would be possible in outdoor makeup, but it would have to be done with the greatest care and delicacy; in fact, with what the art-critics call ‘reticence.’ A very little makeup is sufficient and too much is fatal. You would be surprised to see how little paste is required to alter the shape of the nose and the entire character of the face.”

===Chinese===
“哦，可以，”桑代克答道，“但尺度必须同舞台上的完全不同。假发——尤其是假胡须或髭——边缘必须用真正以透明胶黏贴在皮肤上、又用剪子细心修过的毛发衔接起来。眉毛也是同样的道理；肤色的改变则必须做得微妙得多。波尔顿的鼻子是拿一小层假发膏（toupee-paste）堆叠出来的，两颊的粉刺则是用同一种材料的小颗粒做出来的；整个上色用的是油彩，再极轻地干擦一层粉彩，去掉一些油亮。这些在户外化装里都是可行的，但必须以最大的细心和巧手来做；说实在的，要做到艺术评论家所谓的“含蓄”（reticence）。一点点化装就尽够了，过了头便致命。看见区区多少一点膏子便能改变鼻子的形状、改变整张脸的品貌，你会大吃一惊。”

===Original===
At this moment there came a loud knock at the door; a single, solid dab of the knocker which Polton seemed to recognize, for he ejaculated:

===Chinese===
正在这时，门上传来一声响亮的敲击；门环实墩墩地叩了一记，波尔顿似乎听出了这一声，只见他脱口叫道：

===Original===
“Good lord, sir! That’ll be Wilkins, the cabman! I’d forgotten all about him. Whatever’s to be done?”

===Chinese===
“天哪，先生！准是马车夫威尔金斯（Wilkins）！我把这事儿忘了个精光。这可怎么好？”

===Original===
He stared at us in ludicrous horror for a moment or two, and then, snatching off his wig, beard and spectacles, poked them into a cupboard. But his appearance was now too much even for Thorndyke﻿—who hastily got behind him﻿—for he had now resumed his ordinary personality﻿—but with a very material difference.

===Chinese===
他带着一副好笑的惊骇瞪了我们一两秒，随即一把扯下假发、胡子和眼镜，一股脑塞进小橱里。可他此刻的模样，连桑代克也招架不住——赶忙躲到了他背后去——因为他现在已经恢复了平素的本来面目——只是有一处实实在在的不同。

===Original===
“Oh, it’s nothing to laugh at, sir,” he exclaimed indignantly as I crammed my handkerchief into my mouth. “Somebody’s got to let him in, or he’ll go away.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，这有什么可笑的，先生，”见我用手绢死死堵住嘴，他愤愤地嚷道，“总得有人去放他进来，不然他可就走啦。”

===Original===
“Yes; and that won’t do,” said Thorndyke. “But don’t worry, Polton. You can step into the office. I’ll open the door.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，走了可不行，”桑代克说，“不过别慌，波尔顿。你可以躲进办公室去。我去开门。”

===Original===
Polton’s presence of mind, however, seemed to have entirely forsaken him, for he only hovered irresolutely in the wake of his principal. As the door opened, a thick and husky voice inquired:

===Chinese===
然而波尔顿的沉着似乎弃他而去了，他只是犹犹豫豫地缀在他主人身后打转。门开处，一个浑厚沙哑的嗓音问道：

===Original===
“Gent of the name of Polton live here?”

===Chinese===
“这儿住着一位叫波尔顿的先生吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, quite right,” said Thorndyke. “Come in. Your name is Wilkins, I think?”

===Chinese===
“对，正是，”桑代克说，“请进。您名叫威尔金斯，没记错吧？”

===Original===
“That’s me, sir,” said the voice; and in response to Thorndyke’s invitation, a typical “growler” cabman of the old school, complete even to imbricated cape and dangling badge, stalked into the room, and glancing round with a mixture of embarrassment and defiance, suddenly fixed on Polton’s nose a look of devouring curiosity.

===Chinese===
“正是在下，先生，”那嗓音应道；应着桑代克的邀请，一位旧派“吼车”（growler，四轮出租马车）车夫的典型人物大步走进屋来，连鳞片叠缀的斗篷和垂晃的铜牌照牌都一应俱全；他环顾四周，神色又窘迫又倔强，忽然把一道饥渴难耐的好奇目光，牢牢钉在了波尔顿的鼻子上。

===Original===
“Here you are, then,” Polton remarked nervously.

===Chinese===
“这么说，您到啦。”波尔顿局促地说。

===Original===
“Yus,” replied the cabman in a slightly hostile tone. “Here I am. What am I wanted to do? And where’s this here Mr. Polton?”

===Chinese===
“唔，”马车夫答道，口吻略带敌意，“我不在这儿嘛。找我做什么？还有，那位波尔顿先生人在哪儿？”

===Original===
“I am Mr. Polton,” replied our abashed assistant.

===Chinese===
“我就是波尔顿先生，”我们那位臊眉耷眼的助手答道。

===Original===
“Well, it’s the other Mr. Polton what I want,” said the cabman, with his eyes still riveted on the olfactory prominence.

===Chinese===
“嗐，我要找的是另一位波尔顿先生，”马车夫说，眼睛仍旧钉在那座嗅觉隆起上不放。

===Original===
“There isn’t any other Mr. Polton,” our subordinate replied irritably. “I am the﻿—er﻿—person who spoke to you in the shelter.”

===Chinese===
“根本没有什么另一位波尔顿先生，”我们的下属气呼呼地答道，“我就是那个——呃——在歇脚棚里同您说话的人。”

===Original===
“Are you though?” said the manifestly incredulous cabby. “I shouldn’t have thought it; but you ought to know. What do you want me to do?”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”那位明显满腹狐疑的车夫说，“我可真没瞧出来；不过您自己该清楚。要我做什么？”

===Original===
“We want you,” said Thorndyke, “to answer one or two questions. And the first one is, Are you a teetotaller?”

===Chinese===
“我们要您，”桑代克说，“回答一两个问题。第一个问题是：您是不是滴酒不沾的人？”

===Original===
The question being illustrated by the production of a decanter, the cabman’s dignity relaxed somewhat.

===Chinese===
这一问还辅以一只细颈酒瓶的出示，马车夫的庄严便松动了几分。

===Original===
“I ain’t bigoted,” said he.

===Chinese===
“我这人倒不死心眼儿。”他说。

===Original===
“Then sit down and mix yourself a glass of grog. Soda or plain water?”

===Chinese===
“那就请坐，给自己调一杯格罗格酒（grog）吧。兑苏打水还是兑白水？”

===Original===
“May as well have all the extries,” replied the cabman, sitting down and grasping the decanter with the air of a man who means business. “Per’aps you wouldn’t mind squirtin’ out the soda, sir, bein’ more used to it.”

===Chinese===
“得，配料不妨全上，”马车夫答道，一屁股坐下，一把攥住酒瓶，一副正经办事的架势。“苏打水兴许要劳驾您来喷，先生，到底您使唤得惯。”

===Original===
While these preliminaries were being arranged, Polton silently slipped out of the room, and when our visitor had fortified himself with a gulp of the uncommonly stiff mixture, the examination began.

===Chinese===
这些预备手续正布置着，波尔顿悄悄溜出了房间；等我们的来客灌下一大口那杯烈得异乎寻常的掺酒、给自己壮了胆，问话便开始了。

===Original===
“Your name, I think, is Wilkins?” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“您名叫威尔金斯，我没记错吧？”桑代克说。

===Original===
“That’s me, sir. Samuel Wilkins is my name.”

===Chinese===
“正是在下，先生。我叫塞缪尔·威尔金斯。”

===Original===
“And your occupation?”

===Chinese===
“那您的职业呢？”

===Original===
“Is a very tryin’ one and not paid for as it deserves. I drives a cab, sir; a four-wheeled cab is what I drives; and a very poor job it is.”

===Chinese===
“是个顶熬人的营生，挣的工钱可对不住这份辛苦。我是赶车的，先生；赶的是一辆四轮马车；这可是个苦行当。”

===Original===
“Do you happen to remember a very foggy day about a month ago?”

===Chinese===
“约莫一个月前有个雾特别大的日子，您还记得吗？”

===Original===
“Do I not, sir! A regler sneezer that was! Wednesday, the fourteenth of March. I remember the date because my benefit society came down on me for arrears that morning.”

===Chinese===
“怎么不记得，先生！那可是个地道叫人打喷嚏的日子！礼拜三，三月十四号。日子我记得清楚，因为那天早上互助会（benefit society）还找上门来催欠款。”

===Original===
“Will you tell us what happened to you between six and seven in the evening of that day?”

===Chinese===
“能不能给我们讲讲，那天傍晚六点到七点之间，您遇到了什么事？”

===Original===
“I will, sir,” replied the cabman, emptying his tumbler by way of bracing himself up for the effort. “A little before six I was waiting on the arrival side of the Great Northern Station, King’s Cross, when I see a gentleman and a lady coming out. The gentleman he looks up and down and then he sees me and walks up to the cab and opens the door and helps the lady in. Then he says to me: ‘Do you know New Inn?’ he says. That’s what he says to me what was born and brought up in White Horse Alley, Drury Lane.

===Chinese===
“我讲，先生，”马车夫答道，把杯中酒一饮而尽，算是为这场费力的事给自己垫垫底。“六点差一点，我正在国王十字（King’s Cross）大北部车站（Great Northern Station）的到站那一面候着，看见一位先生和一位太太走出来。那位先生东张西望，随后瞧见了我，便走到车前拉开车门，扶那位太太上了车。然后他对我说：‘你认得新客栈吗？’他说。冲着我这个人生在德鲁里巷白马巷（White Horse Alley）、长在白马巷的人，他居然说出这种话来。

===Original===
“ ‘Get inside,’ says I.

===Chinese===
“‘坐进去，’我说。

===Original===
“ ‘Well,’ says he, ‘you drive in through the gate in Wych Street,’ he says, as if he expected me to go in by Houghton Street and down the steps, ‘and then,’ he says, ‘you drive nearly to the end and you’ll see a house with a large brass plate at the corner of the doorway. That’s where we want to be set down,’ he says, and with that he nips in and pulls up the windows and off we goes.

===Chinese===
“‘唔，’他说，‘你从威奇街（Wych Street）那个大门赶进去，’他说，倒好像他料想我会打霍顿街（Houghton Street）那头进去、再顺着台阶下来似的，‘然后，’他说，‘你差不多赶到头，就会看见门洞子角上带一块大铜牌的房子。我们要在那儿下车，’他说；说罢他一猫腰钻进车，拉起车窗，我们这就出发。

===Original===
“It took us a full half-hour to get to New Inn through the fog, for I had to get down and lead the horse part of the way. As I drove in under the archway, I saw it was half-past six by the clock in the porter’s lodge. I drove down nearly to the end of the inn and drew up opposite a house where there was a big brass plate by the doorway. It was number thirty-one. Then the gent crawls out and hands me five bob﻿—two ’arf-crowns﻿—and then he helps the lady out, and away they waddles to the doorway and I see them start up the stairs very slow﻿—regler Pilgrim’s Progress. And that was the last I see of ’em.”

===Chinese===
“顶着雾赶到新客栈，足足用了半个钟头，因为有一段路我得下来牵着马走。从拱道底下赶进去的时候，我看见门房里的钟是六点半。我顺着客栈里那条道差不多赶到头，在一座门前带大铜牌的房子对面停了车。门牌是三十一号。随后那位老爷磨磨蹭蹭爬下车，递给我五先令——两枚半克朗——接着又扶那位太太下车；俩人摇摇摆摆蹭到门洞，我瞧他们慢吞吞地往楼梯上挪——活脱一出《天路历程》（Pilgrim’s Progress）。打那以后，我就再没瞧见他俩。”

===Original===
Thorndyke wrote down the cabman’s statement verbatim together with his own questions, and then asked:

===Chinese===
桑代克把马车夫的证词连同他自己提的问题一字不差地记了下来，然后问道：

===Original===
“Can you give us any description of the gentleman?”

===Chinese===
“您能把那位先生的模样给我们描述一下吗？”

===Original===
“The gent,” said Wilkins, “was a very respectable-looking gent, though he did look as if he’d had a drop of something short, and small blame to him on a day like that. But he was all there, and he knew what was the proper fare for a foggy evening, which is more than some of ’em do. He was a elderly gent, about sixty, and he wore spectacles, but he didn’t seem to be able to see much through ’em. He was a funny ’un to look at; as round in the back as a turtle and he walked with his head stuck forward like a goose.”

===Chinese===
“那位老爷，”威尔金斯说，“是个看着很体面的老爷，虽说瞧上去像是抿过一小口烈酒，可赶上那种天气，这也怨不得他。不过他心里透亮，还知道起雾的晚上该给多少车钱——这一点可比有些主顾强。他是位上了岁数的老爷，六十来岁，戴着眼镜，可他隔着镜片似乎也瞧不大见东西。他那模样生得好玩儿：背圆得像只乌龟，走路又探着个脑袋，活像只鹅。”

===Original===
“What made you think he had been drinking?”

===Chinese===
“您凭什么觉得他喝过酒呢？”

===Original===
“Well, he wasn’t as steady as he might have been on his pins. But he wasn’t drunk, you know. Only a bit wobbly on the plates.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，他站在两条腿上，比不得平常人那么稳当。不过他没醉，您知道。只是脚底板有点儿发飘。”

===Original===
“And the lady; what was she like?”

===Chinese===
“那么那位太太呢？她什么样儿？”

===Original===
“I couldn’t see much of her because her head was wrapped up in a sort of woollen veil. But I should say she wasn’t a chicken. Might have been about the same age as the gent, but I couldn’t swear to that. She seemed a trifle rickety on the pins too; in fact they were a rum-looking couple. I watched ’em tottering across the pavement and up the stairs, hanging on to each other, him peering through his blinkers and she trying to see through her veil, and I thought it was a jolly good job they’d got a nice sound cab and a steady driver to bring ’em safe home.”

===Chinese===
“她的模样我可瞧不真，因为脑袋裹在一幅毛线面纱里。不过要我说，她可不是只嫩雏儿。年纪兴许跟那位老爷不相上下，不过这我可不敢赌咒。她那两条腿瞧着也有些打晃；说实在的，他们俩是一对怪模怪样的主儿。我瞧着他们相互搀着、颤颤巍巍地挪过人行道往楼梯上蹭——他隔着那对“遮眼罩”眯缝着瞅，她隔着面纱使劲地瞧——我心里想，多亏他们雇着一辆结实的好车、一个稳当的车夫，把他们平平安安送到家，真是天大的造化。”

===Original===
“How was the lady dressed?”

===Chinese===
“那位太太穿的什么？”

===Original===
“Can’t rightly say, not being a hexpert. Her head was done up in this here veil like a pudden in a cloth and she had a small hat on. She had a dark brown mantle with a fringe of beads round it and a black dress; and I noticed when she got into the cab at the station that one of her stockings looked like the bellows of a concertina. That’s all I can tell you.”

===Chinese===
“说不太准，我又不是个行家。她那脑袋裹在这面纱里，活像块布包布丁，头上还戴着顶小帽。她披一件深棕色的斗篷，四围镶着一圈珠穗，身上是条黑裙子；还有，她在车站上车那会儿，我瞧见她一只袜子松松垮垮，皱得跟手风琴的风箱似的。我能奉告的就这些。”

===Original===
Thorndyke wrote down the last answer, and, having read the entire statement aloud, handed the pen to our visitor.

===Chinese===
桑代克记下最后这句答话，随后把整篇证词朗读了一遍，把笔递给我们的来客。

===Original===
“If that is all correct,” he said, “I will ask you to sign your name at the bottom.”

===Chinese===
“如果这些都对，”他说，“就请您在末尾签上您的名字。”

===Original===
“Do you want me to swear a affidavy that it’s all true?” asked Wilkins.

===Chinese===
“您是要我立个字据赌咒，说句句属实吗？”威尔金斯问。

===Original===
“No, thank you,” replied Thorndyke. “We may have to call you to give evidence in court, and then you’ll be sworn; and you’ll also be paid for your attendance. For the present I want you to keep your own counsel and say nothing to anybody about having been here. We have to make some other inquiries and we don’t want the affair talked about.”

===Chinese===
“不必，谢谢，”桑代克答道，“往后我们也许要传您到法庭作证，到那时自然要宣誓，您出庭也另有酬劳。眼下我只求您守口如瓶，对谁也别提您到过这里。我们还要做几桩别的查问，不愿意这事张扬出去。”

===Original===
“I see, sir,” said Wilkins, as he laboriously traced his signature at the foot of the statement; “you don’t want the other parties for to ogle your lay. All right, sir; you can depend on me. I’m fly, I am.”

===Chinese===
“我明白，先生，”威尔金斯一面在证词末尾一笔一划费劲地写着名字，一面说，“您是不愿让旁的行家瞅上您这趟买卖。成，先生；您只管信得过我。我门儿清。”

===Original===
“Thank you, Wilkins,” said Thorndyke. “And now what are we to give you for your trouble in coming here?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，威尔金斯，”桑代克说，“那么，你辛苦跑这一趟，我们该给你点什么？”

===Original===
“I’ll leave the fare to you, sir. You know what the information’s worth; but I should think ’arf a thick-un wouldn’t hurt you.”

===Chinese===
“车钱就听凭您赏了，先生。这消息值多少，您心里有数；不过依我看，半个‘大厚币’（'arf a thick-un，即半镑金币）总归伤不着您。”

===Original===
Thorndyke laid on the table a couple of sovereigns, at the sight of which the cabman’s eyes glistened.

===Chinese===
桑代克往桌上放了两枚沙弗林金币（sovereign，一镑一枚），马车夫一见，眼里顿时放光。

===Original===
“We have your address, Wilkins,” said he. “If we want you as a witness we shall let you know, and if not, there will be another two pounds for you at the end of a fortnight, provided you have not let this little interview leak out.”

===Chinese===
“我们有你的地址，威尔金斯，”他说，“如果需要你作证，我们会通知你；用不着的话，只要这次小小的会面你没有泄露出去，两星期后还有两镑给你。”

===Original===
Wilkins gathered up the spoils gleefully. “You can trust me, sir,” said he, “for to keep my mouth shut. I knows which side my bread’s buttered. Good night, gentlemen all.”

===Chinese===
威尔金斯喜滋滋地把这笔犒赏敛进腰包。“您尽管信我，先生，”他说，“保险把嘴闭得严严实实。我的面包哪面抹着黄油，我还不知道么。晚安，各位先生。”

===Original===
With this comprehensive salute he moved towards the door and let himself out.

===Chinese===
说完这句面面俱到的道别，他便朝门口走去，自己开门出去了。

===Original===
“Well, Jervis; what do you think of it?” Thorndyke asked, as the cabman’s footsteps faded away in a creaky diminuendo.

===Chinese===
马车夫的脚步声吱吱嘎嘎、渐行渐弱地消逝之后，桑代克问道：“怎么样，杰维斯；你怎么看？”

===Original===
“I don’t know what to think. This woman is a new factor in the case and I don’t know how to place her.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道该怎么想。这个女人是案子里一个新的因素，我不知道该把她摆在什么位置。”

===Original===
“Not entirely new,” said Thorndyke. “You have not forgotten those beads that we found in Jeffrey’s bedroom, have you?”

===Chinese===
“并不全新，”桑代克说，“我们在杰弗里卧室里发现的那些珠子，你没有忘吧？”

===Original===
“No, I had not forgotten them, but I did not see that they told us much excepting that some woman had apparently been in his bedroom at some time.”

===Chinese===
“没忘，只是我看不出它们说明了多少问题，无非是有过某个女人、在某个时候进过他的卧室。”

===Original===
“That, I think, is all that they did tell us. But now they tell us that a particular woman was in his bedroom at a particular time, which is a good deal more significant.”

===Chinese===
“我想，先前它们告诉我们的，也就止于此了。可如今它们告诉我们：一个特定的女人，在一个特定的时间，进过他的卧室。这就意味深长得多了。”

===Original===
“Yes. It almost looks as if she must have been there when he made away with himself.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。看来她几乎准是在他自寻短见的那一刻就在屋里。”

===Original===
“It does, very much.”

===Chinese===
“像，非常像。”

===Original===
“By the way, you were right about the colours of those beads, and also about the way they were used.”

===Chinese===
“对了，那些珠子的颜色，你说对了；它们的用途，你也说对了。”

===Original===
“As to their use, that was a mere guess; but it has turned out to be correct. It was well that we found the beads, for, small as is the amount of information they give, it is still enough to carry us a stage further.”

===Chinese===
“至于用途，那不过是瞎猜；不料竟猜着了。找到那些珠子是件好事，因为它们提供的消息虽然微薄，却足以把我们往前送上一程。”

===Original===
“How so?”

===Chinese===
“怎么讲？”

===Original===
“I mean that the cabman’s evidence tells us only that this woman entered the house. The beads tell us that she was in the bedroom; which, as you say, seems to connect her to some extent with Jeffrey’s death. Not necessarily, of course. It is only a suggestion; but a rather strong suggestion under the peculiar circumstances.”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是：马车夫的证词只告诉我们，这女人进了那座房子。珠子却告诉我们，她进过卧室；正如你说的，这似乎在某种程度上把她同杰弗里的死连在了一起。当然，未必一定如此。这只是一个提示；可在这等特殊的情形之下，却是相当有力的提示。”

===Original===
“Even so,” said I, “this new fact seems to me so far from clearing up the mystery, only to add to it a fresh element of still deeper mystery. The porter’s evidence at the inquest could leave no doubt that Jeffrey contemplated suicide, and his preparations pointedly suggest this particular night as the time selected by him for doing away with himself. Is not that so?”

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，”我说，“依我看，这个新事实非但没有解开疑团，反倒给它添上了一层更深的新疑。验尸审讯上门房的证词，已不容置疑地表明杰弗里存心自尽，而他的种种准备更分明指向：他选定的正是这一个夜晚来了结自己。难道不是这样吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly. The porter’s evidence was very clear on that point.”

===Chinese===
“当然。门房的证词在那一点上是非常清楚的。”

===Original===
“Then I don’t see where this woman comes in. It is obvious that her presence at the inn, and especially in the bedroom, on this occasion and in these strange, secret circumstances, has a rather sinister look; but yet I do not see in what way she could have been connected with the tragedy. Perhaps, after all, she has nothing to do with it. You remember that Jeffrey went to the lodge about eight o’clock, to pay his rent, and chatted for some time with the porter. That looks as if the lady had already left.”

===Chinese===
“那么我就不明白这女人是从哪里插进来的了。显然，她在这个当口、以这样古怪而隐秘的情形出现在客栈里，尤其是出现在卧室里，颇有几分不祥的意味；可我仍看不出她能以什么方式同这场悲剧牵连。也许到头来她与此事毫无干系。你总记得，杰弗里八点钟光景到门房去交房租，还同门房聊了一阵。这么看来，那位太太那时已经走了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke. “But, on the other hand, Jeffrey’s remarks to the porter with reference to the cab do not quite agree with the account that we have just heard from Wilkins. Which suggests﻿—as does Wilkins’s account generally﻿—some secrecy as to the lady’s visit to his chambers.”

===Chinese===
“是，”桑代克说，“不过另一方面，杰弗里对门房说的那几句关于马车的话，同我们方才听威尔金斯讲的并不怎么吻合。这就提示——正如威尔金斯的叙述整体上所提示的——对这位太太造访他寓所一事，有着几分隐秘。”

===Original===
“Do you know who the woman was?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你知道那女人是谁吗？”我问。

===Original===
“No, I don’t know,” he replied. “I have a rather strong suspicion that I can identify her, but I am waiting for some further facts.”

===Chinese===
“不，我不知道，”他答道，“我有一个相当强烈的怀疑，觉得自己能指认她，但我还要再等一等进一步的事实。”

===Original===
“Is your suspicion founded on some new matter that you have discovered, or is it deducible from facts that are known to me?”

===Chinese===
“你的怀疑，是建立在你已发现的什么新材料上，还是可以从我已知道的事实里推演出来？”

===Original===
“I think,” he replied, “that you know practically all that I know, although I have, in one instance, turned a very strong suspicion into a certainty by further inquiries. But I think you ought to be able to form some idea as to who this lady probably was.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”他答道，“你所知道的同我所知道的，实际上已相差无几；只不过有一处，我经过进一步查问，把一个非常强的怀疑变成了确定的事实。不过我以为，你应该能够就这位太太多半是谁，形成一个大概的想法。”

===Original===
“But no woman has been mentioned in the case at all.”

===Chinese===
“可是这案子里压根儿没提到过什么女人啊。”

===Original===
“No; but I think you should be able to give this lady a name, notwithstanding.”

===Chinese===
“是没有；但我以为，尽管如此，你应该能替这位太太安上一个名字。”

===Original===
“Should I? Then I begin to suspect that I am not cut out for medico-legal practice, for I don’t see the faintest glimmer of a suggestion.”

===Chinese===
“我能吗？那我可要疑心自己不是干医学法学这行的材料了，因为我连一丝最微弱的暗示都瞧不见。”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled benevolently. “Don’t be discouraged, Jervis,” said he. “I expect that when you first began to go round the wards, you doubted whether you were cut out for medical practice. I did. For special work one needs special knowledge and an acquired faculty for making use of it. What does a second year’s student make of a small thoracic aneurysm? He knows the anatomy of the chest; he begins to know the normal heart sounds and areas of dullness; but he cannot yet fit his various items of knowledge together. Then comes the experienced physician and perhaps makes a complete diagnosis without any examination at all, merely from hearing the patient speak or cough. He has the same facts as the student, but he has acquired the faculty of instantly connecting an abnormality of function with its correleated anatomical change. It is a matter of experience. And, with your previous training, you will soon acquire the faculty. Try to observe everything. Let nothing escape you. And try constantly to find some connection between facts and events that seem to be unconnected. That is my advice to you; and with that we will put away the Blackmore case for the present and consider our day’s work at an end.”

===Chinese===
桑代克慈祥地微微一笑。“别泄气，杰维斯，”他说，“我料想你头一回跟着查病房的时候，也曾怀疑自己不是干医科的材料。我就怀疑过。干专门的工作，需要专门的知识，还需要一种后天练成的运用它的本领。一个二年级的医学生，遇上一个不大的胸主动脉瘤，能看出什么名堂？胸腔的解剖他懂；正常心音和浊音区他也刚摸到门道；可他还不能把各件知识拼合到一处。这时来了位经验丰富的医生，也许根本不做任何检查，光听病人说句话、咳一声，便作出了完整的诊断。他掌握的事实同那学生一样多，但他已经练出了一种本领：一转眼便把功能的反常同相应的解剖变化挂起钩来。这是经验的问题。以你先前的训练，你很快也会练出这种本领来。力求观察一切。什么也别放过。并且要不断尝试，在看似互不相关的事实与事件之间寻找关联。这就是我对你的忠告；说完这个，我们且把布莱克莫尔案暂时收起，就当今天的工作到此为止吧。”

## VIII The Track Chart / 轨迹图

===Original===
As Thorndyke and I arrived at the main gateway of the Temple and he swung round into the narrow lane, it was suddenly borne in on me that I had made no arrangements for the night. Events had followed one another so continuously and each had been so engrossing that I had lost sight of what I may call my domestic affairs.

===Chinese===
桑代克和我走到坦普尔正门，他一拐弯进了那条窄巷，我这才猛然醒悟：今晚的宿处，我还没作任何安排。事变一桩接着一桩，每一桩又都那么夺人心神，以至于我把自己的——姑且这么叫吧——居家私事都丢到了脑后。

===Original===
“We seem to be heading for your chambers, Thorndyke,” I ventured to remark. “It is a little late to think of it, but I have not yet settled where I am to put up tonight.”

===Chinese===
“看这方向，我们是往你的寓所去啊，桑代克，”我大着胆子说，“这话想起来是晚了些，可我今夜宿在哪里，还没有个着落呢。”

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” he replied, “you are going to put up in your own bedroom which has been waiting in readiness for you ever since you left it. Polton went up and inspected it as soon as you arrived. I take it that you will consider my chambers yours until such time as you may join the benedictine majority and set up a home for yourself.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的伙计，”他答道，“你就宿在自己那间卧室里。它自你走后一直收拾停当，专候你回来。你一进门，波尔顿就上去查看过了。我想，在你加入已婚男士的行列、自立门户之前，你尽可以把我的寓所当作你自己的。”

===Original===
“That is very handsome of you,” said I. “You didn’t mention that the billet you offered was a resident appointment.”

===Chinese===
“你可真够大方的，”我说，“不过你当初可没提起，你给的这份差事还带住。”

===Original===
“Rooms and commons included,” said Thorndyke; and when I protested that I should at least contribute to the costs of living he impatiently waved the suggestion away. We were still arguing the question when we reached our chambers﻿—as I will now call them﻿—and a diversion was occasioned by my taking the lamp from my pocket and placing it on the table.

===Chinese===
“房间伙食全包，”桑代克说；我提出至少也该贴补些生活开销，他不耐烦地一挥手，把这提议挥到了一边。我们正争着这桩事，就到了我们的寓所——往后我就这样称呼它了——而我从口袋里掏出灯来搁在桌上，又给这场争论添了个岔子。

===Original===
“Ah,” my colleague remarked, “that is a little reminder. We will put it on the mantelpiece for Polton to collect and you shall give me a full account of your further adventures in the wilds of Kennington. That was a very odd affair. I have often wondered how it ended.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”我的同事说，“这倒是个小小的信物。我们把它搁到壁炉架上，等波尔顿来取。你还得把你后来在肯宁顿蛮荒之地的种种历险，原原本本讲给我听。那可是桩顶古怪的事。结局如何，我常常纳闷。”

===Original===
He drew our two armchairs up to the fire, put on some more coal, placed the tobacco jar on the table exactly equidistant from the two chairs, and settled himself with the air of a man who is anticipating an agreeable entertainment.

===Chinese===
他把我们两把扶手椅拖到炉火跟前，添了些煤，把烟草罐搁在桌上，与两把椅子不偏不倚恰成等距，然后坐定下来，一副专候赏心乐事的架势。

===Original===
I filled my pipe, and, taking up the thread of the story where I had broken off on the last occasion, began to outline my later experiences. But he brought me up short.

===Chinese===
我装好烟斗，从上次打住的地方续起话头，开始大略叙说我后来的经历。他却把我截住了。

===Original===
“Don’t be sketchy, Jervis. To be sketchy is to be vague. Detail, my child, detail is the soul of induction. Let us have all the facts. We can sort them out afterwards.”

===Chinese===
“别讲得那么粗略，杰维斯。粗略就是含糊。细节，我的孩子，细节是归纳的灵魂。把事实统统摆出来。回头我们再分拣。”

===Original===
I began afresh in a vein of the extremest circumstantiality. With deliberate malice I loaded a prolix narrative with every triviality that a fairly retentive memory could rake out of the half-forgotten past. I cudgelled my brains for irrelevant incidents. I described with the minutest accuracy things that had not the faintest significance. I drew a vivid picture of the carriage inside and out; I painted a lifelike portrait of the horse, even going into particulars of the harness﻿—which I was surprised to find that I had noticed. I described the furniture of the dining-room and the cobwebs that had hung from the ceiling; the auction-ticket on the chest of drawers, the rickety table and the melancholy chairs. I gave the number per minute of the patient’s respirations and the exact quantity of coffee consumed on each occasion, with an exhaustive description of the cup from which it was taken; and I left no personal details unconsidered, from the patient’s fingernails to the roseate pimples on Mr. Weiss’s nose.

===Chinese===
我重新讲起，这回用的是顶顶絮烦的路数。我存心使坏，把一副还算牢靠的记性能从半已淡忘的往事里扒拉出来的每一件琐屑，统统塞进冗长的叙述里。我绞尽脑汁搜罗不相干的枝节。我把毫无半点意义的东西描述得精细入微。我把那辆马车里里外外画了幅活灵活现的图；给那匹马作了幅惟妙惟肖的肖像，连马具都一一细说——发现自己竟留意过这些，我颇觉诧异。我描述了餐室的家具和从天花板上垂挂下来的蛛网；五斗柜上的拍卖条子，摇摇晃晃的桌子和愁眉苦脸的椅子。我报出病人每分钟呼吸的次数，每次喝下的咖啡的确切分量，还把盛咖啡的那只杯子描述了个透彻；从病人的手指甲到韦斯先生鼻子上玫瑰色的酒刺，各人的细节我一概不曾放过。

===Original===
But my tactics of studied prolixity were a complete failure. The attempt to fatigue Thorndyke’s brain with superabundant detail was like trying to surfeit a pelican with whitebait. He consumed it all with calm enjoyment and asked for more; and when, at last, I did really begin to think that I had bored him a little, he staggered me by reading over his notes and starting a brisk cross-examination to elicit fresh facts! And the most surprising thing of all was that when I had finished I seemed to know a great deal more about the case than I had ever known before.

===Chinese===
可我这条刻意啰嗦的计策一败涂地。想用过量的事实把桑代克的脑子灌到发腻，无异于想拿银鱼把鹈鹕喂到反胃。他心平气和、津津有味地把它统统吃下，还嫌不够；及至我当真开始以为自己让他有点腻烦了，他竟念起自己的笔记，发起一场干净利落的盘问，逼问出新的事实来，惊得我目瞪口呆！而最出人意料的是，等我讲完，我仿佛比以往任何时候都更了解这桩案子了。

===Original===
“It was a very remarkable affair,” he observed, when the cross-examination was over﻿—leaving me somewhat in the condition of a cider-apple that has just been removed from a hydraulic press﻿—“a very suspicious affair with a highly unsatisfactory end. I am not sure that I entirely agree with your police officer. Nor do I fancy that some of my acquaintances at Scotland Yard would have agreed with him.”

===Chinese===
“那是桩非常离奇的案子，”盘问收场后他说——这场盘问把我折腾得多少像只刚从水压机里取出来的酿酒苹果——“一桩非常可疑、结局又极不如人意的案子。我不敢说我完全赞同你那位警官的看法。也不见得苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）里我那几位相熟的朋友就会赞同他。”

===Original===
“Do you think I ought to have taken any further measures?” I asked uneasily.

===Chinese===
“你看我当初还该再采取些别的措施吗？”我不安地问。

===Original===
“No; I don’t see how you could. You did all that was possible under the circumstances. You gave information, which is all that a private individual can do, especially if he is an overworked general practitioner. But still, an actual crime is the affair of every good citizen. I think we ought to take some action.”

===Chinese===
“不必；我看你还能怎么办呢。在当时的情况下，能做的你都做了。你报了案，而一个平民所能做的也就到此为止，尤其当他还是个忙得团团转的开业医生。但一桩实打实的罪行，终究是每个善良公民分内的事。我想我们该采取点行动。”

===Original===
“You think there really was a crime, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么，你认为当真有一桩罪行？”

===Original===
“What else can one think? What do you think about it yourself?”

===Chinese===
“还能作何想？你自己又作何感想？”

===Original===
“I don’t like to think about it at all. The recollection of that corpse-like figure in that gloomy bedroom has haunted me ever since I left the house. What do you suppose has happened?”

===Chinese===
“我根本不愿意去想。自打离开那座宅子，那具死尸般的人影躺在阴森卧室里的记忆，就一直纠缠着我。你猜事情落到哪一步了？”

===Original===
Thorndyke did not answer for a few seconds. At length he said gravely:

===Chinese===
桑代克有好几秒钟没有作声。末了他正色说道：

===Original===
“I am afraid, Jervis, that the answer to that question can be given in one word.”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯，恐怕这个问题的答案，一个词就够了。”

===Original===
“Murder?” I asked with a slight shudder.

===Chinese===
“谋杀？”我微微打了个寒噤，问道。

===Original===
He nodded, and we were both silent for a while.

===Chinese===
他点了点头，我们俩都沉默了一会儿。

===Original===
“The probability,” he resumed after a pause, “that Mr. Graves is alive at this moment seems to me infinitesimal. There was evidently a conspiracy to murder him, and the deliberate, persistent manner in which that object was being pursued points to a very strong and definite motive. Then the tactics adopted point to considerable forethought and judgment. They are not the tactics of a fool or an ignoramus. We may criticize the closed carriage as a tactical mistake, calculated to arouse suspicion, but we have to weigh it against its alternative.”

===Chinese===
“说到概率，”他顿了顿，接着说，“格雷夫斯先生此刻还活着的概率，在我看来微乎其微。分明有一场谋害他的阴谋，而追逐这一目标时那份从容不迫、锲而不舍的劲头，指向一个非常强烈而明确的动机。再者，所用的种种手法显出相当的深谋与决断。那可不是蠢材或无知之辈的手法。我们固然可以指摘封闭马车是战术上的一步错棋，容易招人起疑，可也得把它跟另一条路掂量掂量。”

===Original===
“What is that?”

===Chinese===
“哪一条？”

===Original===
“Well, consider the circumstances. Suppose Weiss had called you in in the ordinary way. You would still have detected the use of poison. But now you could have located your man and made inquiries about him in the neighbourhood. You would probably have given the police a hint and they would almost certainly have taken action, as they would have had the means of identifying the parties. The result would have been fatal to Weiss. The closed carriage invited suspicion, but it was a great safeguard. Weiss’s method’s were not so unsound after all. He is a cautious man, but cunning and very persistent. And he could be bold on occasion. The use of the blinded carriage was a decidedly audacious proceeding. I should put him down as a gambler of a very discreet, courageous and resourceful type.”

===Chinese===
“唔，设身处地想想。假定韦斯按寻常路子把你请去。你照样会察觉有人用了毒。可那样一来，你就能查明你那位病人的下落，在他住的那一带打听他的底细。你多半会给警方递个话，而警方几乎肯定会采取行动，因为他们有办法查明当事人的身份。结果对韦斯就是致命的。封闭马车惹人起疑，却是极大的保险。韦斯的那套办法到底不算太不高明。他是个谨慎的人，但狡诈，而且极有韧性。兴之所至，也敢弄险。动用一辆遮得严严实实的马车，实在是相当胆大妄为的一步。要我来下断语，他是个赌徒，属于那种极有分寸、有胆量、有急智的类型。”

===Original===
“Which all leads to the probability that he has pursued his scheme and brought it to a successful issue.”

===Chinese===
“而这一切都指向一种可能：他已把那套计划推行到底，并办得成了。”

===Original===
“I am afraid it does. But﻿—have you got your notes of the compass-bearings?”

===Chinese===
“恐怕正是如此。不过——你那些罗盘方位的记录在身上吗？”

===Original===
“The book is in my overcoat pocket with the board. I will fetch them.”

===Chinese===
“本子和垫板都在我大衣口袋里。我去取来。”

===Original===
I went into the office, where our coats hung, and brought back the notebook with the little board to which it was still attached by the rubber band. Thorndyke took them from me, and, opening the book, ran his eye quickly down one page after another. Suddenly he glanced at the clock.

===Chinese===
我走进挂着大衣的办公室，取回那个笔记本和那块小垫板——两者还由橡皮筋连在一起。桑代克从我手里接过去，翻开本子，目光一页接一页飞快地扫下去。忽然他瞥了一眼时钟。

===Original===
“It is a little late to begin,” said he, “but these notes look rather alluring. I am inclined to plot them out at once. I fancy, from their appearance, that they will enable us to locate the house without much difficulty. But don’t let me keep you up if you are tired. I can work them out by myself.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿动手是晚了点，”他说，“可这些记录看着相当诱人。我倾向于马上把它们绘出来。瞧它们这副模样，我想我们不难凭它们定出那座宅子的位置。不过你若是乏了，别陪我熬着。我自己一个人也弄得来。”

===Original===
“You won’t do anything of the kind,” I exclaimed. “I am as keen on plotting them as you are, and, besides, I want to see how it is done. It seems to be a rather useful accomplishment.”

===Chinese===
“你休想，”我叫道，“绘图的兴致我不比你小，再说，我还想看看这东西是怎么个绘法。这似乎是门挺有用的本事。”

===Original===
“It is,” said Thorndyke. “In our work, the ability to make a rough but reliable sketch survey is often of great value. Have you ever looked over these notes?”

===Chinese===
“可不是，”桑代克说，“干我们这行，能做一份粗略却可靠的草图测绘，常常极有价值。这些记录你翻看过吗？”

===Original===
“No. I put the book away when I came in and have never looked at it since.”

===Chinese===
“没有。进门时我把本子收了起来，此后再没看过。”

===Original===
“It is a quaint document. You seem to be rich in railway bridges in those parts, and the route was certainly none of the most direct, as you noticed at the time. However, we will plot it out and then we shall see exactly what it looks like and whither it leads us.”

===Chinese===
“这是份古怪的文书。你们那一带铁路桥未免太富余，而那条路线也实在算不上近便，这一点你当时就察觉了。不要紧，我们把它绘出来，到时候它究竟是个什么模样、把我们引向何处，便一清二楚了。”

===Original===
He retired to the laboratory and presently returned with a T-square, a military protractor, a pair of dividers and a large drawing-board on which was pinned a sheet of cartridge paper.

===Chinese===
他退进实验室，不多时便拿着一把丁字尺、一只军用量角器、一副分规和一块大绘图板回来，板上钉着一张绘图纸。

===Original===
“Now,” said he, seating himself at the table with the board before him, “as to the method. You started from a known position and you arrived at a place the position of which is at present unknown. We shall fix the position of that spot by applying two factors, the distance that you travelled and the direction in which you were moving. The direction is given by the compass; and, as the horse seems to have kept up a remarkably even pace, we can take time as representing distance. You seem to have been travelling at about eight miles an hour, that is, roughly, a seventh of a mile in one minute. So if, on our chart, we take one inch as representing one minute, we shall be working with a scale of about seven inches to the mile.”

===Chinese===
“喏，”他在桌前坐下，绘图板摆在面前，说道，“先说方法。你从一个已知的地点出发，到达了一处目前位置未知的地点。我们要用两个因子来确定那个点的位置：你走过的路程，和你行进的方向。方向由罗盘给出；而既然那匹马似乎始终保持着一副出奇均匀的步速，我们就可以拿时间来代表路程。你的车速看去约为每小时八英里，也就是说，粗略算来每分钟七分之一英里。这样，在我们的图上，让一英寸代表一分钟，我们便是在用大约七英寸比一英里的比例尺作业。”

===Original===
“That doesn’t sound very exact as to distance,” I objected.

===Chinese===
“这么定路程，听着可不大精确。”我提出异议。

===Original===
“It isn’t. But that doesn’t matter much. We have certain landmarks, such as these railway arches that you have noted, by which the actual distance can be settled after the route is plotted. You had better read out the entries, and, opposite each, write a number for reference, so that we need not confuse the chart by writing details on it. I shall start near the middle of the board, as neither you nor I seem to have the slightest notion what your general direction was.”

===Chinese===
“本来就不精确。可这无伤大雅。我们有若干地标，比如你记下的这些铁路拱，等路线绘出之后，实际路程便可据以核定。这些条目还是你来念，并在每条旁边编个号备查，免得我们把细节写到图上，搅乱了它。我打算从图纸中部起笔，因为你我二人似乎都丝毫摸不清你当时的大体走向。”

===Original===
I laid the open notebook before me and read out the first entry:

===Chinese===
我把翻开的笔记本摆在面前，念出第一条记录：

===Original===
“ ‘Eight fifty-eight. West by South. Start from home. Horse thirteen hands.’ ”

===Chinese===
“‘八点五十八分。西偏南。自家中出发。马，十三掌。’”

===Original===
“You turned round at once, I understand,” said Thorndyke, “so we draw no line in that direction. The next is﻿—?”

===Chinese===
“我明白，你们当即就掉头了，”桑代克说，“所以这个方向我们不画线。下一条是——？”

===Original===
“ ‘Eight fifty-eight minutes, thirty seconds, East by North’; and the next is ‘Eight fifty-nine, Northeast.’ ”

===Chinese===
“‘八点五十八分三十秒，东偏北’；再下一条是‘八点五十九分，东北。’”

===Original===
“Then you travelled east by north about a fifteenth of a mile and we shall put down half an inch on the chart. Then you turned northeast. How long did you go on?”

===Chinese===
“那么你们朝东偏北走了约十五分之一英里，我们在图上落下半英寸。随后你们转向东北。走了多久？”

===Original===
“Exactly a minute. The next entry is ‘Nine. West northwest.’ ”

===Chinese===
“整整一分钟。下一条记录是‘九点。西西北。’”

===Original===
“Then you travelled about the seventh of a mile in a northeasterly direction and we draw a line an inch long at an angle of forty-five degrees to the right of the north and south line. From the end of that we carry a line at an angle of fifty-six and a quarter degrees to the left of the north and south line, and so on. The method is perfectly simple, you see.”

===Chinese===
“那么你们朝东北方向走了约七分之一英里，我们便画一条一英寸长的线，与南北线成四十五度角、偏向右侧。再从它的末端引出一条线，与南北线成五十六又四分之一度角、偏向左侧，依此类推。方法简单得很，你瞧。”

===Original===
“Perfectly; I quite understand it now.”

===Chinese===
“简单极了；我现在完全明白了。”

===Original===
I went back to my chair and continued to read out the entries from the notebook while Thorndyke laid off the lines of direction with the protractor, taking out the distances with the dividers from a scale of equal parts on the back of the instrument. As the work proceeded, I noticed, from time to time, a smile of quiet amusement spread over my colleague’s keen, attentive face, and at each new reference to a railway bridge he chuckled softly.

===Chinese===
我回到自己的椅子上，继续一条条念笔记本里的记录，桑代克则用量角器标出一条条方向线，再用分规从仪器背面的等分比例尺上量取距离。随着工作的进行，我不时注意到，一抹暗自好笑的笑意，在我这位同事那张敏锐而专注的脸上漾开，而每念到一座新的铁路桥，他便低低地笑出声来。

===Original===
“What, again!” he laughed, as I recorded the passage of the fifth or sixth bridge. “It’s like a game of croquet. Go on. What is the next?”

===Chinese===
“什么，又来一座！”我念到第五还是第六座桥时，他笑道，“这简直像玩槌球。念下去。下一条是什么？”

===Original===
I went on reading out the notes until I came to the final one:

===Chinese===
我一条接一条念下去，直到最后一条：

===Original===
“ ‘Nine twenty-four. Southeast. In covered way. Stop. Wooden gates closed.’ ”

===Chinese===
“‘九点二十四分。东南。在带顶的廊道内。停。木门关闭。’”

===Original===
Thorndyke ruled off the last line, remarking: “Then your covered way is on the south side of a street which bears northeast. So we complete our chart. Just look at your route, Jervis.”

===Chinese===
桑代克划下最后一条线，说道：“那么你那条带顶的廊道，在一条朝东北去的街道的南侧。图就这样绘完了。瞧瞧你的路线吧，杰维斯。”

===Original===
He held up the board with a quizzical smile and I stared in astonishment at the chart. The single line, which represented the route of the carriage, zigzagged in the most amazing manner, turning, re-turning and crossing itself repeatedly, evidently passing more than once down the same thoroughfares and terminating at a comparatively short distance from its commencement.

===Chinese===
他带着揶揄的笑举起图板，我瞪着那张图，惊愕不已。那条代表马车路线的独线，以最不可思议的方式左折右拐，转了又转，一再自相交叉，显见得不止一次驶过同样的几条街道，而终点离起点竟只有短短一程。

===Original===
“Why!” I exclaimed, the “rascal must have lived quite near to Stillbury’s house!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”我叫道，“那‘恶棍’住的地方，准离斯蒂尔伯里的家很近！”

===Original===
Thorndyke measured with the dividers the distance between the starting and arriving points of the route and took it off from the scale.

===Chinese===
桑代克用分规量取路线起讫两点之间的距离，再到比例尺上比读出数值。

===Original===
“Five-eighths of a mile, roughly,” he said. “You could have walked it in less than ten minutes. And now let us get out the ordnance map and see if we can give to each of those marvellously erratic lines ‘a local habitation and a name.’ ”

===Chinese===
“粗略算来，八分之五英里，”他说，“步行用不了十分钟。现在我们把陆军测量局地图取出来，看看能不能给这些乱得出奇的线条，一条条安上“确切的所在和名字”。”

===Original===
He spread the map out on the table and placed our chart by its side.

===Chinese===
他把地图在桌上摊开，把我们的轨迹图摆在旁边。

===Original===
“I think,” said he, “you started from Lower Kennington Lane?”

===Chinese===
“我想，”他说，“你是从下肯宁顿巷出发的吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, from this point,” I replied, indicating the spot with a pencil.

===Chinese===
“对，从这个点，”我一面回答，一面用铅笔点了点那个地方。

===Original===
“Then,” said Thorndyke, “if we swing the chart round twenty degrees to correct the deviation of the compass, we can compare it with the ordnance map.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”桑代克说，“只要把图转过二十度，校正罗盘的磁差，就能拿它同陆军测量局地图比对了。”

===Original===
He set off with the protractor an angle of twenty degrees from the north and south line and turned the chart round to that extent. After closely scrutinizing the map and the chart and comparing the one with the other, he said:

===Chinese===
他用量角器从南北线起量出一个二十度的角，把图转过那么多。他把地图和轨迹图细细端详、彼此比对一番之后，说道：

===Original===
“By mere inspection it seems fairly easy to identify the thoroughfares that correspond to the lines of the chart. Take the part that is near your destination. At nine twenty-one you passed under a bridge, going westward. That would seem to be Glasshouse Street. Then you turned south, apparently along the Albert Embankment, where you heard the tug’s whistle. Then you heard a passenger train start on your left; that would be Vauxhall Station. Next you turned round due east and passed under a large railway bridge, which suggests the bridge that carries the Station over Upper Kennington Lane. If that is so, your house should be on the south side of Upper Kennington Lane, some three hundred yards from the bridge. But we may as well test our inferences by one or two measurements.”

===Chinese===
“单凭目测，把与图上各线相应的街道认出来，似乎并不太难。就拿临近你目的地的这一段说。九点二十一分，你们在一座桥下向西行去。那似乎是格拉斯豪斯街（Glasshouse Street）。随后你们转向南，分明是沿着阿尔伯特堤岸（Albert Embankment），在那里你听见了拖船的汽笛。接着你听见一列客车在你左边开出；那该是沃克斯霍尔车站（Vauxhall Station）。再往后你们掉头正东，从一座大铁路桥下穿过，这使人想到车站凌跨上肯宁顿巷（Upper Kennington Lane）的那座桥。倘若如此，你那座宅子就该在上肯宁顿巷的路南，离桥约三百码。不过，我们不妨用一两次实测来检验这些推断。”

===Original===
“How can you do that if you don’t know the exact scale of the chart?”

===Chinese===
“你连图的准确比例尺都不知道，这怎么测？”

===Original===
“I will show you,” said Thorndyke. “We shall establish the true scale and that will form part of the proof.”

===Chinese===
“我这就做给你看，”桑代克说，“我们要定出真实的比例尺，而这本身就是证明的一部分。”

===Original===
He rapidly constructed on the upper blank part of the paper, a proportional diagram consisting of two intersecting lines with a single cross-line.

===Chinese===
他在图纸上方空白处迅速画出一个比例图，由两条相交的直线加一条横线构成。

===Original===
The Track Chart, showing the route followed by Weiss’ carriage

===Chinese===
轨迹图，示韦斯马车所行经的路线

===Original===
1. —Starting-point in Lower Kennington Lane.

===Chinese===
1. ——下肯宁顿巷中的出发点。

===Original===
2. —Position of Mr. Weiss’ house. The dotted lines connecting the bridges indicate probable railway lines.

===Chinese===
2. ——韦斯先生宅子的位置。连接各桥的虚线表示推测的铁路线。

===Original===
“This long line,” he explained, “is the distance from Stillbury’s house to the Vauxhall railway bridge as it appears on the chart; the shorter cross-line is the same distance taken from the ordnance map. If our inference is correct and the chart is reasonably accurate, all the other distances will show a similar proportion. Let us try some of them. Take the distance from Vauxhall bridge to the Glasshouse Street bridge.”

===Chinese===
“这条长线，”他解释道，“是图上量出的斯蒂尔伯里的家到沃克斯霍尔铁路桥的距离；较短的那条横线，是从陆军测量局地图上取下的同一距离。倘若我们的推断不差、图又够得上准确，其余所有距离都该呈同样的比例。我们试它几处。就取沃克斯霍尔桥到格拉斯豪斯街桥的距离。”

===Original===
He made the two measurements carefully, and, as the point of the dividers came down almost precisely in the correct place on the diagram, he looked up at me.

===Chinese===
他仔细做了这两处测量；当分规的针脚落下去，几乎不偏不倚正落在比例图上该落的地方，他抬眼看我。

===Original===
“Considering the roughness of the method by which the chart was made, I think that is pretty conclusive, though, if you look at the various arches that you passed under and see how nearly they appear to follow the position of the Southwestern Railway line, you hardly need further proof. But I will take a few more proportional measurements for the satisfaction of proving the case by scientific methods before we proceed to verify our conclusions by a visit to the spot.”

===Chinese===
“考虑到制图方法的粗略，我认为这已经很有定论了；不过，你只要看看你从底下穿过的那几座桥拱，看看它们与西南铁路（Southwestern Railway）线的位置何等吻合，几乎无须再证。但在我们亲赴其地验证结论之前，我还想为着以科学方法证明本案的那份满足，再多测几组比例。”

===Original===
He took off one or two more distances, and on comparing them with the proportional distances on the ordnance map, found them in every case as nearly correct as could be expected.

===Chinese===
他又量取了一两段距离，与陆军测量局地图上的相应距离一一比对，发现每一段都准确得尽如所期。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke, laying down the dividers, “I think we have narrowed down the locality of Mr. Weiss’s house to a few yards in a known street. We shall get further help from your note of nine twenty-three thirty, when which records a patch of newly laid macadam extending up to the house.”

===Chinese===
“成了，”桑代克放下分规，说道，“我想我们已把韦斯先生宅子的所在，收窄到一条已知街道上区区几码的范围之内。你九点二十三分三十秒那条记录还会再帮我们一把——那里头记着一段新铺的碎石路面，一直铺到宅子跟前。”

===Original===
“That new macadam will be pretty well smoothed down by now,” I objected.

===Chinese===
“那段新碎石路面，到这会儿恐怕早碾得平平的了。”我提出异议。

===Original===
“Not so very completely,” answered Thorndyke. “It is only a little over a month ago, and there has been very little wet weather since. It may be smooth, but it will be easily distinguishable from the old.”

===Chinese===
“不至于碾得那么彻底，”桑代克答道，“那不过是一个月稍多一点之前的事，此后又几乎没下过什么雨。它兴许平滑了，可跟旧路面一比，还是一眼就能分辨。”

===Original===
“And do I understand that you propose to go and explore the neighbourhood?”

===Chinese===
“那么我是否可以理解为，你打算去那一带踏勘一番？”

===Original===
“Undoubtedly I do. That is to say, I intend to convert the locality of this house into a definite address; which, I think, will now be perfectly easy, unless we should have the bad luck to find more than one covered way. Even then, the difficulty would be trifling.”

===Chinese===
“当然要去。这就是说，我打算把这座宅子的方位落实成一个确切的地址；而这，我想，眼下已易如反掌，除非我们倒霉，撞见不止一条带顶的廊道。真到那步田地，这点困难也微不足道。”

===Original===
“And when you have ascertained where Mr. Weiss lives? What then?”

===Chinese===
“那等你查明韦斯先生的住处之后呢？接下去怎么办？”

===Original===
“That will depend on circumstances. I think we shall probably call at Scotland Yard and have a little talk with our friend Mr. Superintendent Miller; unless, for any reason, it seems better to look into the case ourselves.”

===Chinese===
“那要看情形。我想我们多半会去苏格兰场走一遭，同我们的老朋友米勒警司（Superintendent Miller）先生聊一聊；除非出于什么缘故，由我们亲自查办更为妥当。”

===Original===
“When is this voyage of exploration to take place?”

===Chinese===
“这趟踏勘之旅，定在什么时候？”

===Original===
Thorndyke considered this question, and, taking out his pocketbook, glanced through his engagements.

===Chinese===
桑代克把这个问题掂量了一下，掏出记事簿，把各桩约会略略翻了一遍。

===Original===
“It seems to me,” he said, “that tomorrow is a fairly free day. We could take the morning without neglecting other business. I suggest that we start immediately after breakfast. How will that suit my learned friend?”

===Chinese===
“依我看，”他说，“明天相当清闲。我们可以占用一个上午，又不致耽误别的事务。我提议早点一吃罢就动身。不知我这位博学的朋友意下如何？”

===Original===
“My time is yours,” I replied; “and if you choose to waste it on matters that don’t concern you, that’s your affair.”

===Chinese===
“我的时间归你支配，”我答道，“你若偏要把它耗在与你不相干的事情上，那是你的事。”

===Original===
“Then we will consider the arrangement to stand for tomorrow morning, or rather, for this morning, as I see that it is past twelve.”

===Chinese===
“那么我们就一言为定，定在明天早晨——不如说今天早晨，因为我看已过十二点了。”

===Original===
With this Thorndyke gathered up the chart and instruments and we separated for the night.

===Chinese===
说了这话，桑代克收起轨迹图和仪器，我们便各自安歇去了。

## II Thorndyke Devises a Scheme / 桑代克定下一计

===Original===
As I entered the Temple by the Tudor Street gate the aspect of the place smote my senses with an air of agreeable familiarity. Here had I spent many a delightful hour when working with Thorndyke at the remarkable Hornby case, which the newspapers had called “The Case of the Red Thumb Mark”; and here had I met the romance of my life, the story whereof is told elsewhere. The place was thus endeared to me by pleasant recollections of a happy past, and its associations suggested hopes of happiness yet to come and in the not too far distant future.

===Chinese===
我从都铎街（Tudor Street）那道门走进坦普尔，此地的景象便带着一种惬意的熟稔气息扑上我的感官。当年我随桑代克经办那桩出色的霍恩比案（Hornby case）——报纸称之为“红拇指印案”（The Case of the Red Thumb Mark）——时，曾在这里度过许多愉快的时光；也正是在这里，我邂逅了自己此生的罗曼史，那段故事已在别处讲过。这个地方因对一段幸福往昔的美好回忆而令我倍感亲切，它的种种关联也令人生出对幸福的憧憬——那幸福即将到来，且为期不远。

===Original===
My brisk tattoo on the little brass knocker brought to the door no less a person than Thorndyke himself; and the warmth of his greeting made me at once proud and ashamed. For I had not only been an absentee; I had been a very poor correspondent.

===Chinese===
我在小铜门环上敲出一串轻快的叩击，应声来开门的竟不是别人，正是桑代克本人；他那热情的问候弄得我又是得意又是惭愧。因为我不仅久未登门，连书信也写得实在稀少。

===Original===
“The prodigal has returned, Polton,” he exclaimed, looking into the room. “Here is Dr. Jervis.”

===Chinese===
“浪子回来了，波尔顿（Polton）。”他望着屋里嚷道，“这位就是杰维斯医生（Dr. Jervis）。”

===Original===
I followed him into the room and found Polton﻿—his confidential servant, laboratory assistant, artificer and general “familiar”﻿—setting out the tea-tray on a small table. The little man shook hands cordially with me, and his face crinkled up into the sort of smile that one might expect to see on a benevolent walnut.

===Chinese===
我跟着他走进屋里，只见波尔顿——他是桑代克的心腹仆人、实验室助手、能工巧匠，又兼一位万能的“使魔”（familiar）——正在一张小桌上摆放茶具。这小个子男人热诚地同我握手，那张脸皱出满脸笑纹，活像在一颗慈眉善目的核桃上才见得到的笑容。

===Original===
“We’ve often talked about you, sir,” said he. “The doctor was wondering only yesterday when you were coming back to us.”

===Chinese===
“我们常常谈起您，先生，”他说，“就在昨天，博士还在念叨，您什么时候才回我们这儿来呢。”

===Original===
As I was not “coming back to them” quite in the sense intended I felt a little guilty, but reserved my confidences for Thorndyke’s ear and replied in polite generalities. Then Polton fetched the teapot from the laboratory, made up the fire and departed, and Thorndyke and I subsided, as of old, into our respective armchairs.

===Chinese===
可我并不完全是他们所指望的那种“回我们这儿来”，心里不免有几分愧疚，但我把知心话留着只说给桑代克听，先用客套的泛泛之词应了过去。随后波尔顿从实验室取来茶壶，添好炉火便退了出去，桑代克和我一如当年，各自陷进自己的扶手椅里。

===Original===
“And whence do you spring from in this unexpected fashion?” my colleague asked. “You look as if you had been making professional visits.”

===Chinese===
“你这是打哪儿突然冒出来的？”我的这位同仁问道，“看你这样子，像是刚出完诊。”

===Original===
“I have. The base of operations is in Lower Kennington Lane.”

===Chinese===
“正是。我的大本营就在下肯宁顿巷。”

===Original===
“Ah! Then you are ‘back once more on the old trail’?”

===Chinese===
“啊！这么说，你又‘重回旧路’了？”

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered, with a laugh, “ ‘the old trail, the long trail, the trail that is always new.’ ”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我笑着回答，“‘旧的路，长的路，永远常新的路。’”

===Original===
“And leads nowhere,” Thorndyke added grimly.

===Chinese===
“可惜这条路哪儿也通不到。”桑代克冷冷地补了一句。

===Original===
I laughed again; not very heartily, for there was an uncomfortable element of truth in my friend’s remark, to which my own experience bore only too complete testimony. The medical practitioner whose lack of means forces him to subsist by taking temporary charge of other men’s practices is apt to find that the passing years bring him little but grey hairs and a wealth of disagreeable experience.

===Chinese===
我又笑了，却笑得不太痛快，因为朋友这话里有一种令人难堪的真相，而我自身的经历恰恰为它提供了再充分不过的佐证。一个行医者若因囊中羞涩而不得不靠临时代理他人的诊所糊口，便很容易发现，流逝的年岁带给他的，除了白发和一大堆不痛快的阅历之外，所剩无几。

===Original===
“You will have to drop it, Jervis; you will, indeed,” Thorndyke resumed after a pause. “This casual employment is preposterous for a man of your class and professional attainments. Besides, are you not engaged to be married and to a most charming girl?”

===Chinese===
“这种营生你必须撒手了，杰维斯；真的必须撒手。”桑代克停了片刻又接着说，“打零工式的营生，对你这样的出身和专业造诣的人来说，实在不成体统。再说，你不是已经订了婚，订的还是一位顶顶可爱的姑娘吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, I know. I have been a fool. But I will really amend my ways. If necessary, I will pocket my pride and let Juliet advance the money to buy a practice.”

===Chinese===
“是，我知道。我一直是个傻瓜。可我真的要改弦更张了。必要的话，我就收起自尊，让朱丽叶（Juliet）出钱替我盘一间诊所。”

===Original===
“That,” said Thorndyke, “is a very proper resolution. Pride and reserve between people who are going to be husband and wife, is an absurdity. But why buy a practice? Have you forgotten my proposal?”

===Chinese===
“这就对了，”桑代克说，“是个非常端正的决心。在即将结为夫妇的两个人之间讲自尊、讲矜持，实在是荒唐事。可为什么非要买诊所？你忘了我的提议？”

===Original===
“I should be an ungrateful brute if I had.”

===Chinese===
“我要是忘了，那就成了忘恩负义的畜生了。”

===Original===
“Very well. I repeat it now. Come to me as my junior, read for the Bar and work with me, and, with your abilities, you will have a chance of something like a career. I want you, Jervis,” he added, earnestly. “I must have a junior, with my increasing practice, and you are the junior I want. We are old and tried friends; we have worked together; we like and trust one another, and you are the best man for the job that I know. Come; I am not going to take a refusal. This is an ultimatum.”

===Chinese===
“很好。那我现在再提一遍。来给我当助手，修习律师业务，跟我一起干；以你的才干，你会有机会成就一番像样的事业。我需要你，杰维斯，”他诚挚地补充道，“我的业务日渐增多，必须有个助手，而我要的助手就是你。我们是久经考验的老朋友；我们共事过；我们彼此喜欢、彼此信任，而据我所知，你是干这份差事最合适的人。来吧；我可不打算接受拒绝。这是最后通牒。”

===Original===
“And what is the alternative?” I asked with a smile at his eagerness.

===Chinese===
“那另一条路呢？”我望着他那副急切劲儿，笑着问。

===Original===
“There isn’t any. You are going to say yes.”

===Chinese===
“没有另一条路。你只管点头就是。”

===Original===
“I believe I am,” I answered, not without emotion; “and I am more rejoiced at your offer and more grateful than I can tell you. But we must leave the final arrangements for our next meeting﻿—in a week or so, I hope﻿—for I have to be back in an hour, and I want to consult you on a matter of some importance.”

===Chinese===
“我想我会点头的，”我回答时不能不动感情，“你这一番美意，我的欢喜和感激实在难以言表。不过具体事宜得留到我们下次见面再敲定——我希望一周左右之内——因为我一小时内就得赶回去，而且我还想就一件相当要紧的事向你讨个主意。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Thorndyke; “we will leave the formal agreement for consideration at our next meeting. What is it that you want my opinion on?”

===Chinese===
“也好，”桑代克说，“正式的协议留到下次见面再斟酌。你想让我参谋什么事？”

===Original===
“The fact is,” I said, “I am in a rather awkward dilemma, and I want you to tell me what you think I ought to do.”

===Chinese===
“实情是这样，”我说，“我眼下陷在一个相当为难的窘境里，想请你告诉我，你觉得我该怎么办。”

===Original===
Thorndyke paused in the act of refilling my cup and glanced at me with unmistakable anxiety.

===Chinese===
桑代克给我续茶时停住了手，带着一目了然的焦虑瞥了我一眼。

===Original===
“Nothing of an unpleasant nature, I hope,” said he.

===Chinese===
“但愿不是什么不愉快的事。”他说。

===Original===
“No, no; nothing of that kind,” I answered with a smile as I interpreted the euphemism; for “something unpleasant,” in the case of a young and reasonably presentable medical man is ordinarily the equivalent of trouble with the female of his species. “It is nothing that concerns me personally at all,” I continued; “it is a question of professional responsibility. But I had better give you an account of the affair in a complete narrative, as I know that you like to have your data in a regular and consecutive order.”

===Chinese===
“不，不；不是那类事。”我一面解读这委婉说法，一面笑着回答；因为对一个年轻而且仪表还算体面的医生来说，“不愉快的事”通常就是跟他那个物种的雌性闹出麻烦的同义语。“这事跟我个人毫无关系，”我接着说，“它是个关乎职业责任的问题。不过我最好还是从头到尾、原原本本地给你讲一遍这桩事情的始末，因为我知道，你喜欢把材料按部就班、有条不紊地拿到手。”

===Original===
Thereupon I proceeded to relate the history of my visit to the mysterious Mr. Graves, not omitting any single circumstance or detail that I could recollect.

===Chinese===
于是我便把造访神秘的格雷夫斯先生的经过原原本本讲了一遍，凡是我能想起的情节和细节，一概没有遗漏。

===Original===
Thorndyke listened from the very beginning of my story with the closest attention. His face was the most impassive that I have ever seen; ordinarily as inscrutable as a bronze mask; but to me, who knew him intimately, there was a certain something﻿—a change of colour, perhaps, or an additional sparkle of the eye﻿—that told me when his curious passion for investigation was fully aroused. And now, as I told him of that weird journey and the strange, secret house to which it had brought me, I could see that it offered a problem after his very heart. During the whole of my narration he sat as motionless as a statue, evidently committing the whole story to memory, detail by detail; and even when I had finished he remained for an appreciable time without moving or speaking.

===Chinese===
桑代克从我一开头便全神贯注地听我讲述。他的脸是我平生所见最不动声色的一张，寻常时候像一副青铜面具一样莫测高深；但对深知他脾性的我来说，总有那么一点迹象——也许是脸色的一丝变化，也许是眼里多出的一星光彩——能让我看出，他那份对探究的奇特热情已被完全点燃。此刻，当我向他讲起那段诡异的行程，以及它把我带到的那座蹊跷而隐秘的宅子时，我看得出这桩案子正中他的下怀。在我叙述的全部过程中，他端坐不动，宛如一尊雕像，显然正在把整个故事一点一滴地记进脑子里；甚至在我讲完之后，他还明显地有好一阵子不言不动。

===Original===
At length he looked up at me. “This is a very extraordinary affair, Jervis,” he said.

===Chinese===
末了他抬头看我。“这桩事非同寻常，杰维斯。”他说。

===Original===
“Very,” I agreed; “and the question that is agitating me is, what is to be done?”

===Chinese===
“确实非同寻常，”我附和道，“而搅得我心神不宁的问题是：该怎么办？”

===Original===
“Yes,” he said, meditatively, “that is the question; and an uncommonly difficult question it is. It really involves the settlement of the antecedent question: What is it that is happening at that house?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”他若有所思地说，“问题就在这儿；而且是个少见的难题。它实际上要先解决一个前提问题：那座宅子里正在发生什么？”

===Original===
“What do you think is happening at that house?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“依你看，那座宅子里正在发生什么？”我问。

===Original===
“We must go slow, Jervis,” he replied. “We must carefully separate the legal tissues from the medical, and avoid confusing what we know with what we suspect. Now, with reference to the medical aspects of the case. The first question that confronts us is that of sleeping sickness, or negro-lethargy as it is sometimes called; and here we are in a difficulty. We have not enough knowledge. Neither of us, I take it, has ever seen a case, and the extant descriptions are inadequate. From what I know of the disease, its symptoms agree with those in your case in respect of the alleged moroseness and in the gradually increasing periods of lethargy alternating with periods of apparent recovery. On the other hand, the disease is said to be confined to negroes; but that probably means only that negroes alone have hitherto been exposed to the conditions that produce it. A more important fact is that, as far as I know, extreme contraction of the pupils is not a symptom of sleeping sickness. To sum up, the probabilities are against sleeping sickness, but with our insufficient knowledge, we cannot definitely exclude it.”

===Chinese===
“我们得稳着来，杰维斯，”他答道，“必须仔细把法律层面的问题同医学层面的问题分开，避免把已知的和猜测的搅在一起。先说这桩案子的医学方面。摆在我们面前的第一个问题是昏睡病，也就是有时被称为黑人嗜眠症（negro-lethargy）的那种病；在这一点上我们遇到了难处。我们的知识不够。我想我们俩谁也没亲眼见过一例病患，而现存的种种描述又不充分。就我对这种病的了解，其症状与你那病例相符之处在于：据说病人郁郁寡欢，昏睡的时段逐渐加长，并与表面康复的时段交替出现。另一方面，据说这种病仅见于黑人；但那大概只是说，迄今为止唯有黑人接触到了致病的环境条件。更要紧的一个事实是：据我所知，瞳孔极度收缩并不是昏睡病的症状。总而言之，种种可能性都不利于昏睡病这一假设，但以我们知识的不足，还不能把它断然排除。”

===Original===
“You think that it may really be sleeping sickness?”

===Chinese===
“那么你认为，它可能真是昏睡病？”

===Original===
“No; personally I do not entertain that theory for a moment. But I am considering the evidence apart from our opinions on the subject. We have to accept it as a conceivable hypothesis that it may be sleeping sickness because we cannot positively prove that it is not. That is all. But when we come to the hypothesis of morphine poisoning, the case is different. The symptoms agree with those of morphine poisoning in every respect. There is no exception or disagreement whatever.
