The common sense of the matter is therefore that we adopt morphine poisoning as our working diagnosis; which is what you seem to have done.”

===Chinese===
“不；就我个人而言，我丝毫不曾怀有那种想法。但我此刻是撇开我们对这问题的个人见解、单就证据而论。我们不得不把它可能是昏睡病当作一个可以成立的假设来接受，因为我们无法确切证明它不是。仅此而已。但说到吗啡中毒这个假设，情况就不同了。各种症状与吗啡中毒的症状在每一方面都相吻合，没有任何例外或抵牾之处。因此，按常理办事，我们就该把吗啡中毒当作我们的工作诊断——看样子你也正是这么做的。”

===Original===
“Yes. For purposes of treatment.”

===Chinese===
“是的。为的是对症施治。”

===Original===
“Exactly. For medical purposes you adopted the more probable view and dismissed the less probable. That was the reasonable thing to do. But for legal purposes you must entertain both possibilities; for the hypothesis of poisoning involves serious legal issues, whereas the hypothesis of disease involves no legal issues at all.”

===Chinese===
“正是。出于医疗的目的，你采纳了可能性更大的看法，抛开了可能性较小的。这样做是合乎情理的。但出于法律的目的，你必须把两种可能性都记在心上；因为中毒这一假设牵涉到严重的法律问题，而疾病的假设则完全不牵涉任何法律问题。”

===Original===
“That doesn’t sound very helpful,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“这话听着可不大管用。”我说。

===Original===
“It indicates the necessity for caution,” he retorted.

===Chinese===
“它指出的是必须谨慎。”他反驳道。

===Original===
“Yes, I see that. But what is your own opinion of the case?”

===Chinese===
“是，这我明白。可你自己对这桩案子怎么看？”

===Original===
“Well,” he said, “let us consider the facts in order. Here is a man who, we assume, is under the influence of a poisonous dose of morphine. The question is, did he take that dose himself or was it administered to him by some other person? If he took it himself, with what object did he take it? The history that was given to you seems completely to exclude the idea of suicide. But the patient’s condition seems equally to exclude the idea of morphinomania. Your opium-eater does not reduce himself to a state of coma. He usually keeps well within the limits of the tolerance that has been established. The conclusion that emerges is, I think, that the drug was administered by some other person; and the most likely person seems to be Mr. Weiss.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”他说，“让我们按顺序来考察事实。眼前这个人，我们假定他正处在中毒剂量吗啡的作用之下。问题是：这一剂是他自己服下的，还是别人给他服下的？倘若是他自己服下的，他服药的目的何在？别人对你讲的病史似乎完全排除了自杀的可能。但病人的状况似乎也同样排除了吗啡瘾的可能。抽鸦片的瘾君子并不会把自己弄到昏迷不醒的地步，他通常让自己稳稳保持在业已确立的耐受范围之内。我认为，由此浮现的结论是：药物是别人给他服下的；而最有嫌疑的人，看来就是韦斯先生。”

===Original===
“Isn’t morphine a very unusual poison?”

===Chinese===
“吗啡不是一种很不寻常的毒药吗？”

===Original===
“Very; and most inconvenient except in a single, fatal dose, by reason of the rapidity with which tolerance of the drug is established. But we must not forget that slow morphine poisoning might be eminently suitable in certain cases. The manner in which it enfeebles the will, confuses the judgment and debilitates the body might make it very useful to a poisoner whose aim was to get some instrument or document executed, such as a will, deed or assignment. And death could be produced afterwards by other means. You see the important bearing of this?”

===Chinese===
“很不寻常；而且除了一次性服下致死剂量之外，它用起来极不方便，因为人对这药的耐受建立得极快。但我们不可忘记，缓慢的吗啡中毒在某些情况下可能再合适不过。它能削弱意志、搅乱判断、损耗身体，这种种手段，对一个图谋诱使某人签署某种契据或文书——比如遗嘱、契约或转让书——的下毒者来说，可能大有用处。事后再用别的手段致人死命也来得及。你明白这话的重大含义吗？”

===Original===
“You mean in respect of a death certificate?”

===Chinese===
“你是说，牵涉到死亡证明书？”

===Original===
“Yes. Suppose Mr. Weiss to have given a large dose of morphine. He then sends for you and throws out a suggestion of sleeping sickness. If you accept the suggestion he is pretty safe. He can repeat the process until he kills his victim and then get a certificate from you which will cover the murder. It was quite an ingenious scheme﻿—which, by the way, is characteristic of intricate crimes; your subtle criminal often plans his crime like a genius, but he generally executes it like a fool﻿—as this man seems to have done, if we are not doing him an injustice.”

===Chinese===
“正是。假定韦斯先生下了大剂量的吗啡，然后把你请去，抛出昏睡病的暗示。你若接受了这个暗示，他就相当安全了。他可以如法炮制，直到弄死他的牺牲品，然后从你手里拿到一纸足以遮掩这场谋杀的证明书。这实在是一条巧计——顺便说一句，错综复杂的罪案往往有这个特点：你那工于心计的罪犯，筹划罪行时往往像个天才，执行起来却多半像个蠢材——眼前这位似乎正是如此，倘若我们没有冤枉他的话。”

===Original===
“How has he acted like a fool?”

===Chinese===
“他怎么就办得像蠢材了？”

===Original===
“In several respects. In the first place, he should have chosen his doctor. A good, brisk, confident man who ‘knows his own mind’ is the sort of person who would have suited him; a man who would have jumped at a diagnosis and stuck to it; or else an ignorant weakling of alcoholic tendencies. It was shockingly bad luck to run against a cautious scientific practitioner like my learned friend. Then, of course, all this secrecy was sheer tomfoolery, exactly calculated to put a careful man on his guard; as it has actually done. If Mr. Weiss is really a criminal, he has mismanaged his affairs badly.”

===Chinese===
“在好几个方面。首先，他本该挑一挑他的医生。一个干练爽快、自信心强、‘心里有主见’的人，才是合他用的那种角色；那种一碰上诊断便一口咬定、死守不放的人；再不就该找个无知的窝囊废，最好还有几分嗜酒的倾向。撞上我这位博学的朋友这样一位审慎而讲科学的行医者，实在是他倒霉透顶。再者，这一套遮遮掩掩纯属蠢不可及的昏招，恰恰足以让一个细心的人起了戒心；事实上也确实起了。韦斯先生倘若真是个罪犯，那他把事情办得一塌糊涂。”

===Original===
“And you apparently think that he is a criminal?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你显然认为他是个罪犯了？”

===Original===
“I suspect him deeply. But I should like to ask you one or two questions about him. You say he spoke with a German accent. What command of English had he? Was his vocabulary good? Did he use any German idioms?”

===Chinese===
“我对他疑心极重。不过我想就他本人问你一两个问题。你说他说话带德国口音。他的英语功底如何？词汇丰富吗？用过什么德语式的说法没有？”

===Original===
“No. I should say that his English was perfect, and I noticed that his phrases were quite well chosen even for an Englishman.”

===Chinese===
“没有。要我说，他的英语无懈可击，而且我注意到，他的措辞即便按英国人的标准也相当讲究。”

===Original===
“Did he seem to you ‘made up’ in any way; disguised, I mean?”

===Chinese===
“在你看来，他有没有什么地方像是‘化过装’的？我是说，有没有乔装改扮？”

===Original===
“I couldn’t say. The light was so very feeble.”

===Chinese===
“说不上来。当时的光线实在太暗了。”

===Original===
“You couldn’t see the colour of his eyes, for instance?”

===Chinese===
“比如说，他眼睛的颜色你没看清？”

===Original===
“No. I think they were grey, but I couldn’t be sure.”

===Chinese===
“没有。我记得像是灰色的，但不敢肯定。”

===Original===
“And as to the coachman. He wore a wig, you said. Could you see the colour of his eyes? Or any peculiarity by which you could recognize him?”

===Chinese===
“再说那个车夫。你说过他戴假发。他的眼睛颜色你看清了吗？或者有什么特征，能让你认出他来？”

===Original===
“He had a malformed thumbnail on his right hand. That is all I can say about him.”

===Chinese===
“他右手的大拇指指甲是畸形的。关于他，我能说的就这些。”

===Original===
“He didn’t strike you as resembling Weiss in any way; in voice or features?”

===Chinese===
“你没觉得他同韦斯有任何相像之处吗——嗓音或相貌上？”

===Original===
“Not at all; and he spoke, as I told you, with a distinct Scotch accent.”

===Chinese===
“毫无相像之处；而且正如我告诉过你的，他说话带明显的苏格兰口音。”

===Original===
“The reason I ask is that if Weiss is attempting to poison this man, the coachman is almost certain to be a confederate and might be a relative. You had better examine him closely if you get another chance.”

===Chinese===
“我问这个的缘故是：如果韦斯正在图谋毒害这个人，那车夫十有八九是同伙，说不定还是亲戚。你若再有机会，最好把他细细察看一番。”

===Original===
“I will. And that brings me back to the question, what am I to do? Ought I to report the case to the police?”

===Chinese===
“我会的。这就又回到我那个问题了：我该怎么办？我该不该把这事报告警察？”

===Original===
“I am inclined to think not. You have hardly enough facts. Of course, if Mr. Weiss has administered poison ‘unlawfully and maliciously’ he has committed a felony, and is liable under the Consolidation Acts of 1861 to ten years’ penal servitude. But I do not see how you could swear an information. You don’t know that he administered the poison﻿—if poison has really been administered﻿—and you cannot give any reliable name or any address whatever. Then there is the question of sleeping sickness. You reject it for medical purposes, but you could not swear, in a court of law, that this is not a case of sleeping sickness.”

===Chinese===
“我倾向于认为不必。你手头的事实还不够。当然，倘若韦斯先生‘不法且恶意地’下了毒，他就犯了重罪，依照1861年的《整合法令》（Consolidation Acts）可判处十年苦役。可我看不出你怎么能宣誓报案。你并不知道是他下的毒——倘若毒确实已经下过——而且你提供不出任何可靠的姓名，也提供不出任何地址。此外还有昏睡病的问题。出于医疗目的你否定了它，可你不能在法庭上宣誓作证，说这绝不是一例昏睡病。”

===Original===
“No,” I admitted, “I could not.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”我承认道，“我不能。”

===Original===
“Then I think the police would decline to move in the matter, and you might find that you had raised a scandal in Dr. Stillbury’s practice to no purpose.”

===Chinese===
“那样的话，我想警方会拒绝插手此事，而你可能发现自己闹出了一场玷污斯蒂尔伯里医生诊所名声的丑闻，却一无所获。”

===Original===
“So you think I had better do nothing in the matter?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你认为我最好对此事袖手旁观？”

===Original===
“For the present. It is, of course, a medical man’s duty to assist justice in any way that is possible. But a doctor is not a detective; he should not go out of his way to assume police functions. He should keep his eyes and ears open, and, though, in general, he should keep his own counsel, it is his duty to note very carefully anything that seems to him likely to bear on any important legal issues. It is not his business officiously to initiate criminal inquiries, but it is emphatically his business to be ready, if called upon, to assist justice with information that his special knowledge and opportunities have rendered accessible to him. You see the bearing of this?”

===Chinese===
“眼下是这样。当然，竭尽所能协助司法，是行医者的本分。但医生不是侦探；他不该越俎代庖去行使警察的职能。他应该眼观六路、耳听八方，而且，尽管一般来说应当守口如瓶，但凡在他看来可能牵涉重大法律问题的事情，他都有责任仔细记下。多管闲事地去发起刑事调查，不是他的分内事；但随时准备好在受到征召时，以他的专业知识和便利条件所能获取的情报来协助司法，则千真万确是他的分内事。你明白这话的意思吗？”

===Original===
“You mean that I should note down what I have seen and heard and say nothing about it until I am asked.”

===Chinese===
“你的意思是，我把所见所闻记下来，在别人问起之前一声不吭。”

===Original===
“Yes; if nothing further happens. But if you should be sent for again, I think it is your duty to make further observations with a view, if necessary, to informing the police. It may be, for instance, of vital importance to identify the house, and it is your duty to secure the means of doing so.”

===Chinese===
“正是；前提是不再发生别的事。但倘若你再度被请去，我认为你就有责任作进一步的观察，以便在必要时通报警方。比方说，查明那座宅子的位置可能至关重要，而你就有责任掌握查明它的手段。”

===Original===
“But, my dear Thorndyke,” I expostulated, “I have told you how I was conveyed to the house. Now, will you kindly explain to me how a man, boxed up in a pitch-dark carriage, is going to identify any place to which he may be carried?”

===Chinese===
“可是，亲爱的桑代克，”我不服气道，“我已经告诉过你我是怎么被送到那座宅子去的。那么，可否请你指点一二：一个人被封在一辆漆黑一团的马车里，如何认出自己被拉到的任何地方？”

===Original===
“The problem doesn’t appear to me to present any serious difficulties,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“在我看，这个问题并不存在什么了不得的困难。”他答道。

===Original===
“Doesn’t it?” said I. “To me it looks like a pretty solid impossibility. But what do you suggest? Should I break out of the house and run away up the street? Or should I bore a hole through the shutter of the carriage and peep out?”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”我说，“在我看来，这几乎是一桩铁板钉钉的不可能。可你有什么高见？要我冲出宅子、沿街狂奔吗？还是要在马车的百叶窗上钻个孔往外偷看？”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled indulgently. “The methods proposed by my learned friend display a certain crudity inappropriate to the character of a man of science; to say nothing of the disadvantage of letting the enemy into our counsels. No, no, Jervis; we can do something better than that. Just excuse me for a minute while I run up to the laboratory.”

===Chinese===
桑代克宽容地微微一笑。“我这位博学的朋友提出的办法，未免失之粗陋，与科学人士的身份不相称；更不必说让敌方探知我方计议的坏处了。不，不，杰维斯；我们能想出比那高明的招数。失陪片刻，我上楼去趟实验室。”

===Original===
He hurried away to Polton’s sanctum on the upper floor, leaving me to speculate on the method by which he proposed that a man should be enabled, as Sam Weller would express it, “to see through a flight of stairs and a deal door”; or, what was equally opaque, the wooden shutters of a closed carriage.

===Chinese===
他匆匆上楼，到波尔顿的那间“圣所”去了，留下我一个人琢磨：照他的设计，一个人怎样才能——用山姆·维勒（Sam Weller）的话说——“透过一段楼梯和一扇杉木门看东西”；或者说，怎样看透同样不透明的、紧闭马车的木制百叶窗。

===Original===
“Now,” he said, when he returned a couple of minutes later with a small, paper-covered notebook in his hand, “I have set Polton to work on a little appliance that will, I think, solve our difficulty, and I will show you how I propose that you should make your observations. First of all, we have to rule the pages of this book into columns.”

===Chinese===
“好了，”约莫两分钟后他回来了，手里拿着一本纸面的小笔记本，“我已经派波尔顿去赶制一件小器具，我想它能解决我们的难题；我再给你演示一下，我打算让你如何进行观察。首先，我们得在这本子的页面上画上栏线。”

===Original===
He sat down at the table and began methodically to rule the pages each into three columns, two quite narrow and one broad. The process occupied some time, during which I sat and watched with impatient curiosity the unhurried, precise movements of Thorndyke’s pencil, all agog to hear the promised explanation. He was just finishing the last page when there came a gentle tap at the door, and Polton entered with a satisfied smile on his dry, shrewd-looking face and a small board in his hand.

===Chinese===
他在桌边坐下，有条不紊地动手画线，把每页都分成三栏，两栏极窄，一栏宽。这道工序费了些时间，其间我坐在一旁，急不可耐又满心好奇地望着桑代克那支铅笔从容而精确的移动，一心巴望着听他兑现许诺的讲解。他刚画完最后一页，门上响起轻轻一叩，波尔顿走了进来，那张干瘦精明的脸上挂着满意的微笑，手里拿着一块小板子。

===Original===
“Will this do, sir?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“这个行吗，先生？”他问。

===Original===
As he spoke he handed the little board to Thorndyke, who looked at it and passed it to me.

===Chinese===
他一边说，一边把那块小板子递给桑代克；桑代克看了看，又递给了我。

===Original===
“The very thing, Polton,” my friend replied. “Where did you find it? It’s of no use for you to pretend that you’ve made it in about two minutes and a half.”

===Chinese===
“正是这件，波尔顿，”我的朋友答道，“你从哪儿找来的？你用不着装模作样，说自己是在大约两分半钟里做出来的。”

===Original===
Polton smiled one of his queer crinkly smiles, and remarking that “it didn’t take much making,” departed much gratified by the compliment.

===Chinese===
波尔顿咧嘴一笑，又是他那种古怪的、皱纹堆起的笑，说了句“本来也没什么可做的”，便领受着这句夸奖，心满意足地退了出去。

===Original===
“What a wonderful old fellow that is, Jervis,” Thorndyke observed as his factotum retired. “He took in the idea instantly and seems to have produced the finished article by magic, as the conjurers bring forth rabbits and bowls of goldfish at a moment’s notice. I suppose you see what your modus operandi is to be?”

===Chinese===
“这老头儿真是个妙人，杰维斯，”桑代克望着他这位万能听差退出去，说道，“他一点就通，成品仿佛是靠魔法变出来的，就像魔术师一招呼就掏出兔子和一盆盆金鱼一样。我想，你已经看得出你的操作法（modus operandi）是什么了吧？”

===Original===
I had gathered a clue from the little appliance﻿—a plate of white fret-wood about seven inches by five, to one corner of which a pocket-compass had been fixed with shellac﻿—but was not quite clear as to the details of the method.

===Chinese===
我从那件小器具上已经摸到一点线索——那是一块约七英寸乘五英寸的白色细工薄木板，一角用虫胶粘着一只袖珍罗盘——但对这套方法的细节还不十分了了。

===Original===
“You can read a compass pretty quickly, I think?” Thorndyke said.

===Chinese===
“罗盘上的方位，你读起来应该相当利索吧？”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Of course I can. Used we not to sail a yacht together when we were students?”

===Chinese===
“那还用说。我们当学生时不还一起驾过帆船吗？”

===Original===
“To be sure we did; and we will again before we die. And now as to your method of locating this house. Here is a pocket reading-lamp which you can hook on the carriage lining. This notebook can be fixed to the board with an india-rubber band﻿—thus. You observe that the thoughtful Polton has stuck a piece of thread on the glass of the compass to serve as a lubber’s line. This is how you will proceed. As soon as you are locked in the carriage, light your lamp﻿—better have a book with you in case the light is noticed﻿—take out your watch and put the board on your knee, keeping its long side exactly in a line with the axis of the carriage. Then enter in one narrow column of your notebook the time, in the other the direction shown by the compass, and in the broad column any particulars, including the number of steps the horse makes in a minute. Like this.”

===Chinese===
“当然是驾过的；趁我们还活着，往后还要再驾。现在说给你听，你怎样定位这座宅子。这儿是一盏随身阅读灯，可以挂在马车的衬里上。这本笔记本可以用一根橡皮筋固定在板子上——像这样。你注意到没有，心思周密的波尔顿在罗盘的玻璃面上贴了一段细线，权作基准线（lubber’s line）。你的操作步骤如下：一被锁进马车，就点亮灯——最好随身带本书，以防灯光被人察觉——掏出表，把板子放在膝上，让板的长边同马车的纵轴恰好齐成一线。然后在笔记本的一窄栏里记下时刻，另一窄栏记罗盘所示的方位，宽栏里记各种详情，包括马每分钟迈的步数。像这样。”

===Original===
He took a loose sheet of paper and made one or two sample entries on it in pencil, thus﻿—

===Chinese===
他抽出一张活页纸，用铅笔在上面做了两笔示范记录，如下——

===Original===
and so on. Note every change of direction, with the time; and whenever you hear or feel anything from outside, note it, with the time and direction; and don’t forget to note any variations in the horse’s pace. You follow the process?”

===Chinese===
如此等等。每逢方向改变，连同时刻一并记下；每逢你从车外听到或感觉到什么动静，也连同时刻和方位一并记下；还有马匹步速的任何变化，别忘了记下来。这套流程你明白了吗？

===Original===
“Perfectly. But do you think the method is accurate enough to fix the position of a house? Remember, this is only a pocket-compass with no dial, and it will jump frightfully. And the mode of estimating distance is very rough.”

===Chinese===
“完全明白。可你认为这方法精确得足以定下一座宅子的位置吗？别忘了，这不过是只没有刻度盘的袖珍罗盘，指针会跳得厉害。何况估算距离的方式也非常粗糙。”

===Original===
“That is all perfectly true,” Thorndyke answered. “But you are overlooking certain important facts. The track-chart that you will produce can be checked by other data. The house, for instance, has a covered way by which you could identify it if you knew approximately where to look for it. Then you must remember that your carriage is not travelling over a featureless plain. It is passing through streets which have a determined position and direction and which are accurately represented on the ordnance map. I think, Jervis, that, in spite of the apparent roughness of the method, if you make your observations carefully, we shall have no trouble in narrowing down the inquiry to a quite small area. If we get the chance, that is to say.”

===Chinese===
“这些都说得完全对，”桑代克答道，“可你忽略了几个重要的事实。你将绘制出的那张轨迹图（track-chart），可以用别的数据来核验。比如那座宅子有一条带顶的廊道，只要你大致知道往哪儿找，凭它就能认出宅子来。还有，你必须记住，你的马车并不是行进在一片毫无特征的原野上。它穿行的街道各有确定的位置和走向，而且都精确地绘在陆军测量局的地图上。杰维斯，我想，尽管这方法看着粗糙，只要你观察得仔细，我们不难把排查范围缩小到很小的一块地域。当然，前提是我们能得到这个机会。”

===Original===
“Yes, if we do. I am doubtful whether Mr. Weiss will require my services again, but I sincerely hope he will. It would be rare sport to locate his secret burrow, all unsuspected. But now I must really be off.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，就看有没有这个机会了。韦斯先生会不会再请我出诊，我心里没底，可我衷心希望他会。能神不知鬼不觉地摸清他那座秘密巢穴的所在，将是难得的乐事。不过现在我实在该走了。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, then,” said Thorndyke, slipping a well-sharpened pencil through the rubber band that fixed the notebook to the board. “Let me know how the adventure progresses﻿—if it progresses at all﻿—and remember, I hold your promise to come and see me again quite soon in any case.”

===Chinese===
“那么，再会，”桑代克说着，把一支削得尖尖的铅笔插进固定本子和板子的橡皮筋底下。“这桩冒险有何进展——倘若真有进展——随时告诉我；还有，别忘了，你答应过我，无论如何很快就再来看我。”

===Original===
He handed me the board and the lamp, and, when I had slipped them into my pocket, we shook hands and I hurried away, a little uneasy at having left my charge so long.

===Chinese===
他把板子和灯递给我，等我把它们塞进衣袋，我们握了握手，我便匆匆走了，心里因为把自己代管的诊所撂下这么久，多少有些不安。

## XV Thorndyke Explodes the Mine / 桑代克引爆地雷
===Original===
We had not been back in our chambers more than a few minutes when the little brass knocker on the inner door rattled out its summons. Thorndyke himself opened the door, and, finding our three expected visitors on the threshold, he admitted them and closed the “oak.”

===Chinese===
我们回到寓所还没待上几分钟，内门上那只小铜门环便嗒嗒地叩响起来传报来客。桑代克亲自去开了门，见我们等候的三位客人果然站在门槛上，便把他们让了进来，随手关上了“橡木门”。

===Original===
“We have accepted your invitation, you see,” said Marchmont, whose manner was now a little flurried and uneasy. “This is my partner, Mr. Winwood; you haven’t met before, I think. Well, we thought we should like to hear some further particulars from you, as we could not quite understand your letter.”

===Chinese===
“您瞧，我们接受您的邀请来了，”马奇蒙特说，他此刻的神气已有些慌乱不安。“这位是我的合伙人温伍德先生；我想你们两位从前没有见过。是这样，您的信我们实在不甚明白，想来再听听进一步的详情。”

===Original===
“My conclusion, I suppose,” said Thorndyke, “was a little unexpected?”

===Chinese===
“我的结论，”桑代克说，“想必有点出人意料吧？”

===Original===
“It was more than that, sir,” exclaimed Winwood. “It was absolutely irreconcilable either with the facts of the case or with common physical possibilities.”

===Chinese===
“何止出人意料，先生，”温伍德嚷道，“它简直同本案的事实绝不相容，同寻常的物理可能性也绝不相容。”

===Original===
“At the first glance,” Thorndyke agreed, “it would probably have that appearance.”

===Chinese===
“乍一看去，”桑代克承认道，“它多半确实像是那副样子。”

===Original===
“It has that appearance still to me,” said Winwood, growing suddenly red and wrathful, “and I may say that I speak as a solicitor who was practising in the law when you were an infant in arms. You tell us, sir, that this will is a forgery; this will, which was executed in broad daylight in the presence of two unimpeachable witnesses who have sworn, not only to their signatures and the contents of the document, but to their very fingermarks on the paper. Are those fingermarks forgeries, too? Have you examined and tested them?”

===Chinese===
“在我看来到如今它还是那副样子，”温伍德说，脸陡然涨红，怒气冲冲，“而且我可以说，我这是以一个当您还在襁褓之中便已执业办案的事务律师的身份在说话。您竟对我们说，先生，这份遗嘱是伪造的；这份遗嘱，是光天化日之下当着两位无可指摘的证人订立的，他们不但宣誓证明自己的签名和文件的内容，连他们按在纸上的指印也都宣誓证明了。莫非那些指印也是伪造的不成？您可曾检验过它们、考验过它们？”

===Original===
“I have not,” replied Thorndyke. “The fact is they are of no interest to me, as I am not disputing the witnesses’ signatures.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”桑代克答道，“事实上它们引不起我的兴趣，因为我并不争议证人的签名。”

===Original===
At this, Mr. Winwood fairly danced with irritation.

===Chinese===
听了这话，温伍德先生简直气得跳脚。

===Original===
“Marchmont!” he exclaimed fiercely, “you know this good gentleman, I believe. Tell me, is he addicted to practical jokes?”

===Chinese===
“马奇蒙特！”他恶狠狠地喊道，“这位好先生你是认识的吧。你告诉我，他是不是有恶作剧的癖好？”

===Original===
“Now, my dear Winwood,” groaned Marchmont, “I pray you﻿—I beg you to control yourself. No doubt﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哎，亲爱的温伍德，”马奇蒙特哼哼唧唧地说，“我求你了——求你克制着点。毫无疑问——”

===Original===
“But confound it!” roared Winwood, “you have, yourself, heard him say that the will is a forgery, but that he doesn’t dispute the signatures; which,” concluded Winwood, banging his fist down on the table, “is damned nonsense.”

===Chinese===
“可是真见鬼！”温伍德吼道，“你自己亲耳听见他说，这遗嘱是伪造的，可他又不争议那些签名；这，”温伍德一拳砸在桌上，就此下结论道，“简直是天杀的胡说。”

===Original===
“May I suggest,” interposed Stephen Blackmore, “that we came here to receive Dr. Thorndyke’s explanation of his letter. Perhaps it would be better to postpone any comments until we have heard it.”

===Chinese===
“我可否提议，”斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔插话道，“我们到这儿来，是为听桑代克博士解释他那封信的。或许等听完之后再发议论也不迟。”

===Original===
“Undoubtedly, undoubtedly,” said Marchmont. “Let me entreat you, Winwood, to listen patiently and refrain from interruption until we have heard our learned friend’s exposition of the case.”

===Chinese===
“正是，正是，”马奇蒙特说，“让我恳求你，温伍德，耐心听着，且莫打岔，等听完了我们这位博学的朋友对案情的阐发再说。”

===Original===
“Oh, very well,” Winwood replied sulkily; “I’ll say no more.”

===Chinese===
“哦，很好，”温伍德绷着脸答道，“我不再言语就是。”

===Original===
He sank into a chair with the manner of a man who shuts himself up and turns the key; and so remained﻿—excepting when the internal pressure approached bursting-point﻿—throughout the subsequent proceedings, silent, stony and impassive, like a seated statue of Obstinacy.

===Chinese===
他一屁股沉进椅子里，那架势像个把自己关起来又拧上钥匙的人；此后在整个过程中他便一直这样——除非内压逼近爆点的时候——沉默、冷硬、无动于衷，活像一尊端坐着的“顽固”雕像。

===Original===
“I take it,” said Marchmont, “that you have some new facts that are not in our possession?”

===Chinese===
“我揣度，”马奇蒙特说，“您掌握了一些我们不曾掌握的新事实？”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Thorndyke; “we have some new facts, and we have made some new use of the old ones. But how shall I lay the case before you? Shall I state my theory of the sequence of events and furnish the verification afterwards? Or shall I retrace the actual course of my investigations and give you the facts in the order in which I obtained them myself, with the inferences from them?”

===Chinese===
“是的，”桑代克答道，“我们有一些新事实，对那些旧事实也做了些新的运用。可是我该怎样把这案子摆到诸位面前呢？是我先陈述我关于事件先后次序的理论，随后再提供验证呢？还是我循着调查的实际历程回溯，按我亲自取得这些事实的先后，连同从它们推出的论断，一一讲给你们听呢？”

===Original===
“I almost think,” said Mr. Marchmont, “that it would be better if you would put us in possession of the new facts. Then, if the conclusions that follow from them are not sufficiently obvious, we could hear the argument. What do you say, Winwood?”

===Chinese===
“我倒是几乎认为，”马奇蒙特先生说，“不如请您先把新事实交给我们。这样，倘若由它们推出的结论还不够明白，我们再听论证不迟。你说呢，温伍德？”

===Original===
Mr. Winwood roused himself for an instant, barked out the one word “Facts,” and shut himself up again with a snap.

===Chinese===
温伍德先生精神一振，狗叫似的迸出一个词“事实”，随即啪的一声又把自己关上了。

===Original===
“You would like to have the new facts by themselves?” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“你们想先单听新事实？”桑代克说。

===Original===
“If you please. The facts only, in the first place, at any rate.”

===Chinese===
“劳驾。至少头一步只要事实。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Thorndyke; and here I caught his eye with a mischievous twinkle in it that I understood perfectly; for I had most of the facts myself and realized how much these two lawyers were likely to extract from them. Winwood was going to “have a run for his money,” as Thorndyke had promised.

===Chinese===
“好吧，”桑代克说；这时我瞥见他的眼睛里闪着一丝促狭的光，那光的意思我完全懂得；因为这些事实我自己大都也掌握，明白这两位律师单凭它们能引申出多少东西来。温伍德这就要“卖足力气、值回票价”了，正如桑代克先前许诺的那样。

===Original===
My colleague, having placed on the table by his side a small cardboard box and the sheets of notes from his file, glanced quickly at Mr. Winwood and began:

===Chinese===
我的同事在身旁的桌上放好一只小纸板盒和从文件夹里抽出的一叠笔记，朝温伍德先生飞快地瞥了一眼，开口说道：

===Original===
“The first important new facts came into my possession on the day on which you introduced the case to me. In the evening, after you left, I availed myself of Mr. Stephen’s kind invitation to look over his uncle’s chambers in New Inn. I wished to do so in order to ascertain, if possible, what had been the habits of the deceased during his residence there. When I arrived with Dr. Jervis, Mr. Stephen was in the chambers, and I learned from him that his uncle was an Oriental scholar of some position and that he had a very thorough acquaintance with the cuneiform writing. Now, while I was talking with Mr. Stephen I made a very curious discovery. On the wall over the fireplace hung a large framed photograph of an ancient Persian inscription in the cuneiform character; and that photograph was upside down.”

===Chinese===
“头一批重要的新事实，是你们把这案子介绍给我的那一天落到我手里的。当天晚上，你们走后，我承斯蒂芬先生盛情相邀，去看了他叔父在新客栈的寓所。我所以想去，是为了要弄清楚——如果做得到的话——死者住在那儿的期间都有哪些习惯。我同杰维斯医生到达时，斯蒂芬先生正在寓所里，我从他那里得知，他叔父是一位颇有地位的东方学者，对楔形文字造诣极深。就在我同斯蒂芬先生谈话的当口，我有了一个非常奇怪的发现。壁炉上方的墙上挂着一大幅装框的照片，拍的是一篇古代波斯铭文，用的楔形文字；而那幅照片竟倒挂着。”

===Original===
“Upside down!” exclaimed Stephen. “But that is really very odd.”

===Chinese===
“倒挂着！”斯蒂芬叫道，“可这真是怪极了。”

===Original===
“Very odd indeed,” agreed Thorndyke, “and very suggestive. The way in which it came to be inverted is pretty obvious and also rather suggestive. The photograph had evidently been in the frame some years but had apparently never been hung up before.”

===Chinese===
“确实怪极了，”桑代克表示同意，“而且非常耐人寻味。它是怎么倒过来的，缘由相当明显，也同样耐人寻味。那张照片装进框子显然已有好些年头，但先前似乎从未挂起来过。”

===Original===
“It had not,” said Stephen, “though I don’t know how you arrived at the fact. It used to stand on the mantelpiece in his old rooms in Jermyn Street.”

===Chinese===
“确实没有，”斯蒂芬说，“虽然我不明白您这个事实是怎么推出来的。它从前一向摆在他杰明街旧居的壁炉台上。”

===Original===
“Well,” continued Thorndyke, “the frame-maker had pasted his label on the back of the frame, and as this label hung the right way up, it appeared as if the person who fixed the photograph on the wall had adopted it as a guide.”

===Chinese===
“是这样，”桑代克接着说，“制框匠把他的标签贴在了框子背面，而这标签挂起来恰好头朝上，看那情形，仿佛把照片钉上墙的人正是拿它当了正倒的凭据。”

===Original===
“It is very extraordinary,” said Stephen. “I should have thought the person who hung it would have asked Uncle Jeffrey which was the right way up; and I can’t imagine how on earth it could have hung all those months without his noticing it. He must have been practically blind.”

===Chinese===
“这真是太蹊跷了，”斯蒂芬说，“我原以为挂画的人总该问一声杰弗里叔叔哪头朝上；而且我实在无法想象，它挂了那么多个月，他怎么会始终没有察觉。他必定差不多是个瞎子了。”

===Original===
Here Marchmont, who had been thinking hard, with knitted brows, suddenly brightened up.

===Chinese===
这时，一直在蹙眉苦思的马奇蒙特忽然脸上放光。

===Original===
“I see your point,” said he. “You mean that if Jeffrey was as blind as that, it would have been possible for some person to substitute a false will, which he might sign without noticing the substitution.”

===Chinese===
“我明白您的用意了，”他说，“您是说，倘若杰弗里瞎到了那个地步，就有人可以把一份假遗嘱掉包换上去，他签的时候也察觉不出被人换了。”

===Original===
“That wouldn’t make the will a forgery,” growled Winwood. “If Jeffrey signed it, it was Jeffrey’s will. You could contest it if you could prove the fraud. But he said: ‘This is my will,’ and the two witnesses read it and have identified it.”

===Chinese===
“那可算不得伪造，”温伍德咕哝道，“只要杰弗里签了字，那就是杰弗里的遗嘱。你们若能证明那欺诈，尽可以去争议这份遗嘱。可他说过：‘这是我的遗嘱’，两位证人也读过它，而且都指认无误。”

===Original===
“Did they read it aloud?” asked Stephen.

===Chinese===
“他们是大声读出来的吗？”斯蒂芬问。

===Original===
“No, they did not,” replied Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“不，没有，”桑代克答道。

===Original===
“Can you prove substitution?” asked Marchmont.

===Chinese===
“您能证明掉包吗？”马奇蒙特问。

===Original===
“I haven’t asserted it,” answered Thorndyke. “My position is that the will is a forgery.”

===Chinese===
“我并没有主张掉包，”桑代克答道，“我的立场是：这份遗嘱是伪造的。”

===Original===
“But it is not,” said Winwood.

===Chinese===
“可它不是，”温伍德说。

===Original===
“We won’t argue it now,” said Thorndyke. “I ask you to note the fact that the inscription was upside down. I also observed on the walls of the chambers some valuable Japanese colour-prints on which were recent damp-spots. I noted that the sitting-room had a gas-stove and that the kitchen contained practically no stores or remains of food and hardly any traces of even the simplest cooking. In the bedroom I found a large box that had contained a considerable stock of hard stearine candles, six to the pound, and that was now nearly empty. I examined the clothing of the deceased. On the soles of the boots I observed dried mud, which was unlike that on my own and Jervis’s boots, from the gravelly square of the inn. I noted a crease on each leg of the deceased man’s trousers as if they had been turned up halfway to the knee; and in the waistcoat pocket I found the stump of a ‘Contango’ pencil. On the floor of the bedroom, I found a portion of an oval glass somewhat like that of a watch or locket, but ground at the edge to a double bevel. Dr. Jervis and I also found one or two beads and a bugle, all of dark brown glass.”

===Chinese===
“这件事我们现在不必争，”桑代克说，“我只请诸位记下一个事实：那铭文是倒挂的。我还注意到，寓所墙上挂着几幅贵重的日本彩印版画，上面有着新近的水渍。我注意到起居室里用的是煤气炉，而厨房里几乎没有存粮，没有剩食，连最简单的炊事的痕迹也几乎没有。卧室里我找到一只大盒子，原装有相当一批一磅六支的硬脂蜡烛，如今已近乎空了。我检验了死者的衣物。靴底上我观察到干泥，同我和杰维斯靴子上的不一样——那是从客栈铺砂砾的院子里沾来的。我注意到死者裤子的两条裤腿上各有一道折痕，仿佛曾半卷到膝盖；马甲口袋里我摸到一截‘康坦戈’（Contango）铅笔头。卧室的地板上，我找到一小块椭圆形玻璃，有点像表玻璃或小盒坠上的玻璃，只是边缘磨出了双斜边。杰维斯医生和我还找到一两颗小珠子和一粒管珠，都是深棕色玻璃的。”

===Original===
Here Thorndyke paused, and Marchmont, who had been gazing at him with growing amazement, said nervously:

===Chinese===
说到这里桑代克停了下来，一直越听越惊愕地瞪着他的马奇蒙特局促地说：

===Original===
“Er﻿—yes. Very interesting. These observations of yours﻿—er﻿—are﻿—”

===Chinese===
“呃——是的。很有意思。您的这些观察——呃——是——”

===Original===
“Are all the observations that I made at New Inn.”

===Chinese===
“就是我在新客栈所做的全部观察。”

===Original===
The two lawyers looked at one another and Stephen Blackmore stared fixedly at a spot on the hearthrug. Then Mr. Winwood’s face contorted itself into a sour, lopsided smile.

===Chinese===
两位律师面面相觑，斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔死死盯着炉前地毯上的一小块地方。随后温伍德先生的脸扭出一个酸溜溜的、歪到一边的笑。

===Original===
“You might have observed a good many other things, sir,” said he, “if you had looked. If you had examined the doors, you would have noted that they had hinges and were covered with paint; and, if you had looked up the chimney you might have noted that it was black inside.”

===Chinese===
“您本可以观察到许许多多别的东西的，先生，”他说，“只要您肯看。您要是查看过那些门，准会发现它们装着铰链，还涂着漆；您要是往烟囱里张望过，说不定还会发现它里头是黑的。”

===Original===
“Now, now, Winwood,” protested Marchmont in an agony of uneasiness as to what his partner might say next, “I must really beg you﻿—er﻿—to refrain from﻿—what Mr. Winwood means, Dr. Thorndyke, is that﻿—er﻿—we do not quite perceive the relevancy of these﻿—ah﻿—observations of yours.”

===Chinese===
“好了，好了，温伍德，”马奇蒙特抗议道，为他的合伙人下一句不知要冒出什么话来而心急如焚，“我实在必须求你——呃——克制些，不要——温伍德先生的意思，桑代克博士，是——呃——我们不大看得出来您这些——啊——观察同案子有什么关涉。”

===Original===
“Probably not,” said Thorndyke, “but you will perceive their relevancy later. For the present, I will ask you to note the facts and bear them in mind, so that you may be able to follow the argument when we come to that.

===Chinese===
“多半是看不出来，”桑代克说，“不过往后你们自会看出它们的关涉。眼下我只请诸位把这些事实记下来、放在心上，等到讲论证的时候，你们才跟得上。”

===Original===
“The next set of data I acquired on the same evening, when Dr. Jervis gave me a detailed account of a very strange adventure that befell him. I need not burden you with all the details, but I will give you the substance of his story.”

===Chinese===
“第二批材料，我也是当天晚上到手的，那时杰维斯医生向我详细讲了一场落到他头上的十分离奇的历险。其中的细节我不必一一劳烦诸位，只把他的故事讲个大要。”

===Original===
He then proceeded to recount the incidents connected with my visits to Mr. Graves, dwelling on the personal peculiarities of the parties concerned and especially of the patient, and not even forgetting the very singular spectacles worn by Mr. Weiss. He also explained briefly the construction of the chart, presenting the latter for the inspection of his hearers. To this recital our three visitors listened in utter bewilderment, as, indeed did I also; for I could not conceive in what way my adventures could possibly be related to the affairs of the late Mr. Blackmore. This was manifestly the view taken by Mr. Marchmont, for, during a pause in which the chart was handed to him, he remarked somewhat stiffly:

===Chinese===
接着他便讲起与我到格雷夫斯先生家出诊有关的种种事件，细说当事各人的相貌特征，尤其是那位病人的，连韦斯先生戴的那副极其特别的眼镜也没有漏掉。他还简单讲解了轨迹图的绘制原理，并把图递给听众们查验。三位来客听得一头雾水，说实话，我自己也是；因为我实在想不出，我的这些历险同已故布莱克莫尔先生的事务能有什么干系。马奇蒙特先生显然也持这个看法，因为在图传到他手里的一次停顿里，他有些生硬地说：

===Original===
“I am assuming, Dr. Thorndyke, that the curious story you are telling us has some relevance to the matter in which we are interested.”

===Chinese===
“桑代克博士，我姑且假定，您给我们讲的这段离奇故事，同我们关心的这桩事情多少有些关涉。”

===Original===
“You are quite correct in your assumption,” replied Thorndyke. “The story is very relevant indeed, as you will presently be convinced.”

===Chinese===
“您这个假定完全正确，”桑代克答道，“这段故事实在关涉得很，这一点诸位马上就会信服。”

===Original===
“Thank you,” said Marchmont, sinking back once more into his chair with a sigh of resignation.

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，”马奇蒙特说，无可奈何地叹了口气，重新缩回他的椅子里。

===Original===
“A few days ago,” pursued Thorndyke, “Dr. Jervis and I located, with the aid of this chart, the house to which he had been called. We found that the late tenant had left somewhat hurriedly and that the house was to let; and, as no other kind of investigation was possible, we obtained the keys and made an exploration of the premises.”

===Chinese===
“几天以前，”桑代克继续道，“杰维斯医生和我借这张轨迹图的帮助，找到了他当初被请去的那所房子。我们发现原先的房客走得颇为仓促，房子正招租；既然再没有别的调查法子可想，我们便弄到钥匙，把那所宅子上上下下查看了一遍。”

===Original===
Here he gave a brief account of our visit and the conditions that we observed, and was proceeding to furnish a list of the articles that we had found under the grate, when Mr. Winwood started from his chair.

===Chinese===
说到这里，他简述了我们的造访和我们观察到的种种情形，正要开列我们在炉栅底下找到的那些物件清单，温伍德先生蓦地从椅子上跳了起来。

===Original===
“Really, sir!” he exclaimed, “this is too much! Have I come here, at great personal inconvenience, to hear you read the inventory of a dust-heap?”

===Chinese===
“说真的，先生！”他嚷道，“这实在叫人忍无可忍！我费了好大的周折亲临此地，难道是来听您宣读一堆垃圾的清单的吗？”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled benevolently and caught my eye, once more, with a gleam of amusement.

===Chinese===
桑代克慈和地微微一笑，又一次含着谐趣向我递了个眼色。

===Original===
“Sit down, Mr. Winwood,” he said quietly. “You came here to learn the facts of the case, and I am giving them to you. Please don’t interrupt needlessly and waste time.”

===Chinese===
“坐下，温伍德先生，”他平静地说，“您到这里来是为了了解案情的事实，我正在把它们交给您。请不要无谓地打岔，浪费时间。”

===Original===
Winwood stared at him ferociously for several seconds; then, somewhat disconcerted by the unruffled calm of his manner, he uttered a snort of defiance, sat down heavily and shut himself up again.

===Chinese===
温伍德凶悍地瞪了他好几秒钟；随后，大约被他那份纹丝不动的镇定弄得有些下不来台，他愤然哼了一声，重重地坐下，又把自己关了起来。

===Original===
“We will now,” Thorndyke continued, with unmoved serenity, “consider these relics in more detail, and we will begin with this pair of spectacles. They belonged to a person who was nearsighted and astigmatic in the left eye and almost certainly blind in the right. Such a description agrees entirely with Dr. Jervis’s account of the sick man.”

===Chinese===
“现在，”桑代克不动声色地继续道，“我们来细细考量这些遗物，先从这副眼镜开始。它的主人左眼近视而带散光，右眼则几乎可以断定是瞎的。这样的描述同杰维斯医生对病人的记述完全吻合。”

===Original===
He paused for the moment, and then, as no one made any comment, proceeded:

===Chinese===
他略停了停，见没有人作任何评论，便接着说：

===Original===
“We next come to these little pieces of reed, which you, Mr. Stephen, will probably recognize as the remains of a Japanese brush, such as is used for writing in Chinese ink or for making small drawings.”

===Chinese===
“接下来是这几小截苇秆，斯蒂芬先生，您多半认得出，这是一支日本毛笔的残余，就是蘸着墨写字、或者画小画的那种。”

===Original===
Again he paused, as though expecting some remark from his listeners; but no one spoke, and he continued:

===Chinese===
他又停了停，仿佛在等听客说点什么；可是没有人开口，他便继续道：

===Original===
“Then there is this bottle with the theatrical wig-maker’s label on it, which once contained cement such as is used for fixing on false beards, moustaches or eyebrows.”

===Chinese===
“再有是这只贴着戏妆假发匠标签的小瓶，原先装的是一种胶，是粘假胡子、假髭、假眉毛用的。”

===Original===
He paused once more and looked round expectantly at his audience, none of whom, however, volunteered any remark.

===Chinese===
他再度停住，用期待的目光环视他的听众；然而没有一个人自愿开口。

===Original===
“Do none of these objects that I have described and shown you, seem to have any significance for us?” he asked, in a tone of some surprise.

===Chinese===
“我描述并出示给诸位的这些物件，看起来竟没有一件对我们具有任何意义吗？”他问道，语气里带着几分诧异。

===Original===
“They convey nothing to me,” said Mr. Marchmont, glancing at his partner, who shook his head like a restive horse.

===Chinese===
“它们对我说不来任何东西，”马奇蒙特先生说，一面瞧他的合伙人，那人像匹闹脾气的马似的直摇头。

===Original===
“Nor to you, Mr. Stephen?”

===Chinese===
“您呢，斯蒂芬先生，也一无所获？”

===Original===
“No,” replied Stephen. “Under the existing circumstances they convey no reasonable suggestion to me.”

===Chinese===
“不，”斯蒂芬答道，“在眼下这些情形之下，它们给不出我任何说得通的提示。”

===Original===
Thorndyke hesitated as if he were half inclined to say something more; then, with a slight shrug, he turned over his notes and resumed:

===Chinese===
桑代克迟疑了一下，仿佛还想多说半句；随后微微一耸肩，翻过他的笔记，重新开口：

===Original===
“The next group of new facts is concerned with the signatures of the recent cheques. We have photographed them and placed them together for the purpose of comparison and analysis.”

===Chinese===
“下一组新事实，关系到近期那些支票的签名。我们把它们翻拍了下来，并排放在一起，以供比较和分析。”

===Original===
“I am not prepared to question the signatures,” said Winwood. “We have had a highly expert opinion, which would override ours in a court of law even if we differed from it; which I think we do not.”

===Chinese===
“我不预备质疑那些签名，”温伍德说，“我们已经得到过一份极为内行的专家意见，即便我们同它相左，到了法庭上它也会压倒我们的；何况我想我们并没有异议。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Marchmont; “that is so. I think we must accept the signatures, especially as that of the will has been proved, beyond any question to be authentic.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”马奇蒙特说，“是这样。我想我们只好接受这些签名，何况遗嘱上的那个签名已被证明千真万确出自本人手笔。”

===Original===
“Very well,” agreed Thorndyke; “we will pass over the signatures. Then we have some further evidence in regard to the spectacles, which serves to verify our conclusions respecting them.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”桑代克表示同意，“签名我们跳过去。其次，关于那副眼镜，我们还有一点进一步的证据，可用以验证我们同它有关的结论。”

===Original===
“Perhaps,” said Marchmont, “we might pass over that, too, as we do not seem to have reached any conclusions.”

===Chinese===
“或许，”马奇蒙特说，“这一项我们也可以跳过去，因为我们似乎并没有得出过什么结论。”

===Original===
“As you please,” said Thorndyke. “It is important, but we can reserve it for verification. The next item will interest you more, I think. It is the signed and witnessed statement of Samuel Wilkins, the driver of the cab in which the deceased came home to the inn on the evening of his death.”

===Chinese===
“悉听尊便，”桑代克说，“这一项很重要，但我们可以留到验证的时候再说。我想，下一件东西诸位会更有兴致。那是塞缪尔·威尔金斯签名并经见证的陈述书——死者身故当天傍晚，载他回客栈的那辆马车的车夫。”

===Original===
My colleague was right. An actual document, signed by a tangible witness, who could be put in the box and sworn, brought both lawyers to a state of attention; and when Thorndyke read out the cabman’s evidence, their attention soon quickened into undisguised astonishment.

===Chinese===
我的同事说得不错。一份白纸黑字的文书，签着一个实实在在、可以传上证人席宣誓的证人，顿时使两位律师凝神起来；等桑代克把车夫的证词宣读出来，他们的专注很快就变成了毫不掩饰的惊愕。

===Original===
“But this is a most mysterious affair,” exclaimed Marchmont. “Who could this woman have been, and what could she have been doing in Jeffrey’s chambers at this time? Can you throw any light on it, Mr. Stephen?”

===Chinese===
“可这事真是太玄了，”马奇蒙特叫道，“这个女人会是谁呢？她那时节在杰弗里的寓所里又能做什么呢？斯蒂芬先生，您能给这事透一点光亮吗？”

===Original===
“No, indeed I can’t,” replied Stephen. “It is a complete mystery to me. My uncle Jeffrey was a confirmed old bachelor, and, although he did not dislike women, he was far from partial to their society, wrapped up as he was in his favourite studies. To the best of my belief, he had not a single female friend. He was not on intimate terms even with his sister, Mrs. Wilson.”

===Chinese===
“不能，实在不能，”斯蒂芬答道，“这在我是一桩彻头彻尾的谜。我叔父杰弗里是个死心塌地的老单身汉；他虽不厌恶女人，却绝谈不上喜欢她们的交际，一门心思埋在他那些心爱的学问里。据我所知，他连一个女性朋友都没有。就连他的妹妹威尔逊太太，他也不亲近。”

===Original===
“Very remarkable,” mused Marchmont; “most remarkable. But, perhaps, you can tell us, Dr. Thorndyke, who this woman was?”

===Chinese===
“非常离奇，”马奇蒙特沉吟道，“离奇之至。不过，桑代克博士，您或许能告诉我们，这个女人是谁吧？”

===Original===
“I think,” replied Thorndyke, “that the next item of evidence will enable you to form an opinion for yourselves. I only obtained it yesterday, and, as it made my case quite complete, I wrote off to you immediately. It is the statement of Joseph Ridley, another cabman, and unfortunately, a rather dull, unobservant fellow, unlike Wilkins. He has not much to tell us, but what little he has is highly instructive. Here is the statement, signed by the deponent and witnessed by me:

===Chinese===
“我想，”桑代克答道，“下一件证据会让诸位自己得出看法。我也是昨天才弄到手的，它一到手，我的案子便十分齐全了，于是我立即给你们去了信。那是另一个车夫约瑟夫·里德利（Joseph Ridley）的陈述书；可惜此人是个相当迟钝、不留心观察的家伙，不像威尔金斯。他讲给我们的东西不多，可他那一点点却极有教益。陈述书如下，具结人签了名，由我作了见证：

===Original===
“ ‘My name is Joseph Ridley. I am the driver of a four-wheeled cab. On the fourteenth of March, the day of the great fog, I was waiting at Vauxhall Station, where I had just set down a fare. About five o’clock a lady came and told me to drive over to Upper Kennington Lane to take up a passenger. She was a middle-sized woman. I could not tell what her age was, or what she was like, because her head was wrapped up in a sort of knitted, woollen veil to keep out the fog. I did not notice how she was dressed. She got into the cab and I led the horse over to Upper Kennington Lane and a little way up the lane, until the lady tapped at the front window for me to stop.

===Chinese===
“‘我叫约瑟夫·里德利，是开四轮马车的。三月十四日，起大雾的那天，我在沃克斯霍尔车站候着，刚送下一位乘客。五点钟上下，来了一位太太，叫我赶去上肯宁顿巷接一位客人。她是个中等身量的女人。多大年纪、长什么模样，我说不上来，因为她头上裹着一种针织的绒线面罩挡雾。她穿的什么衣裳我没留意。她上了车，我牵着马走到上肯宁顿巷，又顺着巷子走了一小段，直到那位太太敲了敲前窗叫我停下。

===Original===
“ ‘She got out of the cab and told me to wait. Then she went away and disappeared in the fog. Presently a lady and gentleman came from the direction in which she had gone. The lady looked like the same lady, but I won’t answer to that. Her head was wrapped up in the same kind of veil or shawl, and I noticed that she had on a dark coloured mantle with bead fringe on it.

===Chinese===
“‘她下了车，叫我等着。随后她就走开，在雾里没了影儿。过了一会儿，一位太太和一位先生打她去的那个方向来了。那位太太看着像就是方才那位，可这个我不敢打包票。她头上裹着同样那种面罩或者披巾，我还注意到她身上穿一件深色斗篷，缀着珠子流苏。

===Original===
“ ‘The gentleman was clean shaved and wore spectacles, and he stooped a good deal. I can’t say whether his sight was good or bad. He helped the lady into the cab and told me to drive to the Great Northern Station, King’s Cross. Then he got in himself and I drove off. I got to the station about a quarter to six and the lady and gentleman got out. The gentleman paid my fare and they both went into the station. I did not notice anything unusual about either of them. Directly after they had gone, I got a fresh fare and drove away.’

===Chinese===
“‘那位先生脸刮得干干净净，戴着眼镜，背驼得厉害。他眼力是好是坏，我说不好。他扶那位太太上了车，叫我赶去国王十字的大北部车站。随后他自己也上了车，我便赶着走了。到站大约是差一刻六点，太太和先生下了车。先生付了车钱，两人一起进了车站。他们俩谁身上我都没瞧出什么异样。他们前脚刚走，我后脚就又接了一趟活儿，把车赶走了。’”

===Original===
“That,” Thorndyke concluded, “is Joseph Ridley’s statement; and I think it will enable you to give a meaning to the other facts that I have offered for your consideration.”

===Chinese===
“这，”桑代克收尾道，“就是约瑟夫·里德利的陈述；我想，有了它，诸位就能给我先前提交诸位斟酌的那些事实赋予一个意义了。”

===Original===
“I am not so sure about that,” said Marchmont. “It is all exceedingly mysterious. Your suggestion is, of course, that the woman who came to New Inn in the cab was Mrs. Schallibaum!”

===Chinese===
“这个我可不敢说，”马奇蒙特道，“这一切都玄妙得很。您的意思，自然是说，坐马车来到新客栈的那个女人是沙利鲍姆太太了！”

===Original===
“Not at all,” replied Thorndyke. “My suggestion is that the woman was Jeffrey Blackmore.”

===Chinese===
“完全不是，”桑代克答道，“我的意思是，那女人是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔。”

===Original===
There was deathly silence for a few moments. We were all absolutely thunderstruck, and sat gaping at Thorndyke in speechless-astonishment. Then﻿—Mr. Winwood fairly bounced out of his chair.

===Chinese===
一时出现了死一般的寂静。我们全都如遭雷殛，张着嘴呆望桑代克，惊得说不出话。随后——温伍德先生整个人从椅子里弹了起来。

===Original===
“But﻿—my﻿—good﻿—sir!” he screeched. “Jeffrey Blackmore was with her at the time!”

===Chinese===
“可是——我的——好——先生！”他尖声叫道，“杰弗里·布莱克莫尔当时明明同她在一起！”

===Original===
“Naturally,” replied Thorndyke, “my suggestion implies that the person who was with her was not Jeffrey Blackmore.”

===Chinese===
“那是自然，”桑代克答道，“我的看法本来就包含着：同她在一起的那个人不是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔。”

===Original===
“But he was!” bawled Winwood. “The porter saw him!”

===Chinese===
“可他就是！”温伍德大吼，“门房亲眼看见他了！”

===Original===
“The porter saw a person whom he believed to be Jeffrey Blackmore. I suggest that the porter’s belief was erroneous.”

===Chinese===
“门房看见的是一个他认定是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的人。我的看法是，门房认错了。”

===Original===
“Well,” snapped Winwood, “perhaps you can prove that it was. I don’t see how you are going to; but perhaps you can.”

===Chinese===
“好啊，”温伍德抢白道，“也许您能证明他认错了。我倒要看看您怎么个证法；不过，也许您证得了。”

===Original===
He subsided once more into his chair and glared defiantly at Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
他又一次缩回椅子里，挑衅地瞪着桑代克。

===Original===
“You seemed,” said Stephen, “to suggest some connection between the sick man, Graves, and my uncle. I noted it at the time, but put it aside as impossible. Was I right. Did you mean to suggest any connection?”

===Chinese===
“您先前似乎，”斯蒂芬说，“暗示过病人格雷夫斯同我叔父之间有什么关联。我当时就留意到了，只是觉得绝无可能，便把它撂在了一边。我撂得对吗？您当时是要暗示什么关联吗？”

===Original===
“I suggest something more than a connection. I suggest identity. My position is that the sick man, Graves, was your uncle.”

===Chinese===
“我暗示的不止是关联。我暗示的是同一人。我的立场是：病人格雷夫斯就是您的叔父。”

===Original===
“From Dr. Jervis’s description,” said Stephen, “this man must have been very like my uncle. Both were blind in the right eye and had very poor vision with the left; and my uncle certainly used brushes of the kind that you have shown us, when writing in the Japanese character, for I have watched him and admired his skill; but﻿—”

===Chinese===
“照杰维斯医生的描述，”斯蒂芬说，“这人必定同我叔父非常相像。两人都是右眼瞎、左眼视力极差；而且我叔父写日文的时候，用的肯定就是您给我们看的这种毛笔，我亲眼看他写过，还佩服过他的手法；可是——”

===Original===
“But,” said Marchmont, “there is the insuperable objection that, at the very time when this man was lying sick in Kennington Lane, Mr. Jeffrey was living at New Inn.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”马奇蒙特说，“这里有一个无法逾越的障碍：这人躺在肯宁顿巷养病的那个时候，杰弗里先生正住在新客栈。”

===Original===
“What evidence is there of that?” asked Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“这有什么证据？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Evidence!” Marchmont exclaimed impatiently. “Why, my dear sir﻿—”

===Chinese===
“证据！”马奇蒙特不耐烦地叫道，“哎呀，亲爱的先生——”

===Original===
He paused suddenly, and, leaning forward, regarded Thorndyke with a new and rather startled expression.

===Chinese===
他猛地顿住，身子前倾，带着一种新异的、颇为吃惊的神情打量着桑代克。

===Original===
“You mean to suggest﻿—” he began.

===Chinese===
“您是想说——”他开了口。

===Original===
“I suggest that Jeffrey Blackmore never lived at New Inn at all.”

===Chinese===
“我的看法是：杰弗里·布莱克莫尔压根儿就没有在新客栈住过。”

===Original===
For the moment, Marchmont seemed absolutely paralysed by astonishment.

===Chinese===
一时间，马奇蒙特像是全然惊呆了。

===Original===
“This is an amazing proposition!” he exclaimed, at length. “Yet the thing is certainly not impossible, for, now that you recall the fact, I realize that no one who had known him previously﻿—excepting his brother, John﻿—ever saw him at the inn. The question of identity was never raised.”

===Chinese===
“这是个惊世骇俗的命题！”他终于叫出声来，“然而这事确实并非不可能，因为经您这一提，我想起来了，凡是先前认识他的人——除了他哥哥约翰——没有一个在客栈里见过他。身份的问题从来没有人提起过。”

===Original===
“Excepting,” said Mr. Winwood, “in regard to the body; which was certainly that of Jeffrey Blackmore.”

===Chinese===
“只有尸体那一层除外，”温伍德先生说，“那具尸体确凿无疑是杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的。”

===Original===
“Yes, yes. Of course,” said Marchmont. “I had forgotten that for the moment. The body was identified beyond doubt. You don’t dispute the identity of the body, do you?”

===Chinese===
“是，是。当然，”马奇蒙特说，“我一时忘了这一层。尸体的身份是毫无疑义地指认过的。您不会争议尸体的身份吧？”

===Original===
“Certainly not,” replied Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“当然不争议，”桑代克答道。

===Original===
Here Mr. Winwood grasped his hair with both hands and stuck his elbows on his knees, while Marchmont drew forth a large handkerchief and mopped his forehead. Stephen Blackmore looked from one to the other expectantly, and finally said:

===Chinese===
这时温伍德先生双手抓住头发，两肘抵在膝上，马奇蒙特则掏出一块大手帕来擦额头。斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔期待地看看这个、又看看那个，终于开口道：

===Original===
“If I might make a suggestion, it would be that, as Dr. Thorndyke has shown us the pieces now of the puzzle, he should be so kind as to put them together for our information.”

===Chinese===
“如果我可以提个建议的话，那就是：桑代克博士既已把这道谜题的碎块一一摆给我们看了，就请他行个好，把它们拼拢起来，让我们也明白个究竟。”

===Original===
“Yes,” agreed Marchmont, “that will be the best plan. Let us have the argument, Doctor, and any additional evidence that you possess.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”马奇蒙特赞成道，“那是最好的办法。博士，请把论证讲给我们听吧，还有您手上任何别的证据。”

===Original===
“The argument,” said Thorndyke, “will be a rather long one, as the data are so numerous, and there are some points in verification on which I shall have to dwell in some detail. We will have some coffee to clear our brains, and then I will bespeak your patience for what may seem like a rather prolix demonstration.”

===Chinese===
“这番论证，”桑代克说，“怕是要相当长，因为材料繁多，验证上还有几处我必须讲得相当细致。我们先喝点咖啡醒醒脑筋，然后，对于一场或许显得颇为冗长的推演，我要预先向诸位讨一份耐心。”

## XIV Thorndyke Lays the Mine / 桑代克布下地雷

===Original===
The information supplied by Mr. Samuel Wilkins, so far from dispelling the cloud of mystery that hung over the Blackmore case, only enveloped it in deeper obscurity, so far as I was concerned. The new problem that Thorndyke offered for solution was a tougher one than any of the others. He proposed that I should identify and give a name to this mysterious woman. But how could I? No woman, excepting Mrs. Wilson, had been mentioned in connection with the case. This new dramatis persona had appeared suddenly from nowhere and straightway vanished without leaving a trace, excepting the two or three beads that we had picked up in Jeffrey’s room.

===Chinese===
塞缪尔·威尔金斯先生所提供的消息，至少在我这里，非但没有驱散笼罩着布莱克莫尔案的疑云，反而把它裹进了更深的暗处。桑代克提出来要我解答的这个新难题，比先前哪一道都棘手。他要我把这个神秘女人的身份查实、把名字叫出来。可我怎么办得到呢？除了威尔逊太太，从头到尾没有提到过任何与此案有牵连的女人。这位新登场的角色（dramatis persona）不知打哪儿凭空冒出，随即消失得无影无踪，只除了我们在杰弗里房间里拾到的那两三颗珠子。

===Original===
Nor was it in the least clear what part, if any, she had played in the tragedy. The facts still pointed as plainly to suicide as before her appearance. Jeffrey’s repeated hints as to his intentions, and the very significant preparations that he had made, were enough to negative any idea of foul play. And yet the woman’s presence in the chambers at that time, the secret manner of her arrival and her precautions against recognition, strongly suggested some kind of complicity in the dreadful event that followed.

===Chinese===
同样丝毫不明白的，是她在这场悲剧里——倘若当真有过什么戏份的话——究竟扮演了一个什么角色。各项事实仍旧同她露面之前一样明白地指向自杀。杰弗里屡次三番暗示自己的打算，又做了种种意味深长的准备，这足以排除任何谋害的念头。然而这女人当时在场，来得诡秘，又千方百计提防被人认出，却又强烈地暗示，她同随后发生的那桩可怕的事有某种牵连。

===Original===
But what complicity is possible in the case of suicide? The woman might have furnished him with the syringe and the poison, but it would not have been necessary for her to go to his chambers for that purpose. Vague ideas of persuasion and hypnotic suggestion floated through my brain; but the explanations did not fit the case and the hypnotic suggestion of crime is not very convincing to the medical mind. Then I thought of blackmail in connection with some disgraceful secret; but though this was a more hopeful suggestion, it was not very probable, considering Jeffrey’s age and character.

===Chinese===
可是，在自杀一案里，又能有什么样的牵连呢？这女人也许把注射器和毒药供给了他，但为达到这个目的，她大可不必亲自跑到他的寓所里去。劝诱啦、催眠暗示啦，种种模糊的念头在我脑子里浮来浮去；可这些解释套不上案情，况且拿催眠暗示来解释一桩罪行，在学医的人心里并不怎么可信。随后我又想到同某件见不得人的秘密相关的敲诈；这个猜想倒更有指望些，只是考虑到杰弗里的年纪和为人，可能性也不很大。

===Original===
And all these speculations failed to throw the faintest light on the main question: “Who was this woman?”

===Chinese===
而所有这些冥思苦想，对那个主要问题——“这个女人是谁？”——连最微弱的一线光也没能照上去。

===Original===
A couple of days passed, during which Thorndyke made no further reference to the case. He was, most of the time, away from home, though how he was engaged I had no idea. What was rather more unusual was that Polton seemed to have deserted the laboratory and taken to outdoor pursuits. I assumed that he had seized the opportunity of leaving me in charge, and I dimly surmised that he was acting as Thorndyke’s private inquiry agent, as he seemed to have done in the case of Samuel Wilkins.

===Chinese===
这样过了两天，其间桑代克再没有提起这个案子。他大部分时间都不在家，在外头忙些什么，我毫无所知。更有些异样的是，波尔顿似乎也丢下了实验室，改做起户外营生来。我估摸他是瞅准了留我看家这个机会，又隐隐约约地猜到，他是在给桑代克当私家探员，就像他在塞缪尔·威尔金斯那桩事里显然干过的那样。

===Original===
On the evening of the second day Thorndyke came home in obviously good spirits, and his first proceedings aroused my expectant curiosity. He went to a cupboard and brought forth a box of Trichinopoly cheroots. Now the Trichinopoly cheroot was Thorndyke’s one dissipation, to be enjoyed only on rare and specially festive occasions; which, in practice, meant those occasions on which he had scored some important point or solved some unusually tough problem. Wherefore I watched him with lively interest.

===Chinese===
第二天晚上，桑代克回到家，明摆着兴致极高，而他进门后的头几个举动便勾起了我满怀期待的好奇。他走到橱柜跟前，取出一盒特里奇诺波利方头雪茄（Trichinopoly cheroot）。要知道，这特里奇诺波利方头雪茄乃是桑代克唯一的一项放纵，非到难得而特别喜庆的场合不拿出来享用；落到实处说，也就是他拿下了重要的一分、或是解开了一道格外难啃的难题的那些场合。为此，我兴致勃勃地注视着他。

===Original===
“It’s a pity that the ‘Trichy’ is such a poisonous beast,” he remarked, taking up one of the cheroots and sniffing at it delicately. “There is no other cigar like it, to a really abandoned smoker.” He laid the cigar back in the box and continued: “I think I shall treat myself to one after dinner to celebrate the occasion.”

===Chinese===
“可惜‘特里奇’（‘Trichy’）是这么一头害人的畜生，”他拿起一支雪茄，斯斯文文地嗅了嗅，说道，“对一个真正不可救药的老烟客来说，天底下再没有别的雪茄比得上它。”他把雪茄放回盒里，接着说：“我想晚饭后该犒赏自己一支，庆贺庆贺这个日子。”

===Original===
“What occasion?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“什么日子？”我问。

===Original===
“The completion of the Blackmore case. I am just going to write to Marchmont advising him to enter a caveat.”

===Chinese===
“布莱克莫尔案了结的日子。我这就要写信给马奇蒙特，劝他提出一份知会备忘。”

===Original===
“Do you mean to say that you have discovered a flaw in the will, after all?”

===Chinese===
“你是说，你到底还是在遗嘱里查出破绽来了？”

===Original===
“A flaw!” he exclaimed. “My dear Jervis, that second will is a forgery.”

===Chinese===
“破绽！”他失声道，“亲爱的杰维斯，那第二份遗嘱是一件伪造文书。”

===Original===
I stared at him in amazement; for his assertion sounded like nothing more or less than arrant nonsense.

===Chinese===
我惊愕地瞪着他；因为他这一断语，听上去不折不扣就是一派胡言。

===Original===
“But the thing is impossible, Thorndyke,” I said. “Not only did the witnesses recognize their own signatures and the painter’s greasy fingermarks, but they had both read the will and remembered its contents.”

===Chinese===
“可这是不可能的，桑代克，”我说，“两位证人不但认出了自己的签名，认出了那位油漆匠油腻腻的指印，而且还都读过遗嘱、记得里面的内容呢。”

===Original===
“Yes; that is the interesting feature in the case. It is a very pretty problem. I shall give you a last chance to solve it. Tomorrow evening we shall have to give a full explanation, so you have another twenty-four hours in which to think it over. And, meanwhile, I am going to take you to my club to dine. I think we shall be pretty safe there from Mrs. Schallibaum.”

===Chinese===
“是啊；这正是本案有趣的地方。这是一道非常妙的题目。我再给你最后一次机会来解它。明天晚上我们就得把全部解释端出来，所以你还有二十四个钟头可以考虑。这期间呢，我要带你上我的俱乐部去吃饭。我想在那儿，沙利鲍姆太太多半寻不着咱们。”

===Original===
He sat down and wrote a letter, which was apparently quite a short one, and having addressed and stamped it, prepared to go out.

===Chinese===
他坐下来写了一封信，看样子相当短，信封写好、邮票贴上，便准备出门。

===Original===
“Come,” said he, “let us away to ‘the gay and festive scenes and halls of dazzling light.’ We will lay the mine in the Fleet Street pillar box. I should like to be in Marchmont’s office when it explodes.”

===Chinese===
“来吧，”他说，“咱们这就上‘那寻欢作乐的场面、灯火辉煌的厅堂’去。咱们要把地雷埋进舰队街的柱式邮筒（pillar box）里。它爆炸的时候，我倒希望能亲身待在马奇蒙特的事务所里。”

===Original===
“I expect, for that matter,” said I, “that the explosion will be felt pretty distinctly in these chambers.”

===Chinese===
“说到这一层，”我说，“我料想这声爆炸，在咱们这寓所里也会震得相当真切呢。”

===Original===
“I expect so, too,” replied Thorndyke; “and that reminds me that I shall be out all day tomorrow, so, if Marchmont calls, you must do all that you can to persuade him to come round after dinner and bring Stephen Blackmore, if possible. I am anxious to have Stephen here, as he will be able to give us some further information and confirm certain matters of fact.”

===Chinese===
“我也这么想，”桑代克答道，“这就提醒了我：明天我要在外面待一整天，所以要是马奇蒙特来了，你务必千方百计劝他晚饭后过来，可能的话，把斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔也一并带来。我急于让斯蒂芬到场，因为他还能再提供一些情况，并证实某些事实。”

===Original===
I promised to exercise my utmost powers of persuasion on Mr. Marchmont which I should certainly have done on my own account, being now on the very tiptoe of curiosity to hear Thorndyke’s explanation of the unthinkable conclusion at which he had arrived﻿—and the subject dropped completely; nor could I, during the rest of the evening, induce my colleague to reopen it even in the most indirect or allusive manner.

===Chinese===
我答应了定要在马奇蒙特先生身上使出我劝说的看家本领——其实不等他嘱咐我也会这么做，因为我此刻正踮着脚尖、心痒难搔，一心想听桑代克解释他得出的那个匪夷所思的结论——于是这话题便彻底撂下了；当晚余下的时间里，任凭我怎样转弯抹角、旁敲侧击，也没能诱使我的同事把它重新揭开。

===Original===
Our explanations in respect of Mr. Marchmont were fully realized; for, on the following morning, within an hour of Thorndyke’s departure from our chambers, the knocker was plied with more than usual emphasis, and, on my opening the door, I discovered the solicitor in company with a somewhat older gentleman. Mr. Marchmont appeared somewhat out of humour, while his companion was obviously in a state of extreme irritation.

===Chinese===
我们先前关于马奇蒙特先生的预料果然完全应验；因为第二天早晨，桑代克离开寓所还不到一个钟头，门环就被叩得比平常格外用力，我开了门，看见那位事务律师同一位年纪略长的绅士站在一处。马奇蒙特先生显得有些不痛快，他的同伴则分明是一副怒不可遏的神气。

===Original===
“How d’you do, Dr. Jervis?” said Marchmont as he entered at my invitation. “Your friend, I suppose, is not in just now?”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯医生，您好？”马奇蒙特应我之请进门时说道。“您那位朋友，我猜，这会儿不在家吧？”

===Original===
“No; and he will not be returning until the evening.”

===Chinese===
“不在；他要到晚上才回来。”

===Original===
“Hm; I’m sorry. We wished to see him rather particularly. This is my partner, Mr. Winwood.”

===Chinese===
“唔；真不巧。我们本来有要紧的事想见他。这位是我的合伙人，温伍德先生。”

===Original===
The latter gentleman bowed stiffly and Marchmont continued:

===Chinese===
后面这位绅士僵硬地鞠了一躬，马奇蒙特接着说：

===Original===
“We have had a letter from Dr. Thorndyke, and it is, I may say, a rather curious letter; in fact, a very singular letter indeed.”

===Chinese===
“我们收到了桑代克博士的一封信，而且我可以说，是封颇为古怪的信；说实在的，是一封非常奇特的信。”

===Original===
“It is the letter of a madman!” growled Mr. Winwood.

===Chinese===
“这是一封疯子写的信！”温伍德先生低吼道。

===Original===
“No, no, Winwood; nothing of the kind. Control yourself, I beg you. But really, the letter is rather incomprehensible. It relates to the will of the late Jeffrey Blackmore﻿—you know the main facts of the case; and we cannot reconcile it with those facts.”

===Chinese===
“不，不，温伍德；决没有那样的事。请千万克制些。可是说真的，这封信实在叫人没法懂。它说的是已故的杰弗里·布莱克莫尔的遗嘱——本案的主要事实您都是知道的；我们实在没法把它同那些事实对到一处。”

===Original===
“This is the letter,” exclaimed Mr. Winwood, dragging the document from his wallet and slapping it down on the table. “If you are acquainted with the case, sir, just read that, and let us hear what *you* think.”

===Chinese===
“信在这儿，”温伍德先生叫道，一面把那文件从皮夹里拽出来，啪地一声拍在桌上。“先生，您既然知道这个案子，就请把它读一读，再让咱们听听*您*的高见。”

===Original===
I took up the letter and read aloud:

===Chinese===
我拿起那封信，高声读道：

===Original===
> “I have gone into this case with great care and have now no doubt that the second will is a forgery. Criminal proceedings will, I think, be inevitable, but meanwhile it would be wise to enter a caveat.
> “If you could look in at my chambers tomorrow evening we could talk the case over; and I should be glad if you could bring Mr. Stephen Blackmore; whose personal knowledge of the events and the parties concerned would be of great assistance in clearing up obscure details.
> I am, 

 Yours sincerely,
 

 **John Evelyn Thorndyke**
> C. F. Marchmont, Esq.”

===Chinese===
> “本案我已极其仔细地查究过，现在毫不怀疑：那第二份遗嘱是一件伪造文书。刑事诉讼程序，我想，势将难免；但在那之前，以先行提出一份知会备忘为明智之举。
> “您若能于明晚拨冗莅临敝寓一叙，我们可以把这案子从容细谈；如蒙携斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔先生同来，我尤为欣幸；他亲历诸事，又与有关各方相识，对于澄清若干隐晦细节，当大有助益。
> 您忠实的，

 约翰·伊夫林·桑代克（John Evelyn Thorndyke）


> C. F. 马奇蒙特先生台启”

===Original===
“Well!” exclaimed Mr. Winwood, glaring ferociously at me, “what do you think of the learned counsel’s opinion?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样！”温伍德先生恶狠狠地瞪着我说，“您对这位博学的出庭律师的高见有何见教？”

===Original===
“I knew that Thorndyke was writing to you to this effect,” I replied, “but I must frankly confess that I can make nothing of it. Have you acted on his advice?”

===Chinese===
“我知道桑代克是给你们写了这么一封信，”我答道，“但我必须坦白承认，我自己也参它不透。你们照他的建议办了吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly not!” shouted the irascible lawyer. “Do you suppose that we wish to make ourselves the laughingstock of the courts? The thing is impossible﻿—ridiculously impossible!”

===Chinese===
“当然没有！”这位动辄发火的律师嚷道。“您以为我们愿意让自家变成法庭上的笑柄吗？这事不可能——荒唐透顶地不可能！”

===Original===
“It can’t be that, you know,” I said, a little stiffly, for I was somewhat nettled by Mr. Winwood’s manner, “or Thorndyke would not have written this letter. The conclusion looks as impossible to me as it does to you; but I have complete confidence in Thorndyke. If he says that the will is a forgery, I have no doubt that it is a forgery.”

===Chinese===
“话可不能这么说，您要知道，”我略带生硬地说，因为温伍德先生的态度把我惹恼了，“否则桑代克决不会写这封信。这个结论在我眼里同在您眼里一样不可能；可我对桑代克有完全的信心。他既然说那遗嘱是伪造的，我就毫不怀疑它是伪造的。”

===Original===
“But how the deuce can it be?” roared Winwood. “You know the circumstances under which the will was executed.”

===Chinese===
“可这见鬼的到底是怎么回事？”温伍德吼道。“遗嘱签订时的种种情形，您都是知道的。”

===Original===
“Yes; but so does Thorndyke. And he is not a man who overlooks important facts. It is useless to argue with me. I am in a complete fog about the case myself. You had better come in this evening and talk it over with him as he suggests.”

===Chinese===
“知道；可桑代克也知道。况且他不是个会把重要事实看漏的人。同我争没有用。我自己对这个案子也是一团雾水。你们不如今晚过来，照他提议的那样，同他当面谈一谈。”

===Original===
“It is very inconvenient,” grumbled Mr. Winwood. “We shall have to dine in town.”

===Chinese===
“这实在大为不便，”温伍德先生咕哝道。“我们只好在城里用晚饭了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Marchmont, “but it is the only thing to be done. As Dr. Jervis says, we must take it that Thorndyke has something solid to base his opinion on. He doesn’t make elementary mistakes. And, of course, if what he says is correct, Mr. Stephen’s position is totally changed.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”马奇蒙特说，“可也只有这么办了。正如杰维斯医生所说，我们只能假定桑代克的见解自有扎实的根据。他是不犯低级错误的。当然，倘若他说的是实情，斯蒂芬先生的处境就全然改观了。”

===Original===
“Bah!” exclaimed Winwood, “he has found a mare’s nest, I tell you. Still, I agree that the explanation should be worth hearing.”

===Chinese===
“呸！”温伍德叫道，“我告诉您，他这是寻着了个‘母马窝’（mare’s nest），空欢喜一场罢了。不过嘛，我也承认，他这套解释总还值得一听。”

===Original===
“You mustn’t mind Winwood,” said Marchmont, in an apologetic undertone; “he’s a peppery old fellow with a rough tongue, but he doesn’t mean any harm.” Which statement Winwood assented to﻿—or dissented from; for it was impossible to say which﻿—by a prolonged growl.

===Chinese===
“您千万别见怪温伍德，”马奇蒙特用抱歉的口吻低声说，“他是个火爆性子的老家伙，嘴上不饶人，可并无恶意。”对这一句评语，温伍德表示了同意——或者表示了反对；因为实在说不上是哪一样——他拖长了声音吼了一声，算作回答。

===Original===
“We shall expect you then,” I said, “about eight tonight, and you will try to bring Mr. Stephen with you?”

===Chinese===
“那么我们就恭候了，”我说，“今晚八点前后。您会设法把斯蒂芬先生一道带来吧？”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Marchmont; “I think we can promise that he shall come with us. I have sent him a telegram asking him to attend.”

===Chinese===
“会带来的，”马奇蒙特答道，“我想我们可以担保他准跟我们同来。我已经给他发了电报，请他到场。”

===Original===
With this the two lawyers took their departure, leaving me to meditate upon my colleague’s astonishing statement; which I did, considerably to the prejudice of other employment. That Thorndyke would be able to justify the opinion that he had given, I had no doubt whatever; but yet there was no denying that his proposition was what Mr. Dick Swiveller would call “a staggerer.”

===Chinese===
说过这话，两位律师便起身告辞，撇下我一个人去琢磨我同事那句惊人之语；我也确实琢磨了，只是大大耽误了手头别的事。桑代克必定拿得出根据来支撑他给出的见解，这一点我毫不怀疑；然而无可否认，他这个命题，照狄克·斯威夫勒先生（Mr. Dick Swiveller）的说法，是件“叫人趔趄的事”（a staggerer）。

===Original===
When Thorndyke returned, I informed him of the visit of our two friends, and acquainted him with the sentiments that they had expressed; whereat he smiled with quiet amusement.

===Chinese===
桑代克回来后，我把我们两位朋友来访的事告诉了他，又把他们表露的情绪向他转述了一遍；他闻言含着笑，暗自玩味。

===Original===
“I thought,” he remarked, “that letter would bring Marchmont to our door before long. As to Winwood, I have never met him, but I gather that he is one of those people whom you ‘mustn’t mind.’ In a general way, I object to people who tacitly claim exemption from the ordinary rules of conduct that are held to be binding on their fellows. But, as he promises to give us what the variety artists call ‘an extra turn,’ we will make the best of him and give him a run for his money.”

===Chinese===
“我原就料到，”他说，“那封信要不了一会儿就会把马奇蒙特引到咱们门口来。至于温伍德，我从来没有见过他，不过我听说，他是那种你得‘千万别见怪’的人。大凡这一路人我一向是看不惯的：他们不声不响地自居例外，仿佛那些公认为约束着旁人的行为准则，到了他们身上便可以不算数。不过，他既然答应给咱们来上一段杂耍艺人所谓的‘加演’（an extra turn），我们也就善加领教，让他卖足力气，也叫咱们值回票价。”

===Original===
Here Thorndyke smiled mischievously﻿—I understood the meaning of that smile later in the evening﻿—and asked: “What do you think of the affair yourself?”

===Chinese===
说到这里，桑代克狡黠地一笑——这一笑的含意，当晚再晚些时候我便明白了——随即问道：“你自己怎么看这件事？”

===Original===
“I have given it up,” I answered. “To my paralysed brain, the Blackmore case is like an endless algebraical problem propounded by an insane mathematician.”

===Chinese===
“我已经放弃了，”我答道。“在我这瘫痪了的脑子里，布莱克莫尔案活像一个疯白数学家出的、一道没完没了的代数题。”

===Original===
Thorndyke laughed at my comparison, which I flatter myself was a rather apt one.

===Chinese===
桑代克被我这比喻逗笑了——我自己夸一句，这比喻倒真是相当贴切的。

===Original===
“Come and dine,” said he, “and let us crack a bottle, that our hearts may not turn to water under the frown of the disdainful Winwood. I think the old ‘Bell’ in Holborn will meet our present requirements better than the club. There is something jovial and roystering about an ancient tavern; but we must keep a sharp lookout for Mrs. Schallibaum.”

===Chinese===
“来吃饭吧，”他说，“咱们去开一瓶酒，免得当那位目空一切的温伍德皱起眉头时，咱们的心先吓成了水。我想，霍尔本（Holborn）那家老‘钟’酒馆（Bell）比俱乐部更合咱们眼下的需要。一家古旧酒馆里自有一种快活豪纵的气派；只是咱们得把眼睛放亮些，提防着沙利鲍姆太太。”

===Original===
Thereupon we set forth; and, after a week’s close imprisonment, I once more looked upon the friendly London streets, the cheerfully lighted shop windows and the multitudes of companionable strangers who moved unceasingly along the pavements.

===Chinese===
于是我们出发了；蹲了一星期的禁闭之后，我又一次望见那亲切的伦敦街道、灯火喜人的店铺橱窗，以及川流不息地在人行道上走动的、那一群群可亲的陌生人。
